# Daybook

Private, shared health tracker for Mark and Shelley. Hosted on GitHub Pages from `main`, root.
Live: https://app.mymedicaldaybook.co.uk/ (since v231; the `CNAME` file names it, and the old https://learning-development667.github.io/Mark_Medical/ redirects there with the path and query kept). The website is https://mymedicaldaybook.co.uk (its own repository).

The full name is **My Medical Daybook** (since v89, Mark's decision of 1 October 2026 after a day of name screening found Daybook on its own taken by a journal app and every short health word crowded); inside the app it is simply **Daybook**, because once you are in, it is obviously yours. Named Daybook since v48 (it was Care Log until then; the repository, the Pages path and older notes below keep the old name). Strapline, on the sign-in screen and for any store listing: "Your companion between appointments."
- The full name appears only on the cover and on the two-page reports (since v139: "Created with **My Medical Daybook** · Page 1 of 2" in every report footer, and since v140 "Created with **My Medical Daybook**" right after the report's title in the masthead, the name in Fraunces teal, `madeBy()` and `.titlerow .madeby` in `report.js`; Mark wanted the reports, which go to clinicians and dietitians, to say where they came from): the opener (`.opener-pre` "My Medical" stacked above the big `.opener-brand` "Daybook", and its aria-label), the four sign-in screens (`#signin`, `#signup`, `#nohousehold`, `#noconfig`: `.brand-pre` above the brand in the card), the static `<title>` and the bare `setBrand()` title, `manifest.json` `name` (the store listing), `README.md`, `LICENSE`, and the pitch and user guide when they are written. It is never written on one line in the app's own type; it is always "My Medical" small and tracked over "Daybook" large, so it fits a 320px card.
- Daybook alone is everywhere inside: the topbar (`setBrand()`), `document.title` per page, `manifest.json` `short_name` and the Home Screen title (`apple-mobile-web-app-title`), the PDF file names, the share title, the first line of the copied notes, every button and hint ("Explain with Daybook Assistant", "Create my own Daybook"), the Settings App card. Do not add "My Medical" to any of those.
- The pasted-document header `=== CARE LOG DOCUMENT ===` is a data format and deliberately unchanged.

Speech to text (since v48): `speakButton(textarea)` adds a big "Tap to speak" button under a text box, using the browser's own speech recognition (`SpeechRecognition`/`webkitSpeechRecognition`: Apple's on iPhone, Google's in Chrome; nothing of ours in between, no key, no cost). Words appear in the box as recognised and can be edited like typed text; it turns warm and reads "Listening, tap to stop" while active. Wired under the Note sheet, the Question sheet, every check-in text step. Not rendered where the browser has no support, where the keyboard's own microphone still works.

## WORKING RULE: ask before a big decision (Mark, 22 October 2026)
When Mark asks for a change that could be read more than one way, or that would remove, replace or change the kind of something already there (a tile, a section, a screen, a count turned into an average, a feature taken off), **ask him first, in one short question with a recommendation, before building it.** Do the clearly asked part straight away; stop and ask about anything beyond it. Never widen a request on your own reading of it. Why: on 22 October 2026 "make the at a glance tiles averages" was taken to mean every tile on every report, so counts he relied on (Flare days, Days at 7 or more, Best day and Hardest day) were replaced without asking and had to be put back (v275 to v276). Small, obvious fixes inside what he asked for need no question.

## COST RULE (amended 2026-10-18, by Mark: "cheap, not free")
- **Cheap and pay-as-you-go, never a fixed monthly bill while usage is small.** Mark accepted on 18 October 2026 that a free-only app was not possible once other circles join. Everything stays on the free amounts of each service and is billed only past them: Firebase on the pay-as-you-go (Blaze) plan with a budget alert (on Blaze for some time, Mark confirmed 23 October 2026; the Spark plan would stop answering past its daily allowance and take the app down for everyone), GitHub Pages, one Cloudflare Worker on the free plan (`worker/`, the "bridge"), Resend's free emails. No service with a fixed monthly charge is added while the free amounts would do (Supabase's $25 a month, a rented server and the like were considered and set aside on 18 October 2026; a rewrite to save money not yet being spent).
- **Reads, writes and storage are designed down, not just paid for.** The bridge's reminder run reads one plan document a run and looks at a household only in the minutes around a medicine time (v209; until then every run read every household in full, which would have used the whole free read allowance at seven households). Any new feature that polls or lists is sized the same way: how many reads a day at 300 circles, and at 3,000.
- **Daybook Assistant is capped per circle** (40 requests a day, `EXPLAIN_LIMIT_REAL`; the demo 12 a day), and the uses that are not a report (explaining a letter, reading a label or plan from a photo, suggesting questions) at 60 a month (`EXPLAIN_LIMIT_MONTH`). **A report summary or brief for a clinician is never refused by the month** (v210, Mark, 18 October 2026: a circle must always be able to summarise a report it is sending to a medical professional; `EXPLAIN_REPORT_KINDS`: report, weekly, mood, pain; only the day's brake applies). Paid from the Anthropic Console credit; `GET /ai-usage?key=<BRIDGE_KEY>` shows every circle's month, reports and the rest apart. A bigger allowance belongs to a paid tier later. The app must work fully without the Assistant, and does: the buttons say "not switched on yet".
- Firebase: only Auth (email and password) and Cloud Firestore. No Firebase Storage, no Cloud Functions. Document pages are stored as base64 JPEG strings in Firestore; each page document MUST stay under 900 KB. Letters and appointment recordings are the heavy part of storage (about 80 MB a busy circle); Cloudflare R2 is the place to move them if storage becomes the big line.
- Secrets never go in the public site. Anything that needs a key (the Apple Health bridge key, the Firebase service account, the AI key, the Resend key) lives in the Worker as a secret, set from GitHub repository secrets by `.github/workflows/deploy-worker.yml`. `config.js` still carries only the public Firebase web config.
- One-off app purchases on Mark's phone (Health Auto Export lifetime, for pushing steps and sleep to the bridge) are fine.
- Rough running costs worked out on 18 October 2026, with the bridge as in v209: 500 downloads (about 300 active circles) is about £5 a month, nearly all storage; the Assistant is the one cost that grows with use rather than downloads (a few pence a request), hence the monthly cap.
- History: until 2026-09-24 the rule was "Firebase and GitHub Pages only, no keys, no server"; from then until 2026-10-18 "free tiers only, nothing with a monthly charge" (the Spark plan, one Worker, a one-off Anthropic credit). Mark relaxed it the first time for nutrition lookups and automatic steps and sleep, the second time for other people's circles.

## Licence
`LICENSE` (since v68): **copyright Mark 1 Apps Ltd** since v151 (company number 17510025, registered in England and Wales, 9 October 2026; Shelley and Mark directors). Mark Brown created Daybook and assigned the code, the names, the artwork, the domains and the Mark 1 Apps name and mark to the company by a Deed of Assignment dated 10 October 2026, signed by Mark, by Shelley for the company and before an independent witness, after a written resolution of both shareholders approving it; the originals are kept with the company papers, never in the repo. The LICENSE, README, the terms' copyright paragraph (dated 10 October 2026), About's licence line, the opener and the Settings card all read "Copyright 2026 Mark 1 Apps Ltd"; About still says Daybook was made by Mark Brown, which the deed allows (clause 4). The code is published to be read (the repository has to be public for GitHub Pages, and a web app hands its code to every browser anyway), not copied, hosted or built on; the name, strapline, artwork and the Mark 1 Apps mark are reserved too. Third-party parts keep their own licences (CoFID under the Open Government Licence, the CDN libraries and the fonts under theirs). The notice also appears on the Settings App card, in `README.md` and in the pitch footer. Copyright is automatic; the file removes the "I assumed it was open source" excuse. The LICENSE names "My Medical Daybook (Daybook)". A trade mark application for the full name is a roadmap item for when the app goes to the stores; a descriptive phrase like this is hard to register and hard for anyone else to register, which is part of why it was chosen.

## What was logged and what was written, never run together (v332, 29 October 2026)
Mark, on Notes for the team: a red day read "Diarrhoea 9/10. Family are here. My brother, nieces and nephew..." as if one caused the other. **Rule for every report from now on: a reading or score and a person's words are two separate lines, never one sentence.**
- **Notes for the team, the weeks:** `alertReasons(inWeek)` (the red-day reasons, out of `plainWeek()`), `loggedWords(why, from, to)` and `noteParts(n)` (the words alone from `src.words`, and where they came from: "evening check-in, mood 7/10", "a note with Oramorph", the carer's view). Each entry is { when, logged, words, from, who, text (the old joined form, kept for anything reading it), alert }; `entryHtml()` in `report.js` draws **Logged** (a red tinted strip with the readings) and then **Mark wrote (evening check-in)** with the words in quotation marks. A day with only one of the two gets that line alone; the lead-in and an entry without the parts print their text as before. With Daybook Assistant the Logged line comes from the app's own readings for the entry's days, and the words are headed "From the notes" with no quotation marks (a summary, not a quote); the bridge's `report` prompt now says an entry is only what the notes say, never starting with a reading.
- **Mood and wellbeing:** Best day's words are their own line under the scores (`q`, `qFrom` on the tile: "Mark wrote (one good thing)" on the report, `.tile-quote` "You wrote" on the screen); Hardest day reads "Mood 2, ... Also logged that day: pain at 9 and a temperature of 38.2." rather than running the reasons on as a list.
- **v333, where a score came from** (Mark: diarrhoea 9/10 "with no context"): a symptom on the Logged line says its source: a check-in side effect in its own word, "Diarrhoea severe (side effect after chemo on 23 Oct, evening check-in)" (the stored 9 is only Severe's number), a Symptom tile reading "Diarrhoea 9/10 (Symptom tile, 14:20)" or "(side effect of Chemo, 14:20)" when a cause was chosen.
- **v334, the side effects after treatment as a table** (Mark: "could be in a table to make it clearer", and his diarrhoea logged on the Symptom tile was missing from it): `fxHtml()` in `report.js` draws one table a session (`afterTreatmentLog()` now returns `d.table` { cols, rows, also }): the side effects down the side, Day 1 to Day 3 with their dates across, each cell the check-in's word (mild pale amber, moderate amber, severe red, Not answered in italics, None of these when answered with nothing), and under **Also logged on the Symptom tile those days, score out of 10** a row per symptom logged on the tile on those days ("9/10 at 14:00", "2 times"), not judged as a side effect. Those tile entries are left out of page 1's Other side effects line (`twoPageSideFx(entries, shown)`, `used`). Tested in the scratchpad's `h/t47.cjs` (the rows, Severe then Mild, the tile row with its time, not repeated on page 1, no overflow, 390 light and 320 dark) and `h/t17`.
- Already separate and unchanged: the Mood report's highs and lows (score, words, source), the carer's area, the Pain report's notes (each under its own pain score, which is what it describes), the Crohn's flare log and the Food diary (facts only).
- Tested in the scratchpad's `h/t46.cjs` (a diarrhoea 9 and a family note on one day: Logged holding the readings only, the words on their own line with who and where from, no page overflowing, the Mood tiles on the screen and report, 390 light and 320 dark, no page errors), `h/t3`, `h/t17`, `h/t31` and `h/t32`.

(`docSummary()` still drops an old pasted header.) **v341, Save to my files** (Mark: no AI app wording; anyone who wants their own AI can use the file): a letter has **Save to my files** (`#doc-save-file`, warm tint, under the pages and above the explanation, everyone who can open the letter), one PDF made by `docPdfBlob(d)` with jsPDF: each page photo on its own A4 page (or a text letter's words), then **Plain English explanation** with the box's words (as on screen) and the not-medical-advice line; through `savePdf()` (Save dialog, share sheet or download), named "Daybook {title} {date}.pdf". ## Send waits for the PDF (v335, 29 October 2026)
Mark, on his phone over 4G, tapped Send to Sarah and got "Still making the PDF. Try again in a moment." The report is made in the background as the sheet opens (html2canvas, a few seconds on a computer, longer on a phone), and Send refused until it was ready. Now `sendDirect()` in `openSendSheet()` waits: the button reads **Making the PDF, then sending** (disabled) until the PDF exists, then sends as before; a PDF that failed is made again once (`makeBlob()`, `blobP`) before the error is shown. Send from my own Mail app cannot wait (the share sheet needs the tap itself), so it says "The PDF is still being made. Tap again in a few seconds." and retries a failed one. Tested in the scratchpad's `h/t49.cjs` (html2canvas held back five seconds: one tap, the button waiting and disabled, nothing sent until the PDF existed, sent once, the message, no page errors).
- **v336, the hang itself:** on Mark's iPhone the button then sat on Making the PDF for good. The hidden frames that draw the PDF (`reportFrame()`, `report-frame-*`) carried v329's `sandbox`, and Safari's html2canvas never finished inside it (Chromium did, so no test caught it). They are no longer sandboxed; a report still cannot run script, because a `srcdoc` frame takes the app's Content Security Policy, which allows no inline script or handler (`h/t50.cjs`: an inline script and an `onerror` in such a frame both blocked). The viewer the person sees keeps its sandbox. Each page's drawing is also capped at 45 seconds in `reportPdfBlob()`, so a stuck page is reported ("the phone took too long drawing page 2") rather than freezing. Not tried on an iPhone from here.

## Security review, part 2 fixed (v329, 29 October 2026)
Mark: "We didn't finish the security review!!!!" A Fable 5.1 reviewer read the app's own code (read only); I checked each finding against the code and fixed these here.
- **PDF letters (high).** pdf.js 3.11.174 runs code from a crafted PDF's font (CVE-2024-4367) unless told not to: `getDocument({ data, isEvalSupported: false })`. Moving to pdf.js 4 is for later.
- **The libraries are ours now:** Chart.js 4.4.1, pdf.js 3.11.174 (and its worker), mammoth 1.6.0, jsPDF 2.5.1, html2canvas 1.4.1 and JSZip 3.10.1 are kept in `vendor/` at fixed versions with their licences beside them, taken from the npm registry (`CDN` in `scripts.js` now names `vendor/` paths; `CDN.jszip` replaced the export's own address). Nothing but Google's Firebase code (gstatic) and Google Fonts comes from outside, and the iPhone app gets the libraries offline. The privacy page and the LICENSE say so.
- **A Content Security Policy** (a meta in `index.html`): `script-src 'self' https://www.gstatic.com; worker-src 'self' blob:; object-src 'none'; base-uri 'self'; form-action 'self'`. **Any new script from elsewhere must be added there, or it will not load.** GitHub Pages cannot send headers, so `frame-ancestors` (clickjacking) is not covered.
- **No script can run in a report:** both report frames (`report-frame-*` and the viewer's) carry `sandbox="allow-same-origin allow-modals"` (the hidden `report-frame-*` lost it in v336: it hung html2canvas on iPhone; the CSP still blocks script there); measuring, html2canvas and Print still work. The Mood report's carer heading (`W.carer.title`, built from the carer's name) was the one unescaped field and is now `esc()`d.
- **Signing out clears the phone** (`signOutAndForget()`, `forgetPhoneData()`, `FORGET_ON_SIGNOUT`): the database's offline copy (`terminate()` then `clearIndexedDbPersistence()`, both now imported; the test stub exports them), the words kept as typed, unfinished check-ins, the cached account, the feedback draft and the operator flag; then the page reloads. Settings that say nothing about health stay. Used by Sign out, the no-circle screen and the delete sheet.
- **Smaller:** contact emails are encoded in `mailto:` links; the service worker only opens Daybook's own addresses from a notification; four stray test files left the repository root; the iPhone project's Info.plist says why it asks for the camera, microphone, Photos and speech.
- **Left for Mark's say:** the operator's master key is also his circle's Apple Health key, readable by family (fix: the operator marked by its own secret, done when the iPhone app reads Apple Health itself and Health Auto Export goes); the diagnostic keys in web addresses (done in v331); the app-only author lock (done in v331).
- **v330, `/beta-info` (Mark: "fix this"):** it no longer returns a code's label (a label may be a nurse's name, and the codes are guessable words), only whether it is good; one internet address gets `BETA_INFO_A_DAY` (30) lookups a day, counted in Cloudflare's cache at the nearest data centre (`betaLookupAllowed()`, no database read or write; the 31st gets 429 with a line to email support). The app's banner and the website's Beta box already read well without the label ("Your Beta code is filled in", "Your code is good"). Tested in the scratchpad's `bt/beta_info_test.mjs` (no label or name in the reply, CORS for the website, a wrong code refused, the 31st refused with no database read, another address still allowed). Personal email addresses remain in old Git history; Mark chose not to rewrite it for now.
- Tested in the scratchpad's `h/t44.cjs` (the policy present and nothing refused, a hostile carer name shown as text with no markup run in the sandboxed viewer, Save as PDF from the sandboxed viewer and from Notes for the team, a PDF letter read by pdf.js from `vendor/`, 390 light and 320 dark, no page errors), `h/t3`, `h/t6`, `h/t13`, `h/t16`, `h/t17`, `h/t21`, `h/t29b`, `h/t31`, `h/t40` and `h/t43`.

## The maintenance keys out of web addresses, and the check-in lock in the database (v331, 29 October 2026)
Mark, after the security review: "Yes do 1 and 2 now".
- **Keys in a header only:** `/last`, `/push-test`, `/remind-now`, `/usage` (GET) and `/ai-usage` read their key from the `X-Care-Log-Key` header (`headerKey()`, `isOperatorKey()` with an even-time compare), never from `?key=`, which lands in Cloudflare's logs and browser history. Use `curl -H 'X-Care-Log-Key: ...' https://care-log-bridge.markbrown667.workers.dev/usage` (`?rebuild=1` still in the address). The app never used these. `worker/README.md` says so. Tested in the scratchpad's `bt/keys_test.mjs` (a key in the address refused, a wrong header refused, the right header let through, for each route).
- **The check-in lock in `firestore.rules`:** every entry the app writes carries the writer's sign-in id, `uid` (`addEntry()`, `saveCheckin()`, which keeps an existing stamp; `myUid()`). The rules (`match /entries`): a new entry may carry no stamp or the writer's own; on update the stamp may be kept or set to the writer's own; a check-in (`type == 'checkin'`) with a stamp can be updated or deleted only by that person; nothing turns into a check-in or stops being one (`staysWhatItWas()`). Ordinary entries stay shared, so Shelley can still fix your food entry; check-ins saved before v331 carry no stamp and stay as they were. In the app `ownsCheckin()` goes by the stamp when there is one (else the name, as v208), and **Fix the words** is not offered on someone else's check-in (`fixWordsLink()`, the sources carry `uid`). Tested on the emulator (`tests/rules/households.test.mjs`, nine of nine, the new case: the author changes and deletes their own, anyone else cannot change, overwrite or delete it, nobody stamps a new entry with someone else's id, ordinary entries stay shared, no type change into or out of a check-in, an unstamped check-in left as it was, read only and other circles refused) and in the scratchpad's `h/t45.cjs` (a stand-in sign-in: Edit on Mark's own card only, a card stamped as Shelley's locked even with Mark's name on it, a new note carrying Mark's uid, 390 light and 320 dark, no page errors), `h/t3`, `h/t7`, `h/t16`, `h/t21`, `h/t31`, `h/t42` and `h/t44`.
- **Publish the rules** with the Actions tab's Publish database rules workflow (real project). Old and new apps both work with the new rules, so the order does not matter.

## Save on the new contact, and closing asks first (v326, 28 October 2026)

In the contacts sheet (`openContactsSheet()`), the new block at the top carries its own **Save contact** button (`.contact-save`, straight under the Email box; it saves the whole list, the same `save()` as the foot's Save). ## The keyboard no longer covers a sheet's words box (v315, 25 October 2026)
Mark, on his iPhone with the evening check-in's "How was today, really?" box: the keyboard sat over the box and he could not see what he typed. `keepSheetAboveKeyboard()` worked the keyboard's height out as `window.innerHeight` less the visible viewport; on his phone the Home Screen app shrinks `innerHeight` with the keyboard while the fixed sheet stays the screen's full height, so the lift came out 0 and the box was never scrolled into view. The height is now the sheet's own laid-out height (`layoutH()`: the larger of `innerHeight`, `#sheet`'s height and the document's), and a focused box is brought into view at 320 ms and again at 700 ms. Not tried on an iPhone from here (no WebKit on the build machine): Mark to check on his phone.

## Foods read as meant: flavours, brands, products with no figures (v302, 23 October 2026)
Mark, after v301: what else could go wrong with food, since it matters most for Crohn's users. A check in the preview found real faults, and a search found no published fix (Open Food Facts' own data-quality pages say serving sizes are free text and often missing; app reviews favour no match over a wrong one). Mark: "give your answers a go", feedback to follow.
- **Flavour words** (`FLAVOUR_CARRIERS`, `FLAVOUR_WORDS`, `FRUIT_FLAVOURS`, `flavourCarrier()` beside `matchFoodText()`): when a phrase ends in a food that comes in flavours (yogurt, jam, juice, cake, bar, ice cream, tea and the like) and is not known whole, the flavour words and unknown words (a brand) before it are folded into it; a real food before it ("chicken pie") is still matched. A fruit yogurt is the table's "Yogurt, whole milk, fruit" (aliases `fruit yogurt`, `fruit yoghurt`). "Black cherry yogurt" and "Activia cherry yogurt" are one fruit yogurt counted in pots; "strawberry jam on toast" is jam and toast, no strawberries. `pieceInfo()` counts the carrier, never the flavour.
- **No guessed spellings inside a longer name:** `fuzzyPhrase()` runs only for a food typed on its own, so "Muller" is never mullet. Two or more unknown words beside nothing but a flavour ("Muller Corner Strawberry") is a product by name, listed as not recognised rather than strawberries; an unrecognised name of more than two words gets no piece count.
- **Not counted, said plainly:** a food not in the table reads "Not counted in the estimate: ... Scan its barcode, or enter it from the packet." in amber.
- **A scanned product with no figures** is still saved to My foods (`noFigures: true`, never over a saved one with figures), so its name is never split into words; `componentMacros()` leaves it out rather than counting 0 kcal, and its row says so. Entering figures from the packet and Save to my foods clears it.
- **Serving or pack** (`labelSize()`): the label's serving when smaller than the pack is saved with `packUnit: 'serving'` (How many servings, "30 g a serving"); a size saved by hand clears it.
- **My foods found by every word typed** in the search ("cherry yogurt" finds the scanned Aldi one), in any order.
- **Two phones saving My foods at once** (`saveCustomFoods(change)`): each change is merged into the stored list inside a Firestore transaction (`runTransaction`, now imported; the test stub exports it), falling back to the plain write when offline.
- **Teas:** "tea" is also a meal word, so green, herbal, fruit, peppermint, camomile and Earl Grey tea have their own aliases.
- Tested in the scratchpad's `h/t27.cjs` (the cases above, the partial search, serving, no figures, 390 light and 320 dark, no page errors), `h/t28.cjs` (everyday phrases and teas), `h/t25.cjs` and `h/t16.cjs`. Not in What's new yet: wording for Mark.

## A scanned product is counted in pots, not by a word in its name (v301, 23 October 2026)
Mark scanned an Aldi black cherry yogurt and the Food sheet asked "How many cherry", about 8 g each (9 kcal), instead of a 150 g pot: `pieceInfo()` matched "cherry" in the product's name to `PIECE_WEIGHTS`. Now a component that is a My foods product (`food.c` starting `custom:`, a scanned, searched or packet-typed food) never takes a piece weight from its words; `defaultQty()` gives `packQty(name)`: **How many pots** (`packWord()`: pot for yogurts, puddings, dips; packet for crisps and nuts; bar; tin; else pack) at the product's own weight, `customFoods[].packG`, from 1, or, with no weight known, **How much** in grams from 100 with 50, 100, 150 and 200 g chips. The weight comes from the label when a product is scanned or chosen from Packaged foods (`packGrams()` of Open Food Facts' serving size, else its quantity: "150 g", "4 x 125 g" gives 125, "0.5 kg"); `saveCustomFood(..., { g })` stores it and keeps an earlier one when the figures are saved again. **Changing it:** a grams row has **Save 150 g as one pot for next time** (`rememberPackSize()`, following the weight typed), and a pot row has **Not 150 g a pot? Weigh it in grams**. Products saved before v301 have no weight until one is saved that way or the product is scanned again. Tested in the scratchpad's `h/t25.cjs` (no cherries, the grams row and its chips, 150 g saved as one pot, How many pots, 165 kcal, the timeline's "1 pot", back to grams, strawberries still counted, 390 light and 320 dark, no page errors) and `h/t16.cjs`.

## Swipe down to close a sheet (v300, 23 October 2026)
Mark: the bar at the top of every sheet looked like a swipe-down-to-close bar and did nothing. Now it is one (`swipeSheetDown()` beside `closeSheet()`): a finger that starts on the bar or the title (the top 88px of the panel), with the sheet scrolled to its top, drags the sheet down; past 90px or a quick flick it closes through `closeSheet()`, so a guard (the Food sheet's "not saved yet") still asks; shorter springs back. Starting lower in the sheet, or with it scrolled, is left alone. Tested in the scratchpad's `h/t24.cjs` (touch through CDP: short drag back, bar and title closing, a drag on the text box not closing, the Food sheet with a food asking, no page errors).

## Naps on the Sleep tile (v297, 23 October 2026)
Mark: "We need to add a nap function to the sleep tile." Asked and answered: kept **separate** from the night. The Sleep sheet opens with a switch, **Last night | A nap** (`sleepKindSwitch()`, `.sleep-kind`, not shown when editing); A nap swaps to `openAdd('nap')`: **Started at** (the time box), **How long?** (15 min, 30 min, 45 min, 1 h, 1 h 30, 2 h, or **Minutes** typed, `#nap-mins`, 5 to 360; empty is Missing information), "That is 45 min, from 14:10 to 14:55." (`addMinutesHm()`), a note. Saved as `entries/{autoId}` { type "nap", value (minutes), note, day, at (the start) }, a type of its own so nothing that reads the night (`sleepOnDay()`, `{day}_sleep`, the short-night flag, the night's hours on every report, the bridge) ever counts it. Timeline "45 min nap", "14:10 to 14:55 · A nap, kept apart from the night" (`.entry.type-nap`, the sleep colour), editable from its "..." (`EDITABLE_ENTRY_TYPES`). The Trends Sleep chart has a lighter terracotta **Naps** part on top of the day's bar (the legend note says so), the sleep table lists naps with their times, and Notes for the team has one teal line, "Naps on 2 days, 1 h 45 min in all; the longest 1 h on 20 Oct. Kept apart from the nights." (short "Naps: 2 days, 1 h 45 min in all"). The viewer role cannot read naps (the rules' allowed types are unchanged). No Guide frame shows the Sleep sheet. In What's new (v298, batch 2026-10-23, Mark: "needs to be on the new card"): "You can now log a nap. Tap Sleep, then A nap, and say when it started and how long it lasted. Naps are kept apart from your night's sleep." Tested in the scratchpad's `h/t23.cjs` (the switch and its pressed state, the nap sheet, an empty length red and not saved, the end time, 44px, no sideways scroll, saved as a nap of 45 with the night untouched, the timeline, Edit filled in with no switch and changed to 60 as one entry, back to the night, the sleep table, the Notes line, 390 light and 320 dark, no page errors); `h/t7`, `h/t4` and `h/t9` pass.

## Download all the records, and Delete (v295, 23 October 2026)
ROADMAP action 8 (Apple's rule for apps with accounts, and the privacy page's promise). Mark wanted it to clear a test circle. More > Settings > **Your records** (`#settings-records`, `syncRecordsCard()` beside `syncHouseholdCard()`; family and read only, never the viewer, the preview or the demos):
- **Download all the records** (`exportRecords()`): JSZip 3.10.1 from cdnjs on first use; every entry, medicine, profile, day, exercise, meal and nutrition record as `records/*.json` (Timestamps as ISO text), `entries.csv` (day, London time, type, who, the common fields, quoted properly), `letters/{date} {title}/page N.jpg` with `text.txt` and `explanation.txt`, `recordings/{day} {question or kind}.m4a` (the parts joined), and a README with the counts. "Ready: N entries, N letters, N recordings, 4.2 MB." then **Save the zip file** (a second tap, since a phone only shares straight after a tap): the share sheet on an iPhone, a download elsewhere, "Daybook records YYYY-MM-DD.zip".
- **Delete this care circle** (the patient, or whoever set it up; `POST /circle-delete { confirm: "DELETE" }`, `handleCircleDelete()`): the circle's invite lookups, its Apple Health key lookup and every member's pointer go first, then every document under `households/{hid}` in rounds of 500 (a kindless all-descendants query, `fs.descendants()`, and one commit, `fs.deleteMany()`; 10 rounds a request under the free plan's 50 outside calls, `bridge/deleting-{uid}` remembering the circle while the app calls again until `done`), then the circle and the caller's own sign-in (Identity Toolkit `accounts:delete`, `deleteSignIn()`). Others keep their sign-ins and land on the no-circle screen. **The operator's own circle (the one holding `BRIDGE_KEY`) is refused**, so Mark's records are never one typed word from gone.
- **Leave and delete my sign-in** (anyone else; `POST /account-delete`, `handleAccountDelete()`): their members record, pointer and push subscriptions, then their sign-in; what they logged stays (the records are the patient's). Someone who runs a circle with others in it is told to hand it over or delete the circle.
- Both open a sheet (`openDeleteSheet()`) saying what goes, **Download the records first** for the circle, a box that must read DELETE (any case) before the red button wakes, progress ("Deleting, 5,000 records so far. Keep this screen open."), then sign out and a message. The privacy page says so (dated 23 October 2026).
- **Sign-up race fixed** (Mark, the same afternoon: after Create an account and the set-up, "not in a care circle yet" was still on screen over the app): `createUserWithEmailAndPassword` fires the sign-in listener, whose check ran before the bridge had made the circle and showed `#nohousehold`; the sign-up then opened the app without hiding it. The listener now waits while `state.creatingCircle` is set, `enterApp()` hides `#signup` and `#nohousehold` whenever it opens the app, and `showNoHousehold()` ignores a late check for a sign-in already in a circle.
- Tested in the scratchpad's `bridge/delete_test.mjs` (a carer refused, a wrong word refused, the operator's circle refused, 6,000 records plus pages and parts deleted in two rounds with 31 outside calls in the first, lookups and pointers gone first, the sign-in only when done and only the caller's, another circle untouched; leaving: the owner and the patient told to hand over, a family member's record, pointer and phone gone with their entry kept and their sign-in deleted), `h/t21.cjs` (a stand-in signed-in circle: the card, the patient's and the carer's and read only's words with the patient named, the zip's files, CSV quoting and London time, ISO dates, the letter's page whole, the delete box off until DELETE, two rounds, signed out, 390 light and 320 dark, no page errors) and `h/t22.cjs` (sign-up with the listener firing first: the no-circle card gone; fails on v294).

## Reports sent only from a confirmed email, and Beta codes changed (v292, 23 October 2026)
From the v291 review. Mark: "If it helps with security then yes, but the patient carer will need to be told why they are doing this and it only needs to be done once."
- **The bridge** (`handleSendReport()`): `verifyFirebaseToken()` returns `emailVerified` (the token's `email_verified`); a sign-in whose address is not confirmed gets 403 `unverified`, so nobody can sign up with someone else's address and send reports in their name.
- **The app** (`openSendSheet()`): Send to Sam first reloads the sign-in; if the address is not confirmed, the send button gives way to a box in the sheet (`#send-verify`, `.verifybox`): **Confirm your email address, once**, why in one paragraph (the report goes in your name, replies come to your address, so nobody can pretend to be you; you only do this once), **Email me the link** (Firebase's `sendEmailVerification()`, back to the app), and **I've confirmed it, send now** (reloads the sign-in, refreshes its token, sends; "Not confirmed yet" until the link is tapped). Send from my own Mail app stays. A bridge answer of `unverified` opens the same box. Accounts made in the console (Mark's, Shelley's) are not confirmed, so each confirms once on the next send.
- **v293:** Mark's link landed in junk. Once sent, the box's line (amber, `hint-warn`) says it often lands in junk or spam and names the sender (noreply@care-log-3d05a.firebaseapp.com, "Verify your email").
- **v294, Daybook's own confirm email** (Mark: Firebase's arrived in junk, "reads horribly", and said care-log-3d05a): Email me the link now asks the bridge, `POST /verify-email` (`handleVerifyEmail()`): any signed-in member with an unconfirmed address, `VERIFY_A_DAY` (5) a day per sign-in in `bridge/verify-{uid}`; Identity Toolkit makes the link (`accounts:sendOobCode` with `returnOobLink`, so Google sends nothing; a cloud-platform token, `accessToken(sa, scope)` now caches per scope) and its `oobCode` goes into Daybook's own link, `https://app.mymedicaldaybook.co.uk/?verify=CODE`, sent through Resend from "My Medical Daybook <reporting@mymedicaldaybook.co.uk>" with the subject "Confirm your email for My Medical Daybook": the app icon (from the app's address), MY MEDICAL over Daybook, the strapline, "Hello Mark,", why in one paragraph, a teal **Confirm my email** button, the link in words, "If you did not ask for this...", and the company line (`verifyEmailHtml()`, tables and inline styles, plus a text part). Without the Resend key the bridge says `not-set-up` and the app uses Firebase's own email as before. The app reads `?verify=` on load (`readVerifyLink()`): `applyActionCode()`, the address bar cleaned, the sign-in refreshed if there is one, "Email confirmed, thank you. You can send reports to your team now."; a used or expired link says so. The line after sending names whichever sender was used. Tested in the scratchpad's `bridge/verify_test.mjs` (the link asked of Google only, a cloud-platform token, from, to, subject, the link in both parts, the name, the icon, no project id, no em dash, an already confirmed address sending nothing, the sixth of a day refused, no key giving not-set-up; `verify_phone.png` is the email drawn at 390) and `h/t20.cjs` (390 light and 320 dark: the bridge asked and not Firebase, the sender named with the junk warning, the fallback, `?verify=CODE` applied with the address cleaned and the message, a used link explained, no page errors). **v296:** Mark's Apple Mail showed a grey box for the logo (it blocks pictures fetched from the internet for a new sender or in junk), so the icon now travels inside the email: `icons/email-logo.jpg` (144px, 4.7 KB) as `EMAIL_LOGO_JPG` in the worker, an inline Resend attachment with `content_id: 'daybook-logo'`, `<img src="cid:daybook-logo" alt="Daybook">`. The password reset email is still Firebase's (Mark to set the project's public-facing name to My Medical Daybook in the Firebase console).
- **Beta codes changed:** the Household admin workflow has **`beta-rotate`** (CODE "NEW1,NEW2", LABEL "Label one,Label two": every other code switched off, these made or switched on, counts printed; the run's log is deleted after, since inputs show in it). Run on 23 October 2026: every old code off, three new ones made; the codes themselves are kept out of the repository.
- Tested in the scratchpad's `bridge/sec_test.mjs` (an unconfirmed token refused) and `h/t19.cjs` (390 light and 320 dark: the box hidden at first, shown on Send with the send button hidden, the words with the address, focus on Email me the link, the link sent, Not confirmed yet, nothing sent, 44px buttons, no sideways scroll, confirmed bringing the send back, no page errors).

## Security review, part 1 fixed (v291, 23 October 2026)
Mark asked for an independent security review before the App Store. A Fable 5.1 reviewer read the rules, the bridge, the workflows and the history (read only); I checked each finding against the code and fixed them here. Mark: "this seems like serious flaws. we need to fix them."
- **Public Actions logs (high).** The repository is public, so every Actions run's log is too, including the env block showing what was typed into a workflow's boxes. `tools/household_admin.mjs` printed member names, Beta codes and labels, and `dose-use` printed every dose of a medicine with its time. **Rule from now on: no tool run from Actions prints a name, a medicine, a Beta code, a label or a time anything was taken or eaten; ids, counts and states only** (`hidden()` masks to two characters). `food-check` and `dose-use` take HOUSEHOLD_ID only (no NAME lookups); `migrate_households.mjs` masks too; the workflow's header says so. The logs of all 16 earlier Household admin runs were deleted on 23 October 2026. The printed Beta codes were switched off and replaced in v292. Moving admin into a private repository is the fuller fix (inputs still show in a public log).
- **Publish rules (medium).** The workflow fell back to the real service account when the demo secret was missing, which could have published the wide-open demo rules to the real project. Each target now takes only its own secret, and `tools/publish_rules.mjs` refuses `firestore.demo.rules` unless the service account is `daybook-demo-34eae`, and `firestore.rules` unless it is `care-log-3d05a`.
- **Bridge paths (medium).** `/member-remove` and `/owner-transfer` spliced the request's `uid` into Firestore REST paths, so `../private/health` reached other documents. `UID_RE` (letters and digits) now guards both, and `loadInvite()` refuses a malformed code.
- **Send to the team (medium).** `/send-report` could email anyone with any sender name and any file. Now: the `to` must be an email saved under Who to contact (`circleContacts()`, `profile/main.calls`, error `not-a-contact`), the attachment's base64 must start `JVBERi0` (`%PDF-`), the file name always ends `.pdf`, and the sender's name must be the member's own (or start with it, letters only).
- **Smaller:** the pre-v75 TRANSITION block is gone from `firestore.rules` (its test now checks the old layout answers to nobody); an unknown sign-in key id refetches Google's keys at most once a minute; a crash in the bridge returns a plain message, the detail kept in the log.
- **Names out of the public notes:** Beta testers are no longer named in CLAUDE.md, ROADMAP.md, the tools or code comments, and Mark's own medicine names and dose patterns were taken out of the notes where they were his (the demo's made-up medicines stay).
- **Still to do:** keys in query strings (`/last`, `/push-test`, `/remind-now`, `/usage`, `/ai-usage`) and Mark's circle using `BRIDGE_KEY` as its Apple Health key (both waiting on Mark, see v329). Part 2 of the review was done in v329; a full-history secret scan on a complete clone (29 October 2026) found no keys or passwords, only the public Firebase web keys and three personal email addresses from before v49.
- Tested in the scratchpad's `bridge/sec_test.mjs` (the real worker with a signed sign-in and Firestore, Google and Resend mocked: a `../private/health` removal refused with the key kept, a path refused by owner transfer, a malformed invite refused, a report to a saved contact sent with its name and file, a stranger refused, a non-PDF refused, `.exe` renamed `.pdf`, a false sender name replaced, a carer sending as herself, junk tokens not refetching the keys) and the rules tests on the emulator (8 of 8).

## Each chart's key under it, and the side effects after treatment on Notes for the team (v289, 23 October 2026)
Mark: "The key to the chart should sit underneath the chart and be inside the same rectangle as the chart", and "the side effects are logged for three days after a treatment so those are what need to be in the report".
- **Keys:** the shared Key cell on page 2 is gone. Every chart config in `twoPageCharts()` carries `legend` [{ c, t, line?, dot? }] (`c` a colour, or 'amber'/'red' for the band shades) and `reportHtml()` draws it as a `.blegend` line under that chart inside its card: Temperature (Raised 37.5 to 37.9, High 38.0 or above), Heart rate (100 to 119, 120 or over), Blood pressure (top and bottom number, top 140 to 159, 160 or over), Oxygen (91 to 93, 90 or under), Sleep (hours asleep, under 5 hours), Pain (the day's check-in average, moderate 4 to 6, severe 7 to 10), Mood (the day's check-in average), Bowels (as v287). Weight has none. With an odd number of charts the last cell is left empty.
- **Side effects after treatment** (`afterTreatmentLog(entries, from, to)` beside `carerWeekLog()`): one row per treatment session whose three days touch the period: "Chemo, 20 Oct", "Worst: tiredness severe, sickness moderate", then "Day 1, 21 Oct: sickness moderate, tiredness severe", "Day 2: none of these", "Day 3: not answered", each day the worse of the morning and evening check-ins' side-effects answers (`sidefx` with `session` that day). Null when nothing in the period was answered, so a report without check-ins is unchanged. Drawn by `carerHtml(R.fx, { cls: 'fxbox' })` (amber edge) on page 2 under the charts; `shrink()` moves the weeks first, then the block (`R.fxTail`, tail kind `f`) to the pages after the second, where it carries on like the carer's. The strip line on page 1 now lists only the side effects not from the check-ins (`!e.fromCheckin`), headed "Other side effects logged." when the block is there.
- **The fuller pain chart** (v290, Mark approved the picture: "That looks good"): the Pain chart on Notes for the team draws the morning check-in's pain in teal, the evening's in ink, the worst of the day dashed terracotta, and the Pain tile's highest reading between the check-ins as red rings (`slotPain()`, `worstDay`, `betweenDay` in `twoPageCharts()`; `renderChart()`'s `se.dash` and `se.hollow`; a `ring` legend item). The pill, its colour and the tiles still follow the day's check-in average (`painDay`), unchanged. Tested in `h/t18.cjs`.

## A drink with food in it counts in both, and a drink can be scanned (v288, 23 October 2026)
Mark: the Drink tile should scan drinks, and a food logged on the Food tile that is a drink should ask its ml: "something like a protein shake would take all the food stats but then also add the mls to the daily drink total". He agreed the list of drink words, that soup counts (jelly and ice cream do not), and that water, tea, coffee and squash stay plain drinks.
- **One entry, counted twice:** a food entry carrying `drinkMl`, with a linked drink `entries/{foodId}_drink` { type "drink", value, note, fromFood }, the same pattern as the water with a medicine (v283): the timeline hides the linked drink (`renderTimeline()`), the food's line reads "300 ml, counted in drinks", the Drinks tile's list and every drinks total and report count the drink, the food totals and the Food diary count the food. Delete takes both (`deleteEntry()` with `foodDrinkOf()`); an edit keeps them in step (`syncFoodDrink()`: changed, added or taken away).
- **What is a drink** (`drinkInfo(name)`): a product Open Food Facts files under beverages, drinks, milks, smoothies, juices, sodas, soups and the like (the bridge's `mapOffProduct()` now asks for `categories_tags` and returns `drink`; a scanned or searched one is saved to My foods with `drink` and its pack size as `ml`, `packMl()` reading "330 ml", "1 l", "50 cl"), or a name with one of `NUTRI_DRINK_RE`'s words (shake, smoothie, milk, juice, latte, cappuccino, flat white, mocha, hot chocolate, cola, lemonade, Fortisip, Ensure, Complan, Build Up, supplement drink, soup, kefir, Yop and the like) and none of `NOT_A_DRINK_RE`'s (cake, biscuits, bars, cereal, pudding, ice cream, porridge, milk chocolate...). The ml offered: the pack size, else `DRINK_ML` (a shake or smoothie 300, soup 300, a supplement drink 200, a can 330, a latte or hot chocolate 250, juice or milk 200).
- **The Food sheet** (second step): **How much did you drink? (ml)** (`#food-drink-ml`, `.food-drink`) under Which meal? for a drink, filled in until typed over, "Type 0 if it was not a drink."; empty is red, past 3,000 Check this. A drink of one thing is sized by its ml (1 ml taken as 1 g, `portions[key].qty` in grams) with no portion row, so the estimate and the totals bar follow the ml.
- **The Drink sheet:** **Scan a barcode** (`foodScanControl()`, with the bridge and a camera): the name and the pack size fill in, a product with figures goes to My foods. A drink with food in it (`drinkInfo().nutri`: the Milk, Juice and Supplement drink presets too) says so under the name (`.drink-nutri`) and is saved as the food entry with its ml (meal from the time, the portion from the ml, remembered as a meal), with "Saved: Milk at 13:10. 200 ml added to drinks." Water, tea, coffee and squash save as drinks as before. An edit of an old drink stays a drink.
- Rules: no change (`entries` and `profile` are family write). The worker deploys with the push. Tested in the scratchpad's `h/t16.cjs` (Water with no line and saved as a drink; Milk with the line, saved as one food with 200 ml, its portion in grams, the linked drink, the drinks total up 200, one timeline row; a protein shake on the Food tile asking with 300, empty red, saved at 350 with the drinks up; edited to 400 with the linked drink following; deleted taking the drink; porridge and chocolate cake not asking, hot chocolate and tomato soup asking; no sideways scroll; 390 light and 320 dark; no page errors), `h/t4` and `h/t8` to `h/t13`.

## Screens and sheets open at the top, and the bowel chart's legend under it (v287, 23 October 2026)
Mark, on his iPhone and Mac: tapping most tiles opened the next place "at the middle or bottom of the screen". **Found on a Mac-sized screen:** at 700px wide and over, `openSheet()` focuses the sheet's first box, and the keyboard helper's `focusin` handler then scrolled that box to the centre (`scrollIntoView({ block: 'center' })`), so the Bowels sheet opened 459px down and the Sleep sheet 93px (the scratchpad's `h/t15.cjs` reproduced it on v286). Now: the first box is focused with `preventScroll`, the centring only happens when a keyboard has really come up (the visual viewport over 120px shorter than the window) and only as far as needed (`block: 'nearest'`), and every sheet's panel is put back to its top on opening (Safari can keep the last sheet's scroll). Pages: `showTab()` already scrolled to the top; it now does so again on the next frame and 80 ms later, and lets go of a button left on the hidden page, since Safari can ignore one scroll made while the old page is still laid out or a flick is still moving. Not tried in Safari itself (no WebKit on the build machine): Mark to check on both.
- **Notes for the team:** the bowel chart's colours are a small legend under the chart itself (`.blegend`: Hard (1 to 2), Types 3 to 5, Loose (6 to 7), Blood), not a line in the key; with an odd number of charts the key takes the half row beside the last chart (`.key.half`) instead of a full-width row, so the demo is back to two pages.
- Tested in the scratchpad's `h/t15.cjs` (seven sheets opening at the top at 1280 wide, after the last one was left scrolled), `h/t14.cjs` (eight pages at the top after scrolling down first), `h/t14b.cjs` (the legend, the key beside the chart, two pages), `h/t8`, `h/t9` and `h/t12`.

## The Bristol types in our own words, and a bowel chart on Notes for the team (v286, 23 October 2026)
Mark asked whether the Bristol Stool Chart is open source: it is not. The 1 to 7 scale was published by Heaton's team at Bristol in 1997; the illustrated chart and its wording are copyright Norgine Ltd. Daybook already drew its own pictures, but its seven descriptions were close to Norgine's words, so `BRISTOL` is now in our own words (Hard lumps, Lumpy and firm, Firm with cracks, Smooth and soft, Soft pieces, Mushy, Watery, each with a fresh line under it). Mark chose not to ask Norgine for permission ("ours is good enough"). **Keep it that way: never copy the chart's own wording or pictures.**
- **The bowel chart** (`twoPageCharts()`, `key: 'bowel'`, "Bowels a day"): only when bowel movements were logged in the period, so everyone else's report is unchanged. A stacked bar a day (`type: 'stack'` in `renderChart()`, new): hard (types 1 to 2) in slate `REPORT_SLATE`, 3 to 5 in teal, loose (6 to 7, or watery stoma output) in terracotta `REPORT_WARM`; a red dot under a day with blood or a black stool (`cfg.dots` with `cfg.dotC`). The pill: "Mostly type 4, loose on 1 day, blood on 1 day", red with blood, amber with a loose day or hard on three or more days. The key adds a line for it. It makes the ninth chart, so the grid is even (ten cells with the key); the extra row can move the weekly notes to page 3 (the preview's demo goes from two pages to three), as the spill rules allow.
- The Guide was retaken (the Bowels sheet in the Crohn's topic, and the Today tiles' chevrons from v282) and copied to the website. Tested in the scratchpad's `h/t13.cjs` (the chart on page 2 with its pill, tone, three colours, the red dot and the key line; nothing overflowing; with no bowels logged no chart and no key line, two pages; no page errors), `h/t3`, `h/t4` and `h/t6`.

Mark: the Food tile should show what was logged for breakfast, lunch, dinner and snacks, and a running total of fats, carbs, protein and the like as food is entered, without extra buttons or tabs. The Food sheet now opens with `foodDayPanel(day, excludeId)` above the search box:

## A medicine taken in water is one entry (v283, 23 October 2026)
Mark, on the Macrogol dose and its "Water with Macrogol sachets 500 ml" line under it: "this should just be one entry. Macrogol sachets taken (user inputs 2) with how much water (user inputs fluids)". A medicine taken in water (`takenInWater(m)`: not a hospital one, and `waterPerOne(m)` from its strength, "in 250 ml water", or its dose and how) now asks on Log now, from the Meds tab or the Medicine tile (`askWaterDose(m)`): **How many this time?** (the stepper and 1, 2, 3 chips) and **How much water was it taken in? (ml)** (`#water-ml`, filled from the number times the water for one until it is typed over; empty or past 3,000 is red), then **Log 2 sachets with 500 ml water**. The dose is saved as "2 sachets, in 500 ml water" and the water the person gave goes to the linked drink (`addMedWater(..., given)`), so every drinks total, chart and report still counts it. The timeline shows the dose only (`renderTimeline()` leaves out drinks with `fromMed`), reading "2 sachets, in 500 ml water, counted in drinks"; the linked drink still shows in the Drinks tile's list and the Food diary, and goes with the dose on Undo and Delete as before. Other time and Change the time or note work as before (the water from the words). Tested in the scratchpad's `h/t10.cjs` (the sheet asking both, the water following the count, 400 typed over, drinks up by 400, one timeline entry with "counted in drinks", the water in the Drinks list, an empty water box red, deleting the dose taking the water, 390 light and 320 dark, no page errors).

## Missing information on every form (v281, 23 October 2026)
Mark, after v280: "do the same for vitals and the other forms". `markMissing(el, on, label)` and `missing(el, why, label)` beside `field()`: the box (its `.field`, a `.bigvalue` row, or a group of chips or rows itself) turns red (`.is-missing`, the v280 colours; a group gets an outline and the red fill) with **Missing information** under it, or **Check this** (`CHECK`) for something typed that cannot be read, a time still to come, a date already taken or an email that does not look right; the message at the top says why; the screen moves to the box; any other red in the sheet is cleared; the red goes as soon as the box is changed (a group: tapped). `missing()` returns `{ hold: true }`, so a getData can `return missing(...)`. Wired into Vitals (nothing typed, a reading that cannot be read, one blood pressure number, an edit with nothing left), Pain and Symptom sliders, the Symptom name, Bowels (the Bristol rows or the stoma output), Food, Note, Question, weight, the save handler's date, the medicine editor's name and dose, How many this time and What was it for, the hospital dose, Change the time of a dose, results, a call, the contacts sheet and the set-up's contact and Crohn's steps, the packs step, a saved meal's name, the day totals, a document's title, the steps sheet, Move to another day, Add sessions, programme items, the answer sheet, Fix the words and the feedback note. Tested in the scratchpad's `h/t8.cjs` (Vitals empty with the temperature row red; a word in the heart rate as Check this with nothing else red; the red cleared on typing; Note, Pain, Bowels, Symptom, Food and Question empty; a contact with no name; 390 light and 320 dark, no page errors), `h/t7.cjs` and `h/t4.cjs`.

## Missing information, in red (v280, 23 October 2026)
Mark: Save on the Sleep tile "took me back to the time I went to bed" because the minutes showed 00 and he had not typed over them, so the time box was really empty (a half-filled time box reads as empty, `validity.badInput`), and it was not clear why. `nightBlock()` now returns `check(required)`: a time box half filled in, or left empty, turns red (`.field.is-missing`: `--warn-bg`, `--warn-border`, `--warn-ink`, the colours of the exercise warning, 8.9:1 light and 9.8:1 dark) with **Missing information** under it (`.missing-note`, `role="alert"`, `aria-invalid`), cleared as soon as it is filled. The Sleep tile needs both times (`check(true)`; the line in the sheet now reads "Not saved yet: fill in the times marked in red, hours and minutes (00 counts), then Save." in the same red); the morning check-in, where the times are optional, stops Next only when the pair is half done (`check(false)` through the step's `wrap._guard`). **Rule from now on: anything that stops a save marks the field itself in red with "Missing information", never only a message at the top.** Tested in the scratchpad's `h/t7.cjs` (a bed box with only the hours typed going red with the words, the line, red gone once filled, then saving; the check-in staying on the sleep step and moving on once filled; 390 light and 320 dark, no page errors).

## "My watch didn't record the whole night" (v279, 23 October 2026)
Mark's watch battery died in the night: Apple Health had a short night and 45 minutes "awake" he never was, and he woke at 07:45. Mark: his own times replace the watch's. On a tracker night, the night block (`nightBlock()`, the check-in's sleep step and now the Sleep tile too, `tracker: tracked ? existing : null`) has a button under the Apple Health line, **My watch didn't record the whole night** (`.night-short`, `aria-pressed`), which opens Went to bed and Woke up (filled with the watch's times) and the how-long-awake question. Saved by `shortNightPatch(day, n, entry)`: `watchShort: true`, the person's `bedAt`, `wokeAt`, `value` (`nightAsleep()`), `awake` (from the answer, else none) and `at`, with the watch's own figures kept as `watch` { value, bedAt, wokeAt, awake, core, deep, rem }; the stages stay (the chart shows the rest as not broken down). Tapped off again, the watch's figures come back from `watch`. **The bridge** (`/health` sleep) reads the night first and, on a `watchShort` night, only refreshes `watch`, so a re-send never overwrites the times given. The timeline says "Times as given: the watch did not record the whole night", `nightSummary()` starts "Watch did not record the whole night", and the Notes for the team Sleep line says how many nights are from the times given.
- **Save says why in the sheet:** Mark tapped Save on the Sleep tile and was "taken back to the entry screen"; nothing had been saved (no times). A Sleep save that cannot save now shows "Not saved yet: add when you went to bed and when you woke up, then Save." in the sheet (`.hint-warn`, `role="alert"`) as well as the message at the top, and moves to the bed box.
- Tested in the scratchpad's `h/t7.cjs` (the line and button, times hidden until tapped, 44px, 07:45 saved as 8 h 40 with the watch kept and no awake, the sheet closing, the timeline, reopening pressed with the watch's figures on the line, tapped off restoring the watch, an empty save saying why and staying open, then saving one night, 390 light and 320 dark, no page errors) and `h/t4.cjs`.

## A saved check-in is never offered as unfinished (v278, 23 October 2026)
Mark, opening the app: it told him yesterday's check-in was unfinished when he had done it. `offerDraft()` ran 1.5 s after start-up whether or not yesterday's and today's entries had arrived, so on a slow start `findCheckin()` found nothing and a kept copy was offered. Now it waits for the recent entries (`state.recentLoaded`, set by `watchRecent()`'s snapshot, cleared in `stopData()`; retried each second, up to 20), and a kept copy of a check-in that is already saved is cleared (`clearProgress()`) rather than offered. The preview is not delayed.

## Counts stay counts (v276, 22 October 2026)
Mark, on v275: "you have made a big change without asking for clarification ... If something is a count then we keep it a count. The averages are for the stats that obviously go up and down like pain scores", and "best day hardest day needs to go back as well". **The rule:** a reading that goes up and down (pain, temperature, heart rate, blood pressure, oxygen, sleep hours, mood score, protein, drinks, bowels a day) is shown as the period's average and coloured by that average; a count (days at 7 or more, doses, evenings felt low, days with blood, times had to go straight away, flare days, fruit and veg days) stays a count with its own colour, as before v275. **Ask before turning one kind of tile into another.**
- Back as they were before v275: Pain's **Days at 7 or more** and **Pain relief** (doses); Mood's **Best day**, **Hardest day** and **Felt low** (or Low days); Crohn's **Blood**, **Urgency** and **Flare days**. Kept from v275: Average pain, every Sleep tile (the average, amber under 5 h), Crohn's Bowels a day coloured by its average, the Food diary's Drinks coloured by its average, and Notes for the team's eight averages (v274, as Mark asked).
- Tested in the scratchpad's `h/t6.cjs` (the four reports' tiles, 390 light and 320 dark, no page errors), `h/t4.cjs` and `h/t3.cjs`.

## Every report's tiles are averages, and Notes for the team uses page 1's room (v275, 22 October 2026)
Mark: "make sure that all the different reports the at a glance are the average readings ... for the duration of the report being chosen ... the colours are always for the average score not the highest; the highest scores will be shown in the charts"; and, with Shelley's weekly review still to come: "ensure the app always creates an efficient report using space to its best".
- **The rule for every report from now on:** the tiles are the period's averages and their colour follows the average; highs, peaks, worst days and counts belong on the charts and in the body. The note under a tile says what was averaged.
- **Pain** (`buildPainReport()`): Average pain (as before), **Worst pain of the day** (the evenings' average; replaced Days at 7 or more), **Pain relief** in doses a day over the period (no colour), Sleep (average, amber under 5 h).
- **Mood** (`buildMoodReport()`): **Mood** average (amber at 4 or under), **Felt low** as the average answer in words (amber from 1.5, the week dots' line), **Worst pain of the day** average, Sleep average. Best day and Hardest day left the tiles (the chart rings the low days).
- **Food diary** (`buildFoodReport()`): protein and energy were already averages; **Drinks** is now coloured by the average (amber at 4 L or more, or under 1 L), the counts of high and low days left the note. Fruit and veg stays a share of days.
- **Crohn's and colitis** (`buildIbdReport()`): Bowels a day average (amber at the usual plus 3), **Blood** as the average grade in words, **Urgency** as the average answer in words, **Tummy pain** average (pain when tummy pain was not asked). Flare days left the tiles (the flare log and the chart triangles carry them).
- **Range words:** every report's range row reads 1 week, 2 weeks, 4 weeks or 8 weeks where it used to say 7, 14, 28 or 56 days.
- **Notes for the team's carer box uses the room there is** (`shrink()` in `report.js`, `R.carerPage1`): page 2 first; when page 2 is full it goes on page 1 under the questions; only when page 1 is full too does it move to a third page. With no new questions the questions block no longer stretches (`.qwrap.nat`), so the box sits straight under "No new questions this time."
- **A page that silently overflowed** (found while retaking the website pictures): `paginate()` (the Mood, Pain, Crohn's and Medicine history reports) judged an item as fitting when it ended above the footer, but on an overfull page that footer is pushed off the paper, so nothing ever counted as overflowing and a Mood report could print as one page with its last weeks cut off. The limit is now the lower of the footer's top and the page's own bottom less its padding and footer. Notes for the team and the Food diary already checked the page's height (`overflowOf()`).
- Tested in the scratchpad's `h/t3.cjs` (with `LONG=1` Shelley's long review lands on page 1 and the report stays two pages, 390 light and 320 dark), `h/t6.cjs` (the four other reports' tiles and their colours, the cancer and Crohn's previews, no page errors) and `h/t4.cjs`.

## The Guide's times in 24 hours (v262, 22 October 2026)
The Guide's frames showed time boxes as "11:10 PM": Chromium draws a time box in the computer's own language, which `--lang` and the context's `locale` do not reach. `tools/guide_shots.js` now launches the browser with `LANG`, `LC_ALL` and `LANGUAGE` set to British English, so they read 23:10. All 73 frames retaken (8 changed: the ones with a time box); the website's copy updated in the same push.

## A Meds card opens by itself once, not on every visit (v261, 22 October 2026)
Mark: "why is Macrogol and Senna always expanded when I go to meds". Both had dose changes, and v215 opened a card on every visit for 14 days after one (closing it was remembered only until the app restarted). Now `medCard()` opens a card for a reason (`medAutoOpenWhy()`: a dose change, a course ending soon, a hospital dose today) once per phone per reason: the reason's words are kept in `localStorage` `daybook.meds.autoOpened` { medId: reason } (`medSeenWhy()`, `markMedSeen()`), the card stays open for the rest of that session (`state.openMeds`), and the next start leaves it closed until a different reason comes (a newer change, a course end). Tested in the scratchpad's `t/t65.mjs` (opened with "Opened because" on the first visit, open through a redraw, closed after a restart, still opens by hand, a newer change opening it again, no page errors).

## Sleep asked simply, for anyone without a watch (v260, 22 October 2026)
Mark: "a very simple version for those not using a health tracker. What time they went to bed, what time they woke up, how many hours that was (we calculate), don't you feel rested, was your sleep interrupted"; then "if they were interrupted they need to be able to say why", both in the check-in and on the Sleep tile, and yes to asking how long they were awake. No tab for tracker extras: Daybook finds an Apple Health night by itself.
- **The night block** (`nightBlock(init, { tracker, ibd })`, beside `sleepOnDay()`): **Went to bed** and **Woke up** (`#night-bed`, `#night-woke`, time boxes) with the hours worked out across midnight ("That is 7 h 45 min in bed, about 6 h 15 min asleep.", `.night-total`, `minutesInBed()`); **Do you feel rested?** Yes, A bit, No (`NIGHT_RESTED`); **Was your sleep broken?** No, Once or twice, Lots (`NIGHT_BROKEN`); when broken, **What broke it?** as chips, several allowed (`NIGHT_WHY`: Toilet, Pain, Feeling sick, Worry or racing mind, Too hot or night sweats, Other, which opens a box for their own words; chips, not a drop-down, since a night is often broken by two things and a sleep report can count fixed reasons) and **Roughly how long were you awake?** (`NIGHT_AWAKE`: under 30 minutes, 30 minutes to an hour, 1 to 2 hours, more than 2 hours, taken off the time in bed at 15, 45, 90 or 150 minutes, `nightAsleep()`). With Crohn's or colitis, Toilet ticked asks **How many times for the toilet?** Once or Twice or more, which answers the morning's "Up in the night for the toilet?" (and No broken sleep answers it as No), so that step is skipped (`nightSaysToilet()` in the wizard's `go()`). A **tracker night** (`isTrackerNight()`: from Apple Health, or carrying stages) shows as a line ("From Apple Health: in bed 22:10 to 07:05, 7 h 8 min asleep.", `.night-tracker`) with no time boxes and no how-long question; only rested, broken and why are asked.
- **The morning check-in's sleep step:** the score slider as before, then the block in place of the old "Roughly how many hours" box; the words box now asks "Anything else about the night? Optional." The check-in keeps `night` { bedAt, wokeAt, rested, broken, brokenWhy, brokenOther, awakeFor, toilet } and `sleepHours` (from the times, to the nearest half hour; a saved check-in keeps its own hours over a tracker night); the review and the card carry `nightSummary()` ("In bed 23:15 to 07:00 · Rested: a bit · Broken lots, by pain and "the dog" · Awake 1 to 2 hours"). On save, `saveNightEntry(day, night, entry)` writes the night to its sleep entry: a tracker night gets only rested, broken, why and how long; an existing typed night is updated; otherwise a new `entries/{day}_sleep` { type sleep, value (minutes asleep), bedAt, wokeAt, awake, rested, broken, brokenWhy, brokenOther, awakeFor } at the wake time (the id Apple Health's bridge merges into, so the two meet on one document). Until v260 a check-in's hours never reached the Sleep chart or the reports; now the night does.
- **The Sleep tile** opens on the same block (filled from the morning's entry), with the tracker's own figures under **Add details from a tracker** (`.sleep-tracker`, a `details`, open for a tracker night); Save with no times says "Add when you went to bed and when you woke up"; a new night is saved as `{day}_sleep` (`data._id`, honoured by the save loop).
- **Set-up:** **Do you use a watch or health app?** Apple Health (iPhone), Health Connect (Android), No (`HEALTH_SOURCES`, `profile/main.healthSource`, full set-up only, after Who to contact; the set-up is now seven steps), with words for each (Health Connect: not yet, it comes with the app store version). It is a starting point only; nothing else reads it yet.
- **Notes for the team:** a teal Sleep line, "Broken sleep on 3 of the 4 nights answered (toilet 2, pain 1). Not rested on 1 morning." (short "Broken sleep: 3 of 4 nights (...)"), from the check-ins' nights and the sleep entries; a period with nights answered and none broken adds "sleep not broken" to the steady line. Facts only.
- The demo's nights on days -4, -3 and -1 carry the answers. The privacy page names the night questions and the health app question (dated 22 October 2026). The Guide's check-in topic shows the times and the chips (two frames). No rules change (`entries` and `profile` are family write). **Not in What's new yet:** the wording is waiting for Mark.
- Tested in the scratchpad's `t/t64.mjs` (390 light and 320 dark: the two times and no hours box; the hours worked out; why and how long hidden until broken; Other's box; asleep with the time awake; 44px chips; no sideways scroll; the review; the check-in's night and 6.5 hours; the `{day}_sleep` entry's value, times, awake and answers; the card; the Sleep tile opening on the same night; Notes for the team's count; a tracker night as a line with no times and no how-long question, its hours untouched; Crohn's Toilet answering the night question, which is never asked, saved as 2; the tile on its own asking for times, then saved across midnight; the set-up step and its Android words, saved; no page errors); `t/t26`, `t/t27`, `t/t40`, `t/t42`, `t/t45`, `t/t48`, `t/t50`, `t/t52` (now seven set-up steps), `t/t61` and `t/t63` pass.

## The Food sheet saves simply, and never loses a food silently (v259, 21 October 2026)
A Beta tester (a Crohn's circle): a lunch was not on the timeline though the app had remembered the meal. With her agreement, the Household admin workflow's new read-only **`food-check`** (NAME, a member's first name, or HOUSEHOLD_ID: entry types, days, times and authors, and saved meals' dates and counts, never what anyone wrote or ate) showed a saved meal created at 14:21:37 and no food entry for it on any day, so Save had been tapped and the entry did not stay (most likely Undo on the "Food saved" message, or the app closed between the two writes). Mark: "Food diary is very important it works simply and correctly for crohns."
- **The entry first, then the meal:** `getData` no longer calls `saveMeal()`; it returns `_remember`, which the save handler runs only after `addEntry()` has written the entry (and never when editing). A meal can no longer be remembered without its food.
- **Never silent:** `addEntry()` catches a write that throws at once (returns `null`; the sheet stays open with everything in it, "Not saved (reason). Please try again.") and a write the database refuses later ("Not saved (reason). Please add it again."; it used to say "It will retry when online", which a refusal never does). The non-edit save closes the sheet only once every entry is written.
- **No Undo on a food save:** the message reads "Saved: Porridge at 12:30. It is on the timeline." (with "for 20 Oct" on another day); a food is changed or deleted from its "..." on the timeline. Every other quick add keeps its Undo.
- **Next, then Save:** the button reads **Next** on the first step (typing or choosing the food) and **Save** on the second (`syncFood()`, `onSaveReady` hands the sheet's button over once it exists), so a tap that only moves on never looks like a save.
- **One tap saves a food the table does not know:** the "picky lunch" nudge used to hold the first Save and show **Save without tags**; now the line ("... is not in the UK food table yet ... It saves either way; add what is in it above for a better match.") shows on the second step as soon as the name is unknown and nothing is typed under What is in it, and Save saves.
- **Closing with a food not yet saved asks** (`state.sheetGuard`, set by the Food sheet; `openSheet()` clears it, `closeSheet(opts)` honours it unless `opts.force === true`): the X, the backdrop or Escape with a food typed shows "Porridge is not saved yet." with **Save it** (second step) or **Carry on** (first step, to say how much) and **Don't save**. Cancel, Save and Don't save close on purpose (`closeSheet({ force: true })`); an empty Food sheet and an edit close at once. Any sheet can set a guard the same way.
- Tested in the scratchpad's `t/t63.mjs` (390 light and 320 dark: Next then Save; saved once with the sheet closed; the message with the name and time and no Undo; the meal remembered; on the timeline; the Creon question still following; an unknown food's line shown with no second button and one tap saving; the X on the first step asking with Carry on, 44px buttons, Carry on going to the second step; Escape there asking with Save it, which saves; Don't save and Cancel saving nothing; an empty sheet closing at once; a Note sheet still closing with the X; a drink keeping its Undo; no sideways scroll; no page errors); `t/t45`, `t/t53`, `t/t54`, `t/t56`, `t/t59` and `t/t61` pass. No Guide step shows the Food sheet. **Not in What's new yet:** the wording is waiting for Mark.

## Fasting today, in the Crohn's morning check-in (v258, 21 October 2026)
A Beta tester (a Crohn's circle) said tracking fasting would be really useful; Mark asked where it should go ("are you fasting yes/no, if yes what type 14:10 16:8") and, after the research (one small 2026 trial of 16:8 in Crohn's, Crohn's & Colitis UK and the BSG on Ramadan fasting), said build it. **One question, not two:** **Fasting today?** in the morning check-in with Crohn's or colitis only (`pack: 'ibd'`, `kind: 'fast'`, after mood and before What are you looking forward to), one row of chips: **No**, **12:12**, **14:10**, **16:8**, **18:6**, **20:4**, **For faith (e.g. Ramadan)** and **Other**, which opens a box for the person's own words ("one meal a day", "5:2"); tap again to clear. Above them a line on what the numbers mean, "Yesterday: Fasting 16:8." when yesterday's morning check-in answered (a one-tap repeat, never pre-selected), and below them a fixed line, the same every time and never set off by what is logged: "If you take medicines with food or during the day, agree fasting with your IBD team or dietitian first."
- **Stored** on the morning check-in as `fast`: `'no'`, the pattern (`'16:8'`), `'faith'`, the words typed for Other (`'other'` with none), `null` when skipped (`FAST_PATTERNS`, `FAST_CHIPS`, `fastKind()`, `isFasting()`, `fastWords()`, `fastText()`: "Not fasting", "Fasting 16:8", "Fasting for faith", "Fasting: one meal a day"; `fastOn(entries, day)`, `fastTally(mornings)`). No rules change (`entries` is family write).
- **Where it shows, as a fact, never judged** (Daybook never says whether fasting helps): the review and the check-in card ("Fasting today: Fasting 16:8"), the card's closed line ("..., mood 7, fasting 14:10"); the Food diary screen, a teal pill under each fasting day's line (`.pill-fast`); the Food diary report, the day's line in the week cards ("Fasting 14:10.") and a point to discuss ("Fasting on 5 days: 14:10 on 4 days, 16:8 on 1 day. Each day's line says which."); Notes for the team, a teal Context line ("Fasting on 5 of the 12 mornings asked: 14:10 on 4 days, 16:8 on 1 day."); the Crohn's and colitis report, a fifth paragraph in the brief, "fasting N days" in the week rows' Also column, and teal dots under the bowel chart on the fasting days (`cfg.dots` in `renderChart()`, below the flare triangles; the key says so). No comparison of bowels on fasting and other days: that would invite reading cause into it.
- **Pagination:** the extra paragraph pushed the Crohn's report's brief past page 1, and `paginate()` failed because the first item could not move. Now the first item on page 1 may start page 2 (page 1 keeps its masthead, tiles and charts); only an item taller than a whole later page fails. This applies to the Mood, Pain, Medicine history and Crohn's reports alike.
- Jo's preview fasts 16:8 while settled, not through the flare, and 14:10 as it eases. The privacy page names the question (dated 21 October 2026). The Guide's Crohn's topic has a step for it (frame 5 of 8). **Not in What's new yet:** the wording is waiting for Mark.
- Tested in the scratchpad's `t/t61.mjs` (390 light and 320 dark: the place in the morning; the eight answers; the hint and the medicines line; no yesterday line on a day with none before; 44px and nothing cut; tap and clear; Other opening its box with the cursor in it; the review in their words; changed from the review; saved as 16:8; the next day's "Yesterday: Fasting 16:8."; For faith saved as faith; the card; Notes for the team's line; the Food diary pills; the Crohn's brief with no judging words; the report's dots matching the brief's count, the key and the weeks, nothing overflowing; the Food diary report's point and day lines; the cancer preview with no question; no page errors); `t/t26`, `t/t27`, `t/t38` to `t/t41`, `t/t43`, `t/t45`, `t/t47`, `t/t48`, `t/t50`, `t/t51`, `t/t53`, `t/t56` and `t/t59` pass.
