## Morning and Night with their own times (v255, 21 October 2026)
Mark, on v254's chips with a screenshot (Morning 08:00, Night 22:00 and an extra 07:00 he had added): "Can we change it to be morning (enter time) night (enter time). We don't need no add extra times." **When do you take it?** is now four rows (`.when-rows`, `.when-row[data-slot]`): a tick and its own time box each, **Morning** 08:00, **Midday** 12:00, **Teatime** 17:00, **Night** 22:00 (`SLOTS`); changing a row's time ticks it; a ticked row is outlined teal (`.is-on`). The Add a time box and the extra-time chips went. Midday and Teatime stay for a medicine taken three or four times a day. A saved time goes to the row its hour falls in (`slotOfTime()`: before 11 morning, before 15 midday, before 20 teatime, else night), the earliest when two share a row, so Mark's 07:00, 08:00 and 22:00 open as Morning 07:00 and Night 22:00. The rest is as v254 (`takenAt`, `times`, `perDay`, Remind me, How many times a day with nothing ticked). The Guide's step was retaken. Tested in the scratchpad's `t/t59.mjs` (four rows with their times and no Add a time; Morning at 07:30 and Night at 21:30 giving the line and saved as such; Edit opening with both ticked at those times; the rest as v254) and `t/shot255.mjs` (old times folding into their rows; no sideways scroll; 390 light and 320 dark).

## When a medicine is taken, and a course that starts later (v254, 21 October 2026)
Mark added a new medicine taken morning and night: "this wasn't an option when I added the drug. It just had my dose as 2 total", and it "was added to my scheduled drugs increasing them to 14 even though I said the course doesn't start until next Monday".
- **Course starts was saved but never read** (it was added in v204 for steroid shading): `activeScheduled()`, the meal prompt, the flare summary and the bridge's reminder plan only checked `courseEnd`. Now `notStarted(m, day)` keeps a medicine whose `courseStart` is after the day out of all of them (the bridge's `planEntryFromDocs()` too, so no reminder until the day; the daily plan rebuild picks it up that morning). The Meds tab lists it under **Starting soon** (`#meds-upcoming-section`, `upcomingCourses()`, `upcomingCard()`: name, dose and how often, the times, "Starts Monday 26 October", Edit), between When needed and Finished courses; on its first day it moves to Scheduled. The course dates' hint says so.
- **When do you take it?** (`.med-when-block` in Edit and Add medicine, straight under the dose, regular medicines only, hidden for a hospital one): chips **Morning 08:00**, **Midday 12:00**, **Teatime 17:00**, **Night 22:00** (toggle; v255 made them rows with their own time boxes and took away Add a time, see above), a teal line "2 times a day, at 08:00 and 22:00.", and **Remind me at these times** (on by default). With no time chosen the old number, now **How many times a day**, is shown instead. Saved as `takenAt` (the times it is taken), `times` (the reminder times, the bridge's field, empty when Remind is unticked) and `perDay` (the number of times chosen, else the box). The separate Reminder times list went (it is the same times). Opening a medicine from before v254 reads its reminder `times` as its times. The open Meds card says "Taken at 08:00 and 22:00" (`medWhenText()`). No rules change (`medicines` is family write).
- The Guide's Medicines topic has a step for it (Senna's Edit, the chips), and the 21 October What's new batch two items. Tested in the scratchpad's `t/t59.mjs` (the block after the dose and before How and when; the four chips; the number box with nothing chosen; Morning and Night giving the line, hiding the box and showing the tick; 44px chips; no sideways scroll; saved as twice a day with takenAt, times and the start; not in Scheduled and under Starting soon with its day and times; on its first day 0 of 2 in Scheduled; Edit opening with Morning and Night pressed; Remind unticked keeping the times with no reminders; When needed hiding the block; no page errors; 390 light and 320 dark); `t/t53` to `t/t56` and `t/t58` pass.

## What's new, and the Beta welcome (v253, 21 October 2026)
Mark, on finding a new tester had joined: should people be told this is a Beta with frequent updates, and what has changed, "only to the cancer or Crohn's that it's relevant to. Do the bundle. Getting loads of messages would get annoying."
- **The list** is `WHATS_NEW` in `scripts.js` (above `renderWhatsNew()`): batches `{ id: 'YYYY-MM-DD', items: [{ text, packs? }] }`, newest first. An item with `packs` (`['cancer']`, `['ibd']`) shows only to a circle with one of them on (`packOn`); without, to everyone. **Bundle changes about twice a week, only things a person would notice, in plain words; Mark approves each batch's words before it ships.** Add a batch in the same version as the change (or the next bundle), never one per tiny fix.
- **The card** (`#whatsnew`, under Provide feedback at the top of Today, a labelled region): the one-off Beta welcome (`BETA_WELCOME`, only while `APP_STAGE` is set) and every unseen item for this circle, "Every change is listed under More, What's new.", and **Got it** (48px), which hides it for good on this phone. For a signed-in member (read-only too), never in the preview, the demo or for the viewer, and only once the circle's profile has loaded (`state.profileLoaded`, set in `watchProfile()`, cleared in `stopData()`), so the pack filter never guesses.
- **Per phone, nothing in the circle:** `localStorage` `daybook.whatsnew` { since (the day this phone first ran v253 or later: batches dated before it are never shown as new, so a new person is not told about old changes), seen (item keys `id:index`, so an item for a pack switched on later still shows once), welcome }.
- **More, What's new** (`#more-whatsnew`, after Guide; everyone, the preview too): a sheet with the welcome and every batch for this circle, newest first, dated.
- The first batch (21 October, everyone): Add a medicine at the top, Remove a medicine, Regular or when needed first, Gel in pumps, water with a medicine counting as a drink. The Guide's More frame was retaken (one more row). Tested in the scratchpad's `t/t58.mjs` (with a stand-in signed-in member and a test list: hidden in the preview; the More sheet's date, items and welcome; the card at the top with the welcome; a cancer circle seeing cancer and everyone items, not the Crohn's one or a batch older than the phone; Got it at 44px, hiding it, still hidden after a reload; Crohn's switched on later showing just its item with no welcome; a Crohn's circle with no cancer item; no sideways scroll; no page errors; 390 light and 320 dark).

## Gel in pumps, Remove a medicine, and Regular or when needed under the name (v251, 21 October 2026)
Mark, with a screenshot from a Beta tester (a Crohn's circle): they had put a when-needed medicine under Scheduled, "didn't realise there was a drop down button saying when needed", added it again as when needed, "can't seem to delete the original one" and could not change the first one to when needed. Mark also asked for Gels as a type with the dose in pumps (her Oestrogel).
- **Gel (pumps)** in `DOSE_FORMS` (`gel`, one "pump", many "pumps"): counted like tablets, so "2 pumps (1.5 mg)" with a strength of one pump ("e.g. 0.75 mg"), asked each time when ticked; `splitDose()` reads "2 pumps" as gel.
- **Remove** (`removeMedicine(m, after)`, beside `openManageMeds()`): a red outlined **Remove** in each Meds card's open part beside Other time (`.med-remove`, in `.med-actions`, so hidden for read-only and viewer; the row wraps at 320px), and **Remove this medicine** at the foot of Edit medicine (`.med-remove-link`, existing medicines only, back to Manage medicines after). It asks first (`confirmSheet`). A medicine with no dose ever logged (`loadMedDoses()`) is deleted; one with doses is set `active: false`, so it leaves the Meds lists and the reminder plan (`touchReminderPlan()`) but its doses stay in Medicine history and the reports, under Stopped, with Undo in the message; Currently in use in Edit medicine brings it back. No rules change (`medicines` is family write).
- **Regular or when needed?** is now two chips straight under the name in Edit and Add medicine (`kindChips`, a hidden `kind` input, "Regular, on a schedule" and "When needed", with a hint that when needed is never counted as missed); the Type select at the foot of the sheet went. Changing a scheduled medicine to when needed was always possible; the select was just out of sight below the hospital tick. The tester can now open the first copy and tap Remove (no doses on it, so it is deleted).
- The Guide's Medicines steps say so (the open card with Remove; Edit's chips and Gel) and the frames were retaken. Tested in the scratchpad's `t/t56.mjs` (the chips under the name and no Type select; Oestrogel added as Gel, 2 pumps of 0.75 mg giving "2 pumps (1.5 mg)", when needed with its own boxes, saved as gel and prn; Remove in the open card at 44px and on screen; a never-logged medicine deleted after the confirm; Oramorph with doses kept as not in use, off the lists, Undo putting it back; Escape keeping it; Remove this medicine in Edit; no sideways scroll; no page errors; 390 light and 320 dark); `t/t53` and `t/t55` pass.

## Water taken with a medicine counts as a drink (v249, 21 October 2026)
Mark: "when medications include fluids add those fluids to the drinks for that day, for example the macrogol. We have been doing it manually." A dose whose amount says how much water it is taken in ("1 sachet, in 250 ml water", the v198 form, or "2 sachets, each in 125 ml water", or the how-and-when "Mix each sachet in 125 ml of water" with the number from the amount) also writes a drink beside it: `entries/{doseId}_water` { type "drink", value (ml), note "Water with Macrogol sachets", fromMed (the dose's id), medId }, so the Today tile, the Food diary, Notes for the team, the Trends chart and table all count it with no change to them (`doseWaterMl(dose, how)`, `WATER_RE`: ml followed by water, fluid or liquid; 10 to 3,000 ml; `addMedWater()` from `logMed()` and the meal prompt's Yes; `addEntry(data, fixedId)` takes the id). A liquid medicine's own ml ("2.5 ml (5 mg)") is a dose, not a drink, and a hospital medicine adds nothing. The message reads "Macrogol sachets, 1 sachet, in 250 ml water, logged, and 250 ml added to drinks". **Kept together:** Undo and Delete on the dose delete its drink (`deleteEntry()` looks for `medWaterOf(id)`); Change the time or note moves it, changes its amount or removes it (`syncMedWater()`); a dose from before v249 gains a drink only if its amount is changed. **Doses logged before v249 are left alone**, since those drinks were typed in by hand; from now on they should not be. The drink itself can be edited or deleted like any drink. No rules change (`entries` is family write). Tested in the scratchpad's `t/t54.mjs` (the amounts read for seven wordings; a Macrogol dose adding 250 ml to the tile, one linked drink named and on the timeline, the message; changed to 3 sachets giving 375 ml; deleting the dose removing it; Oramorph adding nothing; Undo; 390 light and 320 dark; no page errors).

## A new circle starts with no medicines (v248, 21 October 2026)
Mark: "Crohn's users appear to log in and my medication is in their meds and they are unable to log their own." **Why:** since v1 `startData()` ran `seedMedicinesIfEmpty()`, which wrote the starter list `SEED_MEDICINES` into any circle with no medicines; the list was Mark's own from September 2026 (Dalteparin, Co-amoxiclav, Creon, Oramorph...), written when Daybook was only his and Shelley's, and nobody turned it off when other circles joined (v78). No one saw Mark's records: each circle got its own copy of a list in the code. **Now** a real circle starts empty: the seeding and its function are gone, the list is `DEMO_MEDICINES` and only the cancer preview and the shared demo use it (the guide's Meds frames show it; replacing it with a made-up list means retaking them). With no medicines the Meds tab shows one card, **Add your medicines** (`#meds-first`, a line on adding each once, one-tap logging, reminders, the reports and the photo route) with **Add your first medicine** (`#meds-first-add`, `openEditMed(null)`, hidden read-only) in place of the Scheduled and When needed lists (`#meds-lists`). **The copies already made** are removed by the Household admin workflow's `unseed-check` (lists) and `unseed` (deletes): every circle with a `betaCode` (Mark's own has none), a medicine whose id and name are still the starter one, and only when no dose of it was ever logged. ## Send to the team within the free plan's CPU time (v246, 20 October 2026)
Mark, sending Notes for the team to a nurse from the Mac: "No connection to Daybook. Check the signal and try again." **Why:** the app posted the report as one JSON body with the PDF's base64 inside it (a five-page report is about 3 MB), and the bridge parsed it, checked every character with a regex and stringified it again for Resend: about 40 ms of CPU, against the Workers free plan's 10 ms. Cloudflare stops such a request with an error page that carries no CORS header, which a browser can only report as a failed fetch, so the app said "No connection". Nothing was sent. (Every send since v236 made Notes for the team spill onto more pages was at risk; a two-page report sat near the limit.)
- **The app** (`bridgeCall(path, payload, fallback, opts)`): `opts.raw` sends `JSON.stringify(payload)`, a newline, then the raw text, as `text/plain` (the send sheet passes the PDF's base64 there and leaves `pdf` out of the payload); `opts.offline` words the failure ("Could not reach Daybook to send it. Check the connection and try again, or use Send from my own Mail app.").
- **The bridge** (`handleSendReport()`): a `text/plain` body is read as a Blob; only its first 64 KB is decoded to find the JSON line, the PDF is the rest of the Blob (cut at a byte offset, so £ or é in the message cannot shift it), checked by its length (a multiple of 4, under `SEND_PDF_CHARS`) and its first and last 4 KB, and sent to Resend as `new Blob([json before, pdf, json after])`, so JavaScript never walks the megabytes. The old all-JSON form is still taken from an app before v246 (its check of the base64 is now the ends only). Measured: 0.15 to 0.3 ms for a 3.2 MB PDF, against 37 to 41 ms before.
- **An HTML part beside the text** (the bridge only, the same evening): Mark's reports were landing in people's junk mail. mail-tester.com scored a real send 10/10 (SPF, DKIM through `resend._domainkey`, DMARC all pass; no blocklist but Hostkarma's yellow on Resend's shared IP, which is theirs), its two notes being no HTML version and no List-Unsubscribe header (for mailing lists, not a one-to-one email: left out). `reportEmailHtml(text)` beside `SEND_FROM` makes the covering words into escaped paragraphs with line breaks, no images, links or tracking, sent as Resend's `html` beside `text`. The junk folder is then down to a days-old domain sending a PDF: recipients marking it Not junk, and later a DMARC policy of `quarantine` once iCloud and Resend have both sent cleanly for a few weeks. Tested in `bridge/send246_test.mjs` (the HTML part's paragraphs, escaping and nothing else) and `bridge/html_check.cjs`.
- **Rule for any route that takes a large upload** (feedback pictures, photos for Daybook Assistant, recordings if they ever go through the bridge): never `JSON.parse`, regex or re-stringify megabytes in the worker; keep them as bytes.
- Tested in the scratchpad's `bridge/send246_test.mjs` (the real worker with a real signed sign-in, Google, the service account token, Firestore and Resend mocked: the plain text form sent with CORS for the app, Resend's body valid JSON with the PDF whole and the from, to, cc, reply_to, subject and words as before; £, é and a curly quote in the message; the old JSON form still sent; a cut-off PDF, text that is not base64, a broken first line, a bad address and an oversize PDF refused; only the good ones reaching Resend; the handling timed). The worker was pushed before the app, so no phone sent the new form to the old bridge.

## Pain on the NHS 0 to 10 scale, with its bands (v241, 20 October 2026)
Mark: the NHS uses the standard 0 to 10 Numerical Rating Scale with categorical breakdowns, and the pain sliders gave no description. Every **pain** slider now runs **0 (no pain) to 10 (worst imaginable)** (`q.nrs` on the question: morning and evening Pain right now, Worst pain today, Tummy pain or cramps (Crohn's), the carer's Pain today, and the Pain tile; `sliderBlock()` takes `min` 0 for them). As the thumb moves, the band shows under the number (`.pain-band`: **No pain** 0, **Mild pain** 1 to 3, **Moderate pain** 4 to 6, **Severe pain** 7 to 10, `PAIN_BANDS`, `painBandOf()`, `painBandWord()`; teal, amber `--warning-ink`, red `--danger`), a four-cell key under the slider lights the band (`.pain-key`, `aria-hidden`), and the range carries `aria-valuetext` ("6, moderate pain"). Anchors "0 no pain" and "10 worst imaginable". The band is on the review ("6 · Moderate pain"), the check-in card ("6/10 · Moderate pain") and the timeline's Pain reading ("Pain 6/10, moderate pain"). **A 0 is a real answer:** a skipped slider is still `null`, and every reader already tests `!= null`, so 0 counts in averages and charts (the Trends and report pain axes were already 0 to 10; the chart titles now say "0 to 10"). Scores saved before v241 were 1 to 10 and need no change. **Unchanged:** the report limits (amber from 5, red from 7; the NHS's moderate starts at 4, Mark to decide whether amber should follow), and the sliders that are not pain (sleep, mood, appetite, energy, sickness, the Symptom sheet) stay 1 to 10. No rules change. ## Zoom on the report alone in Preview (v239, 19 October 2026)
Mark: pinching the Preview of Notes for the team zoomed the whole screen, and moving round it to read was hard. **Now** the pages sit in their own box that fills the rest of the sheet (`.viewer .viewer-frame`, `height: max(300px, ... - var(--viewer-chrome, 270px))`) and scroll inside it, both ways, and zoom there alone (`viewerZoom(frame, ui)` beside `openReportViewer()`, for every report's viewer): a **− / 42% / + / Fit to screen** row above the pages (`.viewer-zoom`, `#viewer-zoom-out`, `#viewer-zoom-level` with `aria-live`, `#viewer-zoom-in`, `#viewer-zoom-fit`, 44px; steps of 1.25, from the fit (or 40%) up to 250%, `VIEWER_MAX`); **a pinch on the pages** (the frame's `touch-action: pan-x pan-y` stops the browser's own pinch there, two-finger touches are read and the point under the fingers kept still); **a double tap** (to 100% where tapped, again back to fit); Safari's gesture events (iPhone and a Mac trackpad); Ctrl and the wheel (a trackpad pinch in Chrome). On load the frame's body is wrapped in `#viewer-box` (sized by `--bw`/`--bh`) and `#viewer-pages` (826px, `transform: scale(var(--z))`); every one of those rules is inside `@media screen` and the sizes are custom properties, never inline widths, so printing from the viewer is full size and nothing is clipped. Opens fitted to the width (100% on a wide screen). Save as PDF, Send and the fits use their own frames and are untouched. Tested in the scratchpad's `t/t46.mjs` (390 light and 320 dark with touch through CDP: fitted on opening, the box filling the sheet, the pages scrolling inside it, + zooming the report alone with no page scroll and the screen unzoomed, scrolling round inside, Fit to screen, a pinch, a double tap in and out; 1280 with Ctrl and the wheel; printing ignoring the zoom; axe; 44px; no page errors); `t/t40` to `t/t43` pass. Not tried on a phone: the pinch's feel is the thing to check there.

## Check the words before saving, optional (v238, 19 October 2026)
Mark: after Tap to speak, a box before saving that asks you to check how accurate it is, "because I have ended up sending some right drivel"; but optional, since people with dyslexia or reading difficulties might be made to feel excluded and have to trust the process.
- **The setting, per phone, off unless switched on:** More > Settings > **Talking instead of typing** (`#settings-speak`, hidden where the browser has no speech; `#settings-speakcheck`, "Show me what was heard before anything is saved"), and the same tick on the set-up's Talk instead of type step (`#setup-speakcheck`). Kept in `localStorage` `daybook.speak.check` (`speakCheckOn()`, `setSpeakCheck()`, `syncSpeakCheck()`), never in Firestore: it is about the person holding the phone, not the circle. The hint says plainly that leaving it off and trusting the words is fine, and that the words can always be changed in the box.
- **How it works everywhere at once:** `speakButton()` marks a box when speech writes into it (`markSpoken()`, `data-spoken`; the full-screen writing space passes it to its small box). A capture-phase click listener on the document stops any primary button in a sheet (`#sheet .btn-primary`: Save, Save changes, Next, Review, Send, Finish, Save and back to the report) while the check is on and a spoken box on show has words not yet looked at (`uncheckedSpoken()`), and opens **Is this what you said?** (`openSpeakCheck()`, `.scheck`, a dialog over the sheet with the sheet inert): each box's words large under its label (`boxLabelOf()`: the field's label, the week question, or the check-in question), **Read it out to me** (the phone's own voice, `speechSynthesis`, en-GB, Stop reading while it plays, shown only where the phone has a voice), **Yes, carry on** (marks the words seen, `data-spokenOk`, and presses the original button again), **Change the words** (or Escape, or the backdrop: back to the box with the cursor at the end; a typed change then counts as checked, `data-spokenSeen`; more speech marks it again), and a line saying how to switch it off. Typed words are never checked; the set-up's try box is exempt (`data-nocheck`); Save for later on the feedback sheet is not a primary button, so it is not stopped.
- New icon `#i-speaker`. Tested in the scratchpad's `t/t45.mjs` (a stand-in recogniser and voice: off by default with a spoken note saving straight away; the Settings card unticked; the tick kept on the phone; the check stopping the save with the words, the label, focus and the sheet inert; Read it out speaking the words; axe; no sideways scroll; 44px buttons; Change the words back in the box and a typed fix saving without a second ask; Yes, carry on saving as heard; typed words never checked; a check-in step asking at Next with the question as the label and Yes moving on; 390 light and 320 dark; no page errors). `t/t42`, `t/t44` and `t/t37` pass.

Nothing is cut: tested with 14 days of long morning and evening words, four pages, all 28 observations whole, footers "Page n of 4". The copied text and the text for an AI app carry the same section before the notes.

Not tried on a phone.

## Nothing on a report is cut short (v236, 19 October 2026)
Mark: "On all of the reports now, can you ensure that no text boxes are cut short. If you cant read the full content the thing is pointless." **The rule, for every report from now on: text is never shortened, ended with an ellipsis, or left out with "Plus N more in the app"; whatever does not fit moves onto another page.**
- **Notes for the team and the Food diary** (the two fixed-layout reports) used to trim to fit two pages (a sentence off the longest note, the earliest entries dropped, questions and points "in the app"). They now spill: `shrink()` and `shrinkFood()` in `report.js` only ever *move* things. Order for Notes: the weeks move off page 2 (`R.weeksPage3`), then page 1's writing lines go 3 to 2, the questions move one by one (`R.extraQ`), the lines go to 1, and last of all the latest note moves (`R.latestMoved`, page 1 then says "The latest note is in full on page 3"). For the Food diary: the weeks (`F.weeksPage3`), then the dietitian questions (`F.extraQ`), then the points to discuss (`F.extraPts`, one stays on page 1). The moved things are laid out by `tailPagesOf()` / `foodTailPages()` as `.page.tail` sections (ids `page3`, `page4`...), and `spillTail(doc, R)` (called by `fitReport()` once pages 1 and 2 fit) measures every `[data-ti]` item against the page's real limit (`pageLimit()`: page bottom less padding, foot and gap) and moves the first that does not fit, with everything after it, to the next page; a week that is too long **carries on** onto the next page headed "(continued)" (the entries that fit stay). Footers count the real total ("Page 4 of 6"). `overflowOf()` ignores tail pages. A `.page.tail` card has no ruled writing space except the question cards (3 lines).
- **Builders**: `plainWeek()` no longer cuts a note at 220 characters or keeps only six entries a week; `plainLatest()` no longer cuts at 240 (a long first sentence gives the plain title "New or worse symptoms noted" and the whole note below it); `twoPageSideFx()` lists every symptom and every cause; the Food diary's day line names every meal (`foodNames(d.foods, 99)`, not "and 3 more items") and `points` are all kept; the Pain report prints every note under a pain score whole (`WORDS_MAX` and the 900/600 cut are gone; only plain "none" and "nothing new" answers are left out, and the note says how many); the Mood report's Best day tile and the three "own words" quotes are whole (`firstSentence()` went); the IBD report's medicine list names every medicine. Covering emails no longer promise "two pages".
- **Still a choice of the app, not a cut, and unchanged:** Notes for the team shows the most telling note of each day (one a day, whole), the last two weeks as cards with anything earlier as a one-line "Earlier in the period" lead-in; the tiles' "and N more" date lists (a tile has room for three dates; every day is on the charts and in the weeks); the weekly "What came up most" top three; and the Daybook Assistant's own summaries (`report`, `mood`, `pain` kinds ask for a few points, up to six entries a week, short briefs: they are summaries by design, and the plain version is always one tap away).
- Tested in the scratchpad's `t/t40.mjs` (Notes: fourteen long notes, twelve long questions and a long latest note, six pages with every end marker present, nothing clipped, footers "Page n of 6", no ellipsis) and `t/t41.mjs` (Food: eight long dietitian questions and nine meals on one day, three pages; Pain: 24 long notes, four pages, every one whole); `t/t38`, `t/t39`, `t/t26` and `t/t37` pass. `t/t25.mjs` has a date-dependent expectation (its results dates are fixed to the day it was written) and fails on the dates alone.

## The watercolour behind the app (v229, 18 October 2026)
Mark: "we need to add water prints to the pages we can. More page looks like a black abyss." A fixed layer behind every screen in the app (`.app::before`, `position: fixed; inset: 0; z-index: -1; pointer-events: none`), the Firefly garden as a wash: `icons/wash-light.jpg` (the painting lifted toward the page colour) and `icons/wash-dark.jpg` (the same shapes mapped onto deep teal with a warm horizon), both cut from `icons/opener-poster.jpg` (the middle 960 by 720, without the window frames, a light blur, colour lifted), 8 and 17 KB, `background-size: cover` at `34% 50%` like the opener, the dark one chosen by `prefers-color-scheme`. **The rule that keeps it safe:** the pictures are baked so no pixel is darker (light, floor L 0.818) or lighter (dark, ceiling L 0.041) than the limit at which every text colour used on the page itself keeps 4.5:1 or better; measured on the saved files: light, muted 4.85, teal 4.66, success and warning 4.7, danger 5.06, secondary 5.77, primary 12.6; dark, every colour 4.7 or better (danger 4.7, muted 5.3). If the pictures are ever remade, redo that measurement (relative luminance of the worst pixel against each token) before shipping. A lower `--wash` opacity only moves the colours towards the plain page, so it can never break it. **Strength** (`--wash`, set per page by `document.body.dataset.page = name` in `showTab()`): 1 on the hub pages with the most empty page (More, Settings, Guide, Usage, Documents, Reports), 0.7 elsewhere. **Glass cards on the hubs:** on More, Settings, Guide, Usage and Documents the `.card` and `.checkin-row` (not the raised `.is-due` or `.is-flare` ones) are the surface colour at 78%, so a little of the painting shows through the slabs; text still sits on the card colour with a capped tint behind it. Print and forced-colours hide the layer; the report frames are separate documents and are untouched; the sign-in has its own picture. Tested in the scratchpad's `t/t36.mjs` (light and dark: the layer fixed, behind everything and ignoring taps on Today, Meds, Reports and More at 0.7 and 1; Settings and Guide at 1; the right picture per scheme; both files small; a More card 78%; print rule present; no sideways scroll; no page errors) and `t/t28` to `t/t35`.

## The week ends on a day you choose (v228, 18 October 2026)
Mark: his next chemo is on a Friday and he wanted the end of the week for the weekly wellbeing check to be his treatment week. **The setting:** `profile/main.weekEnd`, 0 Sunday to 6 Saturday, Sunday when not set (so a household that never chooses keeps Monday to Sunday, as before). More > Settings has a **Your week** card (`#settings-week`): **My week ends on** (`#settings-week-end`, Monday to Sunday) and, when treatment sessions are on the calendar and the week does not already end on that weekday, **Use my treatment day (Friday)** (`#settings-week-treatment`: the weekday of the next session from today, else the latest one, `treatmentWeekday()` from `sessionDays()`), one tap; saved through `saveProfilePatch()` with "Your week now ends on Friday". The card refreshes when Settings opens (`syncWeekSetting()`), since `syncSettings()` only runs when the profile changes. Disabled for read-only and viewer.
- **What it changes:** `weekEndDay()`, `weekPos(day)` (0 the first day of the week, 6 its last) and `weekBounds(day)` now follow the setting, so `weekCheckFor()` (any week check saved on a day inside the week's seven days counts for it), the sheet's date range ("17 Oct to 23 Oct" for a week ending Friday 23 October), and the row on Today (`renderWeekRow()`): offered over the last three days of the week (Wednesday, Thursday and Friday for a Friday end, "Five minutes, any time up to Friday."), raised on the last day ("Last day of the week. Five minutes."), and earlier in the week only when last week's was started and not finished. The carer's weekly check (`weekcarer`) uses the same week. A check already saved keeps its own day, so changing the end day moves which week it counts for but loses nothing. **Not changed:** the Mood and wellbeing report and Notes for the team count their weeks back from the report's last day (`reportWeeks()`), not from this setting.
- Tested in the scratchpad's `t/t34.mjs` (the clock fixed to a Wednesday: Sunday by default with no row on a Wednesday; the select's seven days; the button naming Friday from a session on the calendar; one tap setting Friday with the message and the button gone; the row now offered "up to Friday"; the sheet covering 17 to 23 Oct; Monday end hiding the row and Thursday end offering it; on the Friday itself the row raised with "Last day of the week"; no page errors) and `t/t35.mjs` (320 dark: no sideways scroll, controls at least 44px). Not tried on a phone.

## Crohn's build 5: the check-ins and the set-up tuned for IBD (v221, 18 October 2026)
Mark approved the proposal ("Build it my friend"). Three one-tap or scored questions with the Crohn's or colitis pack, feeding the Crohn's and colitis report and Notes for the team, and the set-up's Crohn's step asking for the advice line and the regular medicine. The cancer view is unchanged; with both packs on, both sets are asked.
- **Questions** (`CHECKIN_QUESTIONS`): evening **Tummy pain or cramps today** (`gutPain`, 1 to 10, `pack: 'ibd'`, where Sickness sits) and **Did your gut stop you doing anything today?** (`gutStop`, `kind: 'scale'`, the four wellbeing words stored 0 to 3, after Energy); morning **Up in the night for the toilet?** (`nightToilet`, `kind: 'scale'` with its own `words` No, Once, Twice or more, stored 0 to 2, after the overnight symptoms). **Sickness today gives way** with Crohn's or colitis alone (`when: () => packOn('cancer') || !packOn('ibd')`; `checkinSteps()` honours a question's `when`, and an answer already saved is always shown). A `scale` step in the wizard is one row of chips, tap again to clear (`.scale-chips`), read by `scaleWords(q)` and `scaleText(q, v)` on the review, the card and the labels (`CHECKIN_LABELS`); `checkinSummary()` adds "Tummy pain N". Skipped is `null`, as every score.
- **Where they are read:** Notes for the team flags tummy pain like sickness (`scale('gutPain', 'Tummy pain', 5, ...)`: amber from 5, the trend) and adds one **Gut and nights** line (the gut in the way quite a bit or a lot on N of M evenings; up in the night on N of M mornings, twice or more on K; amber only when that was most evenings or most nights twice or more, else teal context, else the green line); the Crohn's and colitis report's Body paragraph carries the average and highest tummy pain, the evenings the gut got in the way and the nights up (`buildIbdReport()`). `patientWord()` gives the first name for those sentences.
- **The set-up's Crohn's step** (`openSetup()`, `key: 'ibd'`): under the usual number, **The IBD advice line** (Name and Phone; saved as a contact with the role "IBD advice line" into `profile/main.calls` and as the flare plan's contact, `flarePlan.contact`, so I think I'm flaring shows it; a name without a number is refused; `draft.adviceAt` stops Back and Next adding it twice) and **Your regular medicine** (a box with a datalist of the usual biologics, immunosuppressants and mesalazines, `IBD_SETUP_MEDS`; `addMedicineByName(name, purpose)` writes a scheduled medicine with the name, purpose "Crohn's or colitis", no dose and no times, so Edit on the Meds tab finishes it; a name already on the list is not added again). Both optional.
- Jo's fixture carries the answers (tummy pain from the day's worst pain, the gut in the way from it, nights from the bowel log's night entries; no sickness). The privacy page's list of check-in questions names the three (dated 18 October 2026). Rules: no change (`entries` and `profile` are family write).
- Tested in the scratchpad's `t/t27.mjs` (the evening order with tummy pain and the gut question, no Sickness, the four chips, tap and clear, the rest of the walk unchanged, the review, what is saved with no sickness field, the card; the morning's question after the symptoms with its three words, saved as 2; the Gut and nights line and the tummy pain flag on Notes; the report brief; the set-up step's boxes and datalist, a name without a number refused, the contact, the flare plan's contact and the medicine saved, Back and Next adding nothing twice; the cancer preview with Sickness and no gut or night questions; no page errors). `t/t24.mjs`, `t/t25.mjs` and `t/t26.mjs` pass.

## Crohn's build 4: the Crohn's and colitis report (v220, 18 October 2026)
Mark: "crack on and finish the entire Crohn's build". Build (4) of the plan: the two-page (or three) report for the IBD team, in the house style (v143), with the Gastro questions. Shown with the Crohn's or colitis pack only (`#rep-ibd` on the Reports tab, under For the team beside Pain, `renderVitals()` hides it without the pack; the cancer view is unchanged). Facts as logged against the person's own usual; the only levels are v145's bowel rules; results as given; it never says whether something is a flare.
- **Data** (`buildIbdReport(n)` in `scripts.js`, from `state.ibdPdf` { from, to, entries, results, questions, filename "Daybook crohns colitis YYYY-MM-DD.pdf" }; `renderIbdReport()` loads the range's entries, the questions and `loadResults()`): the bowel entries by day (`countOf`, `bloodOf`, the grades from `bloodGradeOf()`), urgency, accidents, nights, watery stoma output, loose stools, the usual from Settings (`ibdUsual()`, a stoma from `ibdStoma()`), flare days (`isFlareDay()`, grouped into runs) and the calls (`type: 'call'`), the flare plan (`flarePlan()`, `flareContact()`), injections and infusions (the calendar's repeat sessions, `isRepeat()`, `sessionLabel()`, the next one after the period), steroid courses in the period and in the last 12 months, the IBD medicines on the list (by name, purpose or `medClass()`), temperature, weight, pain, evening energy, the outside-the-gut answers and the results in the period.
- **Page 1:** the masthead ("Crohn's and colitis" with the credit, the sub line naming the condition, stoma and usual from Settings), four tiles (**Bowels a day** against the usual, amber at 3 or more above it; **Blood** days of days logged, the worst grade, red for mostly blood or black; **Urgency** straight-away count with accidents and nights; **Flare days** of the period with the runs and the calls), four charts by `renderChart()` (**Bowel movements a day** as bars with the usual as a shaded band and flare days as triangles; **Blood** 0 to 3 by day, shaded from mixed in; **Temperature** with the 37.5 and 38.0 bands; **Weight**, injection or infusion days as triangles on both), the key, and a three-panel strip (`.strip.ibd`: **Treatment** with the medicines, the sessions in the period, the last and next, and steroid courses in 12 months; **Flare plan** in the team's own words with the advice line; **Results, as the team gave them**, the latest of each with its range). Then the writing body: **The period in brief** (four plain paragraphs: Bowels, Blood and urgency, Flares and treatment, Body), **Week by week** (a day, blood, urgency, weight, also: flare days, calls, steroids, injections), **The flare log** (the flare runs and every call with what they said), **Outside the gut** (each weekly answer), **Results, as the team gave them** (every result in the period with its range, source and note), **Questions for the gastro team** (`isGastroQ()`, labelled Gastro, three lines to write on) and **Notes from the appointment**. `report.js`: `ibdReportHtml(I)`, `IBD_CSS`, `fitIbdReport()` through `paginate()`; as many pages as it needs (Jo's month is three: the third is the notes panel). The questions grid is closed by `close()` with two divs (the first cut left it open, so the foot fell inside the body and nothing paginated: the foot sat at 2099px and every page was one page).
- **Gastro questions move to this report** while the pack is on: `buildNotesReport()` leaves them out (`gastroQ`), the Notes screen and the text say "1 question for the gastro team is on the Crohn's and colitis report", the timeline says "Question for the gastro team". Without the pack they stay on Notes as before.
- **The screen** (`#view-ibd`, `PAGE_TITLES.ibd`, the range 14, 28, 56, 90 or custom with `state.ibdRange` 28 and `wireReportRange('ibd')`; `#view-ibd .segmented` wraps at 320px): Send by email (`SEND_KINDS.ibd`, subject "My Crohn's and colitis report, {range}"), Preview and Save as PDF, the questions, the four tiles, the brief (`#ibd-brief`), the strip as cards, the week cards, the flare log and the results as word cards. `REPORT_KINDS.ibd`, `REPORT_NAMES.ibd`, `fitKey('ibd')` (the data, the name, the days, the medicines, the IBD settings, the flare plan and the contacts), `fittedIbdReport()`, `ibdReportBlob()`, the frame `report-frame-ibd`.
- Tested in the scratchpad's `t/t26.mjs` (the card with the pack; the view and title; the gastro question; the four tiles with the usual, the worst blood, the flare runs; the brief's four paragraphs with nothing judged; the strip's three panels naming the injections, the steroid course, Priya's plan, the advice line and the results with ranges; four week cards; the flare log in time order; every result in the period; Preview fitted and printed as the same number of pages, the masthead and condition, the four tiles and charts, the usual band and the triangles, the three strip panels, the sections in order, the Gastro card, the outside rows, the footers, no em dashes; Notes saying the question is on this report; the cancer preview with no card and Notes unchanged; 320 dark with no sideways scroll). `t/t24.mjs` and `t/t25.mjs` pass.

## Crohn's build 3: results recorded, not interpreted (v219, 18 October 2026)
Build (3) of Mark's Crohn's plan: blood tests and calprotectin as the team gives them, charted, never judged. Shown for every pack (a chemo patient has bloods before every session), with the tests offered following the packs: faecal calprotectin and the biologic drug level only with Crohn's or colitis (or once one has been recorded), the pack's usual tests first (calprotectin, CRP, haemoglobin, ferritin, albumin; otherwise neutrophils, haemoglobin, white cells, platelets, CRP). The one deliberate rule: Daybook never colours, flags or words a result. The lab's own range, where it was printed, is the only context, drawn as a band and always called "as given". A judged result is what would make Daybook a medical device.
- **Data:** `entries/{autoId}` { type "result", test (a key from `RESULT_TESTS`, or "other"), name, value (number), below (true when the lab printed "<5"), unit, range (the words as printed, "" when none), low, high (parsed by `parseRange()`: "0 to 50", "0-50", "<50", "under 50", "over 35", "35+"), source (who gave them, "GP", "Hospital", a team), note, day (the sample date), at (noon that day), addedBy }. Family write, no rules change, nothing to publish; the viewer role cannot read the type (as for bowels and symptoms). `loadResults()` is one single-field query (`type == "result"`); the preview reads `recentEntries`.
- **The Results screen** (`#view-results`, `renderResults()`, `PAGE_TITLES.results`; the Reports tab's Your own records group now holds Documents, "Letters and paperwork", beside **Results**, "Blood tests and calprotectin, as given", `#rep-results`, the `#i-lab` test-tube icon): the hint says what it is and that the team says what each result means; **Add results** and, with the bridge, **Read from a photo** (`.results-actions`, hidden for read-only and the viewer); then one card per test (`resultCard()`, `.rescard`, in the tests' order, others by name): the latest value large with its unit, "15 Oct, from GP. Range as given: 0 to 50." (or "No range given."), the chart with two or more results, and **Every result (n)** (a native `details`: the day, the value, the range, the source and the note; a tap opens the entry options with Edit and Delete).
- **The chart** (`resultSvg()`): an inline SVG, no library, so it draws with no connection and follows dark mode through CSS (`.res-svg` classes on the band, the grid, the line and the dots). Time on the x axis from the first result to the last (the first and last dates, the middle one when it is clear of both), the y axis on rounded steps (1, 2, 2.5 or 5 times a power of ten, `nice()`), the lab's range as a teal band labelled "range as given" (an upper limit alone shades from the bottom, a lower alone to the top), a dot a result joined in order, the last filled. `role="img"` with the count, the dates, the latest and the range in its label.
- **Add results** (`openResultsSheet()`, several from one sample): the date of the sample (no later than today), who gave them (with GP, Hospital and Clinic chips), then a chip per test (`aria-pressed`; a tap adds a sunken `.resrow` with Result (the unit in its label) and Range as given, Remove on the row) and **Something else** (a row with Name and Unit too; several allowed); a note with Tap to speak; **Save the results** writes one entry per row with a value (blank rows are skipped, a word is refused naming the test, "<5" is kept as below). The timeline's "..." on a result, or a row on the card, opens the same sheet as **Edit result** with that one row (`openAdd('result', e)` delegates; `result` is in `EDITABLE_ENTRY_TYPES` and `ENTRY_SHEET_TITLES`).
- **Read from a photo** (`readResultsPhoto()`, the bridge's new **`results`** kind, `EXPLAIN_SYSTEM.results`: JSON only, { day, source, results [{ test, value, unit, range }] }, copied as printed, never a range, a flag or a comment of its own): up to four pages or a text file through `processFiles()`; the names are matched to the known tests by `RESULT_TESTS[].match` (CRP, C-reactive protein; haemoglobin, Hb; calprotectin...), anything else becomes a Something else row with its unit as printed, a value that is not a number is left out; the sheet opens filled in with "Check every number against the sheet before saving". Nothing is saved without Save. The worker deploys with the push.
- **Where else they show:** the timeline ("CRP 12 mg/L", "Result from GP, as given · range 0 to 5 · note", `.entry.type-result`, `--cat-result`); Notes for the team as one teal Context line, the latest of each test in the period with its range (`latestResultsText()`, never a level); the two-page report's strip ("Results, as the team gave them.", `R.results`, `twoPageResults()`); the flare sheet's 7-day summary ("Latest results, as given: ..." from the recent entries). The privacy page's list of records names them (dated 18 October 2026).
- **The IBD preview:** Jo has six months of results (calprotectin 85, 60 then 410 with the flare; CRP 3, 4, 48, 12; haemoglobin, ferritin, albumin), so the charts and the band show. The cancer preview starts empty.
- Tested in the scratchpad's `t/t24.mjs` (the card and the view, the five cards in order, the latest and the range, the chart's dots, band and label, no colour judgement, the sheet's chips and date, nothing typed refused, a word refused and named, two saved with a blank one skipped and what is stored, Something else with "<9,5" kept as below, the view redrawn, Edit from a row filled in and changed in place, Delete, the timeline, the Notes line as context with no level, the flare summary, the cancer preview's empty state and its chips, 320 dark with no sideways scroll and 44px controls) and `t/t25.mjs` (the two-page report's strip, still two pages, nothing judged; the photo route with the bridge mocked: one results call, the rows matched with the unreadable one left out, the date and source, nothing saved before Save, saved as read with the ranges parsed). The harness is `h/harness.mjs` with a Firebase stub (`h/stub.js`), rebuilt this session; keep both.

## The Meds tab's lower sections spaced like the top ones (v218, 18 October 2026)
Mark: the Finished courses and Given in hospital headings sat hard on the card above them, unlike Scheduled and When needed. The same first-child trap as the Exemption certificate section: each heading is the first child of its own wrapper, so `.section-title:first-child { margin-top: 0 }` removed its gap. `#meds-done-section .section-title` and `#meds-hospital-section .section-title` now join the `#meds-docs-section` rule (`margin-top: var(--s-6)`). The Given in hospital rows (`.hosprow`) get the card's full padding, a 19px name with a wider gap before the pill, and the Log something given in hospital button sits `--s-3` below them. Measured in the scratchpad's `t/t23.mjs`: 24px above each of Finished courses, Given in hospital and Exemption certificate.

## Reorder on the Meds card: arrows, and press and hold to slide (v217, 18 October 2026)
Mark, on v216: "Where's the up and down to reorder" (they were in Manage medicines and Edit medicine, not on the cards he looks at) and "hold the medicine down and after say 2 seconds you can just slide it up and down"; then "We don't need the arrows in the manage medicines and edit medicines, remove this change, we only need the last build."
- **On the card** (`medCard(m, day, section)`, `renderMeds()` passes the Scheduled or When needed list): the open part ends with a `.med-order` row, "Place 2 of 5. Or hold the name and slide it." and the up and down arrows (`medMoveButtons(m, after, list)`, section-aware: Senna moves among the scheduled ones), which redraw with the card kept open and focus on an arrow. Manage medicines and Edit medicine carry no arrows and no place row (v216's went).
- **Press and hold** (`medDrag(card, m, handle, section)`, `MED_HOLD_MS` 1000, touch only; a desktop has the arrows): a finger held still on the name for a second lifts the card (`.is-dragging`: floating shadow, teal outline, a short vibration where the phone allows; `body.is-med-dragging` stops the page scrolling), it follows the finger (`translateY`, with the page's own scroll added when the finger nears the top or bottom, which scrolls it), swaps places with the card whose middle it passes (live in the DOM), and letting go saves the new place; the tap that follows a hold is swallowed, so the card does not open. A finger that moves first is a scroll, a plain tap opens the card as before, and the long press menu is suppressed on the name (`-webkit-touch-callout: none`). Read-only and viewer get neither.
- **Saving** (`placeMed(m, target, before)`): the whole list (`sortMedicines()`, stopped and finished ones included) with m put beside the target, numbered 1 to n, only the changed `medicines/{id}.order` written in one batch (the preview mutates `state.medicines`); `moveMed(m, dir, list)` is one step within a list. `medicines` is family write: **no rules change and nothing to publish**.
- Tested in the scratchpad's `t/t22.mjs` (touch events through CDP: a tap opening the card with its place row and two arrows; the up arrow moving it within Scheduled with the card kept open and focus on an arrow, the first card's up disabled; a finger moving before the hold not dragging; a hold lifting the card and locking the page, the DOM order changing as it slides, the drop saved with every order 1 to n, nothing left dragging, the card not opened; a When needed card slid down; a long drag to the foot of the screen scrolling the page and landing two places down; Manage medicines and Edit medicine with no arrows; Log a dose following the order; no page errors). Not tried on a phone: the second's hold, the vibration and the scroll while sliding are the things to feel there.

## The medicines in Mark's own order, and a finished course's dose put right (v216, 18 October 2026)
Mark (a message meant for another chat, then "Build it my friend"): the Meds tab in his own order (morning ones first, then the when-needed ones, night ones after, a new medicine at the bottom), with up and down controls; and "I can't change the dose" on a finished course.
- **The order is `medicines/{id}.order`**, the field the seed list has carried since v1 and `watchMedicines()` has always sorted by (Mark asked for a `sortOrder` field; the one already there does the job, so nothing is migrated). Every list follows it (`sortMedicines()`): the Meds tab's Scheduled and When needed sections, the Medicine tile's Log a dose sheet, Manage medicines. A new medicine still takes the highest order plus one, so it goes to the bottom. Finished courses (newest ended first) and Given in hospital (the day's doses) keep their own order. The v216 arrows sat on Manage medicines rows and as a Place in the list row in Edit medicine; v217 (above) moved them onto the card and took those away. **Mark sets his order himself**: the app cannot know his medicine ids from here.
- **A finished course's dose.** The Finished courses card has **Edit** beside See every dose (`finishedCard()`), and a medicine's history sheet has **Edit (medicine name) (dose, dates, name)** (`#medhist-edit`, family only), both opening the ordinary Edit medicine; Manage medicines > Edit always did, but Mark could not find it. The dose hint there reads, for a medicine no longer taken, "The dose typed here is what its card and the Medicine history show for it, so if it was put up before the course ended, type the dose it was really taken at. The doses already logged keep their own amounts." A dose saved on a medicine not Taking now sets `medicines/{id}.doseSet: true`, and `shownDose()` then shows the box as typed (until then the v187 rule, the dose most logged, still applies; that rule reads `state.recentEntries`, two days, so on the Finished courses card it only ever helped a course ended within two days, which is why Mark's finished course read the newer dose: the card showed the box). No dose change is noted for a medicine no longer taken, as before.
- **The rename:** "Paracetomol" to "Paracetamol" is the Name box in Edit medicine (Manage medicines > Edit, or the card's Edit); logged doses match on `medId`, so they stay with it. Pregabalin is on his list already (his screenshot: 150 mg, twice a day, Pain).
- Manage medicines rows wrap at 320px (`.medrow { flex-wrap: wrap }`; the pill and Edit drop under the name), which the v214 class line had pushed sideways. `t/t21.mjs` tested the v216 arrows and the finished course's Edit; the arrow parts of it are superseded by `t/t22.mjs`.

## The compact Meds card (v215, 18 October 2026)
Mark: "the meds page is quite busy now and a lot of scrolling... just the name of the drug and Log now up front, and an optional expand with all the other data"; my two guards, agreed: the status line stays on the compact card, and a card opens itself when something needs a look.
- **Compact** (`medCard()` rebuilt): `.med-top` holds a `.med-toggle` button (the name, the v214 class word as a small teal tag `.med-tag`, and one `.med-line`: "1 of 2 today" with the green Done for today pill when complete, "Nothing given today" or "2 given today" for a hospital medicine, the when-needed `prnStatus()` pill with "1 of 4 today" after it, "Not given this day" on another day; the chevron `.med-chev` turning when open) and **Log now** (`.med-log`, 48px, "Log a dose" on another day; hidden for read-only and viewer like `.med-actions`). About 100 to 125px a card.
- **Open** (`.med-more`, `aria-expanded` on the toggle): the dose label, the hospital pill and route, the how and when, the purpose pill, the dots and any Course ends pill, the day's time chips (or "Last 17 Oct 08:00 (Mark)", "No dose logged yet"), **Other time** and **Log a side effect**. Which cards are open is kept for the session in `state.openMeds`, so a redraw (a dose logged, the other phone) keeps them open; closing by hand adds the id to `state.closedMeds`.
- **Opens itself** (`medAutoOpenWhy()`, `MED_OPEN_SOON_DAYS` 3): a dose changed in the last 14 days (`recentDoseChange()`), a course ending today or within three days, a hospital medicine given today; the card opens with "Opened because: Dose changed from 1 tablet to 2 tablets on 18 Oct, by Shelley." (`.med-why`) unless it was closed by hand this session. Finished courses, Given in hospital, Manage medicines, Medicine history and the Today Medicine tile are unchanged.
- Tested in the scratchpad's `t/t20.mjs` (the preview's nine cards compact with their tags, lines and Log now; one opened with its dose, how, dots, chip, Other time and side effect link, kept open through a redraw, closed; Log now on Oramorph logging with the line updating to Next from; a dose change and a course ending in two days opening cards with their reason, a card closed by hand not reopening; yesterday's Log a dose and Not given this day; 320 dark with no sideways scroll and 48px buttons; no page errors).

## Drip or injection as a dose form, what each medicine is for, and the course dates in a row (v214, 18 October 2026)
Mark, adding his second chemo (an IV like the first): "Why isn't IV an option at the top? Should I choose liquid?"; the two course date boxes overlapped and ran off the screen; and a third column on Manage medicines saying what each medicine is for ("pain relief", "chemo", "anti sickness", "laxative"); is there a scan feature for new drugs (yes, Read it from a photo on Add medicine, v97).
- **Drip or injection** (`DOSE_FORMS` key `iv`, `UNCOUNTED_FORMS` with `none`): uncounted, the Dose box as typed ("260 mg", "7,500 units", "1000 ml"), `doseFormOf()` null, `doseCount` null, no ask-each-time; a hint under it, "A drip or an injection is logged whole, as one dose. If the hospital gives it, tick Given by the hospital below..." (`ivHint` from `doseControls()`). `splitDose()` opens a medicine saved before v214 on it when the hospital route is drip or injection, or the name or how says inject, infusion, drip, IV, intravenous or subcut (Dalteparin injection does). The Not counted placeholder reads "e.g. apply to skin, or 1 patch".
- **The course dates:** labels "Course starts" and "Course ends" with "Both dates are optional." on the hint; `.field-row` is `align-items: flex-end`, so a wrapped label never misaligns the boxes; and date, time and month inputs in a field get `max-width: 100%; min-width: 0; -webkit-appearance: none; display: block` with `::-webkit-date-and-time-value { text-align: left; min-height: 1.4em }`, since iPhone Safari gives a date box an intrinsic width two in a row could not shrink below (the overlap in Mark's screenshots). Check on the phone: the Vitals and hospital dose sheets' Date and Time rows use the same boxes.
- **What it is for** (`medClass(m)`, `MED_CLASSES`, `PURPOSE_CLASSES`): the name against the usual UK names (Chemo, Immunotherapy, Hormone treatment, Anti-sickness, Laxative, Anti-diarrhoeal, Blood thinner, Steroid, Stomach protection, Digestive enzymes, Crohn's or colitis, Antibiotic, Antifungal, Antiviral, Antihistamine, Anxiety or sleep, Mood, Pain relief (`PAIN_MED_RE`, read at call time since it is defined further down), Blood pressure or heart, Cholesterol, Diabetes, Thyroid, Bones, Mouth care, Fluids (drip), Nutrition, Breathing), then the words in What it is for (Skin, Antihistamine and the rest), else "Other". Shown on each Manage medicines row under the name in teal (`.medrow-class`; `.medrow-title` carries the strike-through for a stopped one). A guide, not a clinical classification, and the medicine's own What it is for words are never changed.
- Tested in the scratchpad's `t/t19.mjs` (the preview's ten medicines classed: Dalteparin Blood thinner, Co-amoxiclav Antibiotic, Creon Digestive enzymes, Oramorph Pain relief, Diazepam Anxiety or sleep, Metoclopramide Anti-sickness, Macrogol and Senna Laxative; Dalteparin opening on Drip or injection with its dose, the hint and the preview; the course labels; Carboplatin added on the chip with 450 mg, the count box hidden, saved as doseForm iv with no count, classed Chemo, reopening on the chip; the two date boxes side by side, inside the screen and level at 320; no page errors).

## Someone else's unfinished check-in is not offered as yours (v213, 18 October 2026)
Mark: "Every time I log in fresh I get this message at the top" ("Your unfinished carer's view was kept, as you left it."), without having opened the carer's view. **Why:** since v160 an unfinished check-in is also kept in the circle's shared folder (`profile/main.checkinDrafts`, so the other phone can carry on with it), and since v184 closing the sheet keeps it; Shelley had started a carer's view on her phone and closed it, and Mark's phone offered that shared copy at every start as his own. `keptCheckins()` now returns who kept it (`by`, and `local` for this phone's own copy), `offerDraft()` only offers a check-in kept on this phone, with no author, or by this phone's name, and each draft is offered **once per phone** (`localStorage` `daybook.draft.offered`, the key and the time offered, `offeredBefore()`, `markOffered()`; a newer save of the same draft is offered again). Nothing else changed: the other person's draft still opens from its own row ("Shelley's unfinished check-in from 09:12 is back", v160). Tested in the scratchpad's `t/t18.mjs` (Shelley's carer draft on Mark's phone not offered; Mark's own offered, not offered again on the next start, offered again once saved anew; a draft with no author offered; no page errors).

## Usage: the operator's dashboard (v211, 18 October 2026)
Mark: "Do you think I need a dashboard that only I have access to ... so I can track what's being used and what it's for and future edits ... how many people are actually completing their logs, how many people are using summarise letters or any of the AI functions", within GDPR. Built as counts, never content.
- **The counts** (`buildUsage()` in the worker, `countCircle()`; `USAGE_PATH` `bridge/usage`, the bridge's own top-level document, denied to everyone by the rules): once a night in the 03:30 London cron run (`USAGE_AT`, skipped when today is already counted), every household but the demo: `profile/main` (packs, set-up done), `bridge/explainLog` (the month's Assistant calls, now with **`monthKinds`**, a count per kind written by `handleExplain()`), a count aggregation of this week's entries (`day >=`), the week's check-ins counted by their deterministic ids (`__name__ IN` 14 references, so no composite index), the latest entry's day (one ordered query), counts of `type == 'sent'`, members and phones. About nine reads a circle (the test: 30 for three), so 3,000 circles is under 30,000 a night. Totals (`circles`, `new7`, `new30`, `active7`, `logging7` (every check-in done), `checkins7` of `checkinSlots` (14 an active circle), `members`, `phones`, `aiCircles`, `aiCalls`, `kinds`, `sent`, `setup`, the three packs, `feedback`, `codes` by Beta code), `history[day]` kept `USAGE_HISTORY_DAYS` (90), and `circles[pid]` with this week's counts where `pid` is the first eight characters of `sha256(hid + ':' + BRIDGE_KEY)`: a pseudonym nobody can turn back into a circle from the page. Nothing anyone wrote, no names, no addresses, ever. At `USAGE_MAX_ROWS` (2,000) the document nears 1 MB: shard the rows then. Firestore helpers added: `fs.run()` (any structured query), `fs.count()` (a count aggregation), `fs.docName()` (v212: the resource name `projects/{id}/databases/(default)/documents/...`, never the REST URL; Mark's first Count again now failed with "Document parent name https://..." because v211 passed the URL as the reference).
- **Who sees it:** `POST /usage { what: 'check' | 'read' | 'rebuild' }` (`handleUsage()`): the sign-in's household must be the operator's, the one whose inbox key is `BRIDGE_KEY` (`householdForKey()`); anyone else, and the demo, gets 403 `{ operator: false }`. `GET /usage?key=<BRIDGE_KEY>` (`&rebuild=1`) for Mark's own curl. In the app `syncUsageRow()` (from `enterApp()`) asks `check` once per phone and uid, caches the answer in `localStorage` `daybook.operator.{uid}` for a day (a no for an hour), and shows **Usage** as the last row of More (`#more-usage`, hidden by default; never in the preview, the demo or for a viewer). `openUsage()` opens `view-usage` (`PAGE_TITLES.usage`, More highlighted): the hint, "Counted 18 Oct at 02:31. The counts are for the week to ..." four tiles (Care circles with new in 30 days; Logged this week, green at half the circles; Check-ins done as a percentage of the slots, with how many did every one; Assistant this month with reports sent), **Features used** (`USAGE_KIND_NAMES` per Assistant kind, then reports sent, phones with reminders, people, set-up done, each pack, feedback), **Where circles came from** (Beta codes), **Day by day** (`.usage-bars`, one bar a night of `active7`, 90 kept), **Each circle** (`.usage-table`, scrolling inside its wrap at 320px: the code, since, what it is set up for, entries, check-ins of 14, last logged, Assistant, sent, phones; quiet rows muted) and **Count again now** (`rebuild`).
- The privacy page has a "Usage counts" bullet under who can see it (dated 18 October 2026). Rules: no change. Tested in the scratchpad's `bridge/usage_test.mjs` (a mocked Firestore with count aggregations: three circles and the demo skipped, the totals, the history trimmed to 90 days and today added, the three rows with pseudonyms of 8 hex characters, no name, note or household id anywhere in the document, a second run the same night skipped, the document read back) and `t/t17.mjs` (the row hidden in the preview; with a stand-in sign-in the `check` call, the row and the cache; the page's title, tiles, lists, codes, bars, table and quiet rows, no names, no sideways scroll; Count again now posting `rebuild` and redrawing; Back; another circle refused with the row hidden and the no cached, no second ask within the hour; 320 dark with the table scrolling inside its card; no page errors).

## The bridge reads one document a run, and the Assistant has a monthly allowance (v209, 18 October 2026)
Mark, asked how many users the free tier could carry: the honest answer was six or seven households with reminders on, because the five-minute reminder run listed every household and read each one's phones, log, profile, medicines and doses (about 25 reads, 288 times a day), and wrote the log every run. He accepted the new cost rule ("cheap, not free") and said build it.
- **The reminder plan** (`bridge/reminderPlan`, the bridge's own top-level document, rules deny everyone): `{ day, built, households { hid: { phones, nudge, oneOff, times [{ medId, t, name, dose }] } } }`. `runReminders()` reads it once a run (`readPlanDoc()`), rebuilds it from the database on the first run of a London day or when it is missing (`buildPlan()`: every household, `buildPlanEntry()`: its phones, profile, medicines and pending one-offs; `planEntryFromDocs()`), and looks only at households that are due (`planEntryDue()`: a medicine time's due and nudge windows, the 10:00 window with the nudge on, or a flagged one-off). `runHouseholdReminders(env, fs, hid, entry, now)` then reads the log, the day's doses (only when a time is due), the phones (only when sending) and the profile (only in the 10:00 window), and writes `bridge/reminderLog` only when something was sent. `GET /remind-now?key=<BRIDGE_KEY>&rebuild=1` forces a rebuild.
- **The app keeps the plan fresh** (`touchReminderPlan(what)` beside `bridgeCall()`, fire and forget, never in the preview): `POST /plan { what: 'refresh' }` (`handlePlan()`, a family member; `touchPlan()` rebuilds that household's entry) after a medicine is saved (Edit medicine and the dose question), reminders switched on or off, or the nudge switched; `{ what: 'oneoff' }` after "Remind me in 15 minutes" writes a reminder, which flags the household until the bridge has sent it. A phone on an older app is picked up at the daily rebuild. With about 5,000 circles the plan document would near Firestore's 1 MB: shard it by the first letter of the household id then.
- **Daybook Assistant's month** (`EXPLAIN_LIMIT_MONTH` 60 per circle on the real project; the day's 40 stays; **v210: the report kinds (`EXPLAIN_REPORT_KINDS`: report, weekly, mood, pain) never count against the month and are never refused by it**, counted apart as `monthReports`, since Mark wants a circle always able to summarise a report it is sending to a clinician): `bridge/explainLog` carries `month`, `monthReal`, `monthTokensIn` and `monthTokensOut` beside the day's figures, reset when the month changes; past the allowance the bridge answers 429 "Your care circle has used this month's Daybook Assistant allowance (60 requests). It starts again on the 1st. Until then use Send to my AI app." `GET /ai-usage?key=<BRIDGE_KEY>` (`aiUsage()`) lists every circle's calls and tokens this month with a rough cost in pence (claude-opus-5-5 at $4 in and $20 out a million tokens, about 80p to the dollar), busiest first, and the totals.
- Tested in the scratchpad's `bridge/plan_test.mjs` (a mocked Firestore over `fetch` counting reads and writes, a real RSA key for the service account token and a real P-256 pair for the push, the clock fixed to a London day: the midnight rebuild at 10 reads with one household in the plan and the phoneless one left out; a quiet run at 1 read; a full day of 288 runs at 345 reads, 6 writes and the 14 right pushes (two medicines at 08:00, one at 20:00, their nudges and the 10:00 nudge, two phones); a dose logged before 08:00 leaving only the other medicine's reminder and nudge; a one-off flagged by the app, not sent before its time, sent after it with the flag cleared and the reminder marked sent; a medicine change refreshing the entry; a household switching reminders on joining the plan; the pure due test). Not tried against the live project: the first cron run after the deploy builds the plan, and `/remind-now?key=...` shows `rebuilt`, `due` and what was sent.

## A check-in belongs to whoever filled it in (v208, 17 October 2026)
Mark: he could change Shelley's carer's views; they should be locked for him, and his for her. Until now every family member could edit or delete any check-in (deliberate in v82, before carers were a role).
- `ownsCheckin(c)`: the saved check-in's `addedBy` is this phone's name (or it has none; the preview is open to all so Mark can try everything there). The summary card (Today and the timeline) shows **Edit** only to the author, and to anyone else a line, "Shelley's check-in. Only the person who filled it in can change it." (`.cc-locked`); the "..." menu drops Edit this check-in and Delete for someone else's and shows the same line; `openCheckin()` refuses a saved check-in of someone else's with that toast (so the carer's view row on the Treatment tab and the Today rows cannot reach it). Your week is already per phone (`weekSlot()`). Reading is unchanged: both phones still see everything.
- App-side only: `entries` stays family write in the rules (an author lock there would need the member's uid on every entry, which they do not carry; a roadmap note for the security tightening). A check-in started by one person and finished by the other keeps the first author, as before.
- Tested in the scratchpad's `t/t16.mjs` (as Mark on a real sign-in: his morning card with Edit, Shelley's carer's view card on the timeline with the line and no Edit, his evening card with Edit, the menu on hers with no Edit or Delete and the line; no page errors).

## Notes for the team: a third page before anything is cut, and only new questions printed (v207, 17 October 2026)
Mark: "we are forcing reports to two pages, we don't need to do this, they read the information on their computers. Keep it close to two but not be held ransom to it." And: five questions already answered in clinic were still printing; "the questions should just say no questions this week if nothing new has been added".
- **A third page when needed** (`reportHtml()`, `shrink()` in `report.js`): page 1 and page 2 are as before, and a `#page3` appears when the questions or the weeks need it. `shrink()` now goes: a week over its space moves **every week whole to page 3** (`R.weeksPage3`; page 2's runner reads "Readings"), and only a page 3 that still overflows shortens a long entry or drops one as before; page 1 over its space takes the writing lines from 3 to 2, then moves the last question to page 3 (`R.extraQ`, drawn in the same cards numbered on from page 1, `data-sec="More question n"`, "3 more questions on page 3" under the page 1 grid), then the lines to 1; "Plus N more questions in the app" happens only when page 3 itself is full. The footers count the pages (`madeBy(n, total)`), the viewer says "3 A4 pages", Save as PDF draws every `.page`. The covering email no longer promises two pages. The Food diary is unchanged (two pages, its trims are a sentence off a weekly line and a point to discuss).
- **Only questions added in the report's period are printed.** `buildNotesReport()` splits the open questions into `questions` (asked from `from` to `to`) and `older` (asked before `from`). With none in the period the report reads "No new questions this time." above the full-width Other points card, and older open ones are one line, "2 older questions are still open in the app, asked before 4 Oct." (`R.older`, `R.olderFrom`). The text for an AI app says the same. On the screen the older ones are a line, "N older questions still open, asked before 4 Oct. Not printed on this report: mark them as answered if the team has dealt with them, or add one again to ask this time." with **Show them** (`state.notesOlderOpen`, the ordinary cards with Mark as answered and Delete). Why five answered questions printed for Mark is not known from the code (every route that answers a question also takes it off the report, which `t15` confirms); the likeliest is that they were answered in the appointment and never marked in the app, which this rule covers either way.
- Tested in the scratchpad's `t/t15.mjs` (the demo's two pages with "No new questions this time."; eleven questions in the period giving three pages with five on page 1, six on page 3 numbered to 11, the page 3 runner, Page n of 3 footers and no "in the app" line; twelve long notes moving the weeks to page 3 with all twelve entries and nothing cut; an August question with the screen's line, Show them, the text, the report's none line and older line, Other points full width; Mark as answered from the shown card filing it; no page errors).

Days: injections -22 and -8 done (a dose logged each, with a note), +6 planned, flare days -7 to -4, moods. Entries: bowels every day from a per-day plan table, daily temperature, heart rate, weight, sleep and the regular doses, pain readings with where it was, symptoms (tiredness, mouth, joint pain), the two side effects after the -8 injection as check-in side effects (`_evening_fx_`), food thinner through the flare, two calls to the advice line (the steroids started; CRP 48 to 12), two notes, three questions (gastro, dietitian, one answered), weekly checks for the week before and the flare week with Alex's own, morning and evening check-ins from -13 to today with `sidefx`, `outside` and the words under the scores. A gastroenterology clinic letter with an example explanation, a flare plan from "IBD nurse, Priya (example)" naming the advice line, three contacts (the advice line with option 2 and an email, the secretary, the GP), a walk and a bike in the programme, saved meals.
- **What Mark can see with it:** the Crohn's Today (the flare row, Bowels entries), the Treatment tab (the flare plan card, the next injection tile, the steroid line, flare and steroid days on the calendar, the side effects card), the Meds tab, Notes for the team with the IBD bowel lines and the Gastro question, the Pain, Food and Mood reports, and the flare sheet's 7-day summary. The two-page Crohn's and colitis report itself is build (4), still to come, and will show here once built.

(`.entry.type-call`, teal; Delete from the "..." menu; `ENTRY_SHEET_TITLES.call`).

Mark approved the Crohn's plan (17 October 2026): (1) the IBD treatment calendar, (2) the flare plan and "I think I'm flaring", (3) results recorded not interpreted, (4) the two-page Crohn's and colitis report, (5) the check-ins and set-up tuned for IBD; Flare mode as a raised row under the check-ins, Results on the Reports tab, side effects asked for three days after an injection. This is (1). Everything shows only with the Crohn's or colitis pack; the cancer view is unchanged.
- **Two more kinds of session** in `TREATMENTS`: **Injection** (`inject`, letter "Inj", `cycle: 'repeat'`, `home: true`, `every: 14`) and **Infusion** (`infuse`, "Inf", repeat, `every: 56`); chemo, radiotherapy and immunotherapy carry `pack: 'cancer'`, the two new ones `pack: 'ibd'`, Other has none. `treatmentsFor()` offers the kinds of the packs that are on plus any kind already on the calendar (the day sheet's chips, Add sessions, the Symptom sheet's Treatment group, `latestTreatmentKey()`, the calendar key's letters, `renderCalKey()` into `#cal-key-letters`); `treatmentOf()` still knows them all, so a saved session renders whatever the packs. **A repeating treatment runs in no cycle** (`isRepeat(t)`): the calendar draws no rest days and no cycle lines for it, the cell's spoken label and the Sessions line use `sessionLabel(info)` ("adalimumab injection"), the day sheet hides Starts a new cycle and reads "Adalimumab (Humira) injection. The one before was 3 Oct, 14 days earlier.", and the tile (`repeatInfo()`) is headed **Next treatment**: "In 14 days", "Next adalimumab injection 31 Oct. Last one 17 Oct, done." (or "not ticked done"), "Day 9" with "No next one marked yet" when nothing is planned. With a cancer cycle running the cycle tile wins. Repeat sessions still count as sessions for the side-effects step and the After each session chart (`sessionDays()`).
- **Which medicine:** `days/{day}.treatName` (free text with a datalist of the medicines list, in the day sheet and Add sessions; moved with the session, `deleteField()` on the old day). **Ticking an injection or infusion done logs a dose** (`logRepeatDose()`): the active medicine whose name contains or is contained by the words, through `logMed()` at noon of that day (now, for today), unless a dose of it is already logged that day. **Add sessions** for a repeat kind: the medicine box, no cycle tick (every one saved `cycleStart: true`), `RUN_EVERY` gains every 6 and 8 weeks, the kind's own default spacing, and `runDays(first, count, every, atHome)` keeps weekends for an injection at home (a hospital session still moves to the Monday); the hint and the count hint say so.
- **Steroid courses:** Edit medicine has **Course starts (optional)** beside Course ends and a tick **A steroid course (prednisolone, budesonide, dexamethasone)** (`medicines/{id}.courseStart`, `steroid`; `remindBits` now skips four children; a when-needed medicine clears both). `steroidCourses()`, `steroidOn(day)`: the calendar cell gets `.is-steroid` (a 4px amber underline) and "on a steroid course (Prednisolone)" in its label, the key shows "Steroid course" (`#cal-key-steroid`) once a course exists, and `renderSteroidLine()` fills `#ibd-courses` under the tile: "Steroid courses in the last 12 months: 1 (Prednisolone, 7 Oct to 6 Nov)."
- **Side effects after an injection or infusion** (`SIDEFX_IBD`, `sidefxItems(type)`): Injection site reaction (or Infusion reaction), Headache, Nausea, Tiredness, Rash, Sore throat or cough, Fever or chills, Joint pain; the question reads "Since your adalimumab injection on Thursday (day 2 after it), any side effects?"; the step's rows, its reader and `fxText()` use the list for the session's kind (`SIDEFX_ALL` keeps the entry ids stable across both lists, `syncFxEntries()`), and the entries' `causeName` is "Adalimumab (Humira) injection".
- No rules change (`days` and `medicines` are family write). Tested in the scratchpad's `t/t11.mjs` (an IBD-only preview household: the tile and key with no sessions, Add sessions offering Injection and Infusion with the medicine box, no cycle tick and Every 2 weeks pressed, six fortnightly Saturdays kept on Saturdays with the name saved, the Next treatment tile, the Sessions line, no cycle lines or rest cells, "Inj" on the cells and the spoken label, the day sheet's line and medicine box with the cycle row hidden, Session done logging 40 mg of adalimumab once and the tile saying done, a prednisolone course added with its dates and tick, 25 shaded cells, the key and the courses line, the evening check-in two days after an injection asking with the IBD rows, Moderate saved and filed as a symptom with the cause and session) and `t/t12.mjs` (the cancer view as before: the cycle tile, the three kinds, no medicine box, the cycle words; both packs listing every kind at 320 dark).
