## Half-typed readings kept (v203, 17 October 2026)
Mark, half way through his vitals: his daughter needed him, the phone closed the app, and the readings already typed were gone. **Why:** the v136 drafts watched text boxes (textareas) only; the Vitals sheet's readings are inputs, so nothing typed there was ever kept.
- **Every typed box in a sheet is now kept** as it is typed (`DRAFT_INPUTS`, `draftable(el)`: textareas and inputs other than a date, time, slider, tick, radio, file or password box, and never a stepper's own number), in the same `localStorage` `daybook.drafts` under the same keys (the sheet's title, `data-draft`, the step, the box's label, `aria-label` or placeholder), cleared when the sheet closes by any route, so they survive only when the app itself went. When the same sheet opens again every kept input is put back (`restoreDraftsIn()`, `input` dispatched so the temperature hint and the weight conversion follow) with **one** line above the first of them, "What you had typed at 14:36 is back, as you left it. Check it, then save." and **Clear them** (every put-back box emptied, the drafts forgotten); text boxes keep their own "Your unsaved words" line. A box that is not on show is never filled (`el.closest('[hidden]')`), so a weight kept under "Done for the day" cannot be saved as a second weight behind the person's back.
- The Vitals sheet sets `data-reopen="vitals"` for a new entry, so the start-up toast reads "Your unfinished vitals were kept on this phone." with **Open it** (`offerDraft()`), as a note or a question does.
- Tested in the scratchpad's `t/t10.mjs` (three readings typed, the drafts kept with `reopen` vitals, the app reloaded: the toast, Open it, the three boxes back with the fourth empty, one line, the temperature hint following, Clear them emptying them and forgetting the drafts, a save clearing the drafts, a weight typed under Change it on a day with a weight kept but not put back into the hidden box, a drink's name kept and its stepper not, Escape forgetting everything, no page errors).

## One recogniser at a time: Tap to speak made reliable (v202, 17 October 2026)
Mark, interrupted mid dictation on the morning check-in, then "various worried messages": the button read "Listening, tap to stop (aborted)" at the same time as the toast "Listening stopped. Tap to speak again, or use the keyboard microphone."; at other times a first tap did nothing until he tapped the text box first, or went Skip then Back. "When the page opens you should just be able to click to talk and off you go."
- **The cause, reproduced** with a stand-in recogniser that behaves like the iPhone's (one at a time: starting a second aborts the first): a button that was listening when its screen was replaced (Next, Skip, Back or a review row in a check-in, the sheet closing, the writing space opening) was never stopped; it kept running unseen with `want` set, so the next button's start aborted it, it restarted 400 ms later and aborted the new one, and the two fought (nine sessions in two seconds) until one hit `SPEAK_DEAD_MAX`: the orphan's toast beside the live one's "(aborted)" label. Worse, closing the sheet while listening left the recogniser restarting for good (the orange microphone dot never went off), and the next sheet's first tap was killed by it, which is the "had to tap the box first" symptom.
- **The fix:** `speechLive` (beside `speakButton()`) is the one button listening now; a tap on any button calls `stopSpeech()` first, so whatever else was listening gives way. Each button has a `quietStop()` (no toast, no focus; the session is aborted so nothing more is heard from it): called by `stopSpeech()`, by the page going to the background, and whenever its box is no longer in the document (`target.isConnected`, checked before every restart and on every result). `closeSheet()`, `closeBigEdit()`, `openBigEdit()` and the check-in wizard's `render()` call `stopSpeech()` (the words are already in the answer, captured before the redraw). `onstart` clears a reason shown on the button (`lastErr`), so a one-off "(aborted)" from Siri or a call goes once the next session really starts; a reason that recurs is still shown. The label and the toast can no longer disagree: "Listening stopped" is only ever shown by the button that then reads Tap to speak.
- Tested in the scratchpad's `t/t9.mjs` (the stand-in recogniser installed before the app loads: the old ping-pong reproduced on v201, then on v202 Next stopping step 1's session with nothing restarted, step 2's tap starting one alone, words landing, a tidy stop, Escape and the X leaving nothing live, the writing space taking over from the small box and Minimise stopping it, a phone-side abort shown then cleared on the restart, a phone refusing every start ending after four with the one toast and the button reading Tap to speak, a Note sheet's first tap working at once, no page errors). Not tried on the phone: Mark to dictate a whole check-in with Next between steps and watch the microphone dot go off when the sheet closes.

## Asked once, on the first dose (v193, 16 October 2026)
Mark, after v191: logged paracetamol from the Meds tab and it still said 500 mg, without asking how many. **Why:** v191 changed nothing already saved, and nothing pointed to Edit medicine, so Paracetamol was still "500 mg" (Not counted) and Log now logged exactly that, as designed.
- **The question** (`needsDoseForm(m)`, `askDoseForm(m)`, beside `logMed()`): a medicine with no `doseForm`, a dose that is only a strength (`parseStrength()`: "500 mg", "4 mg", "7,500 units"), not ticked Given by the hospital and whose name is not an injection, cream, gel, patch, spray, inhaler, drops, liquid or the like (`UNCOUNTED_NAME_RE`) is asked on its next dose, from Log now, Other time or the Medicine tile: "How do you take Paracetamol?", "Asked once. Saved as "500 mg" so far...", the v191 boxes opening on Tablets, 1, with the old dose as the strength, and **Save and log the dose**. The answer is saved on the medicine (`dose`, `doseForm`, `doseCount`, `strength`; no dose change noted, since nothing changed) and the dose logged with it, so it is never asked again on either phone; **Not counted** keeps the dose as it was (and is not asked again); Cancel, the X or Escape saves and logs nothing. Read-only and viewer are never asked. "2 capsules", "2.5 ml (5 mg)", "7,500 units" on Dalteparin injection and anything already set are never asked.
- **The boxes are shared** (`doseControls(parts, opts)`: the chips, How many each time, the strength, the teal line, `result()`), used by Edit medicine and the question.
- **A different number this once:** Other time on a counted medicine has **How many this time** (a stepper starting at the usual count, "Logged as 1 tablet (5 mg).") and logs that dose (`logMed(m, at, note, doseText)`); the medicine keeps its own.
- **The message names the amount:** "Paracetamol, 2 tablets (1,000 mg), logged" with Undo.
- A dose logged before the question at the old amount is put right from its "..." menu, Change the time or note, Amount.
- Tested in `pw/v193.js` (Diazepam "5 mg" asked from Log now on Tablets, 1, 5 mg, nothing logged before Save, axe, + to 2 tablets (10 mg), logged and saved, the message, the card, the next Log now asking nothing; Other time at 2, Fewer to 1 tablet (5 mg), axe, the medicine keeping 2; Escape on Metoclopramide saving and logging nothing; Not counted on Chlorphenamine logging 4 mg and not asking again; Dalteparin injection and Creon never asked; Senna asked from the Medicine tile; no sideways scroll; 390 light and 320 dark). `pw/v137.js` answers the question with Not counted; `v191`, `v97`, `v101`, `v102`, `v138`, `v141`, `v143`, `v174`, `v186`, `v187` and `smoke` pass.

## How many each time: a medicine's dose set once (v191, 16 October 2026)
Mark: paracetamol showed 500 mg on every dose, but he takes two tablets, so 1,000 mg; the easiest way, without being asked for every dose, and should there be a "these are pills" tick box. Instead of a tick box, each medicine's dose is set once, in Edit medicine (Manage medicines, Edit, or Add a medicine), as three boxes, and Log now (and the Medicine tile, and the reminders) records it with nothing asked:
- **How is it taken?** chips (`DOSE_FORMS`: Tablets, Capsules, Sachets, Liquid (ml), Puffs, Drops, Not counted), then **How many each time** (the Food sheet's `stepper()`, halves allowed, the unit word after the number) and **Strength of one tablet (optional, as on the box)** (`#med-strength`, "e.g. 500 mg"), and a teal line under them, "Each dose is logged as 2 tablets (1,000 mg)." (`.med-dose-preview`). **Not counted** keeps the old free Dose box (injections, creams, "7,500 units"), with no count.
- `composeDose(count, form, strength)` makes the dose text: "2 tablets (1,000 mg)" when the strength is a number and a unit (`parseStrength()`: mg, g, mcg, micrograms, units, iu), "½ tablet (250 mg)", "2 tablets of 30/500" otherwise, "2 capsules" with no strength, "2.5 ml (5 mg)" for a liquid, the typed text when Not counted. Saved as `medicines/{id}.dose` as before, so every place that shows or logs a dose (`logMed()`, the cards, Medicine history, the reports, v187's dose changes) is unchanged, plus `doseForm`, `doseCount` (null when Not counted) and `strength` (no rules change: `medicines` is family write).
- **Medicines from before:** `splitDose(m)` reads the stored three, else the dose as typed: "2 capsules" opens on Capsules, 2; "1 tablet (500 mg)" on Tablets, 1, 500 mg; "2.5 ml (5 mg)" on Liquid, 2.5, 5 mg; anything else ("500 mg", "7,500 units", "Apply to skin") on Not counted with the words as they were, so saving without touching it changes nothing. Paracetamol at "500 mg" opens on Not counted; tap Tablets, press + to 2 and save, and it reads "2 tablets (1,000 mg)" from then on, with the change noted (v187). Doses already logged keep the amount they were logged at; one can be put right with Change the time or note on it.
- Tested in `pw/v191.js` (Mark's paracetamol added as Tablets, 2, 500 mg: the line, the unit word, axe, what is saved, the card, Log now recording 2 tablets (1,000 mg); reopening on Tablets, 2, 500 mg; half a tablet; Creon, Dalteparin (Not counted, saved unchanged with no change noted) and Oramorph opening in the right place; Diazepam 5 mg made 2 tablets (10 mg) with the change noted; no sideways scroll; 390 light and 320 dark). `pw/v97.js` (the photo route now opens 1000 ml as Liquid and 2 tablets as Tablets, 2) and `v187.js` (the hint's new words) follow; `v141`, `v143`, `v101`, `v102` and `smoke` pass. `pw/v173.js` fails at the Symptom sheet's chips, which v179 replaced (not this change).

## Packaged foods by name on the Food sheet (v201, 16 October 2026)
Mark, for Crohn's users most of all: typing "jaffa cake" "just finds cake"; is there a better database that costs nothing? The same one the barcode scanner uses, searched by name. (The UK table does in fact hold "Jaffa cakes" and the sheet's search offers it; the "cake" came from the diary's tag matcher, which still reads the words after saving. A chosen packaged product is saved to My foods, which the matcher takes first, so it stops happening for anything picked here.)
- **Bridge** (`handleFoodSearch()`, `searchOff()`, `OFF_SEARCH_MAX` 8, `OFF_HEADERS`): `POST /food { query }` (any signed-in member, the demo too; `{ barcode }` unchanged) runs Open Food Facts' text search (`cgi/search.pl?search_terms=...&json=1`, the fields of a barcode hit), UK products first (`tagtype_0=countries`, `tag_0=united-kingdom`), the whole database when the UK has nothing, each product through `mapOffProduct()`, nameless ones dropped, cached a day at the edge; 2 to 60 characters; a busy database is 502 "Log it as typed for now." Not verified against the live service from the build machine (openfoodfacts.org is blocked here); the search URL is its documented legacy one, which the v2 endpoint does not replace for text. If it returns nothing real, check `cgi/search.pl` still answers JSON.
- **App** (inside the Food sheet, beside `renderPick()`): with the bridge and a sign-in (`explainAvailable()`; never in the preview) and three or more letters, "Looking in packaged foods…" under the table hits, then 450 ms after typing stops (`askPackaged()`, one call per query, cached for the sheet in `packaged`) a **Packaged foods** heading with up to eight rows (the name, "10 cakes · 377 kcal per 100 g" or "Open Food Facts"); a tap (`takePackaged()`) saves it to My foods as a scan does (`saveCustomFood()` with the barcode and source) and moves to stage two, "Found McVitie's Jaffa Cakes (Open Food Facts), saved to My foods"; a product with no figures is chosen by name alone and the toast says so. Also fixed: with no camera the sheet drew the word "null" between the box and the list (`pick.append` of a null scan control).
- The privacy page's barcode bullet now covers the typed words too ("Scan a barcode, or search packaged foods, only when you do it").
- Checked in headless Chromium on the preview with the bridge mocked and a stand-in sign-in (the waiting line, one call with the query, the heading, both rows with the kcal line, the chosen product in stage two with the toast and in My foods with its barcode, nothing asked and nothing shown without a sign-in, no page errors). The harness's Firebase stub must keep one `currentUser` object across its three module imports (`window.__A`), or the auth module's copy is never signed in.

## Your care circle (v199, 16 October 2026)
Mark: "household" is limiting, since a best friend or a carer may not live in the house; he chose **Your care circle** (my suggestion over his "Your Circle of Trust", which reads as the *Meet the Parents* joke), and the rules: the circle is the patient's; only the patient, and whoever set it up until they hand it over, can invite or remove; a carer may set one up for someone and log from day one, but must confirm the patient's agreement; the privacy page must say that only people you choose see your records and that Mark 1 Apps Ltd never looks (faults are in the code, not in anyone's circle).
- **Words only, nothing moved:** every "household" a person can read is now "care circle" or "circle" (the sign-in line "Only the people you choose see your records: your care circle.", the sign-up intro, the no-circle screen, the invite banner, the set-up welcome, the Settings card, the hints, the privacy page and the terms, both dated 16 October 2026). The database, `households/{hid}`, `hcol()`/`hdoc()`, the ids (`#settings-household`, `#nohousehold`), the Household admin workflow and the bridge paths (`/household`) keep their names.
- **Who sets it up** (`relationChips(holder, prefix)`, Create an account and the no-circle screen): **Who is Daybook for?** with **Me** (relation patient) or **Someone I care for** (carer), which shows **Their first name (the circle is theirs)** (`#signup-for`, `#nohousehold-for`) and a tick, "They have agreed to me keeping their records in Daybook, or cannot decide this for themselves and I am acting for them." (`#signup-agree`); `circleSetupError()` refuses a carer's set-up without either, in the form (`auth/circle` passes the words through `friendlyAuthError()`). Family and friends come in by invite only. The bridge (`/household`, `forName`, `carerAgreed`) refuses a carer without the tick, names the circle after the patient (`name`, plus `patientName`) and keeps `consent { by, byName, at, kind: 'carer' }` on `households/{hid}`.
- **Who runs it** (bridge `circleOf()`, `runsCircle()`; app `runsCircle(owner)`): the patient (relation patient) and the owner. `/invite` and `/member-remove` refuse everyone else ("Only the patient, or whoever set the circle up, can invite someone."); an invite for a second patient is refused; nobody removes themselves, the owner, or (unless they are the patient) the patient. Invites carry `createdByRelation`, and `/invite`, `/invite-info` and `/join` return `patientName` and `fromRelation`, so the words are `inviteWords()`: "Mark has invited you to join their care circle" from the patient, "Shelley has invited Sam to Mark's care circle" from anyone else (the banner, the shared invite text). The Family role hint no longer says "and invite others".
- **Handing it over** (`POST /owner-transfer { uid }`): the owner may hand the circle to another family member; a patient whose owner is not the patient may take it themselves (`uid` their own). `households/{hid}.owner` changes, with `ownerChangedAt` and `ownerChangedBy`. In Settings (`syncHouseholdCard()`): the card is headed "Mark's care circle" (the patient member's name, else the circle's `patientName` or name), the patient first in the list, the sub line "the patient", "runs the circle" or "set it up"; Invite someone and Remove only for those who run it (`#settings-invite-hint` says who can); **Hand this circle to Mark** (`#settings-handover`, the owner when the patient is someone else, with a confirm) or **Take charge of your circle** (the patient when the owner is someone else). `offerCircle()` (2.5 s after a real sign-in, once per phone, `localStorage daybook.circle.offered.{uid}`) asks a patient whose circle a carer set up "This is your care circle. Take charge of it?" with Yes. `loadPatientName()` falls back to the circle's `patientName`, so a carer-made circle names its patient on the reports before they join. Mark's and Shelley's circle: Mark is the patient and the owner, so nothing changes for him; Shelley keeps everything but the Invite button.
- **Privacy and terms:** a "Your care circle" section (what a circle is, a carer's confirmation kept with the date, the records are the patient's whoever typed them in, who invites), the short version's three new lines (only the people you choose; Mark 1 Apps Ltd never looks in running Daybook, the exceptions being help you ask for and agree to, or the law; no circle sees another, enforced by the database), the operator's line reworded the same way, the ICO registration named (v200: registration number ZC275185, confirmed 15 October 2026 to mark@mark1apps.co.uk), Children: a carer may set up a circle; the terms' section 2 says who runs the circle and what setting one up for someone else means. No ICO guidance exists for a carer recording in an app; NHS England's guidance on sharing with unpaid carers (reviewed with the ICO) rests on the person's explicit consent, which an invite is. A solicitor's hour on the page before the stores is still advised.
- Rules: no change (`households/{hid}` is member read, nobody writes; the bridge writes). Checked in headless Chromium with the bridge mocked (the sign-in line, the banner from a carer, the chips with Me pressed, the two fields appearing, a carer refused without the name and without the tick with nothing posted, the payload with `forName` and `carerAgreed`, a refusal landing on the no-circle screen with the words carried, the privacy and terms pages with no "household" and no em dashes, no page errors). The bridge's new routes are read-checked only (`node --check`); the worker deploys with the push. Not tried on a phone.

## How many this time, asked on every dose (v195, 16 October 2026)
Mark, after v193: paracetamol was asked once (he set 2 tablets) and from then on logged 1,000 mg though he had taken one; macrogol is one, two or three sachets. He asked whether there was a setting to be asked the number on a dose. There is now, per medicine: **Ask how many each time I log it** (`medicines/{id}.askCount`, a tick under the dose in Edit medicine and in the first-dose question, shown only for a counted form; `doseControls(parts, { ask })` returns it with the dose). `asksEachTime(m)`: a counted medicine (`doseFormOf(splitDose(m).form)`), not given by the hospital, with the tick, or with no answer yet and when needed (`kind: "prn"`); a regular medicine asks only when ticked, so Creon's 2 capsules still log in one tap. `logMed()` then opens `askCountNow(m)` before logging (Log now, the Medicine tile, the meal prompt; Other time already has its own How many this time): the medicine's name, **How many this time?** (the stepper, starting on the usual count, ml in steps of 2.5), quick **1, 2, 3** (2.5, 5, 10 ml for a liquid), the teal line "Logged as 2 tablets (10 mg).", **Log 2 tablets (10 mg)**, Cancel (any close logs nothing), and how to stop being asked. The medicine's usual count is not changed by it. The first-dose question (v193) now logs what it was told without asking twice, and its hint says the answer is kept and to tick Ask how many each time if the number changes. Macrogol saved as "2 sachets, each in 125 ml water" reads as Not counted (the words after the unit), so it needs setting once: Edit, Sachets, 1, the tick. No rules change (`medicines` is family write). ## Fix the words from a report (v194, 16 October 2026)
Mark, reading a report: "really bad spelling mistakes or poor grammar"; he wanted a link from the note to its date, to fix it, and a way back to the report. Rather than leaving the report, the fix opens over it: every note shown on **Notes for the team** (the weekly notes) and on the **Pain and pain relief** screen (Where it was, the word cards) carries its source (`src`: the entry's id, the field holding the words, a label, the day, time and who), and a **Fix the words** link under it (`fixWordsLink()`, the pencil `#i-edit`, 44px, hidden for read-only and viewer). `openFixWords(src, redraw)` opens one box with those words (Tap to speak and Open full screen as every box), **Save and back to the report** writes that one field alone (`updateEntry()`; a check-in field also gets `editedBy` and `editedAt`), the sheet closes on the report, "Fixed. Back to the report." and the report is drawn again, so its pages carry the corrected words; **Back to the report without changing it** closes it. Sources: a note entry or the note on a reading, dose, symptom or bowel entry (`note`), a check-in's text answers (`REPORT_CHECKIN_KEYS`, the carer's `noticed`), the words under a check-in slider (`painNote`, `sleepNote`, `moodNote`, `worstPainNote`), a pain reading's note and a pain relief dose's note. Each week of Notes for the team shows its four most telling notes as before, now with **Show all N notes this week, to check or fix them** (`state.notesAllOpen`, by the week's first day) and back. Not on the Daybook Assistant summary (its words are the assistant's; Show the notes instead first) or inside Preview (the pages are a picture of the report: fix on the screen under it). Checked in headless Chromium on the preview: eight links, a dose's note fixed and found under Show all, 26 links with every note, the Pain report's first word card fixed in place, no page errors. Not tried on a phone.

## Unfinished check-ins survive the sheet closing, and bigger writing boxes (v184, 16 October 2026)
Mark: half way through the morning check-in, if it closes, everything is lost and he starts again; his partner can read what he wrote so far, but if they close it and go back in, it is gone for them too. And: could the text boxes be much bigger, or pop out of the screen.
- **The cause.** v160 kept an unfinished check-in whole (`localStorage` and the household's `profile/main.checkinDrafts`), but `closeSheet()` cleared it whenever the sheet closed by any route (the X, a tap outside, Escape, a swipe), on the assumption that only the app being killed was an accident. And because the household copy is shared, the other person opening the draft and closing it deleted it for both.
- **The fix.** `closeSheet()` no longer clears a check-in's kept copy (`state.checkinProgress` is only reset). It is cleared by **Save check-in** (`finished`, `clearProgress()`) and by **Start again**, and expires after 7 days. Closing writes what is on the step first (`openSheet`'s onClose: `capture()` then `keep()`, only when something has been answered, so opening and closing an empty check-in leaves no "Unfinished" row), so the last 300 ms of typing is kept too. Today's row says "Unfinished, kept as you left it. Tap to carry on" from then on. When it was started by someone else the note reads "Mark's unfinished check-in from 09:12 is back" (`restored.by`). Both phones share one kept copy per slot and day, the newest write wins.
- **Bigger boxes.** Every text box in a sheet (`#sheet-body textarea`) is at least 9em tall, grows as you type up to half the screen (`growBox()`), and has an **Open full screen** link under it (`enhanceTextareas()`, from the same MutationObserver as the drafts, `.bigedit-open`): `openBigEdit()` covers the screen with a writing space (`.bigedit`: the question as a heading, Done at the top, a big box at 18px, its own Tap to speak button, sized to the visual viewport so the keyboard does not hide it). It is a second textarea kept in step with the first (`input` copied across and re-dispatched), so drafts, the kept check-in and Tap to speak all see the same words; Escape or Done closes just it, closing the sheet closes it too, and the sheet is inert behind it.
- Not run in a browser (no `pw/` harness this session): try it on the phone, in particular closing a half-done check-in with the X and reopening it, the other phone opening it, and the full-screen box with the keyboard up.

## The full-screen writing space: Tap to speak and Minimise at the top (v185, 16 October 2026)
Mark, on v184's full-screen box: "it has sort of worked"; Tap to speak should be at the top with a minimise to its right, and the only way to get a truly large box was to open it and then tap the bottom of the screen (the original sheet showed through under the panel until something forced a re-measure). Now `.bigedit` is a full-screen layer that always covers everything (`inset: 0`, opaque), and the `.bigedit-panel` inside it follows the visible viewport above the keyboard (`fit()` on the visual viewport's resize and scroll, the window's resize, and again at 120, 400 and 900 ms after opening, with the page scrolled back to the top). Top row: **Tap to speak** (`.bigedit-bar`, takes the width) and **Minimise** (`.bigedit-done`, the shrink icon `#i-shrink`) to its right; then the question in small grey type; then the box filling the rest. Where speech is not available the bar holds only Minimise. Not run in a browser: try it on the phone with the keyboard up.

## Treatment cycles as the team counts them, and side effects asked in the check-ins (v189, 16 October 2026)
Mark: a cycle is several sessions then a rest (his: three weekly chemo sessions and a rest week), dates get moved (his cycle 2 starts on Wednesday 11 November), anyone must be able to put their sessions in by hand, and side effects come 24 to 48 hours after a session, so the check-ins should ask. Until v189 every chemo session started a new cycle, so weekly chemo never got past day 7 on the chart.
- **Cycles** (`sessionDays()`, `isCycleStart()`, `guessCycleStart()`, `cycleStarts()`, `cycleInfo(day)`; `cycleFor()` kept for old callers): a session (not Other) starts a cycle when `days/{day}.cycleStart` says so, otherwise when it is the first session ever or more than `NEW_CYCLE_GAP` (8) days after the one before (a rest week, or 2- and 3-weekly treatment; weekday radiotherapy stays one cycle across weekends). `cycleInfo()` gives the cycle's number, its sessions, the session's number in it, its length (to the next start; for the last one marked, its sessions plus one more gap, so three weekly sessions make 28 days, or the cycle before's length with a single session), and its rest days (from the last session plus the usual gap). Nothing is migrated: sessions saved before v189 have no `cycleStart` and are guessed. **v190 (Mark: "the rest week also counts, it is a 4 week cycle"):** a cycle's length is its sessions plus one more gap of rest (three weekly sessions make 28 days, rest week included), even when the next cycle starts later; the days of a delay between the end of one cycle and the start of the next belong to neither, are not shaded as rest, and the tile reads "Next cycle" with "Cycle 2 starts 11 Nov, in 26 days."
- **The day sheet** (`openDaySheet()`): "Cycle 2, session 1 of 3, a 28-day cycle." above the boxes, a **Starts a new cycle** tick (shown as guessed or saved, always on and disabled for the first session ever, hidden for Other) saved as `cycleStart`, and **Move to another day** (`moveSession()`: the new day takes the type, done tick and cycle start in one batch; refused onto a day with a session).
- **Add sessions** (`#chemo-add-run` under the Sessions bar, `openAddSessions()`, `runDays()`): the kind, the first date, how many (1 to 60), how often (`RUN_EVERY`: every weekday, weekly, every 2, 3 or 4 weeks; a date falling at a weekend moves to the Monday), "The first one starts a new cycle" (on); a preview names the days and any already taken (left as they are); one batch, the first `cycleStart` as ticked and the rest false; the calendar jumps to the first month. Hidden for read-only and viewer.
- **Calendar and tile:** rest days hatched (`.cal-cell.is-rest`, a key swatch "Rest week"), a line per cycle touching the month under the grid (`#cal-cycles`, `renderCycleLines()`: "Cycle 2, chemo: 11 Nov, 18 Nov and 25 Nov. Rest week from 2 Dec. 28 days."), the cell's spoken label names the cycle and session; the tile reads "Session day", "Rest week" or "Day 10" with "Cycle 2 (chemo), day 10 of 28. Next session 18 Nov, in 3 days."; the Sessions line names the cycle and session of the next one.
- **Side effects in the check-ins** (`SIDEFX_ITEMS`: Sickness, Tiredness, Poor appetite, Mouth, Diarrhoea, Constipation, Tingling, Taste changes; `FX_WORDS` None, Mild, Moderate, Severe; `sessionBefore(day)`, `FX_DAYS` 3): a `sidefx` step after the symptoms question in both the morning and the evening check-ins, only on the three days after a session (`checkinSteps()`), titled "Since chemo on Tuesday (day 3 after it), any side effects?", four chips a row, tap again to clear. Saved on the check-in as `sidefx { session, treatment, grades { name: 0 to 3 } }` (null skipped), worded on the review and card by `fxText()`, and every one above None is also written as a side effect (`syncFxEntries()`: `entries/{day}_{slot}_fx_{n}` { type symptom, what, grade, value 3, 6 or 9, causeKind treatment, causeId, causeName, sessionDay, fromCheckin }; put back to None or skipped, it is deleted), so the Side effects card, the timeline ("Sickness: moderate", "Side effect of Chemo on 13 Oct · From the evening check-in"), Notes for the team and the reports carry it. An edit from the Symptom sheet clears `grade`. No rules change (`entries` and `days` are family write).
- **The chart** ("How treatment goes", was "By day after treatment"): **After each session** (day 0 the session, up to `SESSION_WINDOW` 7) or **Through the cycle** (day 1 the cycle's first session, up to `CYCLE_WINDOW` 42) (`CYCLE_VIEWS`, `state.cycleView`), and a **Side effects** measure (the worst rated that day, none 0 to severe 9; the tooltip gives the word). The sentence and the note under the chart follow the view; with no session it says to mark one.
- Checked in headless Chromium on the in-memory preview with Firebase stubbed (the CDNs are blocked here, so no chart was drawn): the tile, the next-session line and the cycle lines, Add sessions for 11 Nov making 11, 18 and 25 Nov as cycle 2 with a 28-day length and rest days, the day sheet's line, Move to 19 Nov, both views' sentences, the evening check-in's step 7 of 12 with Moderate and Mild, the review, two side effect entries with grade, value, cause and session, the timeline and the Side effects card, no page errors. Not tried on a phone or in dark mode.

Planned days keep the terracotta laser, so gold means done. The key's Done swatch is `.key-gold`; reduced motion gives a still 3px gold ring. ## Treatment days stand out, and the next one has a laser (v182, 15 October 2026)
Mark, wanting better use of the calendar: treatment days to stand out more, "maybe the Mark 1 Apps button laser going around them". The laser is the Forge app's `.forge-laser` (in the `forge-app` repository, `css/styles.css` about line 2335: a conic-gradient comet masked to a 3px ring and turned by an animated `@property` angle). Here, in Daybook's colours: every treatment day (`.cal-cell.is-chemo`) is bolder with a 2px teal ring and a larger number and letter, a done one has a green ring, and **v183 (Mark: "looks pants, only one day, needs to be bigger or faster, not a couple of orange pixels")**: every planned session not yet done (`.is-chemo:not(.is-done)`) gets the comet, a 4px ring with a bright white and gold head and a long fading terracotta tail (about 150 degrees), 1.8 s a turn, each cell starting at a random point (`--laser-delay`), plus a pulsing terracotta glow (`calGlow`); done days stay calm green; the next session's label still says "the next one" (`.is-next`, `nextSession`); under reduced motion a still 3px terracotta ring; the key's swatch reads "Planned". Many planned days animate together (a month of daily radiotherapy is about twenty): if the phone struggles, go back to the next one only. Not run in a browser: look at it on the phone, light and dark. `@property` needs iOS 16.4 or later; before that the cell just keeps its teal ring.

## Remove a symptom you added (v181, 15 October 2026)

## A changed dose kept straight (v187, 16 October 2026)
Mark: Shelley typed a medicine's new, higher dose; the new one showed it, but the old course under Finished courses showed the new dose too, though it had been lower. **Why:** a medicine is one record with one Dose box; Edit overwrites it, and the Finished courses card, Medicine history's list and sheet and the Medicine history report all read that box. So when the old course's box was changed to the new dose and it was then ended, with a new course added, the old one read the new dose too. The doses logged were never wrong: each carries the amount it was taken at (`logMed()` copies `m.dose` into the entry).
- **Shown as taken:** `shownDose(m, doses?)` (beside `medStatus()`): for anything not Taking now (finished or stopped), the Dose box when some logged dose matches it, otherwise the dose most often logged (the latest of equals, so a one-off "2 tablets" does not take over); the box only when nothing is logged. Used by `finishedCard()`, `openMedHistoryList()`, `openMedHistory()` (its head is redrawn once every dose has loaded) and `buildMedsReport()` (finished and stopped rows; "Also logged as" is now measured against the shown dose). Nothing stored changes, so the old course reads its own dose again on its own.
- **Change it in place:** Edit medicine has a hint under the dose for an existing medicine (`.med-dose-hint`, "Changing the dose? Change it here and save." since v191): change the dose here and save, it stays the same medicine, the doses already logged keep their amount and the change is noted, no need to add it again or end this one. A save that changes the dose of a medicine still being taken (in use and not ended) appends `{ from, to, day, by }` to `medicines/{id}.doseChanges` (no rules change: `medicines` is family write). It shows as "Dose changed from 15 mg to 30 mg on 16 Oct, by Shelley." on the Meds card for 14 days (`.med-was`, `recentDoseChange()`), on the medicine's history sheet (every change) and as a pill on its row in the Medicine history report (`doseChangeText()`).
- Tested in `pw/v187.js` (Shelley's way: an old course with two doses at 10 mg, edited to 30 mg and ended, no change noted, a new course at 30 mg added through the sheet; Finished courses, Medicine history's rows and the report showing the old one at 10 mg with no "Also logged as" and the new one at 30 mg; the better way: Senna changed in place noting from, to, day and who, the card and the history sheet saying so; the hint; axe on Edit medicine and the Meds tab; no sideways scroll; 390 light and 320 dark); `v141`, `v143`, `v174` and `smoke` pass.

## Side effects of treatment and medicines (v173, 15 October 2026)
Mark: "we need to add somewhere a place we can put side effects of medications. For example when I'm taking chemo I need to add side effects to it."
- **Not a new kind of entry:** a side effect is a Symptom (the v92 tile: which symptom, how bad 1 to 10, time, note) that says what caused it. Stored beside the symptom as `causeKind` ("med" or "treatment"), `causeId` (the medicine's id, or the treatment's key: chemo, radio, immuno) and `causeName` (as it was called then), via `causeOf()`, `causeValue()`, `causeSelect()`; an edit that clears the cause stores them as `null`. No rules change (`entries` is family write), nothing to migrate.
- **The Symptom sheet** (`openAdd('symptom', entry, preset)`, the third argument pre-selects a cause) has a new **Is it a side effect of something? (optional)** box under the slider: a native select, "No, or not sure", then **Treatment** (Chemo, Radiotherapy, Immunotherapy; only with the Cancer pack) and **Medicines** (every active one, A to Z; a medicine since stopped or removed still shows on an entry that names it). The chip list grew for the usual side effects: Taste changes, Poor appetite, Constipation, Aches and pains, Headache, Hair loss, Sore hands or feet (beside Sickness, Mouth, Skin, Breathing, Dizziness, Tingling, Tiredness, Other).

The timeline's line under the symptom reads "Side effect of Chemo" in place of "Symptom".
- **Notes for the team:** the Symptoms topic names the cause in a flagged line ("sickness (side effect of Chemo) up to 6/10 on 13 Oct"), and a new teal **Side effects** Context line lists every side effect with a cause, mild ones included, by cause: "Side effects logged: Dalteparin injection: tingling up to 4/10; Chemo: mouth up to 3/10, sickness up to 6/10." Levels and thresholds are unchanged. The demo fixture's sickness and mouth symptoms are side effects of Chemo.

## Scan a barcode on the Food sheet (v172, 15 October 2026)
Mark: "a bar code scanner would be amazing" (wanted most for Crohn's users, who eat a lot of packaged food), then, high on his medication, the same question again: can the phone camera read a barcode and tell us what it is. Yes.
- **In the app** (`foodScanControl()`, `startBarcodeCamera()`, `lookupBarcode()`, `barcodeValid()`, all beside the Tap to speak block): a **Scan a barcode** button under the search box on the Food sheet (`#food-scan`, shown only with the bridge and a real or demo sign-in, `scanAvailable()`, and a camera), opening a panel in the sheet with the live camera (`getUserMedia`, rear camera, a framed band in the middle), a status line (`role="status"`), **Or type the number under the barcode** (`#scan-number`, Enter or **Look it up**) and **Close the camera** (`#scan-close`). The phone's own `BarcodeDetector` is used where it exists (Chrome on Android); otherwise the ZXing library, self-hosted in `vendor/zxing-library.min.js` (`@zxing/library` 0.21.3 UMD, Apache-2.0, licence beside it; iPhone Safari has no BarcodeDetector), loaded only when Scan is tapped and fed a frame about nine times a second. A number is trusted only when its GS1 check digit is right (`barcodeValid()`, EAN-13, EAN-8, UPC-A 12, 14 digits); a 12-digit UPC-A is padded with a leading 0 for the database. The camera is stopped on a code, on Close, when the panel's video leaves the page, and a refused camera says "Allow the camera for Daybook in your phone's settings, or type the number below." with focus in the number box.
- **On the bridge** (`handleFood()`, `mapOffProduct()`): `POST /food { barcode }` for any signed-in member (the demo too, since it is a public product lookup): 8 to 14 digits, asks Open Food Facts (`world.openfoodfacts.org/api/v2/product/{code}.json`, the bridge's own User-Agent, nothing about the person, Cloudflare caches the public answer for a day) and returns `{ found, code, name (brand first when the name lacks it), brand, quantity, serving, per100 { kcal (from kJ when needed), prot, carb, fat, sat, sugar, fibre, salt }, hasFigures, source }`. A miss or a record with no name is `{ found: false }`, not an error; a busy or unreachable database is 502 with plain words and "type the food in". No key, no cost, no new secret; deploys with the worker.
- **What a scan does:** a product with figures is saved into My foods (`saveCustomFood(name, per100, { barcode, source })`, which now also keeps sat, sugar, fibre and salt per 100 g and updates `state.profile` at once), then the sheet moves to stage two with that name and says "Found Ambrosia Rice Pudding: 96 kcal, 3.1 g protein per 100 g (Open Food Facts)". **My foods are now matched first, by whole name** (`customFoodRows()`, `matchFoodText()`): a saved or scanned product comes out of the text before any table lookup, as a table-shaped row, so it earns tags from its own label (Protein, Fibre, High sugar, High sat fat) and its figures go into the estimates; "Ambrosia Rice Pudding" is that product, not the table's canned rice pudding. A barcode the database lacks says so and suggests typing the name and using Enter from the packet.
- **Wording:** the privacy page (dated 15 October 2026) has a "Scan a barcode, only when you tap it" bullet (the camera looks on the phone, no picture is kept or sent, only the number goes through the bridge to Open Food Facts, nothing about you goes with it, the found product is saved in My foods) and About's data sources credit Open Food Facts under the Open Database Licence; the LICENSE lists ZXing and Open Food Facts.
- **Not verified from the build machine:** its network blocks openfoodfacts.org and the CDNs, so the Open Food Facts response shape (`status`, `product.nutriments['energy-kcal_100g']`, `proteins_100g` and so on) is from its documented API, tested with a mocked reply; the first real scan on Mark's phone is the real test. If a UK product is missing, it is not in the database yet.
- Tested in `food_test.mjs` (the scratchpad: the real bridge with Open Food Facts mocked: name and figures, kJ to kcal, a comma decimal, the User-Agent and cache, spaces and hyphens, a miss, a nameless record, too few digits, letters, no sign-in, the database busy, no connection) and `pw/v172.js` (Chromium's fake camera playing a real EAN-13 image through `--use-file-for-fake-video-capture`: the button, axe closed and open, the code read by ZXing and the bridge asked for exactly that number, the sheet moving on with the name and the message, the camera let go, a refused camera, the typed number, a wrong check digit caught before any lookup, a miss, a bridge error, no sideways scroll, 390 light and 320 dark). `v171`, `v119`, `v121`, `v153`, `v90`, `v148`, `v116`, `v132`, `v170`, `v110` and `smoke` pass.

## Food search that finds what was meant (v171, 15 October 2026)
Mark, especially for Crohn's users: "when you type on food the responses are terrible", typing "pain au chocolat" returned only chocolate milk.
- **Cause.** The Food sheet's table search wanted the whole typed phrase inside one table name (`includes(tq)`), so a multi-word everyday name found nothing; and `matchFoodText()` then fell back to single words, so the one word it knew ("chocolat", fuzzily "chocolate") quietly became Chocolate, milk.
- **`searchFoodTable(index, query, limit)`** (beside `lookupPhrase()`): first the everyday names the app knows (`FOOD_ALIASES`, whole phrase, or a half-typed multi-word one such as "pain au choc"), then table names that hold every word typed (the last word may be half typed, an earlier word a letter out from six letters), best first. A single word of several is never offered on its own. With no hit the sheet says "Nothing close in the UK food table. Log it as typed, then say what is in it." and keeps the Log as typed row.
- **`matchFoodText()`** now returns content words nothing was found for as `unmatched` when other words did match ("Not recognised: tagine" for "chicken tagine"), so they show in the Food diary and are left out of the estimates as "not counted", instead of vanishing. French linking words (au, aux, de, du, des, la, le, les, un, une) are stop words.
- **New everyday names** in `FOOD_ALIASES` (the table has them under other names): pain au chocolat and almond croissants as Croissants (nearest row; the table has no pain au chocolat), Danish and cinnamon pastries, cappuccino, latte, flat white, mocha, americano, espresso, coffee, hot chocolate, chocolate milk, yoghurt (plain, Greek), rice pudding, korma and tikka masala, naan, cheese toastie, cheese or beans on toast, tomato soup, bone broth, smooth peanut butter, white rice and pasta. Tested against the real table with the scratchpad's `foodtest.mjs` (`SEARCH="a|b" node foodtest.mjs "phrase"` prints the matches and the search results).
- Barcode scanning came next, in v172 (below).
- Tested in `pw/v171.js` (pain au chocolat and pain au choc offering Croissants and no chocolate milk, chicken kor, two words both required, latte, gnocchi saying so and keeping Log as typed, axe, 390 light and 320 dark). `v119`, `v121`, `v153`, `v170` and `smoke` pass.

## Tap to speak keeps listening, and keeps the screen awake (v170, 15 October 2026)
Mark: "the phone times out half way through shutting down whilst you are still talking", then "it's the phone timing out we need to stop it from going to screen saver". Two causes: the phone's recogniser ends its own session after a pause or a time limit even with `continuous` on, and the screen's auto-lock dimmed and locked the phone mid-sentence.
- **`speakButton()` restarts itself** (`want` flag, `begin()`): a session that ends by itself is followed 150 ms later (`SPEAK_RESTART_MS`) by a fresh one whose base is the words already in the box, so nothing is lost or doubled. The button keeps saying "Listening, tap to stop" throughout. It really stops only on a tap (also in the gap between two sessions), a refused microphone, the page going to the background (`visibilitychange`), or `SPEAK_DEAD_MAX` (4) sessions in a row that ended within 1.5 s with nothing heard (a toast says so). Focus returns to the box only on a real stop, so the keyboard does not jump up between sessions.
- **The screen is kept awake while listening** (Wake Lock API, as the recorders use): requested on the tap and by each new session, asked for again when the phone takes the lock back while still wanted, released on stop. Needs iOS 16.4+ in the Home Screen app (reliable from 18.4); an older iPhone cannot be stopped from locking by a web app, and the keyboard microphone still works there.
- Tested in `pw/v170.js` (the harness's new `initScript` option installs a stand-in recogniser and wake lock before the app loads: start, words after what was there, a session dropped by the phone and a new one carrying on with the words joined, several drops in a row, one lock held, a lock taken back asked for again, a tap stopping it and releasing the lock, nothing restarting afterwards, dead starts stopping, a refused microphone, a tap between sessions). `v167`, `v129`, `v136`, `v164`, `v153` and `smoke` pass.

## Changing one answer of a check-in comes straight back to the review (v169, 15 October 2026)
Mark, editing his morning check-in (a symptoms answer that should read "I had an accident in the night that involved a shower and a lot of shame"): "it lets me make the changes but it isn't saving the edits". Reproduced in the preview: nothing was failing to save. Tapping an answer on the review screen opened its step, the main button there was **Next**, which went on to the following question and then the next, and the only thing that saves is **Save changes** on the review at the very end; closing, Cancel or the X on the way threw the edit away.
- **From the review** (`fromReview`, set when a review row is tapped, cleared on Done, Clear this answer and the review's Back): the step's main button reads **Done, back to the review** and returns to the review with the new value in place; the Skip link reads **Clear this answer** and returns to the review with the answer cleared; Back is not offered (the walk is one answer long). The review's hint says, for a saved check-in, "Tap an answer to change it. Nothing is saved until you press Save changes." This is for every check-in, a new one's review included; the ordinary walk from the first question is unchanged (Back, Skip, Next, then Review).
- **Cancel asks before throwing a change away** (`cancelLink()`, `dirty()`): on an edit of a saved check-in, Cancel with a changed answer, score, words or hours (the current step's value read first, empty and missing counting as the same) shows "You have changed an answer and not saved it. Throw the change away?" with **Keep editing** and **Throw it away** (`.wiz-cancelbox`); with nothing changed it closes at once as before. The round X and Escape still close as Cancel does without asking (a sheet-wide close guard is not built).
- Tested in `pw/v169.js` (the review hint; a words answer from the review offering Done and Clear this answer with no Back or Next, axe; Done returning to the review with the words in place and nothing saved yet; Cancel asking, axe, Keep editing putting the link back; a slider answer coming straight back; Save changes storing the words and the score stamped as edited; Cancel with no change closing at once; Throw it away keeping the old answer; a new evening check-in skipped through to its review, a row tapped, Done and the same return; Back from the review still opening the last question; 390 light and 320 dark). `pw/v82.js` and `pw/v131.js` were written around the old route (pressing Next after tapping a review row) and now use the one press; `v129`, `v146`, `v147`, `v156`, `v160`, `v164`, `v166`, `v145` and `smoke` pass.

## The Guide, and the Tap to speak hint (v167, 14 October 2026)
Mark, on Tap to speak: "clear, concise instructions", a walk-through, and a user guide built into More; then "screenshots have real value, even more so if you animate them being completed". So the Guide is made of the real screens, animated.
- **The frames** are made by `tools/guide_shots.js` (in the repo, so they are retaken in one command whenever the look changes: `node tools/guide_shots.js`, Playwright's `playwright-core` on `NODE_PATH`, Chromium at `$CHROME`, the app served at `$DAYBOOK_URL`; offline, `$DAYBOOK_HARNESS` names the scratchpad harness whose `routes()` serves the Firebase stub, the chart and PDF libraries and the fonts). It drives the in-memory preview (made-up data only, never real) at 390 by 844, DPR 2, light, `--lang=en-GB` (Chromium draws its own date and time boxes in its UI language), London time fixed at Friday 16 October 2026 10:30 (so Your week is offered and the morning check-in due), the Preview pill and the Guest chip hidden, and for each step takes a screenshot, converts it to WebP (quality 0.8) in a second page through a canvas, scrolls the next tap target into view first and records where it is as fractions of the frame. Output: `guide/guide.json` { made, width, height, topics: [{ key, title, sub, intro, steps: [{ file, caption, tap? { x, y } }] }] } and `guide/{topic}-{n}.webp` (36 frames, about 2.2 MB in all, 50 to 100 KB each; only a topic's own frames are fetched, one ahead).
- **Eight topics:** Log a reading (reading, 4), The morning and evening check-ins (checkin, 6), Medicines and reminders (meds, 6), A question for the team (question, 3), Your week (week, 4), Reports, and emailing them (reports, 5, the send sheet and the Preview viewer), Who to contact, and the set-up (contacts, 4), A carer's phone (carer, 4). The captions live in the script and nowhere else.
- **The player** (`openGuide()`, `openGuideTopic()`, `guideShow()`, `guidePlay()`, `guideStop()`; `GUIDE_STEP_MS` 2600): **Guide** is the second row of More (`#more-guide`, after Set-up) and `view-guide` (`PAGE_TITLES.guide`, More highlighted): the topics as `.menu-row`s with their step counts, loaded from `guide/guide.json?v=` on first open (an offline first open says it needs a connection); a topic shows a phone frame (`.guide-phone`, 390:844, at most 330px wide) with the step's picture, a pulsing terracotta ring (`.guide-tap`, positioned by the step's fractions; none on a step with nothing to tap), "Step 2 of 6" and the caption, Previous, Pause or Play, Next (48px), and **In words**, the captions as a numbered list with the current one in teal (a tap on a line jumps to it). It plays itself on opening and loops; a tap on the picture, an arrow or a line pauses it; under `prefers-reduced-motion` it opens paused and the ring does not pulse. `document.title` is "Daybook: Guide: Log a reading". **All topics** goes back to the list, Back to More.
- **The Tap to speak hint** (`SPEAK_HINT`, `speakHintFor(btn)`): "Tap, say it, tap again to stop. The words appear in the box and can be changed like typed text." as a `.speakhint` line under the button on the first `SPEAK_HINT_TIMES` (3) sheets on this phone that carry one (counted per sheet through `state.sheetSeq`, which `openSheet()` increments, so every words box of a five-step check-in shows it and counts once), kept in `localStorage` `daybook.speak.hints`; set to 3 the first time the recognition hears something. The Guide page says the same under its list.
- Tested in `pw/v167.js` (the JSON's eight topics with every frame on disk and no em dashes; the hint under the Note sheet's button with the count 1, the Question sheet 2, a check-in 3 and still on its later steps, a fourth sheet with the button and no hint; More's five rows with Set-up then Guide; the Guide list with eight rows, the title and More highlighted, axe; a topic on Step 1 of 4 with the frame loaded, the ring where the recorded tap is, the caption, playing, axe; playing on to step 2 by itself; Next pausing, staying put while paused, Previous, the last step with no ring, Next wrapping, a line of the words jumping, Play going on from the next step, All topics, the carer topic, Back to More; reduced motion opening paused; 320 dark fitting with axe). `pw/smoke.js` expects five rows on More; `v164`, `v136`, `v129`, `v131`, `v147`, `v160`, `v90`, `v153`, `v145` and `v166` pass.

## Your week for carers, as you see it; the row Friday to Sunday; saved as you go (v166, 14 October 2026)
Mark: Your week was showing up on the carer's phone as if it were theirs; it should "either reflect their opinion on the patient or not be there at all"; the week ends Sunday and the option should come up on the Friday; it should save as they go and remind them it is not finished. And "how do we know someone is a carer in a household?": the members record's `relation`.
- **Who is the patient** (`isCarerUser()`, beside `patientFullName()`): `relation` "patient" is the patient; "carer" is a carer; "" (family or friend) counts as a carer when the household's patient is named (Settings' Name on the report, else the member whose relation is patient) and the first name is someone else's; the preview, the demo and a household with no patient named read as the patient. `emailSignOff()` uses it (the same answer as before for every case that existed).
- **The carer's slot** `weekcarer`: `CHECKIN_QUESTIONS.weekcarer` (no pack questions): Tiredness got in the way of their week?, Seemed low, or withdrawn?, Pain or symptoms got in the way of the day?, Needed more help than usual? (`wkcTired`, `wkcLow`, `wkcSymptoms`, `wkcHelp`, 0 to 3 in the same four words), then What helped them this week, as you saw it?, What was hardest for them?, Their week in a sentence (`helped`, `hardest`, `sentence`). Saved as `entries/{day}_weekcarer` { type "checkin", slot "weekcarer" }, one a Monday-to-Sunday week, kept apart from the patient's `_week`. `isPatientCheckin()` leaves it out (never a patient figure); `isWeekSlot()` names both; Notes for the team skips both. `checkinTitle(slot, e)`: on the carer's own phone "Your week, as you see it", on the timeline "The week as Shelley saw it" (the card's rows from the carer questions). `weekSlot()` is the slot this phone asks.
- **The row on Today** (`renderWeekRow()`, `#week-check-title` set per slot): shown from Friday to Sunday for the week of the day on screen ("Five minutes, any time up to Sunday. Last done 8 Oct." and on Sunday, raised, "Last day of the week. Five minutes."); Monday to Thursday only when last week's was started and not finished ("Last week's, started 8 Oct, not finished yet. Tap to carry on", `row.dataset.day` names the week it opens); a started one in the week on screen reads "Started 16 Oct, not finished yet. Tap to carry on" and is raised (`is-due`); done reads "Done 16 Oct 18:05, tap to change" (`is-done`). The demo fixture's weeks moved to -15 and -8 (earlier weeks, so the current week is always offered new) and gained Shelley's `weekcarer` at -8.
- **Saved as you go** (`openWeekCheck(initialDay, slot)`): every chip tap writes at once and every box 800 ms after typing stops (`keep()`, through `saveCheckin(slot, day, answers, existing, { quiet: true, done })`: no toast, and no "edited" stamp while `done` is false, since filling it in is not an edit), `done: false` until **Finish** writes `done: true` ("Your week saved"); **Close, carry on later** (or any close: the sheet's close guard flushes a pending save, and `finishing` stops it writing the unfinished copy back over Finish) keeps what is there. A finished one opens with Save changes and Cancel, and a change to it is saved at once with the edited stamp. `saveCheckin()` returns what it wrote (or null when nothing changed), and `done` is part of its no-change comparison; a check saved before v166 has no `done` and counts as done. `weekCheckFor(day, slot)` and `weekQuestions(existing, slot)` take the slot.
- **The Mood and wellbeing report:** each week block carries the carer's check as one line after the patient's sentence, "As Shelley saw it: Brighter by the weekend, and eating again." (`w.carer`, the sentence, else what was hardest, else what helped; `.wk-carer` on the screen's cards, a `.sm` line in the printed table; `cols.checks` counts carer checks too), and `moodAiText()` adds "Weekly checks by the carer, as they saw it (not the patient's own words)". The privacy page names "a carer's own weekly check on how the patient seemed" (dated 14 October 2026; the terms unchanged).
- Tested in `pw/v166.js` (the harness's new `clock` option fixes the date, here Friday 16 October 2026: the patient's row on Friday, hidden on Wednesday and Monday; a carer's title; the carer's sheet (title, hint, four scored questions, three boxes, no badge, Finish, Close, carry on later) with axe; a tap saved at once as `_weekcarer` with done false by the carer, typing saved after it stops with no edited stamp, the row raised and saying started, reopening with everything in place, Finish writing done true with no edited stamp, the row done, the timeline's "The week as Guest saw it", Save changes and Cancel on a finished one and a change stamped as edited; Wednesday with last week's unfinished offering it and opening 5 to 11 Oct with Shelley's answers; the patient's own sheet with its badge saving a tap at once as `_week`; the Mood screen's card and the printed table with "As Shelley saw it"; 320 dark with axe). `pw/v156.js` runs on the fixed Friday (`clock: FRIDAY`) now the row is weekend-only; `v90.js` and `v148.js` carry the privacy date; `v157`, `v160`, `v147`, `v82`, `v158`, `v133`, `v164` and `smoke` pass.

## Add a new contact and Edit contacts above Who to contact (v165, 14 October 2026)
Mark: two buttons, Add new contact and Edit contacts, above Who to contact on More (Edit contacts alone sat under the list until then). The `.btnrow.calls-actions` under the heading: **Add a new contact** (`#calls-add`, primary) and **Edit contacts** (`#calls-edit`, outlined; hidden by `renderCalls()` while there are no contacts, when Add is the one button). Both open `openContactsSheet(addNew)` (the v110 sheet; the `calls-edit` click handler became this function): Add is titled "Add a new contact" and puts the v158 new block at the top with focus in Name (with no saved contacts the one blank block is the new one, outlined and headed New contact); Edit is "Who to contact" as before, with its own Add a new contact button inside. ## The set-up (v164, 14 October 2026)
Mark, having only just found Settings: "that's one of the most important pages we have, as it's where users get to choose cancer, Crohn's or menopause. I don't think we currently have a set-up process, do we?" There was none: a new household landed on an empty Today with Cancer treatment ticked by default and Settings two taps down under More.
- **The walk-through** (`openSetup(kind)`, in the ordinary sheet, "Step i of n" with Back, Skip and Next; `kind` 'full' or 'phone'): **Let's set Daybook up** (what it covers; Start), **What is Daybook for?** (the packs as tick boxes with their hints, Menopause greyed as coming later; at least one must be ticked, "Tick at least one. It can be changed later in Settings."), **Crohn's or colitis** (only with that pack: which it is, a stoma, the usual number a day, 0 to 30 in halves), **Your name on the reports** (`profile/main.reportName`, the hospital's form of it), **Who to contact** (one contact: name, role, phone, extension, email, the same checks as the contacts sheet, saved into `profile/main.calls`; the draft remembers which slot it wrote, `draft.contactAt`, so Back and Next never add it twice), **Put Daybook on your Home Screen** (the iPhone, Android or desktop steps by user agent; when already installed, `isStandalone()`, "Daybook is on your Home Screen." and **Turn on reminders on this phone**, `setReminders(true)`), **Talk instead of type** (a box to try Tap to speak in; Done). Every step saves as it is passed (`saveProfilePatch()`), the draft keeps the answers for Back, and Done saves `profile/main.setupDone: true` with "You're set. Today is where each day starts." **Finish later** closes it. The 'phone' kind, for someone joining an existing household by invite, is the welcome, the Home Screen and Tap to speak only (three steps): the packs, the name and the contacts are the household's.
- **When it opens:** `state.setupPending` is set to 'full' by Create an account and the no-household screen when a household is made, and to 'phone' by a join (both screens with a code, and `enterApp()`'s pending-invite branch); `enterApp()` opens it 700 ms after the app is in, for family and read-only never the viewer, and clears the flag. **Set-up** is the first row of More's menu (`#more-setup`, `renderSetupRow()` from `syncSettings()`: "Not done yet. What Daybook is for, your name, who to contact, the Home Screen and Tap to speak. Five minutes." with `is-due`, or "What Daybook is for, your name, who to contact, the Home Screen and Tap to speak. Run it again any time."), and Settings opens with "Everything the set-up asked for lives here, and can be changed any time. **Run the set-up again**" (`#settings-setup`). `setupDone()` is `profile.setupDone || profile.packs`, so Mark's and Shelley's household, and anyone who has already ticked a pack, read as done. Hidden for read-only (`body.is-readonly #more-setup`). Rules: no change (`profile` is family write).
- Tested in `pw/v164.js` (the More row first with its words, the six steps in order with axe on each, a pack unticked refused, the IBD step appearing with the pack and 40 refused, the contact checks, Back keeping every answer, what is saved on the profile, Settings agreeing with it, Run the set-up again, Finish later, the set-up opening by itself after Create an account at Step 1 of 6 and after joining at Step 1 of 3 headed Welcome to Daybook, 320 dark). `pw/smoke.js` expects four rows on More with Set-up first; `pw/v78.js` closes the set-up after joining; the harness's Firebase stub exports `deleteField` (the app imports it since v160, so every stub must).

## Pain and pain relief: the report for the pain nurse (v161 to v163, 13 October 2026)
Mark, seeing his pain nurse: "I would like to be able to take a report like the other reports I do. Do we have enough data to write one?" Yes: everything already logged. Built in the house style (v143), as many A4 pages as it needs (one or two), paginated by `paginate()`. No Daybook Assistant on it: the words under the pain scores are short and print as written. Facts, never advice.
- **Data** (`buildPainReport(n)` in `scripts.js`, from `state.painPdf` { from, to, entries, questions, filename "Daybook pain YYYY-MM-DD.pdf", title }): per day the morning and evening **Pain right now** scores, the evening **Worst pain**, the between-times **Pain readings** (`type: "pain"`), the words under each (`painNote`, `worstPainNote`, a reading's note, a pain relief dose's note), every dose of a **pain relief medicine** (`isPainMed(m)`: the medicine's "what it is for" matches `PAIN_PURPOSE_RE` (pain, ache, nerve, spasm, relief...) or its name a known painkiller in `PAIN_MED_RE`; when-needed ones in `prnIds` are the chart's squares), hours asleep, treatment sessions (`state.days[d].chemo`) and hospital days (a dose with `hospital: true`).
- **Tiles:** Pain right now (the average of every score, "morning 3.5, evening 3.5, between times 5.7"; green under 5, amber from 5, red from 7), Days at 7 or more ("1 of 18 days scored", the first three named, the highest and its day; amber, red when half the days or more), Pain relief (doses, "When needed on 5 days of 28: Oramorph on 5 days", amber when a daily maximum was reached), Sleep (the average, short nights, and "Pain at 7 or more on the day before or after" them when any).
- **The charts** (v163, 13 October 2026; the v161 chart, a bar a day from the lowest to the highest score with diamonds and squares, Mark found "very difficult to understand"; the v162 redraw as two lines with the doses as squares still had the "+N" counts colliding with the dates, and he asked for "separate individual charts" for the morning pain, the evening pain and the extra readings, using the space the notes panel took): six small charts two by two under the tiles, drawn by `renderChart()` like the Notes report's (`P.charts`, the configs built in `buildPainReport()`): **Morning pain** and **Evening pain** (the check-in scores, teal), **Worst pain today** (terracotta), **Between times** (the day's highest Pain tile reading, ink), each 0 to 10 with the 7 to 10 band shaded red, a dot a score joined day to day, treatment sessions as triangles under the plot, the stat pill "Average 3.5, highest 7" (red at 7, amber at 5); **When-needed pain relief (doses a day)** (terracotta bars) and **Hours asleep** (teal bars, amber under 5 hours); then `P.key` (the shading is the level Notes for the team flags, the dots, what Between times is, the triangles, the amber bars). The notes panel's minimum is 30 mm (42 until v163). `painChart()` is gone.
- **Then:** **The period in brief** (v163: four paragraphs, `P.brief` and `P.briefNote`, like the Mood report's: without Daybook Assistant the app's own sentences, Overall (the average, the range, days at 7 or more, the second half against the first), Where and when (the time-of-day averages and how many notes are listed below), Pain relief (doses, when-needed days, the maximum) and Sleep, "Worked out by the app from the scores, the doses and the nights."; with it, **Write the brief with Daybook Assistant** (`#pain-brief`, `explainAvailable()`, hidden read-only) sends the bridge's **`pain`** kind (`painAiText()`: the pain medicines on the list, then a line a day with the morning, evening and between-times scores, the worst pain, every dose with its time, the hours slept, sessions and hospital days, the words under it with their scores; `EXPLAIN_SYSTEM.pain` asks for JSON with the four fixed heads under 70 words each, the patient's own phrases for where and how it felt, never invented, no advice or interpretation), `parsePainReply()` keeps it as `state.painAI` for this range and session, the screen's box (`#pain-brief-text`) becomes editable and its paragraphs are the report's (`fitMemo.pain` cleared on edit), the note and the footer say it is AI-written and checked, **Use the app's plain version instead** clears it; the privacy page counts seven features), **By time of day** (four bars, `.tbar`, never `.bar`, which is the base CSS's print toolbar: morning check-in, between times, evening check-in, worst pain today, with the average and the count) beside the **Pain relief** table (one row a medicine: When needed or Regular pill, name, dose and how, "6 doses on 5 of the days, up to 2 a day (the maximum)", the last dose; `relief[]`); **Week by week** (`reportWeeks()`: pain average and range, days at 7 or more of those scored, the worst pain average and highest, doses on days, short nights; above the words since v162, as Mark asked); **Where it was, in {name}'s words** (`words[]`: every note under a pain score in time order, routine ones out, printed whole since v162 (v161 cut them at a sentence at 200 characters, so Mark's dictated note on 6 October ended "really..."; now only a note past 900 characters is cut, at a sentence past 600), at most `WORDS_MAX` 16 with the fullest kept and the heading saying how many more are in Daybook; the time in teal, red at 7 or more, "with Oramorph" for a dose's note); **Questions** labelled **Pain team** (`QUESTION_TEAMS` has `['pain', 'Pain team']`; `isPainQ()`; the question sheet's hint; left out of Notes for the team, which says "1 question for the pain team is on the Pain report"; the timeline says "Question for the pain team"); **Notes from the appointment** (the ruled panel). Footer "Scores, words and doses as logged in Daybook. Not medical advice."
- **The screen** (`#view-pain`, `renderPainReport()`, from the Reports tab's fourth card `#rep-pain` "Pain and pain relief, For the pain nurse or team", `tint-blue`, the bolt icon; `PAGE_TITLES.pain`): the range (14, 28, 56, 90, custom; `state.painRange` 28), Send by email, Preview and Save as PDF, the questions, the four tiles two by two, By time of day (`.todrow` bars), Pain relief cards, Where it was cards (`.wordcard`), Week by week cards. `REPORT_KINDS.pain`, `REPORT_NAMES.pain`, `fitKey('pain')` (the data, the name, `state.days`, `state.medicines`), `SEND_KINDS.pain` (subject "My pain and pain relief, {range}", named "Pain and pain relief: Mark Brown, {range}", the words naming the scores, the worst pain, the words, the relief and the nights, "Attached: Pain and pain relief, {range}."). `fittedPainReport()`, `painReportBlob()`, the frame `report-frame-pain`.
- Demo: the fixture gained four Oramorph doses (two with notes), a pain reading with a note and pain words on two evening check-ins, so the demo report and the test have something to show. Rules: no change.
- Tested in `pw/v161.js` (the Reports card, the Pain team chip and its hint, the timeline wording, Notes leaving it out with its line, the page title and range, the question on the screen, the four tiles' figures and tones, the plain brief read only with its note, the AI button hidden in the preview, the time-of-day rows, the relief card, nine words in time order with their scores and doses, the week cards, axe; Preview: as many pages as laid out and printed as that many, the masthead, the tiles, the six charts in order with their stats and tones, the shaded band, a dot a score, the triangles, the bars with one amber night, the key, the plain brief's four paragraphs, the sections in order, the bars drawn, the relief row, the words with the 7 and 8 marked, the week rows, the question card and the notes panel, the footers, no em dashes; Save as PDF with the title and file name; the email; a 430-character dictated note with no full stop printed whole on the screen and the report with Week by week above the words; with the bridge routed: the pain kind's text with the medicines, the day lines, the doses with times and the words with scores, the box editable with its note, the edit on the report with the AI note and footer, the plain version back; 320 dark with no sideways scroll and axe). `pw/v133.js` expects seven labels; `v114`, `v118`, `v156`, `v159` and `smoke` pass.

## An unfinished check-in kept whole (v160, 13 October 2026)
Mark, mid way through comprehensive pain notes on 3 of 5 of the morning check-in: the phone locked, the app froze, and on reopening "it only saved the first page". The v136 words-only drafts had in fact kept every step's words (reproduced in the harness: all three came back), but the check-in reopened on 1 of 5 with every slider score gone and each step's words only showing on reaching that step, so it looked lost and he retyped it. "This is incredibly demoralising."
- **Kept whole, as you type:** `openCheckin()` writes the whole check-in in progress, `{ answers, step, at, by }`, to `localStorage` `daybook.checkin.{slot}:{day}` (`writeProgress()`) on every step change (`go()`) and 300 ms after any input, change or slider move on the step (`keepSoon()` captures the current step's values through `capture()`), and, for a real sign-in, 1.5 s later to the household as `profile/main.checkinDrafts["slot:day"]` { answers (JSON), step, at, by } (a merge; `profile` is family write, no rules change; the viewer role cannot read it), so a frozen phone, a dead battery, a reinstall or the other phone finds it. `readProgress(slot, day)` takes the newer of the phone's copy and the household's, 7 days at most.
- **Comes back on its step:** a new check-in (not an edit) with a kept copy loads its answers and opens on the step it was left at, with a `.draftnote` line on that step, "Your unfinished check-in from 09:12 is back, as you left it." and **Start again** (clears both copies and reloads blank). Today's row reads "Unfinished, kept as you left it. Tap to carry on"; the start-up toast reads "Your unfinished morning check-in was kept, as you left it." with Open it (`offerDraft()` now also lists `keptCheckins()`). The v136 textarea drafts still run underneath (their restore skips a box already holding the same words).
- **Cleared on purpose only:** `state.checkinProgress` (the slot:day keys the open wizard touched) is cleared by `closeSheet()` whatever closed it (Save, Cancel, X, Escape), `clearProgress()` removing the phone's copy and the household's (`deleteField()`, now imported; the local `state.profile` copy is dropped at once so a Start again does not read the stale snapshot). App death never calls `closeSheet()`, which is the point.
- The privacy page's "On your phone" line says a started check-in is kept whole on the phone and in the household's folder until saved or closed. The harness's Firebase stub (`smoke.js` and the files carrying their own copy) exports `deleteField`.
- Tested in `pw/v160.js` (scores, hours and words on three steps kept with the step and the name, the app reloaded: the toast, the row's line, reopening on 3 of 5 with the pain score, the words and one line, axe, Back to step 2 and 1 with their values, the review, saving everything, nothing kept after, Escape forgetting it, a household copy from the other phone shown on the row and opened on its step, Start again blank; 390 light and 320 dark). `v146`, `v147`, `v82`, `v129`, `v131`, `v136`, `v117` and `smoke` pass.

## Send to the team, sent by Daybook (v159, 13 October 2026)
Mark: the email arrived with the subject and message but no attachment (some share targets, the Gmail app among them, take the text and drop the file), and the Mail app hop was a step too many; could Daybook send it, from a reporting address everyone uses. A web app cannot attach a file to a `mailto:` link and the share sheet cannot fill in the recipient, which is why the v112 route took two hops; a native wrapper could offer the phone's own composer with both filled in (noted for the App Store stage). Mark chose replies going straight to the patient ("if we can get them to reply directly back to the patient that would be amazing") over a shared reply inbox.
- **Bridge:** `POST /send-report` { kind, to, toName, subject, text, filename, pdf (base64), fromName } (`handleSendReport()`): a family member only (the demo and read-only refused), the sign-in must carry an email (`verifyFirebaseToken()` now returns the token's `email` claim), the contact's address checked (`EMAIL_RE`), the PDF base64 under `SEND_PDF_CHARS` (8,000,000, about 6 MB), `SEND_A_DAY` (20) per household in `bridge/sendLog`. Sent through **Resend** (`https://api.resend.com/emails`, the `RESEND_API_KEY` Worker secret, set by the deploy workflow from a repository secret of that name): from "{fromName} via My Medical Daybook <reporting@mymedicaldaybook.co.uk>" (`SEND_FROM` in `wrangler.toml` `[vars]`), to "Sam <nurses@example.org>", **cc and reply_to the sender's own sign-in email**, the subject and message as typed, the PDF attached under its file name. Nothing of the report is written anywhere on the bridge. Without the key: `503 not-set-up` "Sending from Daybook is not switched on yet. Use Send from my own Mail app."; a refusal from Resend comes back as 502 in plain words (the key not accepted, refused, busy) with the raw reason in the worker log.
- **App** (`openSendSheet()`): with the bridge and a real sign-in (`direct`: `explainAvailable()`, not the shared demo, not read-only) the primary button is **Send to Sam** (`#send-go`): the PDF is base64'd (`blobToB64()`) and posted with the kind, the contact, the subject as edited, the message, the file name and `fromName` (`emailSignOff()` without any ", for ..."); the button reads Sending until the bridge answers; then the sheet closes, "Sent to Sam. A copy is in your own inbox." and a record is written as an ordinary entry, `entries/{autoId}` { type "sent", kind, report (the report's name), to, toName, subject, filename, copyTo, day, at, addedBy } (no rules change: `entries` is family write), shown on the timeline as "Notes for the team sent to Sam (example)" with "By email to nurses@example.org, with a copy to guest@example.com" (`.entry.type-sent`, teal, Delete from the "..." menu). The hint says Daybook sends it from the reporting address in your name, with your own address (shown) as the reply address and in copy. **Send from my own Mail app instead** (`#send-other`) is the v112 share route, under it; a bridge error leaves the sheet open with the bridge's words and the button back. In the preview, the shared demo and without the bridge the sheet is as before (Open in my Mail app). All four reports (Notes, Food diary, Mood and wellbeing, Medicine history) go the same way through `SEND_KINDS`.
- **Set-up (Mark):** a free Resend account, the domain `mymedicaldaybook.co.uk` verified there (its DNS records pasted where the domain's DNS lives; without them NHS filters bin the mail), the API key as the `RESEND_API_KEY` repository secret, then Deploy bridge worker. The free tier is 3,000 emails a month; past that it is the first monthly charge (about £16), so the daily cap stays.
- **Wording:** the privacy page names Resend (who can see it; a new bullet under what leaves the household: the PDF, message and address through the bridge to Resend, from the reporting address in your name, your own address as the reply address and in copy, Resend's sending records for a limited time, the bridge's daily count, the household's own sent record; the Mail app route where Daybook cannot send), the bridge's list of jobs, the terms' section 7 and About's built-with list; both legal pages dated 13 October 2026. The Notes screen hint says Send to the team emails the pages from the reporting address. `.hint`, `.legal li`, `.legal p` and `.sendto-email` carry `overflow-wrap: anywhere`, since the 32-character address pushed a 320px screen sideways.
