- Tested in the scratchpad's `send_test.mjs` (the bridge with Resend, Google and Firestore mocked: no key, read-only, a sign-in without an email, a bad address, no PDF, an oversize PDF, a good send with the from, to, cc, reply_to, subject, text and attachment checked, nothing of the PDF written, quotes and brackets stripped from names, Resend's 422 and 401 in plain words, the twenty-first of a day, no sign-in) and `pw/v159.js` (signed in with the bridge routed: the button, the hint with the sender's address, axe, the payload with the edited subject and a real PDF, the sheet closed with nothing shared, the toast, the timeline record and the stored entry, the bridge off leaving the sheet open with its words, Send from my own Mail app instead sharing the PDF, the Food diary, 390 light and 320 dark; the preview unchanged). The harness's signed-in stub now carries an email; `v90.js`, `v116.js` and `v148.js` follow the dates and wording; `v156.js` no longer hard-codes dates. `v112`, `v117`, `v118`, `v119`, `v143`, `v158` and `smoke` pass.

## A new contact at the top, and the patient's full name on the email (v158, 12 October 2026)
Mark, adding a contact: "can that appear at the top please, I nearly overwrote my current first contact" (the empty block appeared out of sight at the bottom, so he typed into the first one). And on the covering email: a professional files correspondence by surname; an email signed "Mark" could be lost.
- **Who to contact, the edit sheet:** the button is **Add a new contact**, above the list; a tap puts the new block at the top (`addRow(c, atTop)`: `rows.prepend`, `.contactrow.is-new` with a teal outline, headed **New contact** until saved, scrolled into view, focus in Name), and the saved blocks keep their numbers below it (`number_()` counts only the blocks without `data-new`). Saved contacts are listed in the sheet's order, so a new one goes first on More. Saved rows are added with `addRow(c)` alone: never pass `forEach`'s index as the second argument (the first cut did, and every saved contact after the first came up as new).
- **The email:** `patientFullName()` is the name on the report (More > Settings > Notes for the team, whose hint now says it goes on every report and in the email's subject and sign-off so the team can file them against the records) else the household's patient as recorded; `""` in the preview and until one is set, when the email reads as before. With it, the subject names the patient (`SEND_KINDS[kind].named(who, range)`: "Notes before our appointment: Mark Brown, 22 Sept 2026 to 5 Oct 2026", "Food diary: ...", "Mood and wellbeing: ...", "Medicine history: Mark Brown, up to 7 Oct 2026") and `emailSignOff()` signs with the full name when the sender is the patient (relation patient, or the first name matches), else "Shelley, for Mark Brown". The report mastheads already carried the name.
- Tested in `pw/v158.js` (the email as before without a name, the setting's hint, the subject and sign-off with one, a carer's sign-off, the Food diary subject) and `pw/v110.js` (the new block at the top, headed New contact and outlined, empty, with focus, the saved ones numbered below, saved first); `pw/v135.js` and `v90.js` (the privacy date) follow. `v112`, `v118`, `v143`, `v156`, `axe117` and `smoke` pass.

## Mood and wellbeing rebuilt: the taps, the journal, Your week and the report with a brief (v156 and v157, 12 October 2026)
Mark printed the v155 report before his therapy session and did not like it: "the comments I have written don't make sense" (every text answer printed raw, in time order, under a one-word label, half of them pain answers), "the black and teal are shit colours together on the graph", too long, and six pages for three. He asked for a rethink of how mood and wellbeing are monitored, "premium", without the app feeling like it is all about mood, weighted by condition, with scored questions for a night when nobody will write, and asked whether every note could be summarised by AI (yes: a month is a few thousand tokens, a few pence; Siri cannot be reached from a web app). He approved the mock-ups (`mock/` in the scratchpad) and said build it.
- **The evening check-in** (`CHECKIN_QUESTIONS.evening`): after Settled, **Over today, how much did you...** (`key: 'wb'`, `kind: 'taps'`): three rows, Feel low?, Feel worried or on edge?, Enjoy something?, four answers each (`WB_WORDS`: Not at all, A little, Quite a bit, A lot; `WB_ROWS`), two by two (`.taps-row`, `.taps-chips`, `aria-pressed`, tap again to clear), stored as a map `wb: { low, worried, enjoyed }` of 0 to 3 or null (null when none answered; Skip is null). Then the mood score with **no words box** (`legacyNote: 'moodNote'`: words saved before v156 still show on the card), then **How was today, really?** (`key: 'journal'`, `kind: 'text'`, its own `hint`: "Skip is fine too; the three taps are enough."), then One good thing. Eleven steps (twelve with Crohn's or colitis). The review and the card word the taps (`wbText()`: "Felt low: a little · Worried or on edge: quite a bit · Enjoyed something: quite a bit"); the card shows the taps and journal rows only on check-ins that have them. The morning is unchanged (sleep with how rested, mood with what is behind it). `saveCheckin()` compares object answers as JSON for the no-change check. Those three taps cover the ground of the GP screening questions in Daybook's own words: no named scale, no licence, no scored threshold.
- **Your week** (`openWeekCheck(day)`, one screen, not the wizard; the fourth row in Today's check-in list, `#week-check`, `renderWeekRow()`: "Five minutes, once a week. Last done 5 Oct." or "Done Sun 18:05, tap to change"): the questions in `CHECKIN_QUESTIONS.week`, scored ones by pack (`kind: 'scale'`, 0 to 3 with the same four words; a question's `pack` may be a list, asked once: cancer: worried about results or scans, felt cut off from people, tiredness got in the way; Crohn's or colitis: tiredness, did your gut stop you going out, worried about getting to a toilet; menopause: mood swings or irritability, anxious or panicky, brain fog or memory, sleep broken by sweats) with the packs on shown as pills, then for everyone What helped this week?, What was hardest?, This week in a sentence (`helped`, `hardest`, `sentence`, Tap to speak). Saved through `saveCheckin('week', ...)` as `entries/{day}_week` { type "checkin", slot "week" }, one per Monday-to-Sunday week (`weekBounds()`, `weekCheckFor(day)`: opening it again that week edits it, with Cancel), `at` 21:00 on a past day. `checkinTitle('week')` and `slotWord('week')` are "Your week"; the timeline shows it as a card ("Your week done 18:05"); `checkinSummary()` gives the sentence. Never on Notes for the team (`buildNotesReport()` skips the slot; the journal and the taps are not in `REPORT_CHECKIN_KEYS` either): pain stays on Notes, wellbeing on this report.
- **The report** (`buildMoodReport(n)` in `scripts.js`, `moodReportHtml(W)`, `moodChart()` and `MOOD_CSS` in `report.js`, paginated by `paginate()`, as many A4 pages as it needs, usually one or two): tiles **Best day** (the highest day-average mood, a day with both scores first, with a sentence from that day's journal or good thing), **Hardest day** (the lowest, with why: in hospital, pain at 9, a temperature, a short night; red at 3 or under), **Felt low** ("6 of 12 evenings", quite a bit or a lot, with worried and enjoyed counts), **Sleep**; **one chart**, "Mood, with pain and sleep behind it": the day's average mood in teal with the low days (4 or under) ringed amber, the day's worst pain (check-in pain, worst pain and pain readings) dashed in terracotta, hours asleep as mist bars, treatment sessions as ink triangles, hospital days (a dose with `hospital: true`) shaded and labelled, one 0 to 10 axis, at most six day labels (teal and terracotta pass the colour-vision separation check; ink and teal did not); **The period in brief** (four paragraphs), **In {name}'s own words** (up to three quotes, the low day's amber), **What helped** and **What was hardest** (two columns), **Week by week** (seven-day blocks from `reportWeeks()`: the taps as four dots from the week's mean, terracotta when low or worried averages 1.5 or more, "3 of 7" quite a bit or a lot, sleep with short nights, the weekly check's sentence with Helped and Hardest under it, each check in the one block holding its day), **Questions** (Wellbeing, the card's own three ruled lines) and **Notes from the session**. The raw notes are never printed.
- **The brief, two ways.** Without Daybook Assistant (`briefNote` "Worked out by the app from the scores and the weekly check."): Overall (the average, the range with dates, low days, the second half against the first), Feeling (the taps' counts), Sleep and pain (the averages, how many low days had pain at 7 or more), The weeks; the quotes are the three longest non-routine journal, mood or sleep notes, whole sentences up to 220 characters (`firstSentence(text, max, min)`, never a lone "Rough." when more follows), "as written"; helped and hardest are the weekly checks' words by week. With it, **Write the brief with Daybook Assistant** (`#mood-brief`, `explainAvailable()`, hidden read-only) sends the bridge's new **`mood`** kind (`moodAiText()`: one line a day with the scores, pain, sleep, taps, sessions and hospital days, the notes under it, then the weekly checks; `EXPLAIN_SYSTEM.mood` asks for JSON: brief with the four fixed heads under 70 words each, three quotes tidied for dictation with the meaning and phrasing kept and never invented, `low` for a day at 4 or under, helped and hardest as up to four themes each; no diagnosis, advice or reassurance). `parseMoodReply()` keeps it as `state.moodAI` for this range and session; the screen's box (`#mood-brief-text`) becomes editable and its paragraphs ("Head. text") are the report's (`fitMemo.mood` cleared on every edit); the note reads "Written by Daybook Assistant, an AI helper, from the daily scores and notes. {name} read it before sending. Every note is in Daybook." and the footer says the brief is AI-written and checked; **Use the app's plain version instead** clears it. The privacy page names the sixth feature (dated 12 October 2026).
- **The screen** (`#view-mood`): the range, Send, Preview and Save as PDF, the questions, the four tiles two by two, **The period in brief** (the button, the note, the box), **Week by week** as cards (`.wkcard`: dots, counts, sleep, the sentence, Helped and Hardest). The v155 word list and good-things list went.
- **A period with no taps or weekly checks (v157, the same day).** Mark's first real v156 report had every week row reading "No taps" and "No weekly check", since neither existed before that day. The report now shows what was logged: `W.cols` { taps, checks } from `buildMoodReport()`; with no taps in the period the third tile is **Low days** ("5 of 18 days scored", the first three named) and the week table's columns are Week, Mood (the week's average, "lowest 4"), Low days ("2 of 7 days") and Sleep (`wkCols()` in `report.js` sets the grid); the sentence column appears only with a weekly check; the heading note follows ("Mood, low days and sleep, week by week."); the plain brief drops its Feeling and The weeks paragraphs rather than say what was not answered; the screen's week cards and hint do the same. Tested in `pw/v156.js` (a household with the taps and the weekly checks stripped).
- **The blank pages.** Mark's six-page PDF came from Print on the iPhone: `printThroughShare()` makes the PDF and the iPhone print dialog, at 100%, spills each edge-to-edge A4 image onto a second sheet. `reportPdfBlob()` now draws each page inset by `PDF_INSET_MM` (5 mm sideways, in proportion down) while that route runs; Save as PDF and the emails stay edge to edge. To be confirmed on Mark's phone.
- Demo: the fixture's evening check-ins carry taps and journals, and two weekly checks (1 and 8 October). Rules: no change (`entries` is family write with no field validation). Tested in `pw/v156.js` (the evening order with the taps, the chip rows at 44px, axe, a chip pressed, cleared and changed, no box under the evening mood, the journal step, the review wording, what is saved, the card; the Your week row fourth in the list, the sheet's cancer questions and three boxes with the badge, axe, saved as `_week` with the scores and words, the row done, opened again this week editing the same one with Save changes, one entry edited, the timeline card; the Mood screen's four tiles, the plain brief read only with its note, the AI button hidden in the preview, the week cards, no word list, axe; a Wellbeing question on the screen; Preview: as many pages as laid out and printed as that many, the masthead, the tiles' words and tones, the chart's two strokes, rings, bars, triangles, hospital band and legend, the plain brief's four paragraphs, three whole-sentence quotes as written, the lists from the weekly checks, the sections in order, the week rows with each check once, the question card, the notes panel, the footers, no em dashes; Save as PDF edge to edge; the email; with the bridge routed: the mood kind's text, the box editable with its note, the edit on the report with the AI note, the tidied quotes and the themes, the plain version back; Print with an iPhone user agent drawing the PDF 5 mm inside the paper; 320 dark with the week sheet fitting and axe). `pw/v147.js`, `v145.js` and `v129.js` follow the new evening order; `pw/v155.js` is retired; `smoke`, `v82`, `v114`, `v117`, `v131`, `v133`, `v136`, `v143`, `v146` and the scratchpad's `explain_e2e.mjs` pass.

## Provide feedback: Save or Send, and no clock on the questions (v154, 12 October 2026)
Mark, on the v153 sheet: "the feedback shouldn't be timed": someone activates, gets busy for three days and comes back to "Day one"; "one button they can click that says Provide feedback. Save so they can keep adding more, or Send so I get it right away."
- The row is **Provide feedback** (the title; the sub line still says "Tell Mark what you think" and, until the questions are sent, "Eight quick questions about getting started, whenever you have a minute."; after, "... N sent so far."; and "Something saved on this phone is waiting to be sent. Add to it, or send it." whenever a draft exists). The sheet's title is Provide feedback; the questions block is **Eight quick questions** "About getting started. All optional, whenever you have a minute", no day-one wording anywhere.
- **Save for later** beside **Send** on both the questions and the note (`.btnrow`): the answers (chips, the Something else box and the text boxes), the note and its picture are kept in `localStorage` `daybook.feedback.draft.{uid}` (`saveDraft()`, only the parts that have something in them; the key is removed when empty) and put back next time the sheet opens, on any day, so a half thought is added to and sent whole. Sending clears that part of the draft; the questions still disappear only once sent. "Not now" went: Save covers it. Nothing else changed on the bridge.
- The website's `beta.html` and both privacy pages name the Provide feedback button; the three Beta messages in the Claude doc "Daybook Beta messages" do too.
- Tested in `pw/v153.js` (now also: Save for later on the questions and on a note sending nothing, the row saying something is waiting, the answers (a chip, Something else with its box, a text box) and the note back in place after a reload and a fresh sign-in, the note added to and sent whole with the draft cleared, the saved answers sent as restored, the row's waiting line gone and the count right).

## Tell Mark what you think: the Beta feedback (v153, 11 October 2026)
Mark: "feedback is critical"; a lot of it, added to as people use the app, and the first-day questions done on day one; a survey, an email or a call. Built before any invite goes out.
- **The row.** At the very top of Today, above the day, while `APP_STAGE` is set (`#feedback-row`, `.feedback-row`: a raised row with a warm stripe and the paper-plane icon, "Tell Mark what you think"), for a signed-in member with the bridge, any role; never in the preview or the shared demo (`feedbackShown()`, `renderFeedbackRow()` from `renderToday()`). Until the first-day questions are answered on that phone it reads "Day one: eight quick questions, two minutes. Then tap here any time."; after, "Anything confusing, wrong or not what you expected. 3 sent so far." (or "Nothing sent yet."). Clearing `APP_STAGE` at the stores takes it away with the other Beta markers.
- **The sheet** (`openFeedbackSheet()`, "Tell Mark what you think"): a line saying Mark reads every one and that nothing here reaches the care team; then, until answered once, **Day one: eight quick questions** (`FIRST_DAY_QUESTIONS`, all optional, in a sunken block: who they are, which conditions (several, Something else with a box), how easy setting up was (1 hard to 5 easy), Home Screen yes, no or did not know how, what they logged first, anything confusing, the part they expect to use most, anything else; chips tap again to clear, the text boxes have Tap to speak; **Send my answers**, or **Not now**, which puts them away for this opening only); then a box "What happened, or what you think" with Tap to speak, **Add a picture or screenshot** (one picture from Photos through `processFiles()`, shown small with Remove), **Send to Mark**; **Sent so far from this phone** (date, time, the screen, the first words, "with a picture"); and the contact line, "Prefer email? support@mymedicaldaybook.co.uk", with "Or ring Mark during the Beta: 07..." as a `tel:` link when the bridge gives a number.
- **Through the bridge, never the household.** `POST /feedback` { kind "note"|"survey", text, image? (bare base64 JPEG, under 900,000 characters), answers?, screen (`document.title` when the sheet opened), version, device (the user agent, 160 characters) } for any member (the demo refused): written by the service account to the top-level **`feedback/{id}`** { household, uid, name, role, kind, text, image?, answers? (JSON text), screen, version, device, at }, which the rules deny to everyone (tested), so only Mark reads it, in the Firebase console for now (a digest is a later action); `FEEDBACK_A_DAY` (40) per household in `households/{hid}/bridge/feedbackLog` { day, count }. Then one push to every phone in the operator's household (the one `BRIDGE_KEY` is the inbox key of, `householdForKey()`), "Daybook feedback: New feedback from a Beta circle." or "... has answered the first-day questions.", never the words themselves, and never when the sender is in that household (Mark's and Shelley's own tests do not ping him). `POST /feedback-info` gives { email, phone }: the phone is the optional `BETA_PHONE` Worker secret (the deploy workflow sets it from a repository secret of that name, like the push key), shown to signed-in members only, never on the website. What this phone has sent, and that the questions were answered, live in `localStorage` only (`daybook.feedback.sent.{uid}`, `.survey.{uid}`); nothing about feedback is written to the household.
- The privacy page names it (dated 11 October 2026); the website's `beta.html` promised the button and the questions in these words.
- Tested in the scratchpad's `feedback_test.mjs` (the bridge with every outside call mocked, a real P-256 phone key and a real signing key: nothing to send refused, a read-only member's note with its picture, screen and version written and one push to the operator's phone, the day's count, the survey kept as JSON, Mark's own household taken but not pushed, the fortieth of a day refused, feedback-info with and without the secret, no sign-in refused) and `pw/v153.js` (the row above the day with the first-day words and 48px, axe on Today and the sheet, the eight questions in order before the note box, chips and Something else, a chip cleared, the answers sent with the screen, version and device, the questions gone and the row's words, the email and the tel link, an empty note refused, a picture through the file chooser shown with Remove, the note sent with the picture, the box cleared and the note listed, the row's count, remembered across a reload, no questions the second time, no phone without the secret, Not now, the row still asking, hidden in the preview, 320 dark with no sideways scroll). `pw/smoke.js` passes.

## Beta codes, and the report's lines joined (v152, 11 October 2026)
Mark's Beta plan (11 October 2026): the app goes to nurses, who offer it to patients he will never know by name, and to friends by WhatsApp, so sign-up is neither open nor by named invitation. **A new circle needs a Beta code, one per route in** (a ward, a clinic, a WhatsApp group), so Mark knows where each Beta circle came from; the Beta page on the website (`beta.html` in the `mymedicaldaybook-site` repository) says so. Free throughout the Beta, and Beta circles keep the full app free for a year after the App Store, with everything logged (Mark's promise, on the Beta page and the website's close).
- **Bridge:** while `BETA_CODES_REQUIRED` is set in `wrangler.toml` `[vars]` (delete the line at the stores, with `APP_STAGE`), `POST /household` takes `betaCode` and `heardFrom`: the code is tidied (`normaliseBetaCode()`: upper case, letters and digits only, 24 at most) and looked up in `betaCodes/{CODE}` { label, active, uses, createdAt, lastUsedAt }; a missing, unknown or switched-off code is refused with `{ error: 'beta', message: BETA_CODE_MESSAGE }` (400, "The Beta is by invitation. Type the Beta code from whoever told you about Daybook, or email support@mymedicaldaybook.co.uk and say who that was."); a good one makes the household as before with `betaCode` and `heardFrom` (free text, 80 characters) on `households/{hid}`, and bumps the code's `uses` and `lastUsedAt`. `POST /join` (an invite into an existing circle) needs no code. `GET /beta-info?code=` says whether a code in a link is good and whose it is ({ required, valid, label } or the message). Never name a local `f` in the worker.
- **Codes are made from the Actions tab** (Household admin: `beta-code` with CODE and LABEL, the **switch this code off** tickbox (the workflow input is `switch_off`: until 18 October 2026 it was named `off`, which YAML reads as the word false, so the tickbox did nothing; the tool still reads the `OFF=yes` environment variable) to switch one off, a leaked one for instance; `beta-codes` lists them with uses; `list` now prints each household's code and "who told you"). The codes and their labels live in Firestore, never in the repository (a label may be a nurse's name). The rules deny `betaCodes` to everyone (tested in `tests/rules/households.test.mjs`; the bridge's service account bypasses the rules).
- **App:** Create an account has **Beta code (from whoever told you about Daybook)** (`#signup-beta`, capitals keyboard) and **Who told you about Daybook? (optional)** (`#signup-heard`) between Password and the invite code, shown only while `APP_STAGE` is set and hidden when an invite is pending (`showSignup()`); `betaPayload()` adds both to the `/household` call. The no-household screen has the same two (`#nohousehold-beta`, `#nohousehold-heard`), carrying over whatever was typed on sign-up, so a refused code lands there with the words kept and the code put right in place. `?beta=CODE` in the address (`readBetaLink()`, from an email or a WhatsApp message) is tidied, kept as `state.pendingBeta`, dropped from the address bar, filled in on Create an account, and the banner says "Your Beta code from The ward is filled in" from `/beta-info` (or the bridge's refusal, and nothing is filled in).
- **The report's lines joined.** Mark, from the website's picture of the demo report: "why do the charts always look disjointed?" `renderChart()` in `report.js` broke the line at every day without a reading (the approved design's rule), which with readings every few days drew dots and stubs. The line now runs from reading to reading across days with none, as the app's own Trends charts do: the dots mark the readings, the line only joins them. The demo fixture (`buildDemoFixture()`, the `daily` table) logs a temperature, the vitals and a weight on every day of the fortnight, as Mark does, keeping any hand-written reading; the shared demo takes it on its nightly reset. The website's pictures of the report were taken again (`pw/report_shot.js`).
- Tested in the scratchpad's `beta_test.mjs` (the bridge with every outside call mocked: no code, an unknown and a switched-off code refused with nothing written, a good code typed with a space and a hyphen making the household with the code and who told them and bumping the uses, the member and pointer as before, `/beta-info` both ways, and no code needed without the var) and `pw/v152.js` (the boxes and their order and labels, axe, the call with the code as typed, a `?beta=` link's banner, tidied lookup and filled box, a wrong link explained and not carried, an invite hiding the boxes and joining with no code, a refused code landing on the no-household screen with the words carried and put right there, axe, 320 dark; the report's every series one unbroken line, a temperature every day and both blood pressure lines). `pw/smoke.js`, `v78.js` and `v117.js` pass.

## Copyright moves to the company (v151, 10 October 2026)
The deed was signed on 10 October 2026 (the first scan was refused: the witness's details had been added on a computer afterwards, which does not witness a deed; it was signed again with the witness present and writing in pen). Every "Copyright 2026 Mark Brown" became "Copyright 2026 Mark 1 Apps Ltd"; the terms' section 9 now says the names, strapline, artwork and the Mark 1 Apps name and mark belong to Mark 1 Apps Ltd. Tested in `pw/v148.js` (terms, About's licence line, the Settings card, the opener foot), `pw/opener.js`, `pw/v90.js` and the smoke test.

## The Mark 1 Apps logo on white (since v150, 10 October 2026)
The mark is neon: electric blue glow on midnight (#030214). **On a white or light background, never put it in a dark box and never simply invert it** (inverting turns the glow into a fuzzy blue haze with black outlines). Use `icons/mark1apps-line.png` (780 by 670, 109 KB): the film's last frame traced into clean solid lines, laurel, lightning, globe and "MARK 1 APPS" in one blue (#0A4FA8) on white. Made by keeping only the bright cores of the neon (luminance above about 120, solid above 190) and dropping the glow. Mark approved it on the Deed of Assignment ("remember that for when we have white backgrounds"). Use it for letterheads, deeds, invoices, the reports and any printed or light-page Mark 1 Apps material; the neon film and `mark1apps.jpg` stay for dark pages (the opener, the Settings card).

## Branch and hosting
- All work is committed directly to `main`. No feature branches, no pull requests.
- Scope (Mark, 1 October 2026): only the Learning-Development667 repositories. Never read, write or search Innovation-mag (Mark's work organisation) unless Mark expressly gives permission in that conversation. Nothing in Daybook needs it.
- Served at the root of app.mymedicaldaybook.co.uk since v231: `manifest.json` start_url and scope are `/`, `sw.js` is registered with the scope worked out from the page's own address, and every asset path is relative, so it still runs from a sub-path locally. Until v231 the address was the case-sensitive github.io `/Mark_Medical/`.

## Stack
- Vanilla HTML, CSS and JS. No frameworks, no build step.
- Firebase JS SDK v10 modular from the gstatic CDN.
- Chart.js, pdf.js (3.x UMD build), mammoth.js, jsPDF (2.5, for "Save as PDF" on the reports), html2canvas (1.4.1, since v117, to turn the two-page Notes for the team into a PDF file) and JSZip, loaded lazily when needed; from cdnjs until v329, kept in `vendor/` since.
- Fonts: the two-page report alone uses Fraunces (headings) and Inter (body) from Google Fonts, as its approved design does; the app itself is unchanged.
- Fonts (since v122, the report look): Fraunces 600 (headings and large values) and Inter (body, numbers with even-width digits, small labels), from Google Fonts. Bebas Neue, DM Sans and DM Mono went in v122.

## Files
- `ROADMAP.md` (since v72; a plain action list since v106, in five stages: Build, UI, UX, Website, App stores, with the paperwork alongside): what is still to do, in order, with who does what and what done looks like; update it as actions finish. Read it at the start of a piece of work and keep it current. **After any change to `ROADMAP.md`, republish the live copy in the same turn** (the Artifact tool, url https://claude.ai/artifact/PCi6gEEHuHR9idt9fPbwks, with its Updated date and the version on it); it sat at v167 from 14 to 19 October 2026 because that step was missed. The page is built by `tools/roadmap_page_gen.py` (rows are written in that file; edit them to match ROADMAP.md, run it, publish `roadmap.html`).
- `index.html`, `styles.css`, `scripts.js`, `report.js` (the two-page Notes for the team, since v117), `sw.js`, `manifest.json`, `config.js`, `config.example.js`, `guide/` (the Guide's frames and `guide.json`, generated by `tools/guide_shots.js`, since v167), `icons/` (the app icons plus the Mark 1 Apps film `mark1apps.mp4`, 720p, 188 KB, no sound, its last frame `mark1apps-poster.jpg`, the cropped mark `mark1apps.jpg` for the Settings card, and `mark1apps-line.png`, the mark for white backgrounds, see below), `firestore.rules`, `CLAUDE.md`, `data/cofid.json` (the UK food table, generated, see Food entries), `tools/build_cofid.py`.
- `worker/` is the bridge (see "Bridge" below): `wrangler.toml`, `src/index.js`, `README.md`. Plain JavaScript module worker, no build step, no dependencies. Run `node --check worker/src/index.js` before committing like any other JS file.
- `tests/rules/` (since v75): the break-in tests for `firestore.rules` on Google's Firestore emulator (`households.test.mjs`, `npm test` there; Java needed; `.github/workflows/rules-test.yml` runs them on every push touching the rules). `firebase.json` and `firebase.demo.json` name the rules file for the emulator and the publish workflow.
- `tools/`: `lib/firestore_rest.mjs` (Firestore over REST with a service account, shared by the Node tools), `migrate_households.mjs` and `household_admin.mjs` (see Households), `reset_demo.mjs`, `build_cofid.py`, `vapid_keys.mjs`. Workflows on the Actions tab: "Households, move the data in", "Household admin", "Publish database rules", "Reset the demo", "Deploy bridge worker", "Build the food table", "Rules tests".
- `config.js` holds the real Firebase web config and nothing else (the `users` map it used to carry went in v49; accounts are `users/{uid}` records, see Access). `index.html` loads it before `scripts.js`. It is a normal project file like any other; edit it the same way as the rest of the codebase. (Until 2026-09-22 this file carried a standing "Mark only, never edit" restriction; Mark removed that rule himself after repeated friction over a small, low-risk edit. `config.example.js` stays as the template for setting the project up from scratch.)

## Standards
- Body text minimum 17px. Tap targets minimum 48px (44px is the hard floor for secondary controls). The user may be tired or drowsy: clarity beats density.
- Palette (since v122, Direction D "Report look" from the brand board, the look of the two-page reports): page #F6F4EF (warm white), surface #FFFFFF, mist #EEF3F6, ink #17263A, secondary #4B5B6B, muted #56667A, line #D5DEE5, teal #18727A for text and #1E7F86 for fills (buttons start there), green #2D7354 text / #3F8F6B fill (done, steady), amber #8A5E10 text / #E0A030 fill (37.5 to 37.9°C, worth a mention), red #B0372E text / #C8453B fill (38.0°C and above, raise promptly), warm terracotta #C2703D (emphasis only). The fills (`--teal-fill`, `--success-fill`, `--warning-fill`, `--danger-fill`) are for stripes, bars and dots, never text. Dark: page #0F1B24, surface #172633, teal #5CC3C9. Full dark mode via prefers-color-scheme. Calm, never loud. Until v122: ink #18262B, background #EDF1F2, teal #1E5F74.
- Service worker: network-first ALWAYS. skipWaiting() on install, delete ALL caches on activate, network-first on fetch, never cache-first.
- Cache busting: `?v=VERSION` on `scripts.js` and `styles.css` in `index.html`, and the `APP_VERSION` constant in `scripts.js`. Bump on every commit.
- Run `node --check` on every JS file before committing.
- **The Guide follows the app** (Mark, 20 October 2026). Any change to a screen, button, row or wording that a Guide topic shows updates its step in `tools/guide_shots.js` and retakes the frames in the same version; a new key feature gets a topic or a step. Then copy `guide/` to the website repository and bump `guide.json?v=` in its `js/site.js`. Retake with `node tools/guide_shots.js` (see the v242 section for running it offline), then look at every changed frame before pushing.
- UK English throughout. No em dashes anywhere in UI copy.
- No health data in the repo, ever. Only code.
- Accessibility: WCAG 2.2 level AA, checked in v53 with axe-core (the App card under More > Settings says so, with the date) on every screen and sheet in both colour schemes (zero violations) plus hand checks for what a scanner cannot judge. Keep it that way: every sheet is a real dialog (`openSheet()` makes `.skip-link, .topbar, #main, #tabs` inert, moves focus to the title, or the first field on wide screens, and `closeSheet()` returns focus to whatever opened it; Escape closes, and `confirmSheet()` treats any close as Cancel); one keyboard focus ring for everything (`:focus-visible`, teal, never shown on tap); a skip link to `#main`; the toast is `role="status"`; the active tab carries `aria-current="page"`; `document.title` names the screen; every chart canvas gets `role="img"` and an `aria-label` built in `makeChart()` from the card's heading, the plotted range and its note; the diary donut is `aria-hidden` because its legend carries the numbers; an `aria-label` on a plain `div` or `span` needs a role (`group`, `img`); no tap target under 24px (the app's own floor is 44px) and nothing overflows at 320px wide, so the medicine purpose pill wraps, the two custom-range dates wrap, the five range buttons wrap under 360px and the calendar cells drop their 48px minimum there; in dark mode `--teal` is #5FB0C8 and `--teal-light` #142B34 (both chosen for 4.5:1), and `--teal-dark` is a button colour there, not a text colour (`.note-who`, `.speakbtn`, `.diary-macros`, `.macro-total`, `.painbtn.is-teal` switch to `--teal` in dark). AAA is not claimed: 1.4.6 enhanced contrast and 2.4.13 focus appearance are not met throughout.
- Design tokens live at the top of `styles.css` and are used everywhere; no hex values scattered through the code (the only exception is `PDF_TEAL` in `scripts.js`, because PDFs are always on white paper). Colour: `--surface`, `--surface-raised`, `--surface-sunken`, `--border-subtle`, `--text-primary/secondary/muted`, `--teal/-dark/-light`, `--warm/-dark/-light` (terracotta #C2703D, the one warm accent: attention and human warmth, used sparingly), `--success`, `--warning`, `--danger` (thresholds unchanged), `--cat-*` timeline category colours, `--sleep-deep/-core/-rem` (a single-hue ramp for the sleep chart, deepest to lightest) and `--mood-1..5` for the calendar. Elevation: `--elev-sunken`, `--elev-flat`, `--elev-raised`, `--elev-floating` (plus teal and sheet variants), all soft two-layer shadows. Radius: `--r-sm` 10 (chips, inputs), `--r-md` 16 (cards), `--r-lg` 22 (sheets, primary buttons). Spacing: `--s-1` to `--s-8` on a 4px scale. Older names (`--ink`, `--line`, `--teal-soft`, `--amber` and so on) are aliases of these, so `cssVar()` in the JS keeps working. Every text token is at least 4.5:1 on the page, a card and the sunken surface, in both colour schemes.
- Three visual levels: SUNKEN for read-only information (`.status`, the segmented track: inset shadow, a touch darker than the page, no border, never looks tappable); RAISED for anything you can tap (`.card`, `.entry`, `.qa`, `.report`, `.checkin-row`, `.docitem`, `.goal`: white gradient, lit 1px top edge, two-layer shadow, press state of translateY plus reduced shadow); FLOATING for the primary action and open sheets (`.btn-primary`, `.checkin-row.is-due`, `.sheet-panel`: teal gradient, stronger shadow). Next to a primary, the partner button is outlined and lighter (`.med-actions .btn-secondary`, `.btnrow .btn-secondary`).
- Motion: screens enter with each block fading in and rising 8px, 50ms apart (`enterView()`, `.view.is-entering`); tile numbers count up via `countTo()`; timeline and document rows animate in only when new (`seenIds` sets plus `markNew()`), never on data updates; Chart.js lines draw in left to right with the area fill behind them (`drawIn()`); the tab indicator slides (`moveTabIndicator()`). All of it is off under prefers-reduced-motion.
- Charts share one style (`chartTheme()`, `lineSeries()`, `bandSeries()`, `barChart()`): gradient area fills, 2.5px rounded lines, small points with only the latest one filled and ringed, light horizontal grid only, DM Mono axis labels, temperature thresholds as soft amber and red bands, blood pressure systolic teal and diastolic warm, pain and mood on a calm scale with no red. The sleep chart is a stacked bar (Deep, Core, REM, plus a muted "Not broken down" segment for nights logged without the stage split); bar height always equals the night's logged hours asleep, the same figure as the Latest tile and the short-night flag. Awake-in-bed minutes are not part of that total and show only in the tooltip.
- Icons are inline SVG symbols in `index.html` (`icon()` in JS). No emoji anywhere: the Chemo calendar shows mood as a small dot (OK, Good, Great) or ring (Low, Rough) on the `--mood-*` teal-to-warm scale (`moodMark()`), and the legend matches. Empty states (`.empty`, `data-art`) get a muted teal line drawing from the `--art-*` masks, one line of text and the action button that already sits beside them.
- Type (since v122): Fraunces 600 for the brand, screen titles, section headings (sentence case, in ink, about 21px), sheet titles, card titles and large values; Inter for body, buttons, numbers (`--font-mono` is Inter, and every rule using it carries `font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums`; never set tabular figures on body text, as Inter then widens hyphens and "check-in" reads "check - in") and small uppercase labels. The topbar reads "Daybook" in Fraunces with the page in Inter after it.

## Data model (Firestore)
Since v75 every collection below sits inside a household: `households/{hid}/entries/...`, `households/{hid}/medicines/...` and so on (see Households). The paths in this section are written without that prefix; in the app they are reached only through `hcol(name, ...)` and `hdoc(name, ...ids)`, which prepend `households/{state.household}`, and on the bridge through `hp(hid, path)`.
- `entries/{autoId}`: { day "YYYY-MM-DD", at Timestamp, type "med"|"temp"|"drink"|"food"|"weight"|"vitals"|"note"|"sleep", medId?, medName?, dose?, value? (number), heartRate? (number, bpm), systolic? (number, mmHg), diastolic? (number, mmHg), oxygen? (number, %), note?, amount? (food: "A few mouthfuls"|"About half"|"Most of it"|"All of it"), detail? (food: what went with it), deep?/rem?/core?/awake? (sleep stages, minutes), addedBy, createdAt }
  - Daily check-ins: `entries/{YYYY-MM-DD}_{morning|evening}` (deterministic id, so a day and slot can only ever have one document and reopening edits it): { type "checkin", slot, day, at, addedBy, createdAt, updatedAt, then one field per answer. Morning (asked in this order since v146): sleep (1 to 10), sleepHours (number), symptoms (text), pain, mood, lookingForward (text). Evening (in this order since v147): pain, worstPain, sickness, appetite, energy, symptoms, settled, mood, goodThing }. A skipped slider is stored as `null`, skipped text as `""`, never 0. Questions are defined in `CHECKIN_QUESTIONS`; the flow (`openCheckin`) is one question per screen with a progress bar, Back and Skip on every step, a Today/Yesterday picker on the first step, and a review screen before saving. Saving mirrors mood to `days/{day}.mood` (1 to 5, `ceil(mood/2)`) and the evening `goodThing` to `days/{day}.good`, so the Chemo calendar faces, Notes for the team and the low-mood flag keep working; the Chemo day sheet no longer edits mood or "one good thing". Home shows both slots with their state (Due now before 15:00 for morning, after for evening; Done HH:MM; Missed; Later today), and since v54 the Question for the team row sits under them in the same list, so the three align.
  - Pain readings: `entries/{autoId}` { type "pain", value 0 to 10 (1 to 10 before v241), note? } from the Pain tile in Today's quick-add grid (`openAdd('pain')`, since v54; until then a "Log pain now" button above the grid, dropped because the check-ins already ask twice a day and the between-times reading deserved a tile, not a banner). The Trends Pain chart draws the line from check-in `pain` scores and shows these as hollow points; the Mood chart uses check-in `mood`. Notes for the team flags pain 4 to 6 amber (5 to 6 before v243) and 7+ red (check-in or reading), worstPain 7+, sickness 6+, appetite or energy 3 and under, and lists the check-in text answers as notes.
  - Sleep entries: `value` is minutes asleep for the night before, logged against the morning (`day` is the wake-up date). Since 2026-09-24 they arrive automatically from Apple Health through the bridge as `entries/{day}_sleep` (`addedBy: "Apple Health"`, see Bridge); the Sleep sheet is for corrections and missed nights. If the selected morning already has a sleep entry the sheet opens it for editing (`editId`, `updateEntry()`) instead of adding a second one. The four stage fields (awake, rem, core, deep, in Apple's order) are always shown, and `bedAt`/`wokeAt` ("HH:MM" strings) record the night's window, with the entry's `at` set to the wake time. "Woke at" fills itself in from bedtime plus time asleep plus any awake minutes until it is typed in by hand. Shown on the Today timeline, as a Latest tile and a bar chart in Trends, in the readings line of Notes for the team, and flagged there as a short night under 5 hours.
  - Food entries: `note` is the meal name, `detail` the optional "what is in it" text. The Food diary (Trends > Reports) lists food entries day by day with each day's drinks total, over 7 to 90 days or a custom range (see Report ranges below), and has a Print button (print stylesheet in `styles.css`).
  - Calorie and macro estimates (since v42, optional): gated everywhere on `profile/main.detailedNutrition` (bool, account-wide, the toggle under More > Settings > Food, `#settings-nutrition`, `syncSettings()`). Off by default and off means nothing runs, nothing is stored and the app is exactly as it was. On, a food entry may carry `portions?: { [componentKey]: { size "S"|"M"|"L", override: null | { kcal, prot, carb, fat, per "portion"|"100g", grams } } }`, keyed per component by `macroComponentKey()` (`c:` plus the CoFID/ADDED_FOODS food code, or `u:` plus the typed phrase for something the table has nothing for). Nothing else about macros is stored; `entryMacros(index, entry)` sits beside `entryNutrition()` and recomputes on render from the same `matches` (the tag logic is untouched): grams = the component's food group's Small/Medium/Large default (`portionGroupFor()`, "typical portion, estimate", Medium by default) times `FOOD_AMOUNT_SCALE` for the entry's existing "how much" answer (0.25/0.5/0.75/1), then the row's per 100 g figures. A manual "Enter from the packet" override always wins (per portion is used as typed; per 100 g is scaled by its own `grams`); next comes a food in `profile/main.customFoods` (`[{ name, kcal, prot, carb, fat, addedBy }]` per 100 g, written by "Save to my foods" in the override panel, matched by the typed name, `customFoodByName()`; `ADDED_FOODS` in the code stays separate and curated); then the table row. A component with none of those is left out and counted ("not counted", `unmatchedChunks()` catches an unknown ingredient beside known ones, which the tag matcher would otherwise drop silently). Four render sites, all gated: the Food sheet (behind the Estimate line since v54: one row per component with S/M/L buttons showing the grams, the override panel, a totals line; a saved meal chosen there restores its own `meals/{id}.portions`), Today's timeline (`entrySub()`, "about N kcal, N g protein"; `ensureFoodIndexForToday()` fetches the table in the background and redraws once), the Food diary and its PDF/print (`diaryRow()` line per entry, `.diary-macros` per day, `dayMacros()`), and the per-day readings line of Notes for the team (`dayReadings()`, so screen, PDF and the Send/Copy text all carry it). Estimates, not medical advice, and the UI says so.
  - Editing an entry in place (since v37): food, drink, weight and note entries can be edited, not just deleted, via the same "..." entry menu (`entryOptions()`, `EDITABLE_ENTRY_TYPES`) reopening `openAdd(type, entry)` pre-filled with the entry's own values and date/time, saving with `updateEntry()` instead of creating a new one. Food diary rows (`diaryRow()`) get their own "..." menu too, so a wrong "how much" or a missing "what's in it" can be fixed from the diary itself, not just from Today's timeline; `renderFoodDiary()` re-runs after a save or delete made from there so the diary reflects the change immediately, since it fetches its own range with `loadEntriesFrom()` rather than following the live listener Today uses. Since v105 temperature and vitals readings edit too (Mark, from a hospital bed, expected a view and an edit where the menu only offered Delete): the "..." menu is titled by the entry's kind (`ENTRY_SHEET_TITLES`: Vitals, Temperature, Medicine and so on, no longer "Entry"), and for a reading lists one line per value (`readingRows()`, a `.readings` definition list: Heart rate 88 bpm, Blood pressure 122/82 mmHg, Oxygen 98%, coloured amber or red with the Notes for the team thresholds), then **Edit**, which opens the Vitals sheet as "Edit vitals" or "Edit temperature" with only that entry's boxes, filled in (the other blocks hidden; weight keeps its own edit sheet), the note, date and time, and **Save changes**. A cleared box is saved as `null` so it really goes; an edit with every box empty is refused with "use Delete"; a changed date moves the reading (the update now sets `day` when the sheet has a Date box). Tested in `pw/v105.js` (the view and its levels, Edit vitals and Edit temperature filled in with the other boxes hidden, a cleared heart rate, one entry kept, the empty edit refused, a temperature moved to yesterday and shown amber there, axe, 320px dark). Medicines, sleep, sessions and check-ins have their own edit routes or stay delete-and-re-add.
  - Nutrition tags (since v29): `data/cofid.json` is the official UK food table (CoFID 2021, McCance and Widdowson, Open Government Licence; 2,887 foods, per 100 g: kcal, prot, fat, sat, carb, sugar, starch, fibre (AOAC), nsp, salt unused), built and committed by `.github/workflows/build-cofid.yml` (`tools/build_cofid.py`, run by hand from the Actions tab; the gov.uk download only works from a runner). The app fetches it lazily (`loadFoodTable()`, cached in `foodIndex`) when the Food sheet or Food diary first needs it. `matchFoodText()` splits an entry's `note` plus `detail` on commas and "and/with/on", drops quantities and stop words, and looks each phrase up: the curated `FOOD_ALIASES` first (everyday words to table names, including composites such as "roast dinner", "fajitas", "full English", "homity pie"), then the table's own head words (`buildFoodIndex()`, best plain cooked form picked by `pickRepresentative()`), then two-word windows and single words, with a one-letter typo fallback for longer words. Cooking words never match on their own. `foodTags()` gives each matched food label-style tags per 100 g: Protein (10 g or 20% of energy with 5 g), Fibre (over 3 g or 1.5 g per 100 kcal), Wholegrain (starchy group plus a wholegrain word in the name), Fruit and veg (groups D and F except potatoes, juices), Dairy (group B except desserts), Starchy carbs, Good fats (nuts, oily fish, avocado, mostly unsaturated), High sugar (over 22.5 g, not fruit or veg), High fat (over 17.5 g), High sat fat (over 5 g). Small-amount groups (sauces, sugars and spreads, herbs, fats and oils) earn no tags. An entry's tags are the union of its components' tags in `FOOD_TAG_ORDER`. The diary shows the tags as chips coloured by group (`.tag.is-veg/-protein/-carb`, `NUTRI_GROUP` in the JS, since v41): Fruit and veg, Protein plus Good fats, and Fibre/Wholegrain/Starchy carbs each get their own hue (`--nutri-veg/-protein/-carb`, chosen and checked against colour-blindness with the data-viz palette validator, both colour schemes); Dairy stays a plain neutral chip and the "High ..." ones reuse the app's existing warm accent, since that is a status signal, not a fourth identity colour, kept deliberately to three so they stay tellable apart. Each day with any tags also gets a small donut chart of that day's colour mix (`donutGradient()`, a CSS conic-gradient) with a coloured-swatch legend beside it, above the existing per-day tag count line, a per-day tag count line, "Not recognised: ..." for phrases nothing matched (screen only), and an overview (`#food-overview`, days with each tag out of the days shown). The PDF has the same overview line, the same donut per day (drawn as a solid pie of triangle wedges with a white circle punched over the middle for the hole, `wedge()`/`donut()` in `buildPdfBlob()`) and a two-column table per day (`{ kind: 'table', head, rows }`) whose Nutrition cells carry the same per-tag colours, wrapped word by word (`wrapTags()`) since jsPDF has no built-in multi-colour text run. The Food sheet nudges once when a name with no detail matches nothing ("picky lunch"): a shaded box explains it will still save either way, with its own "Save without tags" button, never a silent second tap. Nothing about the tags is stored; they are recomputed on render, so improving `FOOD_ALIASES` improves every past entry. Not medical advice, and the diary says so.
  - `ADDED_FOODS` in `scripts.js` (next to `buildFoodIndex()`) holds branded products the official table was never going to have, added by hand from a photo of the product's own label, per 100 g, in the same shape as a table row (`g: null`, since CoFID's group codes don't apply). Each carries its own `aliases`, which take priority over a generic table guess for the same words. First entry: Whey protein powder, Gold Standard, strawberry (real label figures: 79 g protein, 3.3 g sugar, no fibre per 100 g; a stand-in like the table's "Build up" shake would have wrongly tagged it "High sugar"). Its generic aliases (`whey protein`, `protein powder`, `whey`) mean any whey product gets this profile until a closer match is added; close enough for whey specifically, since brands don't vary hugely, but worth another one added if a very different product turns up.
  - Questions for the team (since v45): `entries/{autoId}` { type "question", note (the question), answered (bool), addedBy, day, at } from the "Question for the team" row on Today (`#question-now`, since v54 the third row of the check-in list, same shape as the two check-ins, with a status line from `renderQuestionRow()`: "2 waiting for the next appointment, tap to add another" or "Nothing waiting, tap to add one"; `watchQuestions()` keeps `state.questions` live for it, the demo reads `recentEntries`; `openAdd('question')`, so it edits in place like a note). Shown on the timeline with a teal bar; the "..." menu offers Mark as answered / Reopen. Open questions from any date (not just the range) head the Notes for the team report, numbered, with a Mark as answered button there too; questions marked answered inside the range are counted in one muted line. `loadQuestions()` fetches them with a single `where('type', '==', 'question')` query.
  - Notes for the team (Trends > Reports), rebuilt in v45 and v46 to fit a ten-minute appointment. Four sections, on screen, in the PDF and in the Send/Copy text: (1) Questions for the team (in the PDF each has a box to tick, "Asked by Mark, 24 Sep" and, since v55, three faint ruled lines under it to write the answer on in clinic; the print stylesheet leaves the same room under each `.question` card); (2) Summary (`summaryRows()`): a few plain sentences, worst first, not statistics. Only topics worth checking or mentioning get a line of their own ("Pain has increased, from about 4/10 in the first half of the period to 6/10 in the second; 5 or more on 7 days, worst 8/10 on 23 Sep", "High temperature (38.0 or over) on 2 days of the 12 with a reading, peaking at 38.7 °C on 24 Sep"); a trend compares the second half of the period with the first when both halves have at least three scores (pain, sickness, appetite, energy). Amber topics that happened exactly once with no worsening trend are folded into one "One-offs worth a mention: sickness 6/10 on 25 Sep; heart rate 104 bpm on 20 Sep" line; every topic with nothing of concern shares one green line with a key figure each ("Blood pressure (116/74 to 128/82), oxygen (lowest 95%) and weight (steady at about 78.6 kg): nothing of concern"); the when-needed medicines share one teal line (amber if a daily maximum was hit). Topics: temperature, heart rate, blood pressure (the line names the reading that crossed the line, whichever number did it), oxygen, pain (check-in pain and worst pain plus pain readings), sickness, appetite, energy, sleep, weight, drinks, eating (with the food estimate per day when the Food setting is on), mood, when-needed medicines. Levels red (Check), amber (Mention), teal (Context), green (Fine), `LEVEL_WORD`/`LEVEL_RANK`, thresholds unchanged (temp 37.5/38.0, heart rate 100/120 or 50 and under, BP 140/90 and 160/100 or systolic 90 and under, oxygen 93/90 and under, pain 5/7, sickness 6, appetite and energy 3 and under, nights under 5 h, weight down 2 kg (red at 4), days under 1 L, days with nothing eaten, low-mood days); on screen as `.flag.is-*` rows with a pill, in the PDF as a `{ kind: 'flag' }` block with a coloured bar and the level word (`FLAG_RGB` in `buildPdfBlob()`); (3) Letters and documents in the range (excerpt on screen and in the PDF, full explanation in the text; `docSummary()` drops the pasted "=== CARE LOG DOCUMENT ===" header so only the explanation shows); (4) Notes: only days with written notes (`type: "note"` entries, notes attached to temp, weight, vitals, med and pain entries, and the check-in `symptoms` and `settled` answers, `REPORT_CHECKIN_KEYS`), each headed by who wrote it in bold (`.note-who`, a `{ kind: 'note' }` block in the PDF, "Mark, 10:39 (morning check-in): ..." in the text). No per-day readings, feeling or when-needed lines: the detail for any one day stays in the app. Not medical advice, and the UI says so. Letters and documents whose `docDate` falls in the range are included (title, date and full explanation in the text; a card with an excerpt and "Open the document" on screen). Send/Copy prefix the text with `NOTES_PROMPT`, asking the person's AI app for questions for the oncologist or nurse. "Save as PDF" (`savePdf()`, jsPDF, A4, Helvetica) makes a people-facing copy without the prompt. On desktop Chrome/Edge (since v40) it opens the browser's own "Save as" dialog (`window.showSaveFilePicker()`, asked for straight away, before the PDF itself is built, so the tap still counts as a direct user gesture) so it can be saved straight to the Desktop or any folder; elsewhere it hands the file to the share sheet when files can be shared (Save to Files, Mail, AirDrop), otherwise downloads it. The Food diary has the same button. Print uses the same print stylesheet as the Food diary. A "Preview" button next to it (since v38, `previewPdf()`) opens the same PDF in a new tab to look at without sending or downloading it; the tab is opened synchronously before the PDF itself is built (`buildPdfBlob()`, the shared PDF-drawing code both `savePdf()` and `previewPdf()` call), which is what stops browsers treating the later navigation as a blocked pop-up.
- `meals/{autoId}`: { name, parts (string, what goes with it, may be empty), portions? (the same per-component map a food entry carries, only written when the Food setting is on), addedBy, createdAt, updatedAt, and since v54 lastAt (Timestamp), uses (count) and slots ({ breakfast, lunch, dinner, snack } counts, from the time being logged: 05 to 10, 11 to 14, 15 to 20, else snack, `mealSlot()`) }. Every logged meal is remembered automatically (the "Remember this meal" tick box went in v54; an existing meal's parts and portions are updated if changed, and its usage counted, `saveMeal(id, name, parts, portions, usedSlot, existing)`). Managed under More > Saved meals.
  - The Food sheet (rebuilt in v54, search first): stage one is the "What was eaten?" box with **Recent** under it (`recentFoods()`: the last five distinct foods from the past two days, topped up from saved meals by `lastAt`) and **Usual for breakfast / lunch / dinner / Usual snacks** (`usualMeals()`: saved meals by their count in the current slot, then by uses, then A to Z), as tappable rows (`.pickrow`) rather than chips, so the list never grows into a wall. Typing replaces them with live matches: a "Log "..." as typed" row when no saved meal has that exact name, saved meals (prefix matches first), My foods, then up to six UK food table names from two characters (`foodIndex.foods`, starts-with before word-start before anywhere, shorter first). Enter takes what was typed. Choosing a row moves to stage two: the chosen name in a sunken card with Change, What is in it (filled from the meal), How much, and with the Food setting on one **Estimate** line (`.estline`, "About 376 kcal, 11 g protein. Adjust portions", `aria-expanded`) that opens the same per-ingredient portion rows and packet override as before; the time shows as "Time 08:37 Change" and only becomes a field when tapped (editing an entry opens straight on stage two with the field showing), so a forgotten breakfast can still be logged at 15:00 with the right time. The "picky lunch" nudge is unchanged.
  - Quantities (since v55): a food is never named with a number in it. `parseQuantityName()` turns "2 x kiwi", "2 kiwis", "kiwi x2", "two eggs" or "half a banana" into the clean name plus a count, in the search box, on the Recent and Usual rows (shown cleaned, the old saved meal renamed on the next save via `pickedRawName`) and in the "Log 2 kiwi" as-typed row. In stage two every component gets its own row, always visible: **How many** (a minus, number, plus `stepper()` with the unit word following the number, 1 egg, 2 eggs) for foods in `PIECE_WEIGHTS` (about 60 everyday items with a typical weight each, one and many labels; `pieceInfo()` matches the typed words first, then the table food's name), **How much** in grams with quick chips (small handful 20 g, handful 30 g, 50 g, 100 g) for anything under 5 g a piece (nuts, grapes, berries), and the Small, Medium, Large chips for everything else (only with the Food setting on). A count written in the text ("two eggs, wholemeal toast") is the row's starting number (`countInText()`), and `entryMacros()` keeps one component per piece key so an egg named in the meal and again in the detail is counted once. Stored per component as `portions[key].qty = { unit "piece"|"g", n, each?, name, many? }`, whatever the Food setting says (they are amounts, not estimates; with the setting off only rows with a quantity are stored); `portionGrams()` uses it before the size, and "How much was eaten" is hidden when every component is counted or weighed (the amount saves as "All of it"). `quantityText()` prints them everywhere the meal shows: the timeline and Food diary ("2 kiwis · 30 g almonds", in place of the amount), the diary PDF row and the copied text. The Drink sheet has the same How many stepper, multiplying the amount of each.
  - The Vitals sheet logs temperature, heart rate, blood pressure, oxygen and, since v54, weight together (the Weight quick-add tile went, so the grid is a clean three by two: Vitals, Pain, Drink, Food, Note, Sleep). Temperature is saved as its own `type: "temp"` entry (so the 37.5 / 38.0 colouring, the Today tile and the chart all keep working) and weight as its own `type: "weight"` entry (so the chart, the summary and editing in place are untouched); the other readings save as one `type: "vitals"` entry. All are optional; at least one is required to save. Manual entry only, no HealthKit or Shortcuts integration.
- `medicines/{id}`: { name, dose, how, purpose, kind "scheduled"|"prn", perDay?, minGapHours?, maxPerDay?, courseEnd? "YYYY-MM-DD", active, order, times? (["HH:MM"], reminders, since v58), withMeals? (bool, since v58) }
  - The Meds tab has its own day-nav (`#meds-day-prev/-label/-next`), sharing `state.selectedDay` with Today (since v36): the same day picked on either tab shows its own dose history on the other, rather than the Meds tab always meaning "right now". `renderMeds()`/`medCard()` take that day rather than always reading `todayStr()`, so the dose dots, "X of Y" counts, time chips and course-end pill all reflect the day being viewed; only genuinely real-time things (the PRN "Can be given now"/"Next from"/"Maximum reached" pill, and the "Log now" button, which always means this instant) are hidden when the viewed day isn't today, leaving "Log a dose" (the old "Other time", pre-filled to the viewed day) as the way to log or backfill a dose for a day other than today. A day with nothing logged reads "Not given this day" rather than showing a stale "Last ..." line from a different day.
- `documents/{autoId}`: { title, docDate, kind "images"|"text", category "general"|"chemo"|"exemption", pageCount, text?, explanation, addedBy, addedAt, updatedAt }
  - `category: "chemo"` documents are the chemo plan, listed in the Chemo tab as well as in Documents.
  - `category: "exemption"` is the NHS prescription exemption certificate, listed on the Meds tab (`meds-docs-section`, "Add exemption certificate") as well as in Documents, so it is one tap away at the pharmacy counter. Excluded from the Notes for the team report (`buildNotesReport()`), since it is not a clinical letter. `openAddDocument('exemption')` pre-fills the title with "NHS Medical Exemption Certificate".
  - Title, date and category can be changed after saving ("Edit title, date or category" on the document screen).
- `documents/{autoId}/pages/{pageNumber}`: { data base64 JPEG, width, height }
- `days/{YYYY-MM-DD}`: { chemo (bool, a treatment session this day; the field keeps its pre-v63 name), treatment? ("chemo"|"radio"|"immuno"|"other", since v63, chemo when missing), chemoDone (bool), mood? (1 to 5, mirrored from the check-ins), good? (string, mirrored from the evening check-in), updatedBy, updatedAt }
- `cheers/{autoId}`: retired in v59 (the cheer board on the Chemo tab went, replaced by the carer's view and the cycle chart, see "Chemo days and after"). Old documents may still exist; the rules and the demo reset script still name the collection so they can be cleared.
- `exercise/{YYYY-MM-DD}`: { day, steps? (number), done: { [programme item id]: bool } (the original four keys are pressups, situps, plank, squats), workouts? ([{ name, minutes, km?, kcal?, start? "HH:MM" }], from Apple Health, since v69), addedBy, updatedAt }
  - Steps arrive automatically from Apple Health through the bridge (`exercise/{day}.steps`, `addedBy: "Apple Health"`); the Steps sheet is for corrections. The Exercise tab has day arrows, and the Steps sheet has its own month calendar (`openStepsSheet()`, days already logged shown green, future days disabled) so any past day can be logged directly, retrospectively and in any order. Save writes the tapped day and keeps the sheet open, so several days can be logged in one sitting; switching to a different day, or leaving via Done, the round close button or the backdrop, saves whatever is typed and not yet saved first (`saveIfDirty()`, and the generic `openSheet(title, body, onClose)` close-guard it uses), so nothing typed is ever silently dropped.
- `nutrition/{YYYY-MM-DD}` (since v51): { day, kcal, prot, carb, fat, source "apple-health"|"manual", addedBy, updatedAt }. The day's food totals from whatever app the person logs food in. Arrive automatically through the bridge from Apple Health (Health Auto Export metrics Dietary Energy, Protein, Carbohydrates, Total Fat, summed per day; MyFitnessPal, Nutracheck and Apple's own app all write to Health), or typed in from the Food diary's "Day totals from your food app" button (`#food-totals`, for Android or anyone without the feed; family only, hidden read-only). `watchNutrition()` keeps `state.nutrition`; `loggedTotals(day)` returns a day's totals or null. Where a day has them they replace the app's own estimate: the Food diary day line reads "From Apple Health ..." or "From your food app ..." instead of "Estimate ..." (shown even with the estimates setting off, since they are logged figures, not estimates), and the Notes summary's Eating line averages them, saying where they came from. The protein target comparison uses whichever figure applies.
- `profile/main`: { calls: [{label, role?, number?, email?}] (Who to contact, see below), exerciseGoals?: { pressups, situps, plankSeconds, squats } (pre-v69 amounts, still read as the starting programme), programme? ({ items: [...] }, since v69, see Exercise programme), physio? ({ from, given, notes }), detailedNutrition? (bool, the Food setting), customFoods? (see Calorie and macro estimates), proteinTarget? (number, grams a day, from the dietitian, More > Settings > Food, since v50) }
  - Protein (since v50, asked for by the dietitian): every estimate line carries the meal's or day's protein share of energy ("12 g protein, 24% of energy", `fmtMacroLine()`), and when `proteinTarget` is set each Food diary day says "protein target 100 g met" or "protein 12 g under the 100 g target" (`proteinTargetText()`), and the Notes summary's Eating line reads "... against the 100 g protein target, under on 5 of 7 days" (amber when under on more than half the days).

## A leaner Notes for the team (since v114)
Mark, after his first real report (5 October 2026): the summary was too detailed for a note, the answered questions would grow and clutter it, and letters did not belong in it. The report (screen, PDF and the text for an AI app) is now **Questions for the team** (open ones only), **Summary**, **Charts** and **Notes**.
- **Summary as talking points.** Every row from `summaryRows()` carries `short` beside its full `text` (`push(level, topic, text, brief, short)`): "High temperature on 4 days, up to 38.6 °C", "Heart rate 120 or over once, up to 126 bpm", "Blood pressure over 160/100 twice, highest 116/107", "Oxygen down to 90%", "Pain rising, about 4 to 6/10, worst 8/10", "Sickness 6 or more on 3 evenings", "Short nights (under 5 h): 5 of 17", "Weight down 2.4 kg, now 78.1 kg", "Under 1 litre of drinks on 4 days", "Nothing eaten logged on 6 days", "Bowels: loose on 2 days, hard and loose together", "Mood low on 2 days", "One-offs: heart rate 104 bpm, a short night and sickness 6/10" (dates stripped from the briefs), "Steady: sickness, appetite and weight" (the green line, names only), "When-needed medicines: Oramorph every day, Diazepam on 10 days (daily maximum reached)", "Given in hospital: Morphine sulphate, Sodium chloride 0.9%". The PDF prints `short` under "Highlights to talk about, from simple checks by the app; not medical advice. The detail for every day is in Daybook."; the screen leads with `short` (`.flag-short`) and keeps the full sentence behind a native **Details** (`details.flag-more`); the text for an AI app keeps the full sentences, which help it. Levels and thresholds are unchanged.
- **Answered questions are filed, not printed.** The PDF and the text no longer carry an Answered block; the screen shows one line, "2 questions answered in this period, kept under Letters, results and paperwork as "Questions answered"", with **Open the answers**. `syncAnswerDocs()` keeps one text document per day on which questions were answered: `documents/answers-{day}` { title "Questions answered, 2 Oct to 5 Oct 2026" (`fmtDayRange()`, from the first of them asked to the day answered), docDate the day answered, kind "text", category "answers", text (`answerDocsFrom()`: "Questions answered on Monday 5 October 2026.", "Asked between ...", then per question its number, "Asked by Mark on ...", "Answer: ..." or that a recording is saved and where to play it, or "Marked as answered. No answer written down."), explanation "", pageCount 0, auto true, addedBy "Daybook" }. It runs when Notes for the team or the documents list is drawn (`openDocs()`), writes only when the title or text changed, deletes a day's document when none of its questions is still answered (a question reopened from the timeline drops off), never for read-only or viewer, and in the preview edits `state.documents`. Every way of marking a question answered now stamps `answeredAt` (`stampNow()`; the Notes button and the timeline's Mark as answered did not before), which is what files it. In the documents list it reads "Kept by Daybook from your answered questions" with a teal tick; opened, it shows the text with the explanation block (`#doc-explain-block`), Edit and Delete hidden and a hint (`#doc-auto-hint`). Rules: no change (`documents` is family write).
- **No letters.** The Letters and documents section went from the screen, the PDF and the text: the letters are the team's own, an AI explanation of them adds nothing for a clinician and only sends more out of the household, and they stay in the app.
- **No stranded headings.** A heading block may carry `keep` (points it needs below it); `buildPdfBlob()` starts a new page first when that much room is not left. Charts asks for 230, Notes for 90, so the Charts heading always shares its page with a chart.
- Tested in `pw/v114.js` (the open question kept and the answered ones filed as one line, the document's title range and text, Open the answers and the hidden explanation, edit and delete, the list line, an ordinary document unchanged, reopening dropping a question off, the short rows and their Details, no Letters, the PDF without Answered or Letters and with the short rows, the Charts heading sharing its page with a chart through PyMuPDF, axe, 320 dark); `pw/v64.js`, `v111.js`, `v113.js` and `smoke.js` were updated to the leaner report.

## Notes in weekly blocks (since v115; seven-day blocks counted back from the last day and the summary through the `report` kind since v117, see the two-page report)
The Notes section of Notes for the team (screen and PDF) is grouped by week, Monday to Sunday, clipped to the report's range (`notesByWeek()`, `weekStartOf()`, `weekLabel()`: "22 to 27 Sept", "28 Sept to 4 Oct", or the day itself for a one-day week), headed with the week and its note count. Without a summary each week shows at most four notes (`WEEK_NOTES_MAX`): written notes before check-in answers, routine answers left out (`ROUTINE_ANSWER`, `isRoutineNote()`: none, nothing new, same as yesterday and the like, judged on the words after the label), the longest first, then in time order, with "And 13 more notes in Daybook" (the PDF) or "..., on Today for each day" (the screen). **Summarise each week** (`#notes-weekly`, only with the AI service, `explainAvailable()`, hidden read-only) sends the week's notes, routine answers left out, to the bridge as `{ kind: "weekly", text }` ("Week from YYYY-MM-DD" headings, one line per note); `EXPLAIN_SYSTEM.weekly` asks for JSON only, `{ weeks: [{ from, points }] }`, two to four points a week under 20 words each, their own words where clear, no advice, interpretation or reassurance; the same daily cap. The reply (code fence tolerated, em dashes turned into commas, at most four points, only weeks that were sent) is kept for this session and this range only (`state.notesWeekly`, `weekPoints()`, never stored), shown as bullet points with "Summarised from the notes by Daybook Assistant, an AI helper. ..." (v116), and used by the PDF and Send to the team; **Show the notes instead** (`#notes-weekly-undo`) clears it, a different range ignores it, and an unreadable reply leaves the notes as they were. The text for an AI app keeps every note day by day. The bridge's `notes` prompt no longer mentions letters. Tested in `pw/v115.js` (the weekly blocks and their order, at most four notes and the "more" line, no routine answers, the button, the PDF blocks, the bridge request, the points on screen and in the PDF, a different range, back again, Show the notes instead, an unreadable reply, axe, the in-memory preview with no button, 320 dark) and the scratchpad's `explain_e2e.mjs` on the new worker.

## Who to contact (since v110; "Who to call" until then)
More's contacts list (`renderCalls()`, `#calls`, `#calls-edit` "Edit contacts") is still stored as `profile/main.calls` (no rename, no migration): each contact { label (the name), role?, number?, email? } needs a name and a phone number or an email address. The card shows the name, the role, the number and the email (`.call-info`), with **Call** (`tel:`) and **Email** (`mailto:`, the phone's own Mail app, nothing through Daybook) stacked beside it (`.call-btns`, wrapping under the details at 320px). The edit sheet ("Who to contact") is one sunken block per contact (`.contactrow`, "Contact 1" and so on, renumbered on Remove) with Name, Role (optional), Phone and Email; Save refuses a contact with no name, one with neither phone nor email, and an email without an @ and a dot after it (`EMAIL_RE`), naming the contact and focusing the box. The privacy page's list of what a household records says "who to contact (names, roles, phone numbers and email addresses)". Asked for by Mark after a rushed first appointment, so the report can go to the right person beforehand. Tested in `pw/v110.js`.
- **Extension or option (v135, 6 October 2026).** Mark: some numbers need a menu choice after dialling (the trial's 24-hour line: "dial that number and then select option 2"). Each contact has an **Extension or option (optional)** box between Phone and Email (placeholder "e.g. option 2, or ext 4521"), stored as `ext` on the contact (`profile/main.calls[].ext`, "" when empty; no migration). Save refuses an option with no phone number. The card shows it under the number in ink and bold (`.call-ext`, `extWords()`: "Then option 2", bare digits as "Then press 2", "Then ext 4521"), and the Call button's label names it ("Call Sarah Cannon Research Institute, then option 2"). **Call dials the number alone** (`telHref()` unchanged): a pause or wait in a `tel:` link is handled differently by each phone, so an automatic key press could land before the menu listens, or stop the Call button working at all on an emergency line. The privacy page names "any extension or menu option" (dated 6 October 2026). Tested in `pw/v135.js` (the box's place and placeholder, an option with no number refused, what is saved, Then option 2, the Call link and its label, no line on other contacts, Then press 2, axe on the sheet and More, 320 dark); `pw/v110.js` and `v90.js` follow the new field and date.

## Wording put right, and Daybook Assistant (v116, 5 October 2026)
Mark asked for a check of every claim in the app, then: "we shouldn't call it Claude but name it Daybook Assistant; some people are still scared of AI and some might not know what Claude is."
- **The name.** The AI helper is **Daybook Assistant** in every button, hint, toast and bridge message ("Explain with Daybook Assistant", "Daybook Assistant is not switched on yet. Use Send to my AI app for now.", "Summarised from the notes by Daybook Assistant, an AI helper. It can miss or misread things; every note is in Daybook."). The photo hints say "Daybook Assistant (AI from Anthropic, in the US) reads it ... It can misread a label". The privacy page says once, plainly, that Daybook Assistant is Claude, made by Anthropic, a US company, which five features use it, that nothing is sent without a tap, and what happens to the text; terms section 6 is "Daybook Assistant (the AI features)"; About lists "Anthropic's Claude, behind Daybook Assistant". Keep that disclosure: UK GDPR transparency needs the processor named, and clinicians trust a named model more than a vague one. Never write "Daybook's own AI service"; there is none. The share-sheet route still names no assistant ("Send to my AI app").
- **Temperature words** (`tempWord()`): no "Normal range". 38.0 and over "High, 38.0 or above. Follow your alert card." (red); 37.5 to 37.9 "Raised, 37.5 to 37.9. Check your alert card for when to call." (amber); under 36.0 "Low, under 36.0. Check your alert card." (amber); otherwise "Under 37.5." (no colour). The chemo alert card, not the app, says when to ring.
- **Within limits.** The green summary level reads "Within limits" (`LEVEL_WORD.green`) and its line "... within the app's simple limits." (short form "Within the app's limits: ..."); the app checks a few fixed thresholds, it does not judge "nothing of concern". Protein lines say "Estimated protein" unless every day came from a food app (`protWord`).
- **Accurate claims elsewhere:** the sign-in line ("Each household's records are seen only by its own members."), the accessibility line on the App card (WCAG 2.2 AA, last checked 28 September 2026 with axe-core and by hand), the privacy page (dated 5 October 2026: the phone keeps a copy of opened records, push addresses and the AI daily count are stored, not end-to-end encrypted, the viewer role sees mood, sleep and drinks, the operator's access, Cloudflare and Anthropic named), the Apple Health sleep hint naming Health Auto Export, the exercise auto-tick hint ("if the Apple Health feed is set up under Settings"), the page title "Notes for the team", and the PDF file names ("Daybook notes for the team ...", "Daybook food diary ...").
- Tested in `pw/v116.js` (the four temperature words and colours, no "Normal range", the page title, the Within limits pill, the Daybook Assistant hints, the privacy, terms and About wording, the Settings card, no em dashes); `pw/v60.js`, `v90.js`, `v96.js`, `v114.js`, `v115.js` and the smoke test were updated; `ai_err_test.mjs` and `explain_e2e.mjs` pass on the new worker.

## Charts in Notes for the team (v111 to v116; replaced by the two-page report in v117)
The Notes for the team PDF (Save as PDF, Preview) carries a **Charts** section after the Summary and before Letters: the Trends charts redrawn by `reportCharts(entries, from, to)` for the report's own range and drawn as vectors by a `{ kind: 'chart' }` block in `buildPdfBlob()` (`chart()`: shaded threshold bands, a light grid, y labels, day labels thinned to about eight, stacked bars or lines with points, a legend when there is more than one series, a one-line note). Colours are fixed RGB for white paper (`PDF_RGB`), so a phone in dark mode makes the same PDF; kg and °C like the rest of the report.
