Charts: Temperature (35 to 40, amber 37.5 to 37.9 and red 38 bands, each point coloured by level), Heart rate (amber 100 to 119, red 120 and over), Blood pressure (systolic teal, diastolic warm), Oxygen (80 to 100, amber 91 to 93, red 90 and under), Weight (at least a 4 kg span, `minSpan`, so a small wobble is not drawn as a cliff), Sleep (stacked Deep, Core, REM, Not broken down), Pain (check-in line plus hollow extra readings), Mood, Drinks (ml a day), Bowels (types 1 to 5 and loose stacked); only charts with readings in the range are offered. Which go in: a tick list above Preview and Save as PDF (`#notes-charts-box`, `.presets` with `aria-pressed`, `renderChartPicker()`, short names in `REPORT_CHART_NAME`); until touched, `chosenReportCharts()` ticks Temperature, Sleep, Pain and Weight (`REPORT_CHART_DEFAULTS`) plus any topic the summary flags amber or red (`CHART_TOPIC_KEY`); once touched, the choice (`state.notesCharts`) holds for the session across ranges. `currentNotesPdf()` builds the blocks at the moment of Save or Preview, so the ticks always apply (`state.notesPdf` keeps `report` and `charts`, no longer prebuilt blocks). Asked for by Mark so the report reads like a doctor's report without screenshots. Tested in `pw/v111.js` (the offered list and its order, the defaults, the Charts section's place, the default charts, the temperature note and the pain legend, adding blood pressure and dropping drinks, the choice holding across ranges, nothing ticked leaving no section, axe, 320 dark), with the pages rendered to images through PyMuPDF in the scratchpad (`pylib`).

## The two-page Notes for the team (since v117, 5 October 2026)
Mark's brief and the approved design (`clinical-summary-report.html`, kept in the scratchpad as `report/design.html`): replace the seven-page linear export with two A4 pages a patient hands to a nurse or doctor, that scan in under a minute and leave room to write. Preview, Print, Save as PDF and Send to the team all use it; the screen version of Notes for the team (questions, summary, weekly notes) is unchanged apart from the weeks. The v111 chart tick list, `reportCharts()`, `notesPdfBlocks()`, `currentNotesPdf()` and the jsPDF flag, question, note and chart drawers went (the Food diary still uses `buildPdfBlob()` for headings, tables and donuts).
- **`report.js`** (an ES module, `import('./report.js?v=' + APP_VERSION)` on first use): `reportHtml(R)` returns the whole A4 document in the design's CSS (tokens as in the brief: ink #17263A, slate, mist, line, teal #1E7F86, amber #E0A030, red #C8453B, green #3F8F6B; Fraunces 600 headings, Inter body at 8.6pt; `@page { size: A4; margin: 0 }`, each `.page` 210 by 297 mm with `overflow: hidden`, `print-color-adjust: exact`); `renderChart(cfg, days)` is the design's `render()` (one config per chart: title, pill, tone, min, max, ticks, bands, series; `null` breaks the line; `type: "bars"` with `flagBelow` 5 for sleep); `overflowOf(doc)` names any `[data-sec]` section whose bottom passes its page's foot or whose content is taller than its box; `shrink(R, over)` gives one step (writing lines from 3 to 1, then the newest questions out to "Plus N more questions in the app", then a sentence off the longest weekly entry, then the earliest non-alert entry); `fitReport(frame, R)` writes `srcdoc`, waits for the fonts (3 s at most), checks, shrinks, and returns `{ ok, R, doc }` or `{ ok: false, over }`. Nothing in `report.js` calculates anything. The writing lines are real `<i>` rules (40 per card, clipped), not the design's repeating gradient, which printed as thick double bars.
- **`buildTwoPage(n)`** in `scripts.js` (from `state.notesPdf`, which now carries the range's `entries`): the masthead ("A patient summary for the nursing and medical team. Fortnight to 5 October 2026."; Week for 7 days, Fortnight for 14, else "N days"), the patient (`profile/main.reportName`, set under More > Settings > Notes for the team, "Name on the report", else the household member whose relation is patient, `loadPatientName()`, else the signed-in name), the period ("22 Sep to 5 Oct 2026", `rDay()`/`rRange()`/`rPeriod()` with three-letter months, since `toLocaleDateString` gives "Sept"), prepared today.
  - **Tiles** (`twoPageTiles()`): Temperature (peak, "High on 1 day, peak on 30 Sep. Latest 36.8 °C on 5 Oct."), Heart rate (peak), Blood pressure (the count over 160/100, each reading named as taken when three or fewer, "Over 160/100: 162/104 on 30 Sep, 116/107 on 2 Oct", since "over 160/100" means either number crossed; the old "highest 116/107" was a diastolic-led pick, now the Summary's short line lists the readings too), Oxygen (lowest), Pain (average of the daily averages, worst and its day, days at 5 or more), Sleep (nights under 5 hours of those logged), Protein (days under the dietitian's target, or the average with "No target set", or "Not tracked" with the estimates off), Mood (low days from `days/{day}.mood`). Tone: red if any red reading, amber if any amber, green "Steady." otherwise, plain mist with "None" and "No readings in this period." when nothing was logged. The app's own thresholds throughout, so the tiles agree with the Summary: blood pressure is amber over 140/90 (the brief had no amber), pain is red at 7 (the brief had pain and mood amber at most; Mark to confirm).
  - **Strip** (`twoPageMeds()`): when-needed medicines with "daily" or "on N days", any given in hospital ("Given in hospital (3 and 4 Oct). Morphine sulphate, sodium chloride 0.9%.") and "In hospital since"; **Steady** lists the Summary's unflagged topics outside the tiles (`summaryRows()` now returns `fine` on its array), hidden when there are none.
  - **Medicine names** (`fixMedWords()`, `MED_WORDS`, `medDistance()`): generic names in lower case, brands capitalised, and dictation slips put right when a word of six letters or more is within one edit (two from eight letters) of a known name with the same first letter ("dakteparun" dalteparin, "oromorph" Oramorph, "Diazipam" diazepam); applied to questions, the banner and the weekly entries; case variants merge in lists. Never named `editDistance` (that is the food matcher's).
  - **Questions:** the open ones, oldest first, with their app numbers (the same as the recording markers, so not always 1 to 7), "Asked by Shelley, 27 Sep", never cut; up to seven, then "Plus N more questions in the app"; the teal "Other points from today" card always last, full width when it would otherwise sit alone.
  - **Charts** (`twoPageCharts()`): eight, as in the design (the brief says seven but lists eight): the day's highest temperature and heart rate, the day's blood pressure pair with the highest systolic (two lines, dark systolic, teal diastolic), the day's lowest oxygen, the day's last weight (axis from the period's lowest to highest with a kilogram either side, at least 4 kg), hours asleep as bars, pain (the day's check-in average, readings where there was none), mood (check-in average). Axes widen to fit any reading; bands as the thresholds (pain 7 to 10 red; mood and weight none). Titles say "1 to 10" for pain and mood, the app's scale.
  - **Weeks:** seven-day blocks counted back from the last day (`reportWeeks()`; the screen's weekly notes use the same blocks since v117, not Monday to Sunday), the two most recent on page 2 (one, full width, for a 7-day period). Without Daybook Assistant (`plainWeek()`): the theme is the week's flagged Summary lines, then the most telling note of each day (written notes before check-in answers, routine answers left out, a long note cut at a sentence), at most six, and every day with a red reading, a dose given in hospital, pain or a symptom at 7 or more or blood in a stool is an alert row that starts with what made it red ("Temperature 40.1 °C, heart rate 126 and blood pressure 162/104."); a note of a new or worsening symptom is an alert too. The heading says "The most telling note of each day. Every note is in Daybook."
  - **Latest banner:** the most recent note of the last two days, shown only if it is a new or worsening symptom (`plainLatest()`: a "New or worse symptoms" answer, a symptom or pain context, or `WORSE_WORDS`), "Today / 5 Oct, 06:44", title from its first sentence.
- **Summarise with Daybook Assistant** (`#notes-weekly`, relabelled and moved up beside the other report buttons): the report's two weeks of notes, the open questions and the medicine list go to the bridge's new **`report`** kind (`EXPLAIN_SYSTEM.report`; the `weekly` kind stays for phones still on v116): JSON with `latest` (or null), per week a theme and up to six dated entries (`to` for merged days, `alert`), and the questions with spelling and typos tidied but never shortened. `parseReportReply()` keeps it in `state.notesAI` for this range and session; the screen's weeks show the theme and entries; the report uses it, and its heading says "Summarised from the daily notes by Daybook Assistant, an AI helper. Every note is in Daybook." Show the notes instead clears it.
- **Fitted, then printed or saved:** `fittedReport()` fits the report in a hidden frame (`#report-frame`, off screen, `aria-hidden`); if it cannot fit, a toast names the section and nothing is produced. **Preview** opens the fitted pages in a new tab with a "Print or save as PDF" bar (hidden in print), **Print** does the same and starts printing, both as vector pages through the browser's own print. **Save as PDF** and **Send to the team** use `reportPdfBlob()`: html2canvas draws each page at 2.5 times on a canvas made in the frame's own document (a canvas in the app's page has no Fraunces or Inter and drew the words in a fallback at the report's spacing), placed on an A4 jsPDF page, about 0.9 MB; titled "Notes for the team, Mark, 22 Sep to 5 Oct 2026". `savePdf(filename, title, makeBlob)` and `previewPdf(makeBlob)` now take a function that makes the file (the Food diary passes its `buildPdfBlob()`). The send sheet says "Attached: Notes for the team, ..., two pages."
- Tested in `pw/v117.js` (the full fortnight: printed to exactly two pages with neither page overflowing, Fraunces, masthead, the banner with Oramorph put right, the eight tiles in order with each figure and tone, both blood pressure readings named, the strip and Steady, questions oldest first with who and when, dakteparun put right, the long question whole, writing lines on every card, the eight chart titles and pills, two blood pressure lines and sleep bars, the temperature axis reaching 40, the key, two week cards with dated entries and alert rows, Diazipam put right, the footers, no em dashes, the frame fitted; Save as PDF two A4 pages under 3 MB; Send attaching it; twelve questions giving at most seven plus "Plus N more" and still two pages; a quiet fortnight with no banner, every tile None, only the Other points card full width and every chart "No readings"; with the bridge routed: the report kind, the screen's themes, the banner, the tidied question, merged days, the em dash taken out, Show the notes instead), `pw/axe117.js` (axe on the Notes page with the frame and on Settings, light and 320 dark, the name from Settings on the report) and `pw/foodpdf117.js` (the Food diary's Save as PDF and Preview after the refactor). The test context blocks the service worker (`serviceWorkers: 'block'`): requests from the frame otherwise go through `sw.js` and miss Playwright's routes. `pw/v111.js` was retired with the chart list; `v112`, `v114`, `v115` and `v116` were updated.

## Mark 1 Apps Ltd named (v148, 9 October 2026)
The company was registered on 9 October 2026: **Mark 1 Apps Ltd, company number 17510025**, England and Wales. "Mark Brown, trading as Mark 1 Apps" went everywhere: the privacy page and the terms say Daybook is "made and run by Mark 1 Apps Ltd, a company registered in England and Wales (company number 17510025)", the company is the data controller and the operator with administrative access, and every "Mark 1 Apps" in the terms (permission to store, may change or withdraw, not liable, may close a sign-in) is "Mark 1 Apps Ltd"; both pages are dated 9 October 2026. About reads "Made by Mark Brown, first for his own cancer treatment and the people around him, and run by Mark 1 Apps Ltd, ...". The Settings App card reads "Made by Mark 1 Apps Ltd" with "Company number 17510025, registered in England and Wales. Copyright 2026 Mark Brown. All rights reserved."; the opener foot "Made by Mark 1 Apps Ltd" and "Copyright 2026 Mark Brown. All rights reserved."; the contact fallback "Contact: Mark 1 Apps Ltd.". **Copyright stays with Mark** ("Copyright 2026 Mark Brown", and in the terms "The names ... belong to Mark Brown and are used by Mark 1 Apps Ltd") until the assignment is signed: claiming the company owns them before then would be untrue. The LICENSE and README carry the company line; the LICENSE's third-party list now names html2canvas and the fonts in use (Fraunces and Inter). Still to come: the ICO registration number on the privacy page. Tested in `pw/v148.js` (the privacy page's controller and operator wording and date, the terms' company, permission, liability, closing and copyright lines, About, axe on both sheets, the Settings card, the opener foot, no "trading as" left, 390 light and 320 dark); `pw/opener.js` and `v90.js` follow the new lines; `pw/v132.js` no longer has a version number written into it.

## The check-ins in time order, and hours slept from the night (v146 and v147, 9 October 2026)
Mark found the morning check-in "slightly in disarray": it went sleep, pain, mood, then back to the night for symptoms. `CHECKIN_QUESTIONS.morning` now runs the night, then right now, then the day ahead: **How did you sleep?**, **Any new or worse symptoms overnight?**, **Pain right now**, **How is your mood?** (the last score, since it is coloured by everything before it), **What are you looking forward to today?**. Only the order changed: the same keys, so saved check-ins, the card, the review, Notes for the team and the reports read as before, now listed in the new order. The evening followed in v147, the same day, once Mark liked the morning: **Pain right now**, **Worst pain today**, **Sickness today**, **Appetite today**, **Energy today**, (with Crohn's or colitis, the weekly outside-the-gut question), **Any new or worse symptoms today?**, **Anything that has settled since yesterday?**, **How is your mood?**, **One good thing about today**: the body first, then the words, then mood as the last score beside the good thing, as in the morning. Same keys, nothing migrated; the mood mirror to the calendar is unchanged.
- **Hours slept filled in.** A new morning check-in (not an edit) takes "Roughly how many hours" from that morning's sleep entry (`sleepOnDay(day)`: type sleep, `day` the wake-up date, the latest; Apple Health's `{day}_sleep` or one typed in), rounded to the nearest half hour, with a line under the box: "From the sleep logged for last night by Apple Health: 6 h 52 min asleep. Change it if it is wrong." ("by Apple Health" only when it came from there). The Today/Yesterday picker re-reads it for the day chosen; a saved check-in keeps its own hours. Nothing new is stored.
- Tested in `pw/v146.js` (no sleep entry leaving the box empty with no line, 412 minutes from Apple Health giving 7 and the line, axe, the five questions in the new order, the review and the card in that order, 7 saved, an edit keeping 7 after Apple Health changed, Yesterday's typed 440 minutes giving 7.5 and the line without Apple Health, back to Today, 390 light and 320 dark); `pw/v82.js` and `v131.js` follow the new order. The evening is tested in `pw/v147.js` (the nine questions in order, the mood step's own box and axe, the review and the card in that order, what is saved, the mood mirrored to the calendar, the closed line, 390 light and 320 dark); `pw/v129.js` and `v145.js` follow it (the outside-the-gut question is now step 6 of 10).

## Condition packs, and Crohn's and colitis bowels (v145, 7 October 2026)
Mark chose Crohn's and colitis as the first condition after cancer (the research report `reports/IBD and menopause tracking.md`, kept out of git), and said menopause "might link all of them", so conditions are **packs that combine**, not one profile per person. Stages 0 and 1 of the IBD plan are built; the flare plan, steroids and results, and a two-page IBD report are next (`ROADMAP.md` action 9).
- **Packs** (`PACKS`, `packs()`, `packOn(key)`): `profile/main.packs` { cancer, ibd, menopause }, household-wide. A household saved before v145 has no field and is `{ cancer: true }`, so Mark and Shelley see no change. Settings opens with **What Daybook is for** (`#settings-packs`, `renderPacksCard()`, called from `syncSettings()`): a tick box and one-line hint per pack, Menopause greyed out as coming later, more than one allowed, saved as ticked (`saveProfilePatch()`), read-only and viewer see it disabled.
- **What belongs to Cancer treatment:** the chemo alert card wording in `tempWord()` ("Follow your alert card" and the like, otherwise the bands alone: "High, 38.0 or above.") and the UKONS triage limits for bowels. `bowelRules()` is `cancer` when that pack is on (it wins when both are), `ibd` with only Crohn's or colitis, `plain` with neither (the cancer limits without "4 or more in a day is the point to ring the team").
- **Crohn's or colitis settings** (`#settings-ibd`, shown with the pack): which it is (`IBD_KINDS`: Crohn's disease, Ulcerative colitis, IBD unclassified, Not sure yet), a stoma (`STOMA_KINDS`: No, Ileostomy, Colostomy), and the usual number a day on a settled day ("... open your bowels?" or "... empty your bag?", 0 to 30, halves allowed, no placeholder), stored as `profile/main.ibd` { kind, stoma, usual } (`ibdInfo()`, `ibdStoma()`, `ibdUsual()`).
- **The Bowels sheet with the pack** (`openAdd('bowel')`): the Bristol rows as before, then **Any blood?** (`BLOOD_GRADES`: No blood, Streaks on the paper or the outside, Mixed in, Mostly blood; the Mayo bleeding grades in plain words) and **How long could you wait?** (`URGENCY`: Could wait, Had to hurry, Had to go straight away), each one tap and tap again to clear, then the chips Black or tarry, Mucus, **Accident or leak**, Pain or straining, At night (the cancer Blood (red) and Urgent chips are replaced). With a stoma: **What was the output like?** (`STOMA_OUTPUT`: thick, thinner, watery) in place of the chart, no urgency, **Bag leak**, "No stoma output today". Stored beside the old fields, which stay true to them: `bloodGrade` 0 to 3 (`blood` = above 0), `urgency` 0 to 2 or null (`urgent` = straight away or an accident), `accident`; a stoma entry has `stoma: true`, `output`, `types: []`, `bristol: null`. Editing an entry saved with these fields shows them whatever the packs. `bowelFlagWords()` words them ("Blood: mixed in · Had to go straight away · Accident or leak"); the timeline and table say "Stoma output, watery"; `bloodGradeOf()` reads either form.
- **Outside the gut** (`OUTSIDE_GUT`): an evening check-in step with the pack, "Over the last week, have you had any of these?" (joints, eyes, skin, mouth ulcers, and for anything but ulcerative colitis pain, swelling or discharge around the bottom), tick boxes with None of these (`kind: 'ticks'`), asked only when no evening check-in in the six days before answered it (`checkinSteps(slot, day, existing)`: a question's `pack` and `weekly`). Stored as `outside` [keys] ([] for none, null skipped) on the evening check-in; only saved when asked (`saveCheckin()` writes the keys in `answers`), shown on the card and review, and in Notes for the team as an amber "Outside the gut: joints and mouth ulcers" line.
- **Reports** with `ibd` rules: one Bowels line against the usual ("stools about 3.7 a day on the 3 days logged against a usual 2, most 7 on 6 Oct"), blood by days and worst grade, black, had to go straight away, accidents or leaks, nights, watery stoma output, days with none, and the one hard line in the guidance, Truelove and Witts on the patient's side: "6 or more stools with blood on 6 Oct, with temperature 38.1 °C that day" (temperature over 37.8, pulse over 90). Red for that, black stools, mostly blood or three days with none; amber for 3 or more above the usual, any blood, urgency, accidents, nights, two watery days or two with none. Report lines for the team, never a prompt to ring (a triggered "contact your team" is what would make Daybook a medical device). The table: mixed in or more, or black, red; any blood, an accident, straight away, at night or watery, amber. The two-page weeks' alert rows take blood mixed in or more, not streaks.
- Privacy page: the household's records now name bowel movements (with the IBD details), symptoms and which conditions Daybook is set up for, and the weekly question. Rules: no change (`entries` and `profile` are family write with no field validation).
- Tested in `pw/v145.js` (the default household as before: Cancer ticked, the old chips, the alert card, nine check-in steps; Crohn's on and Cancer off, ulcerative colitis, 40 refused and 2 saved, no placeholder, axe; no alert card; the sheet's questions, grades and chips, tap again to clear, axe, a save with every field, the timeline words; six with blood and a temperature giving the red line against the usual with no "ring"; the table's levels; ten check-in steps, the weekly step's options, axe, None of these cleared, the review, what is saved, the Notes line, not asked again within six days, Crohn's adding around the bottom; the stoma label, sheet, axe, a save and the timeline; both packs bringing the cancer limits back; 320 dark). `smoke`, `v82`, `v83`, `v90`, `v91`, `v92`, `v96`, `v109`, `v114`, `v116`, `v117`, `v119`, `v129`, `v131` and `a11y.js` (no violations) pass.

## The subject at the top of the email (v144, 7 October 2026)
Mark: the email "works fantastic" but arrived with no subject. iPhone Mail ignores the `title` a web app passes to `navigator.share()` with files, and nothing a web app does can fill Subject or To there. He chose to keep the PDF attaching itself and the address copied, and to "add our own subject header": the shared text now starts with the subject line, a blank line, then the message (`openSendSheet()`), so the recipient sees what it is even when Subject is empty, and it can be copied into Subject. The hint and the toast say so ("... is copied. Paste it into To, then add a subject if it is empty."). The `mailto:` route on a desktop is unchanged (it fills Subject itself). A picture (the logo) cannot go in these emails: the share sheet and `mailto:` both carry plain text only. Tested in `pw/v112.js` (the shared text headed by the edited subject) and `pw/v143.js`.

## The house style for reports, and the Medicine history rebuilt in it (v143, 7 October 2026)
Mark sent a design for the Medicine history (kept in the scratchpad as `report/meds-design.pdf`; it holds his real medicines, so it never goes in the repo) and asked that **every report he asks for from now on follows this style**. The style, which the two-page reports already mostly share, is the rule for any new report:
- **Masthead:** the title in Fraunces 600 with "Created with **My Medical Daybook**" after it (the design left the credit out; Mark asked for it in v139 and v140, so it stays), a one-line slate description under it, the patient in Fraunces on the right with "Up to" and "Prepared" under it, an ink rule below. Later pages carry the runner (title left, "patient · up to date" right).
- **Tiles first:** four soft panels with a 4px top stripe (`.tile`, `.tile.teal/.amber/.red/.green`, mist with a line stripe for neutral): a small label, a large Fraunces number with its unit in Inter, one plain sentence of facts.
- **Sections:** a Fraunces heading with a slate note beside it (`h2.sec` with `small`), then a table: a mist header row in small uppercase tracked labels over a 1.3px ink rule, thin column dividers, the first column flush left, alternate rows shaded (`tr.alt`), the main value in ink and the detail under it in slate (`.sm`), exceptions as a small mist pill (`.also`). Rows that belong together sit under a full-width group row with a coloured pill and a count (`tr.grp`: teal for regular, amber for when needed). A row with a status carries a pill above its name (`.mpill.tag`: green finished, slate outline stopped, red given in hospital).
- **Bars** for a rate (`.mbar`: a teal fill on a line-coloured track, capped at 100%), with the footer saying what they show.
- **A panel to write on** at the end (`.mnotes`, teal-soft with real ruled `<i>` lines, never a repeating gradient: Mark's design PDF printed its gradient lines as solid black blocks). Only as many lines as the panel holds: ruled lines running past the paper, even clipped, make Chrome print a blank page after the report, so `fitMedsReport()` counts them (`M.noteLines`) after the rows are placed and renders once more.
- **Footer:** what the figures are and "Not medical advice." on the left, on one line, `madeBy(n, total)` on the right.
- Paper only: fixed colours, A4, facts not judgements, no em dashes.

**The Medicine history in it** (`medsReportHtml()`, `MEDS_CSS`, `fitMedsReport()` in `report.js`; `buildMedsReport()` in `scripts.js`):
- Tiles: **Taking now** (teal, "9 medicines", "On the list and still being taken."), **Regular** ("4 scheduled", their short names, `short()` dropping strengths and words like injection or sachets: "Dalteparin, Creon, Senna and Zomorph.", four then "and N more" past five), **When needed** (amber, "5 medicines", from the days each was used in the last 14 complete days: "Four were used on 11 or more of the last 14 days.", one by name, "None used on more than half of the last 14 days.", "None used in the last 14 days."), **No longer taken** ("5 items", "One finished course, three stopped, one given in hospital.").
- **Taking now** (one table): group rows **Regular** (teal, "Taken on a schedule · 4"), **When needed** (amber, "Taken as required · 5") and, for a medicine ticked Given by the hospital that is still on the list, **Hospital** (red). Each row: name and purpose; dose, frequency, how and when, any other dose as an "Also logged as 10 mg (31 times)" pill; Since and Last dose; the doses in bold, "on N days", the bar, "Last 14 days: 28 of 28 due" (scheduled, taken against due) or "Last 14 days: 53 doses on 14 days" (when needed, days used of the window).
- **Finished, stopped and given in hospital** (one table, "No longer on the list, or not taken at home"): finished courses newest first, then stopped, then hospital doses with no medicine on the list, each with its pill. No bars.
- **Medicine changes and notes from the team** panel, at least 42 mm, filling the rest of the last page (a new page when there is less room).
- Items are `{ t: 'sec'|'grp'|'row'|'notes', s, g, r }`; pagination keeps a section heading and a group row with their first row, a page starting part way through a group repeats it as "Regular continued" under "Taking now, continued", and the notes panel is judged by its minimum height.
- Tested in `pw/v143.js` (which replaces `pw/v142.js`: the buttons, axe, as many A4 pages printed as laid out with nothing past a foot, the credit, the four tiles with their tones, counts, names and sentences, the two sections, the group rows and counts, the bars and the footer explaining them on one line, the row labels, the antibiotic's other dose as a pill, shaded rows, the notes panel on the last page, Save as PDF, the email and its Sent from line; forty more medicines giving four pages with continued sections and groups, every page numbered, 390 light and 320 dark).

## Medicine history to print, save or email, and every email signed off (v142, 7 October 2026)
Mark: "a print or save PDF or email medicine history", and the email should say it was sent from Daybook.
- **The buttons:** Medicine history (`openMedHistoryList()`) opens with a two by two grid (`.medhist-actions`): **Send by email** (primary, `#medhist-send`, hidden for read-only and viewer), **Preview** (plum), **Print** (green), **Save as PDF** (warm), the same tints as the other reports. Opening the list sets `state.medsPdf` { from (the first dose ever, filled in by the fit), to (today), filename "Daybook medicine history YYYY-MM-DD.pdf" }.
- **The report** (`buildMedsReport(doses)` in `scripts.js`, `medsReportHtml(M)` and `fitMedsReport(frame, M)` in `report.js`): the report look, as many A4 pages as it needs (unlike the two-page reports). Masthead "Medicine history" with "Created with **My Medical Daybook**" after the title, "Every medicine on the list, with its dose, how often and the doses logged, including finished courses.", the patient (as on Notes), "Up to" and "Prepared"; later pages carry a runner. Sections, each only when it has rows: **Taking now** (teal bar), **Finished courses** (green), **Stopped** (slate), **Given in hospital** (hospital doses with no medicine on the list, grouped by name). A four-column table per section (Medicine with its purpose under it; Dose and how often: the dose, the frequency once (`medFrequency()`, left out when the how-and-when text already starts with it, "Twice a day" never above "Twice a day, morning and evening"), the how-and-when text, and "Also logged as 2 tablets (once)" for a dose that differed; Taken: "Since 2 Oct 2026" and "Last dose 7 Oct, 08:00", a finished course's "5 Oct to 6 Oct 2026" and "Course ended 6 Oct 2026"; Doses logged: "6 doses", "on 2 days", and for a medicine taken now "Last 14 days: 26 of 28 due" (scheduled, complete days only, from its first dose) or "Last 14 days: 9 doses on 5 days" (when needed)). Every dose comes from one query, `loadAllMedDoses()` (`where('type', '==', 'med')`). Footer "Doses as logged in Daybook. Not medical advice." with "Created with My Medical Daybook · Page 1 of 3" (`madeBy(n, total)`, total 2 by default for the two-page reports). **Pagination:** `fitMedsReport()` renders every heading and row (each with `data-i`), finds the first whose bottom passes its page's foot, moves it and everything after it to the next page (a heading never left alone at a page's foot; a section carried over is headed "Taking now, continued"), and renders again until every page fits; a single row taller than a page fails with a toast.
- **Viewer, Print and Save:** `openReportViewer('meds', printNow)` through `REPORT_KINDS` (notes, food, meds: data, fit, blob, html) and `REPORT_NAMES`; the viewer counts the pages ("Exactly as it prints, 3 A4 pages.") and sizes the frame to them. Print on an iPhone goes through the share sheet as before; Save as PDF and Send use `reportPdfBlob()`, titled "Medicine history, Mark, up to 7 Oct 2026". `fitMemo`/`fitQueue` have a `meds` slot keyed on `state.medsPdf`, the name and `state.medicines`.
- **Send by email:** `SEND_KINDS.meds` (subject "My medicine history, up to 7 Oct 2026", "Here is my medicine history from Daybook: every medicine on my list, with its dose, how often I take it and the doses logged, including courses I have finished.", "Attached: Medicine history, up to 7 Oct 2026."). **Every covering email** (Notes for the team, Food diary, Medicine history) now ends, after "Thank you" and the name, with a line "Sent from My Medical Daybook" (`SENT_FROM`), editable like the rest of the message; html2canvas is warmed for every kind when the send sheet opens.
- Tested in `pw/v142.js` (the four buttons, axe on the list, the viewer and the send sheet; one A4 page printed as one with nothing past the foot, the title and credit, the three sections, the antibiotic's dose, how and when, other dose, course end and count, Since and the last 14 days, the footer and Page 1 of 1, no em dashes; Save as PDF one A4 page with its title and file name; the email's subject, words, attached line and the Sent from line, the shared PDF and text; the Food diary email's Sent from line; forty more medicines making four pages, printed as four, nothing past any foot, no heading alone, continued headings, every page numbered of the total, all forty present, 390 light and 320 dark).

## Finished courses and the medicine history (v141, 7 October 2026)
Mark: the Today tile went from 11 doses to 8 when his antibiotic course ended. The count was right (`activeScheduled(day)` leaves out a medicine whose `courseEnd` is before the day), but the antibiotic vanished from the Meds tab with nowhere to see its dose, how often it was taken or when. Nothing about the counting changed; three ways to see a finished medicine were added, all reading the existing dose entries (which carry the dose as it was when logged), with no new fields and no migration.
- **Finished courses** on the Meds tab (`#meds-done-section`, under When needed, hidden when empty, "No longer counted in the day's doses. Every dose taken is kept."): every scheduled medicine whose course ended before the day on screen and within the last 14 days (`finishedCourses(day)`, `FINISHED_SHOW_DAYS`), newest first, as a card (`finishedCard()`, `.is-finished`): name, "1 tablet · 3 times a day" (`medFrequency()`), how and what for, a green **Course complete** pill, "Ended 6 Oct, last dose 6 Oct 22:05", and **See every dose**. On the course's own days it is an ordinary card as before (the day nav shares `state.selectedDay`).
- **Medicine history** (`#meds-history`, beside Manage medicines in a `.btnrow`; shown to read-only and viewer too, since it only reads): `openMedHistoryList()` lists every medicine under Taking now, Finished courses and Stopped (`medStatus()`: Taking now teal, Course complete green, Stopped muted, `.pill-muted`), each row with its dose and frequency ("When needed, at least 4 hours apart, at most 4 a day" for a when-needed one) and the end date; a tap opens that medicine.
- **A medicine's doses** (`openMedHistory(m, fromList)`, titled with its name): the status pill, dose and frequency, how, what for, the course end, then "6 doses logged from 5 Oct to 6 Oct 2026, on 2 days." and one sunken row per day, newest first (`.medhist-day`: the day, "3 doses", and every time with who logged it, the dose in brackets when it differs from the medicine's current dose, "22:05 (2 tablets), Mark"); **All medicines** goes back when opened from the list. `loadMedDoses(m)` fetches every dose with `where('type', '==', 'med')` and `where('medId', '==', id)` (equality only, so no composite index, and the type filter lets the viewer role's rules accept the query); the preview filters `recentEntries`. Hospital doses with no `medId` stay under Given in hospital.
- Manage medicines marks a finished course **Course complete** (green) in place of Scheduled; it stays editable, so a course can be extended by changing its end date.
- Rules: no change. Tested in `pw/v141.js` (the Today tile without the finished course, not among today's scheduled cards, the Finished courses card with its pill, dose, frequency and last dose, axe on the Meds tab, See every dose with its summary, head and two days newest first with a changed dose named, axe on it, the course's last day as an ordinary 3 of 3 card with Finished courses hidden, Medicine history's groups, the antibiotic row and a when-needed row, axe on it, a row opening the doses and All medicines going back, Manage medicines' pill, the two top buttons side by side and tall enough, no sideways scroll, 390 light and 320 dark).

## The "..." on a dose works again, and a dose's time can be changed (v137, 7 October 2026)
Mark logged diazepam from the Medicine tile, needed the time he really took it, and found the "..." on the timeline did nothing.
- **The cause:** since v97 `entryTitle()` returns `null` in the hospital-pill slot for a med entry (and in the amount slot for a drink with no ml), and `entryOptions()` cloned every item (`n.cloneNode`), so it threw before the sheet opened, silently, for every ordinary dose on the timeline and every dose time chip on the Meds tab. It now filters the nulls first. Keep it that way: anything mapping over `entryTitle()` must `filter(Boolean)`.
- **Change the time or note:** the Medicine entry sheet offers **Change the time or note** for an ordinary dose (a hospital dose keeps its own Edit, `openHospitalDose()`). `openDoseEdit(e)` ("Change Diazepam"): Date (no later than today), Time taken, Amount and Note with Tap to speak, filled in from the dose; Save changes updates the same document (`day`, `at`, `dose`, `note`; `medId` kept, so it still counts on its card and in the when-needed gaps) and toasts "Diazepam now at 21:15 on 6 Oct"; a time still to come is refused. Editing a hospital dose now moves its `day` too when the date changes.
- Tested in `pw/v137.js` (a dose logged from the Medicine tile, the dots opening the Medicine sheet, axe on it and on the change sheet, the boxes filled in, 23:59 refused, the dose moved to yesterday 21:15 with a note as one entry with its medId, the toast, gone from today and shown yesterday, the Meds tab chip opening the sheet, a drink with no amount opening too, 320 dark).

## Unsaved words kept, and no blank band after the lock screen (v136, 7 October 2026)
Mark: half way through a note the phone closed; back in Daybook the note was gone and "a floating blank spot" sat on the screen.
- **The blank band.** `keepSheetAboveKeyboard()` lifts the sheet by the keyboard's height (`--kb`). With the keyboard up when the phone locked, iPhone did not always report its going on return, so the sheet stayed lifted over an empty band. Now `--kb` is only ever above 0 while a text box inside the sheet has focus (`typing()`); with nothing focused it is 0 and `--vvh` is the window's height. The sheet is measured again on `visibilitychange` (now, 400 ms and 1 s later), `pageshow`, window `resize`, `orientationchange` and `focusout` inside the sheet, as well as the visual viewport's own events. Keep it that way: never trust the visual viewport alone for the lift.
- **Unsaved words.** Nothing typed into a sheet was kept until Save, so an app closed by the phone lost it. Every textarea in `#sheet-body` is now copied to `localStorage` `daybook.drafts` as it is typed or dictated (Tap to speak's own input events count; only the app's own put-back is skipped, `draftRestoring`), keyed `title|tag|step question|box label` (`draftKey()`; the tag is `data-draft` on the sheet's body: `new` or the entry id in `openAdd()`, `slot:day` in `openCheckin()`), with `reopen` from `data-reopen` (`note` and `question` for a new one, `checkin:slot:day`). `closeSheet()` clears that sheet's drafts first (`clearSheetDrafts()`), so they survive only when the app itself went with the sheet open. When the same box appears again (a `MutationObserver` on the sheet body, so check-in steps drawn later count too), the words go back in with a `.draftnote` line, "Your unsaved words from 12:21 are back." and **Clear them** (puts the box back as it was and forgets the draft). `offerDraft()` runs 1.5 s after a real sign-in's `startData()` (4.2 s into the preview, after its welcome toast): the newest draft with a `reopen` gets "Your unfinished note was kept on this phone." with **Open it**. Drafts older than 7 days or empty are dropped on read; read-only and viewer are never offered. The privacy page says words are kept on the phone until saved or closed, 7 days at most (dated 7 October 2026). Nothing leaves the phone.
- Tested in `pw/v136.js` (a fake visual viewport: the sheet on a 336px keyboard, the box blurred with no resize reported and the page made visible, the sheet back on the bottom with `--kb` 0 and the words still there, never lifted with nothing focused; the draft kept as typed with its key and reopen, a fresh start with the toast and Open it, the words back with the line, axe, Clear them, Save forgetting it and saving the note, a question kept under its own key, Cancel forgetting it, light and 320 dark).

## Questions labelled, not numbered; dietitian questions on the Food diary (v133, 6 October 2026)
Mark: "I don't think questions need numbering at all. Labelling would be good: General, Gastro, Oncology, dietitian, unknown. Then the dietitian question can go to the dietitian report."
- **The label.** `QUESTION_TEAMS` in `scripts.js`: Oncology, Gastro, General, Not sure, Dietitian (that order is the listing order, so the oncologist's come first; "unknown" became "Not sure"). Stored as `entries/{id}.team`; a question saved before v133 has none and `teamOf()` reads it as General (no migration). The question sheet has **Who is it for?** chips (`.chips` of `.preset`, General pressed by default, the saved team when editing) and a hint that changes with the choice ("Goes to the top of Notes for the team..." or "Goes on the Food diary screen and report, for the dietitian..."); the placeholder is "What do you want to ask?". Suggested questions from Daybook Assistant are added as General. `sortQuestions()` orders by team then oldest first; `isDietitianQ()`; `teamLabel()`.
- **No numbers anywhere.** `questionNumbers()` and `questionNumber()` are gone. The label shows instead as a small uppercase pill (`.pill.q-team`, teal on `--teal-light`, `--teal-dark` text, `--teal` in dark): on the Notes for the team cards (`questionCard(q, redraw)`, shared with the Food diary), the two-page report's question cards (`report.js` `questionHtml()`: `.team` above the question, the number circle kept only as the "+" on Other points), the answer sheet head (`.answer-q`), the appointment sheet rows (`.apptq-row`, the pill above the words, `.apptq-n` gone, the hint says "Tap a question as they start answering it"), the copied text ("- Oncology: ... (Mark, 27 Sep)"), and the Questions answered documents ("Oncology: ..."). The appointment recording chips show the first three words of the question ("Scan results when… 3:15", `questionWords()`, the full question in `aria-label`; a mark still stores `n`, now its place in that sheet's list, for the old chips' fallback). A recording's file is "Daybook answer Is the tiredness expected 2026-09-28 14-05.m4a" (the first four words). The text sent to Daybook Assistant for the `report` kind still numbers the questions Q1, Q2 by position, and `parseReportReply()` maps the reply back by position; the bridge prompt is unchanged.
- **Dietitian questions go to the Food diary.** `buildNotesReport()` leaves them out of `questions` and counts them as `dietitian`; the Notes screen and the copied text say "1 question for the dietitian is on the Food diary, with its report"; the two-page Notes report never shows them. `renderFoodDiary()` lists the open ones under **Questions for the dietitian** (`#food-questions-section`, hidden when none, the same cards with Record or write the answer and Mark as answered, `renderFoodDiary` redrawn after) and puts them in `state.foodPdf.questions`; `buildFoodReport()` carries them as `F.questions` and `report.js` draws them on page 1 in the right column between Points to discuss and Notes from the dietitian (`.dq`: the question in bold, "Asked by Mark, 6 Oct" under it, "Plus N more questions in the app" when `shrinkFood()` has to drop the newest, which it does before dropping a point to discuss). The covering email for Send by email adds "My question for you is on the first page." (`SEND_KINDS.food.words(range, n)`).
- Rules: no change (`entries` is family write with no field validation). Tested in `pw/v133.js` (the five chips with General pressed, the two hints, axe on the sheet, five questions saved with their teams, the timeline lines, Edit opening with Dietitian pressed, the Notes cards in team order with no numbers and no dietitian, the dietitian line, the copied text, the two-page report's labels and single "+" circle, the answer sheet head, the appointment rows, the Food diary section and its card, the food report's block above the writing space, the covering email, Mark as answered hiding the section, axe on Notes, the answer sheet and the Food diary, 320 dark); `pw/v64.js`, `v113.js` and `v114.js` follow the labels and the new file name; `pw/v80.js` (the numbering) is retired.

The pill (`.pill-stage`, muted, 14px, Inter) sits under the strapline on the opener (`.opener-stage`, a translucent white on the navy) and under the brand on the four cover screens (`#signin`, `#signup`, `#nohousehold`, `#noconfig`); the App card and About carry one line, "Daybook is in Beta while it is readied for the App Store and Google Play. It works in full; some things may still change, and anything that goes wrong is worth telling Mark about." **Never in the topbar:** a Beta pill there pushed "Daybook: Treatment plan" to "Daybook: Treatment p..." at 390px and "D..." beside Read only at 320px, so it was taken out before shipping. Everything can be typed in instead, so Daybook works in full without it." Mark's own copy is the lifetime purchase (see the cost rule); nobody else needs it unless they want the feed.

## Weight once a day in the Vitals sheet (v127, 5 October 2026)
Mark: weight only needs doing once a day; after the first entry it should stay until the next day. Anyone without a watch or smart scales still types it in; Apple Health fills it in for those who have it. In the Vitals sheet, when the chosen day already has a weight (typed or from Apple Health, `weightOnDay(day)`: the day's latest across `recentEntries` and `dayEntries`), the weight boxes give way to a sunken line (`.weightdone`, `role="group"`): "Today: 80.2 kg (12 st 9 lb), BMI 24.8 at 06:50, by Apple Health. Done for the day." with **Change it**, which brings the empty boxes back with "Saving a weight here replaces 80.2 kg for today." and focus in the box; the save then updates that entry in place (`updateEntry`, value, at and addedBy) rather than adding a second, and the toast's Undo puts the old value back (copied before the update). The Date box re-checks the day (`syncWeightDay()`); editing a reading never shows the line. Never put the number inside the box (v93 and v94: a number sitting in the box read as entered and was never saved). The other readings save as before, the weight untouched. Tested in `pw/v127.js` (the box on a day without a weight, the first save, the line and no box on reopening, no number in any box, axe, other readings saving with the weight untouched, Change it with the empty box, the hint and focus, a change replacing the weight with one entry only, Undo, a past day with and without a weight, an Apple Health weight with its BMI, 320 dark with no sideways scroll); `pw/v94.js` and `v96.js` press Change it before typing a second weight on a day.

## Weight and BMI from Apple Health (v126, 5 October 2026)
Mark ticked Weight and BMI in the Health Auto Export automation. The bridge's `/health` reads the metrics `weight_body_mass` (also `body_mass`, `weight`; kg, with lb, st and g converted) and `body_mass_index` (also `bmi`), keeps the day's latest reading of each, and writes one entry per day, `entries/{day}_weight` { type "weight", value (kg, one decimal), bmi? (one decimal), day, at (the reading's time), addedBy "Apple Health", source "health-auto-export", createdAt (first write only), updatedAt }, by merge, so a re-send updates it and a BMI sent later joins the day's weight. A BMI on a day with no weight is left out (never a weightless entry); readings outside 20 to 300 kg or a BMI outside 10 to 80 are skipped and named in the reply's `skipped`; the reply counts `weight`. It is an ordinary weight entry, so the chart, the table, Notes for the team, the two-page report and Edit all take it as they are; a weight typed by hand the same day is a second point. The app shows the BMI where a weight shows: the timeline line ("BMI 24.8 · by Apple Health"), the Latest weight tile ("today 07:30, BMI 24.8", allowed to wrap, `#vt-weight-sub`) and the weight table. The Settings Apple Health card and the privacy page name weight and BMI. Tested in the scratchpad's `health_weight.mjs` (the real `/health` with Firestore mocked: two days written, the day's latest reading, BMI on it, a re-send keeping createdAt and BMI, pounds converted, a lone BMI left out then added once its day has a weight, an 8 kg reading skipped, steps unaffected) and `pw/v126.js` (the timeline, the tile, the table, 320 dark with no sideways scroll).

## Stretch ticks and timers on an iPhone, and no hospital switch (v124, 5 October 2026)
- **Ticking a Gentle exercise and its Timer did nothing on Mark's iPhone.** Only those rows are wrapped by `swipeToRemove()`, whose touchstart revealed the red Remove button behind the row (`.is-dragging`, opacity 0 to 1) and nudged the row with every pixel of finger wobble; iPhone Safari treats a tap that makes something appear as a hover and drops the click. Now nothing on screen changes until the finger has moved 10px sideways, and more across than down (a drag up the page never opens Remove); a plain tap is left alone for the tick or the Timer. Keep it that way: no visible change on touchstart anywhere. The timer itself opens on "Ready when you are" and counts down from **Start**, as designed. Tested in `pw/v124b.js` with real touch events through the DevTools protocol (a tap with a few pixels of wobble ticks, unticks and opens the Timer with no change to the row during the touch, Start counting down, a real swipe opening Remove and closing it, a vertical drag not opening it); before the fix the same tap changed the row four times. Confirmed on Mark's iPhone the same day: ticks and timers work. The harness's `setup()` takes `hasTouch` and `isMobile`.
- **The At home | In hospital switch went** (Mark: not needed). `#meds-place`, `renderPlace()`, `setPlace()` and `.placecard` are gone; `hospitalHidden()` returns false, so medicines ticked Given by the hospital always show on the Meds lists and in Log a dose; the Given in hospital section still shows only on a day with something given (log a new one from the Medicine tile's Given in hospital button); the "In hospital since" lines left the Summary and the two-page report; the bridge no longer reads `inHospital` or `pauseRemindersInHospital`, so a value still stored from v103 cannot silence reminders (`remind_place.mjs` updated). The fields stay in `profile/main` unread; no migration. Tested in `pw/v124.js` (no switch with an old `inHospital` stored, the hospital medicine on the list and in Log a dose, the section hidden today and shown on the drip's day, no "In hospital since", axe, 320 dark); `pw/v97.js` opens the sheet from the Medicine tile; `pw/v103.js` is retired.

## The rebrand: the report look (v122, 5 October 2026)
Mark: the app "all looks grey and drab"; on the brand board (https://claude.ai/artifact/1fsbTaxNRjB8RgQ47jrFV2) he chose Direction D, "Report look", the look of the two-page reports he had approved ("I loved the last one you showed me"), and asked for everything to be rebranded.
- **Tokens, not one-off colours:** the palette and type above replaced the old values in the token block at the top of `styles.css` (light and dark), so every screen changed at once; the older alias names still resolve. `meta theme-color` follows the page in light and dark, and `manifest.json` has the new background and theme colours.
- **Colour that means something** (the `/* ---------- The report look (v122) ---------- */` block at the end of `styles.css`): the read-only tiles (`.status .tile`, Today's three, the Reports tab's eight, the cycle tile, the Food diary counts as `.nutri-cell`) are no longer transparent cells in a grey well but their own soft panels with a 4px stripe along the top, the report's tile: teal by default, green steady (`is-green`), amber worth a mention (`is-amber`), red to raise (`is-red`), from the same classes the JavaScript already set. Each quick-add button carries a short stripe and its icon in its category colour (`--qa-c`: vitals, pain warm, drink, food, note teal, sleep, bowels, symptom, medicine), from the timeline's own `--cat-*` colours. Primary buttons run from the report teal to a deeper teal.
- **Headings:** every rule set in `--font-head` is Fraunces 600 with -0.01em tracking and no forced capitals (Bebas had only capitals, so headings written in sentence case now show as written); sizes came down a little, as Fraunces is wider (tile values 30px, the day label 25px, check-in titles 20px, sheet titles 25px). The opener's "Daybook" is Fraunces with its blue glow; "My Medical" above it and on the sign-in card is Inter, tracked and in capitals.
- Not changed: the Home Screen icon (still the Care Log heartbeat, action "The icon" in `ROADMAP.md`, Mark's Firefly image), the opener's film and navy, the sign-in watercolour, the reports themselves (already this look).
- Checked: axe-core on every screen and sheet in light and dark through `pw/a11y.js` (zero violations; the only "needs review" is the opener overlay), every new colour pair worked out at 4.5:1 or better before use, and the whole browser suite (see the v122 commit). Screens photographed in `report/rb-*.png` in the scratchpad.

## The Food diary PDF on an iPhone, printing on an iPhone, and faster report buttons (v121, 5 October 2026)
Mark, after v120: the Food diary's Save as PDF said "Could not make the PDF (The operation is insecure.)", while Notes for the team's worked; Print opened the pages but did nothing on his iPhone, on either report; the preview looked crowded; and the report buttons were sometimes slow.
- **"The operation is insecure"** is Safari refusing to read back a canvas it considers tainted. Only the Food diary used SVG `<pattern>` fills (the hatched bars) and SVG pictures as CSS backgrounds (the hatched swatch and chip); both are gone. Hatching is plain white `<line>`s across a bar (`hatchLines()` in `report.js`), the key's swatch an inline SVG of the same lines (sized by `.c.key svg.sw`, or the chart rule stretches it across the page), and a left-out day's chip a plain mist fill with its "?". Keep it that way: no `<pattern>`, no `url(#...)` fills and no `data:image/svg` backgrounds anywhere in a report.
- **The crowded preview** was iPhone text inflation inside the frame; the report sets `-webkit-text-size-adjust: 100%`.
- **Print on an iPhone or iPad** (`IS_IOS`, iPadOS counted by touch on "MacIntel"): Home Screen apps cannot print a frame, so Print, from the header or the viewer, makes the PDF and opens the share sheet, which has Print (`printThroughShare()`: "Making the PDF. Choose Print in the share sheet."). Elsewhere Print still prints the viewer's frame.
- **Faster buttons:** each report has its own hidden frame (`.report-frame`, `report-frame-notes` and `report-frame-food`), the fitted pages are kept (`memoFit()`, keyed on the report's data object, the Daybook Assistant summary and the settings the report reads; fits on one frame are queued, never run at once) and a report screen fits its pages and loads jsPDF and html2canvas in the background a second after it is drawn (`warmReport()`), so Preview, Print, Save and Send normally find them ready.
- Tested in `pw/v121.js` (the background fit, no patterns, pattern fills or SVG backgrounds in the Food diary, the hatching as lines, no text inflation, the swatch a small square, a second Preview fitting nothing again, a new range fitting again; with an iPhone user agent: the Food diary's header Print and viewer Print sharing the PDF without calling the frame's print, Notes for the team's Print the same). `pw/smoke.js` now routes jsPDF and html2canvas, which the warm-up fetches.

## Preview inside Daybook, and PDF failures named (v120, 5 October 2026)
Mark, on his iPhone (Daybook on the Home Screen): the Food diary's Preview opened a blank "about:blank" page, and Save as PDF said only "Saving a PDF needs a connection".
- **Preview and Print open inside Daybook** (`openReportViewer(kind, printNow)`, both reports): the ordinary sheet titled "Food diary" or "Notes for the team", **Print** (green) and **Save as PDF** (warm) above the two fitted pages in a frame (`.viewer-frame`, the report's own HTML as `srcdoc`, scaled to the sheet with a screen-only `zoom` in `<style id="viewer-zoom">`, so printing is full size), "Exactly as it prints, two A4 pages. Pinch to zoom in." Print calls the frame's own `print()`, so only the two pages print; the header Print buttons open the viewer and print once it has loaded; Save as PDF reuses the fitted pages. Escape inside the frame closes the sheet too. The new-tab route (`openReportPage()`) went: on an iPhone Home Screen app `window.open` gives a separate browser that cannot open a `blob:` address made by the app, which is why Mark saw about:blank. Never open a report in a new tab again.
- **Failures say what went wrong:** `errText()` and `pdfErrorText()`: a library that would not load is "Making a PDF needs a connection. Try again when you have signal."; anything else is "Could not make the PDF (the reason). Use Preview, then Print, instead.", in `savePdf()` and on the send sheet (which until now kept saying "Still making the PDF"). `loadScript()` no longer keeps a failed load for good, so a weak signal does not break PDFs until the app is reopened. `reportPdfBlob()` draws at 2.2 times (was 2.5), frees each page's canvas before the next, and names a page the phone could not draw ("the phone ran out of memory drawing page 2").
- The real reason Save as PDF failed on Mark's iPhone is not yet known: the headless Chromium here cannot run Safari, so the next failure will name it. Preview, then Print, then the share button in the print sheet makes a PDF on an iPhone either way.
- Tested in `pw/v120.js` (the Food diary Preview in the sheet with no new tab, the two buttons, the pages scaled to the sheet, no sideways scroll, axe, Print printing the frame, Save as PDF from the viewer making two pages, Escape and focus back on Preview, the header Print opening and printing, Notes for the team the same, a named html2canvas failure, no connection then a retry working without a reload; light and 320 dark). The harness's `previewHtml()` reads the viewer's `srcdoc` now.

## The two-page Food diary, and one set of report rules (v119, 5 October 2026)
Mark's handoff brief for both reports and the approved design (`food-diary-report.html`, kept in the scratchpad as `report/food-design.html`): the ten-page linear Food diary PDF became two A4 pages in the same design system as Notes for the team. Preview, Print, Save as PDF and Send by email all use it. The jsPDF builder (`buildPdfBlob()`, its tables, donuts and `wrapTags()`, `previewPdf()`, `PDF_TEAL`) and the Food diary's PDF blocks are gone; `savePdf(filename, title, makeBlob)` remains for both reports. The Food diary screen itself is unchanged.
- **One food-day calculation for both reports** (`foodDaysFor(entries, from, to, index)`): the logged period runs from the first to the last day with a food or drink entry inside the range; a day's totals are the food app's (`loggedTotals()`, Apple Health or typed in) where it has some, else the app's estimate (`dayMacros()`, Food setting on); a day whose food-app kcal is under half (`FOOD_LEFT_OUT_SHARE`) of what three or more logged entries add up to is **left out** as unreliable, drawn as "?" and explained in Points to discuss (Mark has not yet said which figure he trusts; until he does, it stays out and the report says so); complete days are every logged day but today; usable days are complete days with totals; `u` counts items with no nutrition data (estimate days only). The Notes for the team protein tile now uses the same calculation, so the two reports agree over the same days.
- **One set of weeks:** `reportWeeks()` (seven-day blocks counted back from the period's last day) for both reports, the two most recent shown as Week 1 and Week 2 in both; anything earlier in the period becomes a lead-in entry at the top of Week 1 (Notes: "Earlier in the period: ..." from `plainWeek()` over those days; Food: "Lead-in days. Protein 44 g and 64 g. Mostly ...").
- **`buildFoodReport(n)`** (`state.foodPdf` now carries the range's `entries`): the masthead ("Food diary", "What was eaten and drunk, summarised for the dietitian, nurse or doctor.", the patient as on Notes, "Logged: 18 Sep to 5 Oct 2026 (18 days)", prepared); the teal **banner** (protein target: "Met on 2 of 16 days. Average 72 g a day." with the days it is based on, the left-out days and a day still in progress; no target, no figures and nothing logged each have their own words); four **tiles** (protein average, amber under target with how many days came within 10 g; energy average, neutral, "An estimate. Likely to be low where items had no data."; drinks average, amber with any day at 4 L or more or under 1 L, "including the last three"; fruit and veg days, green at 80% of days); the full-width **protein chart** (a bar a day, values above when 21 days or fewer, the dashed target line, green where met, hatched where items were not counted or the day is in progress, "?" for a left-out day, a call-out when one food gave 40% of a day at 1.5 times the target); the **food-group grid** (seven food groups in teal, High sat fat, High fat and High sugar in amber under "Worth a look", the last 21 days at most, counts from the cells); the **drinks chart** (amber at 4 L, the dashed 4 L line); **Points to discuss** (facts only: protein met and within 10 g, drinks at 4 L and the run at the end, under 1 L, days with nothing eaten, days with items not counted, each left-out day); **Notes from the dietitian** (ruled lines filling the column); page 2: energy, protein/carbs/fat stacked, entries logged and items with no data, a key, and two week cards (pills for protein, kcal and drinks, a theme from the figures, seven protein chips: green met, teal within 10 g, hatched "?" left out, dashed in progress, "None" with nothing logged; one line a day with its protein, the meal names, items not counted and drinks over 4 L, the week's lowest and highest marked; What came up most, by days). The footer carries the estimate caveat. Facts only throughout; no Daybook Assistant on this report yet.
- **`report.js`**: `foodReportHtml(F)`, `foodBars()` (the design's `bars()`, hatch patterns inside each SVG so html2canvas draws them), `stackedMacros()`, `shrinkFood()` (a sentence off the longest weekly line, then What came up most, then the earliest line, then a point to discuss); `fitReport(frame, R, { html, shrink })` serves both. Hatched swatches and chips use SVG data URIs, not CSS gradients, so they print and rasterise alike. `fittedFoodReport()`, `foodReportBlob()`, `openReportPage(print, 'food')`; the PDF title is `R.docTitle`.
- Tested in `pw/v119.js` (estimates on, a 100 g target, a shake day, three 5 litre days and an Apple Health day under half its entries over 30 days: two pages with nothing overflowing, the masthead and logged period, the banner and the left-out day, the tiles and the amber drinks tile, the chart's target line, "?" and the call-out, the grid's counts matching its cells, the drinks chart, the points, the dietitian's space, the four charts and the key, the two weeks with seven chips, the lead-in and What came up most, the footers; Notes for the team over the same 30 days still two pages, with the same two week ranges, its lead-in and its protein tile agreeing with the Food diary's banner; Save as PDF two A4 pages; Send by email attaching it; estimates off; nothing logged), with the shared helpers now in `pw/harness.js`. `pw/foodpdf117.js` was retired; `pw/v118.js` follows the new covering words.

- Tested in `pw/v118.js` (the tab and page title, the feature card and the two tinted cards, the Trends heading, the Food diary's order and three distinct button colours, its send sheet and the shared PDF, the Notes order, axe on Reports, the Food diary, its send sheet and Notes, no sideways scroll, all light and at 320 dark). `pw/v79.js` now expects ten chart cards (Bowels joined in v91).

## Send to the team (since v112)
A primary **Send to the team** button on Notes for the team (`#notes-send`, above Preview and Save as PDF; hidden read-only; since v117 it attaches the two-page report) opens `openSendSheet()`: **Who to?** lists the contacts from Who to contact that have an email (`sendableContacts()`, pick rows with the role and address; with none, "No contacts with an email address yet..." and **Add a contact**, which opens the contacts sheet), then the compose step: a sunken **To** box (name, role, address, **Copy address**), **Subject** ("Notes before our appointment, 22 Sept 2026 to 5 Oct 2026", editable), **Message** (greets the person by first name, or "Hello," for a ward, unit, team or clinic, `greetingFor()`; mentions the charts when any are ticked; signs off with the sender's name; editable), "Attached: Notes for the team, ..., 5 charts.", a hint, **Open in my Mail app** and **Choose someone else**. The PDF (`currentNotesPdf()`, so the ticked charts apply) is built as soon as the contact is chosen, with jsPDF warmed when the sheet opens, so the tap stays a direct gesture. On a phone that can share files: the address is copied (inside the tap) and `navigator.share({ files: [PDF], title: subject, text: message })` hands it to the share sheet and Mail; the toast says the address is copied to paste into To (the share sheet cannot fill in a recipient). Elsewhere: the PDF downloads as "Daybook notes for the team YYYY-MM-DD.pdf" and a `mailto:` opens addressed with the subject and message, to attach it to. Nothing goes through Daybook or the bridge: the email is sent from the person's own account, so the hospital knows the sender and replies come straight back (one-tap sending through the bridge was offered and set aside: a third party would carry the health details, and the domain would need setting up). Tested in `pw/v112.js` (the button's place, only email contacts listed, To, subject, message and attachment line, focus, the shared file, title and text, the copied address, the download and the addressed email, no email contacts, axe on both steps, 320 dark).

## Recording the whole appointment (since v113)
Per-question recording (v64) was too fiddly in a rushed appointment, so Notes for the team has a big warm **Record the appointment** button under the Questions heading (`#notes-record`, `.recbtn.recbtn-appt`, with a one-line hint; hidden for read-only and where the browser cannot record, `canRecord()`). It opens `openAppointmentSheet()` ("Appointment"): consent first ("Has everyone agreed to be recorded?", Yes, start recording / Not now; nothing records before Yes; Mark says the opening words himself), then one recording (`MediaRecorder`, 32 kbps, up to an hour, `APPT_MAX_SECONDS`, the screen kept awake with the Wake Lock API and re-requested when the page is visible again, since a locked iPhone stops recording), with every open question listed by its number (`.apptq-row`, live only while recording). A tap on a question marks where its answer starts, two seconds back so the start is not clipped ("Answer marked at 3:15. Tap again to move it to now."). Stopping, or closing the sheet, saves it: `saveAppointmentRecording()` writes the parts in batches of eight (`PARTS_PER_BATCH`, under Firestore's 10 MB per write) and the record itself last, as `recordings/{id}` { kind "appointment", questionId null, questionIds, marks [{ questionId, n, text, s }], day, at (the start), mime, ext, seconds, bytes, parts, addedBy, createdAt }; each marked question is marked answered with its `recordings` count up by one, so it moves to the Answered cards. Notes lists the range's appointment recordings (`renderAppointmentList()`, `#notes-appts`): "Appointment · 5 Oct 10:30 · 24:10", a chip per marked answer ("Q2 3:15", plays from that point, `playFrom()`), Play, Save or share ("Daybook appointment 2026-10-05 10-30.m4a"), Delete with the inline confirm (`deleteAppointmentRecording()` takes the count off each marked question). A question's own answer sheet lists the appointment recording too (`loadRecordings()` now also queries `questionIds` array-contains, a single-field query) with **Play the answer** from its mark and no Delete there ("Saved, shared or deleted from Notes for the team."). Storage: an hour is about 14 MB, so the free tier's 1 GB holds dozens of appointments. Rules: no change and nothing to publish (`recordings` is family write; parts keep the `pageSizeOk()` guard). The terms say "Record the answer" and "Record the appointment" record people speaking. Tested in `pw/v113.js` with Chromium's fake microphone (the button and hint, consent first with the rows waiting, the numbered questions, recording with the screen kept awake, a mark two seconds back, the saved line, the wake lock let go, the stored marks in time order, the Notes row and chips, the open and answered questions, a chip's player, Q1's count, Q1's answer sheet and Play the answer, the file name, Delete taking the counts off, axe on every step, 320 dark, closing mid-recording still saving); `pw/v64.js` still passes.

## Chart or table (since v79)
Every Trends chart card has a Chart / Table switch beside its heading (`wireChartViews()` builds the `.chart-head`, the `.viewtoggle` and an empty `.chart-table` after the `.chart-wrap`; `setChartView(key, view)`); the table lists the same readings newest first, Date, Time and the value (blood pressure as systolic/diastolic; sleep's time column is "Bed to up" with the stages and awake time under the hours; pain and mood say which check-in or "Reading"; drinks carry the day's total under the first row of each day; a note shows under the value). Values are coloured with the Notes for the team thresholds (`tableLevel()`: temperature, heart rate, blood pressure, oxygen, pain, a night under 5 hours; mood, weight and drinks carry no level). The tables follow the 7/14/30 range and are built by `renderChartTables()` before Chart.js is fetched, so they work with no connection. The choice is remembered per card on the phone (`localStorage` `daybook.trends.tables`, `state.tableViews`). Asked for by Mark after a night of readings minutes apart made the temperature line unreadable. On the Treatment tab in the same version: the calendar card got its gap back under the Sessions card (`.card + .card.cal`) and the six measure chips became a three-column grid (`.segmented-wrap`).

## Treatment plan (since v63; "Chemo plan" until then, and the ids, `data-tab="chemo"`, `view-chemo`, `PAGE_TITLES.chemo` and the document category value `chemo` keep the old name)
The tab is called Treatment, the page "Treatment plan", so it fits radiotherapy, immunotherapy and surgery as well as chemo. Each session day carries a type (`TREATMENTS`: Chemo C, Radiotherapy R, Immunotherapy I, Other O; `treatmentOf(info)`, chemo when the field is missing) chosen with one row of chips in the day sheet (shown once "Treatment session this day" is ticked, `days/{day}.treatment`). The calendar cell shows the letter top right (`.cal-type`) beside the number and the mood mark, the legend spells the letters out, the progress line reads "Next session: radiotherapy on Friday 2 October, in 4 days", and the documents button and category read "Treatment plan".

## Treatment days and after (since v59)
The Treatment tab's third section (between the calendar and the plan documents; it replaced the cheer board): a cycle tile, the carer's view row and the "By day after treatment" chart. Hidden for the viewer role (`body.is-viewer #chemo-cycle-section`).
- Cycle day: `chemoDays()` are the calendar days with `chemo` set (planned or done), whatever the type; `cycleStarts()` picks the ones that start a cycle: every chemo or immunotherapy session, the first day of a run of consecutive radiotherapy days (daily radiotherapy would otherwise restart the count every day and the chart would say nothing), never an Other day. `cycleFor(day)` counts from the most recent start on or before the day, so a session that was never ticked done still starts a cycle. The tile (`renderCycleTile()`) reads "Chemo day" or "Day 4", "after chemo on 25 Sep, next radiotherapy in 3 days" ("after radiotherapy started on 22 Sep" for a run); with sessions but no cycle-starting one it says so.
- Carer's view: a third check-in slot, `entries/{YYYY-MM-DD}_carer` { type "checkin", slot "carer", energy, sickness, appetite, pain, mood (1 to 10 or null), noticed (text), addedBy, day, at }, from the row on the Chemo tab (`#checkin-carer`, today; the flow's Today/Yesterday picker covers a missed evening). Same `openCheckin()`/`saveCheckin()` machinery (`CHECKIN_QUESTIONS.carer`, questions worded "as you see it"); no mirror to `days/{day}`. Kept out of every patient figure by `isPatientCheckin()` (the Notes summary, the Latest pain and mood tiles, the Trends pain and mood charts); it shows on the timeline under its own title (`checkinTitle()`), in Notes for the team as a note ("Shelley, 20:30 (carer's view): What the carer noticed: ...", `REPORT_CARER_KEYS`), and as the dashed line on the cycle chart.
- The chart (`renderCycleChart()`, canvas `chart-cycle`): six measure chips (`CYCLE_MEASURES`: Energy, Sickness, Appetite from the evening check-in; Pain and Mood from both patient check-ins averaged per day; Sleep from the morning score; `state.cycleMeasure`), x is the day in the cycle from 0 (the session) up to the next session, today or day 28 (`CYCLE_MAX_DAY`), y the average across every cycle (`cycleSeries()`), patient solid teal, carer dashed warm (omitted for Sleep or when there is no carer data). Above it one sentence (`cycleSentence()`): "Energy is usually lowest on day 1 and best on day 6. Based on 3 cycles." (worst/easiest for sickness and pain; "much the same through the cycle" when the range is under a point; a single cycle says the pattern gets clearer with each one). Data: `cycleEntries()` fetches every `type == "checkin"` entry with one single-field query (no composite index), keeps those since the first session or 180 days (`CYCLE_LOOKBACK_DAYS`), cached a minute (`state.cycleEntries/cycleFrom/cycleFetched`; `saveCheckin()` clears it). The demo reads `recentEntries`.
- Demo: the fixture has sessions on days -17, -10 and -3 with evening check-ins and carer's views showing the dip on days 1 and 2; an already seeded shared demo gets them once through `topUpDemo()` (runs when `seedDemoIfEmpty()` finds data but no carer entry).
- The AI wording (since v59): the app never names one assistant. The document and Notes buttons read "Send to my AI app" / "Copy for my AI app", the hints say the text goes to that company's servers and to leave out anything you would not want sent, and the paste box says "Paste the explanation here". `CLAUDE_INTRO`/`CLAUDE_FORMAT`/`NOTES_PROMPT` are the prompts and keep their names.

## Recorded answers (since v64)
Each question for the team can carry the clinician's answer, recorded straight into the phone, so nobody has to wait while notes are taken. The answer sheet (`openAnswerSheet(q)`, "Answer", from "Record or write the answer" on the Notes for the team question card and in the timeline's "..." menu for a question) has: the question with its number (`questionNumber(id)`, since v80 its place among the questions that exist, oldest first, answered ones included, so it stays put when one is answered and closes up when one is deleted; the Notes list, the PDF, the copied text and the recording file names all use the same `questionNumbers()` map, where until v80 the list counted only the open ones and "2" opened "Q3"), an ask-first hint (the doctor or nurse should know the phone is recording), a big warm **Record the answer** button that first asks "Has the person speaking agreed to be recorded?" with Yes, start recording / Not now (`.consent`, inline in the sheet; the microphone opens only on Yes), then records (`.recbtn`, MediaRecorder on `getUserMedia({ audio })`, `audio/mp4` on iPhone or `audio/webm` in Chrome, 32 kbps, up to five minutes with a running timer, tap again to stop), the list of recordings (Play, which builds an `<audio controls>` from the stored chunks; Save or share, which hands the file to the share sheet, Files, Mail or the person's AI app, or downloads it, named `Daybook Q3 answer 2026-09-28 14-05.m4a`; Delete), and "Answer in words", a text box with Tap to speak (the phone's own live transcription; a saved recording is not transcribed after the fact). Saving a recording or the words marks the question answered.
- Data: `recordings/{autoId}` { questionId, questionText, day, at, mime, ext, seconds, bytes, parts, addedBy, createdAt } with the audio as base64 in `recordings/{id}/parts/{n}` { n, data }, chunks of 700,000 characters (`REC_PART_CHARS`), written in one batch, so no file storage is needed and the cost rule holds; the same `pageSizeOk()` guard applies in the rules (family write, read-only read). The question entry gains `answerText?`, `answeredAt?` (Timestamp) and `recordings?` (count). The preview keeps recordings in memory (`state.demoRecordings`, with the Blob itself).
- Notes for the team: answered questions that carry an answer (text or a recording) and were answered inside the range appear under the open ones as "Answered" cards (`report.answers`: number, text, who, asked and answered dates, the answer, "1 recording saved in Daybook"), in the PDF (a sub heading and plain text) and in the copied text ("Answered:" block); questions merely marked answered stay in the one muted count. The timeline's question row shows an excerpt of the answer and the recording count.
- Tested with Chromium's fake microphone (`pw/v64.js`: record, list, play, download name, save the words, the Notes card, the copied text, delete).

## Exercise programme (since v69)
The Exercise tab keeps the steps tile, the Log steps sheet and the week chart exactly as they were (steps arrive from Apple Health through the bridge). The four fixed goals became a programme of the person's own exercises:
- `profile/main.programme.items`: [{ id, name, section "exercise"|"physio", kind "reps"|"seconds"|"minutes"|"sets"|"lengths"|"distance"|"do", amount (reps, seconds, minutes, reps per set, lengths, a distance in `dunit`, or 1 for "do"), sets?, pool? (metres, lengths only, 25 by default), dunit? ("km"|"m", distance only), time? (target minutes, lengths and distance), match? ("walk"|"swim"|"run"|"cycle"|"any", since v73), days? ([getDay() numbers], none or empty meaning every day), note?, since? "YYYY-MM-DD" }] (`PROGRAMME_KINDS`, `itemTarget()` prints "20 reps", "1 minute", "3 sets of 20", "30 × 25 m in 30 minutes", "1.5 km", "once"; distances read as people say them, `fmtMetres()`: 750 m, 1.2 km). An account with no `programme` gets `defaultProgrammeItems()`: Press-ups, Sit-ups, Plank, Squats with the old `exerciseGoals` amounts and the old ids, so every day already ticked still counts. `since` is set on anything added in the app: before that day the item is not due (`itemDue()`), so adding an exercise never turns past days into missed ones.
- Ticking from Apple Health (since v73): an item with `match` is ticked by that day's workouts of that activity (`workoutActivity()` reads the workout's name: walk or hike, swim, run or jog, cycle or bike; "any" takes every workout), added together, once they reach the target (`itemHealth()`: minutes for a minutes item, metres for lengths and distance, any matching workout for the rest). The row shows "Apple Health: 35 min · 800 m", or "Apple Health so far: 18 of 20 min" / "400 m of 750 m" short of it. `isItemDone()` is the one test everywhere (rows, `dayStatus()`, the streak): ticked by hand, or reached through Health and not unticked by hand; unticking a Health tick writes `exercise/{day}.off[id] = true` and ticking again clears it. The item sheet's "Tick it from Apple Health" chips (Off, Walk, Swim, Run, Cycle, Any workout) follow the typed name until chosen by hand ("Evening swim" picks Swim).
