- The tab (`renderExercise()`): a **From Apple Health** card when the day has `workouts` (name, "18 min · 1.1 km · 70 kcal · 08:40" or "35 min · 800 m", then "Counts towards Swim" in green when a matching item is due that day, otherwise **Add to programme**, which opens the item sheet pre-filled by `itemFromWorkout()`: a swim as a distance in metres, anything else as minutes, with matching on); **Daily exercises**, one `.goal` row per item due that day (name, note, target; tap to tick, `toggleGoal(day, id)` writes `exercise/{day}.done[id]`), then "Not today: Walk (Mon, Wed, Fri)" for the rest (`.ex-not-today`); **Physio plan**, the same for `section: "physio"`, headed by the plan's details ("From Community physio, given 17 Sept 2026. Twice a day. Stop if pain goes above 5 out of 10.", `profile/main.physio`), hidden when there is neither; **Edit programme**. The streak counts days on which everything due was ticked, passing over rest days with nothing due (`dayStatus()`: done, rest or missed; `exerciseStreak()`).
- Programme sheet (`openProgrammeSheet()`): the items by section with move up and down, Edit and Remove (inline Yes, remove / Keep, never a second sheet), Add exercise, Add physio exercise, **Add from a photo or PDF of the plan** (only with the bridge, `explainAvailable()`), Physio plan details (`openPhysioDetailsSheet()`: who gave it, when, their instructions with Tap to speak), Done. Every change is saved as it is made (`saveProgramme(items, physio?)`, a merge on `profile/main`; in the preview it edits `state.profile`). The item sheet (`openItemSheet(item, isNew, after)`): name, Exercise or Physio, how it is counted (chips, the amount label follows), amount and sets, Every day or the day chips, a note.
- From a photo, PDF or Word file (`importPlanPhotos()`, "Add from a photo or PDF of the plan", since v70 for files, so an emailed plan works too): the files go through the document screen's own `processFiles()` (photos and PDF pages become page images, a `.docx` or `.txt` becomes text), at most `PLAN_MAX_PAGES` (8, the bridge's limit), posted to the bridge as `{ kind: "programme", pages }` or `{ kind: "programme", text }` (`EXPLAIN_SYSTEM.programme` asks for JSON only: from, given, physio, notes, items with name, kind, amount, sets, days, note; counts against the same daily cap, nothing kept). `parsePlanReply()` tolerates a code fence and prose around the JSON. The review sheet (`openPlanReviewSheet()`) lists what was read with a tick box each (all ticked), Daily exercises or Physio plan chips (Physio when the plan says so), the header for checking, and **Add the ticked ones**; a physio plan's header fills `profile/main.physio` for anything missing. Nothing is added without that tap.
- Demo: the fixture programme has the four originals, a Walk on Mon, Wed and Fri (matching walks), a Swim of 30 lengths on yesterday's weekday and two others (matching swims, `since` yesterday) and two physio exercises, past days ticked in full, today part done, an Outdoor Walk workout each day and a 0.8 km Pool Swim yesterday. Tested in `pw/v69.js` and `pw/v73.js` (units, lengths, distance, ticking from Health, unticking staying off) (bridge routed to a canned plan, the photo through the file chooser). Mark has to tick Workouts in the Health Auto Export automation for the card to fill.

## Check-in summary card and editing (since v82)
A submitted check-in is no longer a "Done 08:42, tap to change" row. On Today, a slot with a saved check-in shows `checkinCard()` instead of the row (`#checkin-morning-card` / `#checkin-evening-card`, the old row is hidden; a slot not yet done keeps its row and its Due now, Missed or Later today text). Closed it is one line, "Morning check-in done 08:42, pain 3, mood 6" (`checkinLine()`: submitted time from `createdAt`, falling back to `at`); open (tap) it lists every answer of that slot as saved from `CHECKIN_QUESTIONS` (scores as "6/10", sleep with its hours, a skipped score "Skipped", an empty text box "Nothing added" in muted text, text shown exactly as written, `white-space: pre-wrap`), then "Submitted by Mark at 08:42" and, once changed, "Edited by Shelley at 09:10", then an **Edit** button (not for read-only). The same card sits in the timeline for every check-in, any day (`renderEntry()`; the "..." menu also offers Edit this check-in and Delete); previous days' check-ins therefore open and edit the same way. Which cards are open is held by id (`state.openCheckins`, `setCheckinOpen()` updates every copy on screen), so Today and the timeline stay in step and a live update from the other phone does not fold an open card shut.
- **Editing:** Edit calls `openCheckin(slot, day)`, which, when a check-in already exists, opens straight on the review screen (every answer listed, tap one to change it, scores and text alike, `step = qs.length`), with a **Cancel** link on the review and on every step (closes with nothing saved) and a **Save changes** button. `saveCheckin()` writes the same document id (`{day}_{slot}`, never a second one) with the existing `createdAt` and `at`, keeps the first author in `addedBy` (until v82 an edit overwrote it with the editor's name), and adds `editedBy` (the editor's name) and `editedAt` (server time). A save that changes nothing writes nothing and adds no stamp ("No changes to save"). No fields were renamed or migrated; `editedBy` and `editedAt` are the only additions, and only on edited check-ins. The Chemo tab's carer's view row opens on review the same way once saved.
- **Live on the other phone:** nothing new was needed. The Today and timeline cards are drawn from `state.dayEntries` and `state.recentEntries`, which the existing `onSnapshot` listeners keep current, and `renderCheckins()`/`renderTimeline()` redraw from them. Mark and Shelley can both edit either check-in (`entries` is family write).
- **Rules: no change and nothing to publish.** `firestore.rules` lets family write any field of any `entries` document (`allow write: if isFamily(hid)`, no field validation), so the new `editedBy`/`editedAt` fields are already allowed, and the live rules are correct as they are (Mark confirmed, 2 October 2026). Do not tell Mark to publish the rules for this feature or for adding fields to check-ins. `sw.js` is network-first with no version of its own, so the `?v=82` on `scripts.js` and `styles.css` is the cache bump.
- Tested in `pw/v82.js` (the closed one-line card, the open list, who and when, Edit opening on review, Cancel, clearing a text box to "Nothing added", fixing a score, the edited-by stamp, one document only, a save with no changes making no stamp, the timeline card sharing the open state, a previous day, axe on Today and on the edit screen); `pw/v81.js` was adapted to the new edit flow.

## Gentle exercises (since v83, tick-list picker and swipe-to-remove since v85; the v81 starter stretches, honesty line, check-in step and streak went in v83)
Mark's v2 picker spec. **The library:** `EXERCISE_LIBRARY` in `scripts.js`, 24 exercises in three groups (`EX_GROUPS`: upper, lower, full), each { id, group, name, tags, how, target, starter? }. Every one has reps or a time in `target`. Tags (`EX_TAGS`): arms (arms, shoulders or hands used), legs (hips, legs or ankles), spine (neck or back moved or bent), stand (on your feet, holding a chair or wall); 4-8-8 breathing carries none, so the filter can never hide it. `starter: true` marks three per group a poorly person could begin with (upper: neck rotation, shoulder rolls, overhead reach and breathe; lower: ankle pumps, ankle circles, hip marching; full: sit to stand, buttock squeezes, 4-8-8 breathing); they sort first in their group with a neutral "Gentle start" pill, and the picker's hint says they are a place to begin if the care team agrees. Sources, on the Settings App card under "Exercises based on": NHS Live Well sitting, flexibility and strength exercises; Memorial Sloan Kettering Cancer Center, Chair Exercises; plus "Body area tags are a guide only. Your care team has the final say."
- **The picker** (`openExercisePicker()`, a sheet from **Choose exercises** on the Exercise tab, `#ex-pick`; rebuilt in v85 as a plain tick list after the v83 drop-down plus radio list "kept bouncing backwards and forwards", since every tap re-rendered and scrolled): in order, the warning box (`exWarningBox()`, `EX_WARNING`: always shown, no collapse or dismiss, `role="region"`, red border, light red fill, dark red text, the warning icon `#i-warning`; tokens `--warn-bg/-ink/-border`, 8.9:1 light and 9.8:1 dark), then **"I've been told to avoid:"** with four tick boxes (Arms, Legs, Spine, Standing, each label a coloured pill) and "Hidden exercises: N" under them (`role="status"`), the hint about Gentle start, then **all 24 exercises at once** under three headings (`.exgroup-title`: Upper body, Lower body, Full body), each a `label.exrow` wrapping its own tick box (`input#plan-{id}`) beside the name, how-to, reps or time and tag pills; the starters sort first in their group with the Gentle start pill. The list is built once: ticking never re-renders or scrolls (`setInPlan()` saves and redraws the tab behind the sheet only), and the avoid filter hides rows in place (`applyFilter()` sets `hidden` on each row and shows a per-group "No exercises left in this group with your current filters." line, `.exempty`). No group drop-down, no how-to view and no Done-logging: the tick is the whole interaction, then the "Not in the list?" box, then Done (closes).
- **Add to plan (since v84, the tick itself since v85):** a ticked exercise sits in **My stretches** on the Exercise tab: `setInPlan(ex, on)` adds `libToItem(ex)` to the programme (`section: "stretch"`, `lib: ex.id`, `targetText` keeping the library's own wording such as "Hold 5 to 10 seconds, 5 times", `kind` seconds or minutes when the target is purely a time so the Timer applies, otherwise reps with the first number, `note` the how-to, `tags`, `since` today) or removes the item with that `lib`; `inPlan(ex)` reads it back (so removing a row from My stretches clears its tick next time the picker opens). `itemTarget()` prefers `targetText`. My stretches is therefore formed from the ticks, plus anything typed into the picker's **Not in the list?** box (`ownBox()`: a name, then the usual item sheet pre-filled under Stretch, which returns to the picker), and it stays hidden while empty. Editing a library item in the item sheet keeps its `lib` and `tags`.
- **The tab since v86:** the Choose exercises button comes first (above the heading, as Mark asked), then the **Gentle exercises** heading with the chosen ones listed under it as programme rows (`#ex-stretch`, `section: "stretch"`; "My stretches" as a name is gone from every screen, the programme sheet and the toasts, though the section value and the ids keep the word), "Nothing chosen yet. Tap Choose exercises to pick from twenty-four gentle ones." (`#ex-stretch-empty`) while the list is empty, and nothing else: since v87 the old picker log rows are not listed on the tab (they stay on Today's timeline, deletable there) and `doneTodayCount()` counts programme ticks only, so the tile and the list agree. The heading's gap under the button is `#ex-stretch-section .section-title { margin-top: var(--s-6) }`, as the heading is the first child of its own wrapper (the same first-child trap as the Meds documents section). The hint: "The ones you chose, plus any of your own. Tap to tick one off, swipe a row left to remove it. All optional."
- **Side cards (since v86):** every programme row is one exercise card beside one side card of the same height, background, radius and shadow: `.goal, .goal-side` share the raised declaration, `.goal-wrap` is a two-column grid with `align-items: stretch` (these cards never expand; switch to `start` if one ever does), the reps or time is plain centred mono text in the side card (no grey pill any more) and a timed item's Timer button sits under it inside the same card, centred and stacked. Mark's ask on 1 October after the grey pills looked "rubbish" beside the white cards.
- **Duplicate entries (1 October, v86):** two "Chest stretch (seated)" rows at 10:22 by Mark. Cause: a second tap on the v84 picker's Done button. Its handler disabled the button while it awaited `logExercise()`, but `addEntry()` does not await the Firestore write (it fires `setDoc` and returns the id at once), so the await resolved in a millisecond and the redraw put back an enabled button; the "Done 1 time today" line also appeared above the button after the first tap and pushed it under the finger. Not a double-bound handler (the button was rebuilt with one listener each redraw), not touchend plus click (no touch handlers), not a sync echo (a local write and its confirmation share one document id; the two rows were two documents). Fixes: (1) `exerciseLogFor()` folds a second identical entry (same `exId`, same `addedBy`) within `DUP_WINDOW_MS` (5 s) and notes the stray ids; `cleanDuplicateExercises()`, run from `renderExercise()` on a family phone (never read-only or viewer), deletes each stray once (`duplicatesCleaned`), so the stray document itself goes the first time the tab is opened on v86; (2) `addEntry()` returns the first id and writes nothing for an exercise entry matching the last one by the same person within 5 s (`lastExerciseWrite`); (3) `toggleGoal()` ignores a second tap on the same item within 600 ms (`goalTapAt`), since a double tap would otherwise tick then untick. The same exercise twenty minutes apart is two real entries and stays. `window.daybookPreview` (`state`, `addEntry`, `renderExercise`, `exerciseLogFor`) is set in `startDemoData()` only, so the harness can drive the preview; it can never reach Firestore.
- **Swipe to remove (since v85):** every My stretches row (family only) is wrapped by `swipeToRemove(row, item)` in a `.swipe` shell with a red **Remove** button behind it (`.swipe-remove`, `--danger` with `--danger-ink`, 7.3:1 light and 6.2:1 dark). Touch handlers on the `.goal-wrap` follow the finger up to 104px left; a release past 48px opens it to 96px (`.is-open`, the button shown and tappable), shorter springs back; the button is `opacity: 0` and `pointer-events: none` until dragged (`.is-dragging`) or open, so it can never be hit by accident. Remove saves the programme without that item and toasts "Removed ..." with **Undo**, which puts the same item back. Edit programme's Remove and unticking in the picker are the other two ways, so a keyboard or mouse is never stuck. The section hint says to swipe.
- **Aligned rows (since v84):** every programme row is a `.goal-wrap` of the tick button and a `.goal-side` column of one fixed width (104px): the reps or time as a `.pill-target` (wrapping inside the column) and, for anything counted in seconds or minutes, a round **Timer** pill under it, so reps rows and timed rows line up. The target is also in the button for screen readers (`.sr-only`).
- **Tags as pills** (`tagPills()`, `.extag.is-arms/-legs/-spine/-stand`): a fill and an ink per tag (`--tag-*` and `--tag-*-ink`, 6.9:1 or better in both colour schemes), always with the word, never colour alone.
- **The avoid filter:** `exAvoid()` is a Set kept in `localStorage` `daybook.exercise.avoid` on that phone only, never in Firestore; `exHidden(ex)` hides an exercise carrying any ticked tag from the list and from the chosen view; an emptied group reads "No exercises left in this group with your current filters."
- **The log (v83 and v84 only):** until v85 the picker's Done wrote an `entries` document { type "exercise", exId, note (the name), group, tags, day, at, addedBy, createdAt }. Nothing writes them any more (a stretch is ticked off in My stretches, `exercise/{day}.done`, like any programme item), but any already logged still render: on Today's timeline (`entryTitle()`/`entrySub()`, `--cat-exercise`, deletable from the "..." menu), in `#ex-logged` under the Choose button (`exerciseLogFor(day)`, hidden when the day has none) and in `doneTodayCount()`; the bridge's nudge still accepts one as "something logged". The viewer role cannot read `exercise` entries (its allowed types are unchanged).
- **No streak** (Mark: not motivational). The Streak tile became **Today: N exercises done** (`doneTodayCount()`: programme ticks due that day plus picker logs); `exerciseStreak()` and `dayStatus()` are gone, `allGoalsDone()` only feeds the "All done for today" toast.
- **Programme stretches** (`section: "stretch"`) are still supported for anyone who added some (Mark may have, on 1 October): shown as "My stretches" only when there are any, with their timers; "Add a stretch" stays in the programme sheet. The v81 starter set, its notice, `stretchHonesty()` and the check-in tick list are gone.
- **The check-in is a wellness check, not an exercise check:** no stretches step. The morning review screen ends with one signpost line (`.wiz-signpost`): "Gentle exercises and stretches are on the Exercise tab, for when you are up and ready. Optional."
- **The 10:00 nudge** (household-wide, off until switched on): More > Settings > Reminders, "Gentle nudge at 10:00 if nothing is logged on the Exercise tab" (`#settings-stretch-nudge`, `profile/main.stretchNudge`, synced by `syncSettings()`). The bridge (`runHouseholdReminders()`, `STRETCH_NUDGE_AT` 10:00 London, the usual 20 minute window, once a day via `reminderLog` key `stretch|day`) reads `profile/main`, and when the switch is on checks for an `entries` document of type exercise that day (`fs.query`, two equality filters, no composite index needed) and for a ticked stretch in `exercise/{day}.done` (`stretchTickedOn()`, honouring an item's `days`); if neither, every phone with reminders on gets "Your gentle exercises are there if you feel up to them. No pressure." (`appUrl('exercise')`; no medicine is named, so a private phone gets the same body). `stretchNudgeDue()` is the pure decision, unit-tested in the scratchpad (`nudge_test.mjs`, twelve cases) and exported on `_test`. Nothing is guilt-shaped: one line, once, only if asked for.
- **Rules: no change and nothing to publish.** `entries` is family write with no field validation, so a new `type` value is already allowed, `profile/main.stretchNudge` is a merge on a document family may write, and the avoid ticks never reach Firestore. The live rules are correct as they are (Mark, 2 October 2026). Do not tell Mark to publish the rules for this feature. The cache bump is `?v=87` on `scripts.js` and `styles.css` (`sw.js` is network-first with no version of its own).
- **Timers** (since v81, unchanged): any programme item counted in seconds or minutes gets a **Timer** button beside its row (`.goal-wrap`); `openTimerSheet(item, day)`: three seconds to get ready, the countdown from an end time (a busy phone still shows the right time), Pause, Resume, Start again, beeps and vibration where the phone allows, the ring and clock turning green, **Tick it off**; the screen is kept awake with the Wake Lock API; an iPhone's silent switch mutes the beep, so the green screen is the dependable signal, and the sheet says so.
- Tested in `pw/v86.js` (the addEntry safety net, a different exercise still writing, a stray duplicate folded on screen then deleted with the real one kept, the same exercise twenty minutes later kept, the button above the heading, the hint, no My stretches wording, every side card matching the exercise card's height, background, radius and shadow, no grey pill, Plank's time and Timer in one centred card, a double tap toggling once, a tap a second later toggling back, axe) and `pw/v85.js` (the button, the sheet with the warning first and no drop-down, three headings, 24 label rows each with a tick box, how-to, target and a pill, starters first, a tick not scrolling or rebuilding the list, the outlined ticked row, unticking, Arms hiding 11 in place, Arms plus Spine showing Upper body's empty line only, Full body keeping the untagged breathing, the free-form box through the item sheet and back, My stretches with the library wording and one side-column width, the Remove button hidden and unreachable until swiped, a short swipe springing back, a long swipe opening to 96px, Remove with Undo, the cleared tick after a removal, a time target getting a Timer pill, the programme sheet, axe on the picker and the tab) and `pw/v83.js` (trimmed to the tile, the morning review with five answers and the signpost, the nudge switch, the About text, axe on Settings); `pw/v81.js` covers the timers and "My stretches" only. The v83 and v84 picker tests were retired with the drop-down.

## Keyboard and Vitals boxes (v93 and v94, 5 October 2026)
Two things Mark hit from a hospital bed.
- **The keyboard covered the check-in text box.** iPhone Safari lays the on-screen keyboard over the page instead of shrinking it, so the bottom-anchored sheet sat behind it and answers were typed blind. `keepSheetAboveKeyboard()` (next to the Escape handler) follows `window.visualViewport` (resize and scroll) and sets `--kb` (the keyboard's height: `innerHeight - vv.height - vv.offsetTop`) and `--vvh` (the visible height) on `#sheet`; `.sheet-panel` sits at `bottom: var(--kb, 0px)` with `max-height: min(92vh, calc(var(--vvh, 100vh) - 16px))`, and a focused text field inside the sheet is scrolled to its middle 320 ms after focus, once the keyboard has risen. The viewport meta carries `interactive-widget=resizes-content`, so Chrome on Android shrinks the page instead and `--kb` stays 0 there. Applies to every sheet, not just the check-ins.
- **Daily weights not saving.** Mark weighed himself daily but Trends held two readings in a week while oxygen had many. The save path is correct (tested three ways in `pw/weight.js`); the likely cause was the Vitals sheet showing the last weight inside the weight box as a grey placeholder ("78.6"), which reads as a value already entered, so Save went with the box empty. Every Vitals box is now empty when the sheet opens (no placeholder numbers at all, the grey "0"s in heart rate, blood pressure and oxygen went too), and the last temperature and weight show as a line under their boxes ("Last: 80.6 kg, 3 Oct 09:11."). Keep it that way: never put a plausible reading in a placeholder.
- **v94, the real cause found.** Mark checked the database itself: two weights, though he had typed one daily and seen "Vitals saved". The oxygen from the same sheets was all there, so the weight value was being lost between the box and the save. The boxes were `type="number"`, and when a number box cannot read what is in it (iPhone keypads and number boxes disagree over decimal points and stray characters) the browser reports it as empty, so the save went ahead without the weight and said nothing. Every Vitals box is now a text box with a number keypad (`inputmode` decimal or numeric, so the keypad is unchanged) read by `readNum(el)`: a comma or a full stop, spaces and a trailing unit are accepted ("80,6", "80.6 kg"); `{ empty }` when nothing was typed, `value: null` when something was typed that is not a number. `getData` checks every box against a believable range (temperature 30 to 45, heart rate 20 to 250, systolic 50 to 260, diastolic 20 to 160, oxygen 50 to 100, weight 20 to 300) and anything typed that cannot be read stops the whole save with a message naming the box ("Could not read the weight "eighty". Type just the number, like 80.6.") and focus on it; blood pressure needs both numbers. Nothing from the sheet is saved until every box reads. The saved message lists what was written (`savedVitalsText()`: "Vitals saved for 1 Oct: oxygen 96%, weight 80.6 kg"), so a missing reading shows at once. The sheet also gained a **Date** box beside Time (`dateIn`, no later than today, defaulting to the day Today shows), since it had always saved under whichever day Today had selected, which is today when opened from Trends' Log vitals; `openAdd()`'s save uses `dateIn` when a sheet sets one. The temperature box carries `class="input"` for the standard box style now it is a text box. Keep it that way: never let a typed reading be dropped without a message. Tested in `pw/v94.js` (the Date box from Trends and from Today, a past day filed correctly and shown in Trends, a future date refused, a comma, a unit, a word refused with nothing saved, the corrected save listing both readings, a weight under 20 refused, one blood pressure number refused, the keypads, no placeholder numbers, axe, no sideways scroll).
- Tested in `pw/v93.js` (a fake visual viewport with a 336px keyboard: the sheet's bottom on the keyboard's top, the check-in text box fully visible, typing landing in it, the sheet returning when the keyboard goes; no number in any Vitals placeholder; the two "Last:" lines).

## Go to a day, and weight in stones (v95 and v96, 5 October 2026)
- **Go to a day.** The date between the arrows on Today, Meds and Exercise (`#day-label`, `#meds-day-label`, `#ex-label`, `aria-haspopup="dialog"`, spoken as "Choose a day") opens `openDayPicker(current, onPick, marked)`: a month calendar in the sheet built from the Steps sheet's `.cal` styles (`.daypicker`), opening on the month of the day on screen, previous and next month (next disabled at this month; the title returns to this month), days after today disabled, today ringed (`.is-today`), the day on screen filled (`.is-selected`, `aria-current="date"`), days with anything logged tinted green (`.is-logged`, the key says "Something logged"), **Go to today** and Cancel. A tap closes the sheet and moves the tab to that day (Today and Meds share `state.selectedDay` through `refreshDay()`; Exercise sets `state.exerciseDay`). The arrows still step a day at a time; until v95 tapping the date jumped to today, which Go to today now does. Logged days: `loggedDaysIn(month)` uses `state.recentEntries`, and for a month older than the live window one range query on `day` (two inequality filters on one field, no composite index), cached per month for the session in `loggedMonths`; the Exercise picker marks days with steps or a tick from `state.exercise`. Asked for by Mark, who found stepping back day by day slow.
- **Weight in stones (v96; v95's Units card in Settings went the same day).** Mark: "everything should be in metric", but people here talk in stones, and a choice for everything overcomplicated it. Readings are stored and shown in kg and °C everywhere (the timeline, the tiles, Notes for the team, the PDF, the copied text). One choice per phone, kg or stones and pounds (`localStorage` `daybook.weightUnit`, `weightPref()`/`setWeightPref()`), set from a small **kg | st and lb** switch (`weightSwitch()`, `.unitswitch`, `aria-pressed`, the full words as `aria-label`) in two places that share it: under the Weight chart's heading (`wireWeightSwitch()`, "Show in"; the chart is plotted in pounds with stones-and-pounds ticks, the tooltip and the weight table give stones with kg under them; the Latest tile stays kg) and beside the weight box in Vitals and the weight edit sheet (`weightEntry(initialKg)`, "Weight in": one box "Weight (kg)", or two, "Weight: stones" and "and pounds", 14 pounds or more refused; switching carries a typed weight across; a line under the boxes gives the other unit, "= 80.7 kg, which is what is saved" or "= 12 st 10 lb"; the "Last:" line gives both). The saved message reads "weight 80.7 kg (12 st 10 lb)" when stones are chosen. `renderVitals()` re-syncs the chart's switch, so a change made in the sheet shows there. Temperature has no choice: the box is °C, but a number from 90 to 110 can only be Fahrenheit, so `readTemp()` converts it and the hint says "99.5 °F is 37.5 °C. Raised. Keep an eye on it"; anything else outside 30 to 45 is refused. `fmtKg()`, `fmtStLb()`, `fmtTemp()` format; `readNum()` strips "lb", "st" and "°F".
- Tested in `pw/v96.js` (the picker's spoken name, this month with today and the logged days, future days disabled, a day five days back, the month of the day on screen, the previous month, Go to today, the arrows still stepping, Meds and Exercise, axe; no Units card; the Weight chart's switch starting on kg, the table in stones with kg under, remembered, the Latest tile still kg; the Vitals sheet opening on stones from the shared choice, temperature in °C, "= 80.7 kg, which is what is saved", switching to kg carrying 80.7 across and showing 12 st 10 lb, 99.5 read as Fahrenheit and judged raised, 80.7 kg and 37.5 °C stored, the saved message metric with stones alongside, the timeline in kg and °C, 15 pounds refused, 60 degrees refused, axe on the sheet and Trends, Notes for the team in kg and °C, 320px and dark with the switch fitting). `pw/v95.js` was retired with the Units card.

## Given in hospital, and medicines from a photo (v97, 4 October 2026)
Mark, back in hospital, was given a bag of sodium chloride 0.9% on a drip and wanted it in Daybook, marked as given by the hospital, and asked whether a photo could fill in the details.
- **Logging it:** the Medicine tile's Log a dose sheet has a **Given in hospital** button (with "For a drip, an injection or anything the hospital gave that is not on your list."), and the Meds tab a **Given in hospital** section (`#meds-hospital`, `renderHospitalDoses()`, the day on screen, "Nothing logged as given in hospital today." when empty) with **Log something given in hospital** (`#meds-hospital-add`, hidden for read-only and viewer). Both open `openHospitalDose(edit, prefill)`: What was given, Amount (placeholder "e.g. 1000 ml", never a reading), How it was given (chips Drip (IV), Injection, By mouth, Other; `ROUTE_WORDS` also knows skin and inhaled for a photo), Date (no later than today) and Time, Note with Tap to speak, and, for a new one, "Also add it to my medicines list, to keep taking at home", which opens Add medicine pre-filled after saving. Stored as an ordinary `entries` document { type "med", medId null, medName, dose, route, hospital true, note }, so it never counts towards a listed medicine's doses, the PRN limits or the bridge's reminders (all of those match on `medId`).
- **Shown:** the timeline title carries a teal **Given in hospital** pill (`.pill-hosp`) and the bar turns teal (`.entry.type-med.is-hospital`); the sub line adds the route ("1000 ml · Drip (IV) · Over 4 hours"); the "..." menu offers Edit (reopens `openHospitalDose()` pre-filled, Save changes). Notes for the team gets one teal Context line, oldest first: "Given in hospital: Sodium chloride 0.9% 1000 ml drip (iv) on 3 Oct; Ondansetron 4 mg injection on 4 Oct."
- **Read it from a photo** (only with the bridge, `explainAvailable()`): offered at the top of the Given in hospital sheet and of Add medicine. `readMedicinePhoto(onRead, onCancel)` takes one image through `processFiles()`, shows "Reading the label, about half a minute" with Cancel, posts `{ kind: "medicine", pages }` to `/explain` (the same daily cap; nothing kept), and `parseMedReply()` reads the JSON (code fence and prose tolerated) into { name, amount, how, purpose, route, whenNeeded, perDay, maxPerDay, minGapHours }, then reopens the sheet filled in for checking. Nothing is saved without Save. No name read: "Could not read a medicine name in that photo. Fill it in by hand." `openEditMed(m, prefill)` takes the same shape (whenNeeded makes it a when-needed medicine with its gap and maximum). The bridge's `EXPLAIN_SYSTEM.medicine` asks for JSON only and never to guess anything not printed (a hospital bag has no directions, so "how" is null). Needs the `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` secret like every Explain feature; without it the bridge says "not switched on yet".
- **Given by the hospital, on the medicines list (v101).** Mark's first real photo put the saline bag on his list as an ordinary medicine; he wanted it marked as hospital monitored. The medicine editor has a **Given by the hospital (hospital monitored)** tick box under What it is for, with a hint, and once ticked **How it is given** chips (`ROUTE_WORDS`, Drip by default) while the reminder times and Ask after every meal are hidden (`remindBits`, the scheduled fields after the first two). Stored as `medicines/{id}.hospital` (bool) and `route` (null when unticked); a ticked medicine saves with no `times` and `withMeals: false`. A photo whose reply says `route: "drip"` arrives ticked (`openEditMed()` prefill). The card carries the teal pill under the name (`.pill-hosp.med-hosp`), the route after the dose ("1000 ml · Drip (IV)") and `.is-hospital`; `logMed()` adds `hospital: true` and the route to the dose, so it shows with the pill on the timeline, in the Meds tab's Given in hospital list and in the Notes line, and still counts on its card (it keeps its `medId`; `openHospitalDose()` now keeps an edited dose's `medId` too). `promptMealMeds()` skips it, and the bridge's `runHouseholdReminders()` skips any medicine with `hospital: true`. Rules: no change (`medicines` is family write with no field validation). Tested in `pw/v101.js` (the box and its hint, the chips and hidden reminders, what is saved, the card, Log now marking the dose, the list, the timeline, editing the dose keeping the count, Edit medicine opening ticked, unticking, a drip photo arriving ticked, axe, 320px dark).
- **Counted, not scheduled (v102).** A second saline bag arrived the same day and the v101 card, still "1 of 1, Done for today" from the medicine's doses per day, could not show it. A hospital medicine now has no doses per day (the field is hidden once the box is ticked, saved as `perDay: null`): its card shows no dots and, once anything is logged, "2 given today" (or "this day") with every dose's time chip, never Done for today; the Log a dose sheet's pill reads "2 given today"; the Today doses tile leaves hospital medicines out (never owed); and the Notes line groups the same medicine on the same day, "Sodium chloride 0.9% 1000 ml drip (iv) on 4 Oct (2 times)". Tested in `pw/v102.js` (the hidden field, what is saved, no dots before a bag, one, two and three bags, the tile unchanged, the dose sheet pill, a third from the Medicine tile, the grouped Notes line, axe); `pw/v101.js` was updated to the new wording.
- **At home or in hospital (v103).** Mark: hospital medicines should not show unless he is in hospital, with a switch. The Meds tab opens with an **At home | In hospital** switch (`#meds-place`, a `.placecard` with a two-button `.segmented`, `renderPlace()`), saved household-wide in `profile/main` as `inHospital` (bool) and `inHospitalSince` ("YYYY-MM-DD", set on the switch, cleared at home), so both phones agree (`watchProfile()` now redraws the Meds tab). At home, `hospitalHidden(m)` keeps medicines ticked Given by the hospital off the Meds lists and the Log a dose sheet (they stay in Manage medicines), and the Given in hospital section (`#meds-hospital-section`) is hidden on a day with nothing in it; a day with something given still shows its list. The Medicine tile's Given in hospital button stays, for a clinic visit. In hospital: "In hospital since 4 Oct.", a **Pause medicine reminders while in hospital** tick (`pauseRemindersInHospital`, on unless unticked) and a hint that the ward usually gives the medicines; Notes for the team gets a teal "In hospital since 4 Oct." line. The bridge reads `profile/main` once per run (`runHouseholdReminders()`): when in hospital with the pause on it sends no "is due", "still to take" or exercise nudge, but a "Remind me in 15 minutes" one-off still goes (tested in the scratchpad, `remind_place.mjs`, the real reminder run with every outside call mocked). The viewer role cannot read the profile, so it sees everything and no switch; read-only sees the switch disabled. Rules: no change (`profile/main` is family write). Tested in `pw/v103.js` (the switch and its empty box at home, the hidden section, a hospital medicine hidden at home but kept in Manage, the dose sheet, In hospital with since and the tick, the section and the medicine showing, unticking saved, the Notes line, back home, a past day with a drip still showing, axe, 320px and dark); `pw/v97.js`, `v101.js` and `v102.js` now switch to In hospital first.
- **Amber on the status strip (v103).** The Trends Latest tile with a raised temperature put `--warning` (#9A5B00) on the sunken strip at 4.42:1, under AA; a new `--warning-ink` (#8F5400 light, 4.98:1; the dark amber unchanged in dark) is used for that tile's label and sub line. Found by `pw/v96.js`, which saves a 37.5 reading.
- Also fixed: the reminder time box in the medicine editor had no label (axe, since v58); it reads "Reminder time to add". The Trends range buttons now bind only to `.seg[data-range]`.
- Rules: no change and nothing to publish (`entries` is family write with no field validation). Demo: the fixture has the saline drip yesterday at 10:30. Tested in `pw/v97.js` (the button and hint in Log a dose, the empty sheet, the four chips, no reading in a placeholder, save refused without a name, the timeline pill, sub line and teal bar, Dalteparin's count unchanged, the Meds list today, yesterday and an empty day, Edit pre-filled and saved once, the Notes line, a routed bridge: kind medicine with one page and the ID token, the sheet filled from the photo, Also add opening Add medicine pre-filled, a when-needed pharmacy label filling Add medicine, axe on the sheets and the Meds tab, 320px and dark).

## The three by three quick-add grid (v91 and v92, 4 October 2026)
Mark's ask: somewhere to log a bowel movement and describe it, with the questions from the medical sources, and a think about where it fits. The grid grew to a third row rather than scrolling sideways (nothing on Today is hidden; a tired person on a 320px phone would never find a tile that slides off screen): Vitals, Pain, Drink / Food, Note, Sleep / **Bowels**, **Symptom**, **Medicine**. Nine is the ceiling; anything more belongs under a tile or on its own tab, so the grid stays a glance and not a menu. The three new tiles are things logged several times a day between check-ins.
- **Symptom** (v92, `openAdd('symptom', entry)`, icon `#i-symptom`): "Which symptom?" as single-choice chips (`SYMPTOMS`: Sickness, Mouth, Skin, Breathing, Dizziness, Tingling, Tiredness, Other; Other reveals a text field, up to 40 characters), "How bad right now?" as the 1 to 10 slider (1 mild, 10 severe), time, a note with Tap to speak. Stored { type "symptom", what (the chip label or the typed word), value, note }. Timeline "Sickness 6/10", sub "Symptom · note", bar `--cat-symptom` (a plum, #7A4E7E light, #C48BC8 dark); in `EDITABLE_ENTRY_TYPES`. Notes for the team: a Symptoms topic (worst per symptom; 7 or more red, 5 or 6 amber, like pain: "Symptoms logged between check-ins: breathing up to 8/10 on 4 Oct; sickness up to 6/10 on 2 Oct (3 readings)"; one amber reading folds into the one-offs; otherwise the green line gets "symptoms (2 readings, none above 4/10)"); symptom notes join the Notes section ("with sickness 6/10"). Kept separate from the evening check-in's sickness score, so nothing is counted twice. No Trends chart (the kinds vary); the timeline and the Notes line carry it.
- **Medicine** (v92, `openDoseSheet()`, the pill icon; the only tile not routed through `openAdd()`): "Log a dose", one `.pickrow.doserow` per medicine, the when-needed ones first with their `prnStatus()` pill (Can be given now, Next from 14:20, Maximum reached), then today's scheduled ones with "1 of 2 today" (green once complete, teal until then); a tap closes the sheet and calls the Meds tab's own `logMed(m)`, so a blocked when-needed dose still asks "Log anyway?" first. "For another time, use the Meds tab." No medicines yet shows the pill empty state. The most frequent between-times action was the furthest away until this.
- **Bowels** (v91, below). Tested in `pw/v92.js` (nine tiles in three rows, the Symptom sheet's chips, Other field, slider, save, Edit, a typed symptom, the dose sheet's rows and pills, a dose logged, Next from, Log anyway and Cancel, the red Symptoms line, axe on Today and both sheets, dark and 320px with no label clipped).

## Bowels (since v91, 4 October 2026)
A seventh quick-add tile on Today (**Bowels**, `data-add="bowel"`, icon `#i-bowel`).
- **The sheet** (`openAdd('bowel', entry)`): "What was it like?" then the Bristol Stool Chart as seven rows (`BRISTOL`, `.bristol-row`, one tap, `aria-pressed`; a small line drawing each from `bristolArt(t)`, the wording from the NHS charts: separate hard lumps, lumpy sausage, cracked sausage, smooth and soft, soft blobs, mushy, watery); "Anything else? Tick what applies" as six chips (`BOWEL_FLAGS`: blood (red), black or tarry, mucus, urgent or an accident, pain or straining, at night, the things the UKONS 24-hour triage tool and Macmillan ask about); time; a note with Tap to speak; and **No bowel movement today / this day** (`.btn-plain`, not shown when editing), because constipation is an absence and cannot be flagged without a record of it. Save without a type says "Tap the type first". Stored as `entries/{autoId}` { type "bowel", none (bool), bristol (1 to 7, null for none), blood, black, mucus, urgent, pain, night (bools), note, day, at, addedBy, createdAt }. **Several types in one movement (v109):** Mark's movement one night was a mix, so the seven rows toggle (tap again to untick) and the hint says "If it was a mix, tap every type in it."; saved as `types` (every type ticked, lowest first) with `bristol` kept as the loosest of them, so older readers err towards the type that matters. Everything reads through `bowelTypes(e)` (falls back to `[bristol]` for entries before v109), `isLoose()` (any type 6 or 7), `isHard()` (any 1 or 2) and `bowelTypeText()`; the timeline reads "Bowels: mixed, types 2 and 6" with the names under it, the table "Types 2, 6", and Notes for the team adds "hard and loose in the same movement on 5 Oct" (amber at least). Tested in `pw/v109.js`; `pw/v91.js` unticks before changing type. A "none" is refused when the day already has one or a real movement; a real movement saved on a day with a "none" deletes the none. `bowel` is in `EDITABLE_ENTRY_TYPES`, so the "..." menu offers Edit and the sheet reopens pre-filled.
- **Timeline:** "Bowels: type 6, mushy" with the flags and note under it ("Blood (red) · At night · Cramps first · by Mark"), or "No bowel movement"; bar colour `--cat-bowel` (an ochre, #8A6D3B light, #C9A86A dark). The viewer role cannot read `bowel` entries (its allowed types in the rules are unchanged); family and read-only can.
- **Trends:** a "Bowel movements per day" card after Drinks per day, a stacked bar (types 1 to 5 in teal, loose, type 6 or 7, in warm; a "none" day is empty) with the Chart / Table switch (`CHART_TABLE_KEYS` has `bowel`; the table shows "Type 6" with the name, flags and note under it, "None" for a none day; `tableLevel('bowel')`: red for blood or black, or loose at night; amber for loose; else plain).
- **Notes for the team** (`summaryRows()`, the Bowels topic, thresholds from the UKONS triage tool and Macmillan; loose means type 6 or 7): red for 4 or more loose in a day (the text adds "4 or more in a day is the point to ring the team"), any blood or black stool, loose at night, or three days in a row with none; amber for 1 to 3 loose in a day, two days in a row with none, or hard stools (type 1 or 2) on three or more days; a single loose day with nothing else folds into the one-offs line; otherwise the green line gets "bowels (5 movements, types 3 to 5, none on 1 day)". A "none" day counts only where nothing else was logged that day. Bowel notes join the Notes section ("with bowels type 6, blood (red)", `noteContext()`).
- Demo: four entries over three days (two loose on day -2, one of them urgent). Rules: no change and nothing to publish (`entries` is family write with no field validation). Tested in `pw/v91.js` (the seven tiles, the sheet's rows, drawings, chips and none button, save without a type, one pressed row, save with flags, Edit pre-filled, a none day and its refusal, a real one replacing the none, the Trends card, the table's levels and fit, the red Notes line, axe on Today, the sheet, Trends and Notes, dark and 320px).
- Sources: Macmillan (ring the team at 4 or more a day), the Bristol Stool Chart (Bladder and Bowel UK), the UKONS 24-hour triage tool and NHS England's treatment-induced diarrhoea guidance (grade 1 up to 3 over usual, grade 2 four to six, grade 3 seven or more; constipation grade 2 at 48 hours, grade 3 at 72), CTCAE v5 via eviQ.

## Privacy, terms and About (since v90, roadmap item 14 until the reorder of 4 October 2026)
Three plain English documents inside the app, as `<template id="tpl-privacy|tpl-terms|tpl-about">` in `index.html`, shown in the ordinary sheet by `openLegal(which)` (`LEGAL_PAGES`), so they open from the sign-in screen as well as from inside the app. Reached from: the Settings App card (three `.legal-links` buttons: Privacy, Terms of use, About Daybook), the sign-in card foot (`.legal-foot`, three `.legal-link` text buttons), the sign-up hint (`.btn-inline` links to the terms and the privacy policy, "By creating an account you agree to..."), the About page's own Privacy and Terms buttons, and `?page=privacy|terms|about` in the address (`readPageLink()`, which drops `page` and keeps any `tab`), so the store listings and the ICO can be given `https://learning-development667.github.io/Mark_Medical/?page=privacy`. Any element with `data-page` opens its page (one delegated click handler).
- Privacy: who is responsible (Mark Brown trading as Mark 1 Apps), the short version, what is stored (sign-in, the household's records, what the phone keeps), where (Firestore, London, europe-west2, over HTTPS; the app from GitHub Pages), who can see it (members by role, the operator's administrative access and what it is and is not used for, Google, nobody else), what leaves the household's folder and when (the AI service only on a tap, the person's own AI app, PDF/print/share/copy, Tap to speak, Apple Health and the bridge's trimmed last message, reminders through the push services with the nameless default, fonts and libraries from Google Fonts and cdnjs, the bridge on Cloudflare), the legal basis (the service asked for plus explicit consent for health data), how long (until deleted; export and erase on request within 30 days until action 8 in `ROADMAP.md`, Export and delete, ships), rights and the ICO, children (18 or over to hold a sign-in; carers may record for someone they look after), the demo, changes. Terms: twelve short numbered sections (what it is and not medical advice with 111 and 999, your account, your records, fair use, recordings, the AI features, availability, no warranty and limited liability, the code and the name, ending, law of England and Wales, changes). About: the strapline, what it is, made by, the version (`data-version`), not medical advice, accessibility, Add to Home Screen, built with, data sources, licence.
- Contact details: `window.DAYBOOK_CONTACT = { email }` in `config.js` (optional, documented in `config.example.js`) fills every `[data-contact]` span with "Contact: " and a mailto link; without it the pages read "Contact: Mark Brown, Mark 1 Apps." and name no address. Not set as of v90: Mark decides which address is published before the app is offered beyond the household.
- Dated "Last updated 1 October 2026" (`.legal-date`); change the date when the text changes. Styles under `/* Privacy, terms and About */` in `styles.css` (`.legal h3` small Bebas headings, 17px body at 1.5 line height). Read-only and viewer roles see them like anyone else. Tested in `pw/v90.js` (the sign-in foot, each page's headings and wording, no em dashes, Escape and the X, the About page swapping to Terms, the sign-up links, `?page=terms&tab=meds`, an unknown page, the Settings buttons in dark and at 320px with no sideways scroll, focus returning to the button, axe on every sheet and the sign-in screen).

## Sign-in picture (since v65)
`icons/signin-bg.jpg` (a Firefly watercolour Mark made: an open window onto a misty garden at dawn, in the app's teal; 1400 px wide, 93 KB) sits behind the sign-in card only (`.signin`, cover, centred, under a gradient wash of the page colour, heavier in dark mode). Every word on that screen is inside the opaque card, so no text ever sits on the picture and the contrast checks are unaffected. Nothing else in the app has a background picture: on the data screens a picture would fight the cards and charts and cost contrast for a tired reader, so the tabs stay plain by design.

## Opener (since v62, rebuilt v88; the Mark 1 Apps film was replaced by the watercolour clip in v222, see above: the film, navy and glow described here are history)
The loading page, `#opener` in `index.html` (a fixed overlay above everything, `role="status"`, `--opener-bg` #030214 sampled from the film itself so the video box has no visible edge, the same in both colour schemes). Top to bottom: **Daybook** as the headline (`.opener-brand`, Fraunces 600 at `clamp(60px, 19vw, 110px)` since v122, Bebas Neue until then, a soft text glow in the mark's own electric blue `--opener-glow` #0093FF, used nowhere else in the app) with the strapline under it; the Mark 1 Apps film, smaller than the name (`.opener-film`, `min(72%, 360px)` wide, its edges feathered with a radial mask so the mark floats on the page); the foot (`.opener-foot`): Skip while the film runs, then "Made by Mark 1 Apps", "Copyright 2026 Mark Brown, Mark 1 Apps. All rights reserved." and the version, all small mono. Behaviour in the `opener()` block at the end of `scripts.js`: shown on every cold start while the account check runs; the film plays every time (until v88 only on a phone's first visit, `daybook.opener.seen`, then a still, which Mark rightly called broken-looking), from one second in (`OPENER_START_S`, the quiet lead-in, so about three seconds of a four-second film; seeked on `loadedmetadata` before `play()`), with Skip and a tap anywhere ending it, a decode error or `OPENER_CAP_MS` (4.5 s) ending it too; the page leaves (a 420 ms fade, then `hidden`) once the film is done and `#app`, `#signin` or `#noconfig` is visible (a `MutationObserver` on `hidden`), half a second at least, and after 15 s regardless. Reduced motion: no film, the still frame for as long as loading takes. The Settings App card carries the mark and "Made by Mark 1 Apps" (`.madeby`). The harness sets `localStorage` `daybook.opener.nofilm` in its init script so the film never delays a test (the headless Chromium cannot decode H.264 anyway, so the seek is verified only by reading the code and on a phone); `pw/opener.js` covers the layout, the foot, the film on a later visit, the tap, reduced motion and the harness flag.

## Daybook Assistant (since v60; "Explain in Daybook" until v116)
The second of two routes to a plain English explanation, for people with no AI app of their own. The first route, unchanged, is the share sheet ("Send to my AI app" / "Copy for my AI app", any assistant or the phone's own Writing Tools, reply pasted back). Both are offered side by side and the hint under them says plainly that either way the text goes to an AI company's servers and that nothing is as private as keeping it to yourself.
- App: `window.DAYBOOK_BRIDGE.url` in `config.js` is the bridge's address; without it the two buttons stay hidden (`explainAvailable()`, which also needs `auth.currentUser`, so the in-memory preview never shows them; the shared demo does). `bridgeExplain(payload)` posts to `/explain` with the person's Firebase ID token. On a document, **Explain with Daybook Assistant** (`#doc-explain`, above the share buttons, which became secondary) sends the text or the page photos, puts the reply in the explanation box with `NOT_MEDICAL_ADVICE` under it and saves it (asks first if one is already saved). On Notes for the team, **Suggest questions to ask** (`#notes-explain`) sends the report text without `NOTES_PROMPT` and opens a "Questions to ask" sheet (`openSuggestedQuestions()`, `parseNumberedList()`): one row per question with an Add button that writes a `question` entry, so nothing is added without a tap; Done re-renders the report. `withBusy()` shows "Explaining, about half a minute" on the button. Errors from the bridge come back as a toast in the bridge's own words ("not switched on yet", the daily limit, a refusal). Hidden for read-only (`body.is-readonly`).
- Bridge: `handleExplain()` in `worker/src/index.js`: `verifyFirebaseToken()` (RS256 against `securetoken@system.gserviceaccount.com`'s JWKs, cached by Cache-Control; audience must be the service account's project or `DEMO_PROJECT_ID`), the household from `users/{uid}.household` and a family check on its members record for the real project (the demo uses its one household), the daily cap in `households/{hid}/bridge/explainLog` { day, real, demo, tokensIn, tokensOut } bumped before the call (`EXPLAIN_LIMIT_REAL` 40 per household, `EXPLAIN_LIMIT_DEMO` 12), then `askClaude()`: one non-streaming Messages call over fetch (`EXPLAIN_MODEL` claude-opus-5-5 since v98, claude-opus-5 until then, `max_tokens` 4000, adaptive thinking by default, `fallbacks: "default"` with the `server-side-fallback-2026-07-01` beta and a one-off retry without it if the account lacks that beta), page photos as base64 image blocks, the two system prompts in `EXPLAIN_SYSTEM` (UK English, calm, no em dashes, ends with "Worth asking the team:"; questions as a numbered list of at most eight). `stop_reason: "refusal"` becomes `422 refused`. Since v98 a failed call to the AI service no longer comes back as a bare error (which the app could only show as "Could not reach Daybook's AI service"): `claudeError()` keeps the status and the API's own message, `handleExplain()` catches it, gives the day's count back and answers 502 with `aiErrorMessage()`'s plain words (key not accepted for a 401, no credit left when the API says the credit balance is too low, not allowed for a 403, model not available for a 404, too large, limit reached for a 429, busy for a 5xx, otherwise the API's reason in brackets); the raw reason goes to the worker's log (`console.error`, seen with `wrangler tail` or the Cloudflare dashboard), and the top-level catch now carries a `message` too. Unit-tested in the scratchpad (`ai_err_test.mjs`). **v100, the real cause:** from v75 until v100 Explain never worked for a real household. `handleExplain()` read the household and role with the module's field reader `f()`, then further down declared its own `const f` for the daily count, so every real sign-in hit "Cannot access 'f' before initialization" (shown on the phone as 'f2', the bundler's renaming) before the AI service was ever called; the demo skips those lines and the app tests route the bridge, so nothing caught it. The local is now `logFields`. Never name a local `f` in the worker. `explain_e2e.mjs` in the scratchpad runs `/explain` for a real family sign-in end to end (a signed ID token, the service account token, Firestore and the Anthropic API all mocked) and reproduced the error on v99; run it after any change to `handleExplain()`. The reply is returned and nothing about the letter is kept; only token counts go into the log for Mark's accounting. No SDK, no dependency, in keeping with the worker's no-build rule. `export const _test` exposes the two functions for the scratchpad harness.
- Set-up: an Anthropic Console account with a small one-off credit (the cost rule allows about $5; a letter costs a few pence at most, so it lasts years), an API key in the `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` repository secret, the deploy workflow's "Set the AI key" step hands it to the worker. Until then the bridge answers "not switched on yet" and the app says so.

## Medicine reminders (since v58)
Push notifications at the times set per medicine, sent by the bridge, and a with-meals prompt in the app.
- `medicines/{id}.times` (array of "HH:MM", scheduled medicines only) and `withMeals` (bool) are set in the medicine editor ("Reminder times", chips with a time input and Add time; "Ask after every meal"). `medWithMeals(m)` treats a medicine saved before the field existed as with-meals when its how-and-when text contains "meal" (Creon's "With each meal"), so the prompt worked from day one.
- With-meals prompt: after a food entry is saved (not edited), `promptMealMeds(mealAt)` opens a "With this meal" sheet with one block per active with-meals medicine that has no dose logged within 45 minutes of the meal: "Yes, 2 capsules at 14:41" (logs the `med` entry at the meal's time, Undo in the toast), "Remind me in 15 minutes" (only on a phone with reminders on: writes `reminders/{autoId}` { at, medId, medName, dose, sent: false, addedBy }), "Not this time". Works in the preview and the demo (Yes logs in memory or the demo project; the reminder is not offered).
- Reminders on this phone (More > Settings > Reminders, `#settings-reminders`, `syncReminders()`/`setReminders()`): a Web Push subscription per phone, made with the public VAPID key in `config.js` (`window.DAYBOOK_PUSH.publicKey`, generated by `tools/vapid_keys.mjs`) and stored as `pushSubs/{id}` { endpoint, keys { p256dh, auth }, private? (bool, since v74), addedBy, uid, agent, addedAt } (`pushSubId()` hashes the endpoint). Off deletes the document and unsubscribes. The switch is disabled with a reason where push is not possible: the preview and the demo, a browser without push, an iPhone that has not added Daybook to the Home Screen (Safari only allows push for installed web apps), or notifications blocked in the phone's settings. `state.pushEnabled` reflects this phone.
- The bridge (`worker/`): a cron trigger every five minutes (`[triggers] crons` in `wrangler.toml`, free plan) runs `runReminders()`, household by household (`runHouseholdReminders()`): for each active scheduled medicine with `times`, in London time (`londonNow()`, `Europe/London`), a notification "Dalteparin 7,500 units is due" within 20 minutes after each time unless a dose for it was logged today from 60 minutes before the time onwards, and one nudge "Still to take: ..." 30 minutes after the time if still not logged; due `reminders` documents are sent ("Reminder: Creon 2 capsules") and marked `sent` (deleted a day later). `households/{hid}/bridge/reminderLog` { day, sent map } stops repeats across runs. Each notification goes to every `pushSubs` document in that household; a 404 or 410 from the push service deletes that subscription. `GET /push-test?key=<inbox key>` sends "Reminders are working on this phone." to every phone in that household, `GET /remind-now?key=<BRIDGE_KEY>` runs the check by hand for every household and returns what it did. Web Push is done with WebCrypto alone (`sendPush()`: VAPID ES256 token from the `VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY` secret, a JWK, `vapidHeader()`; payload encrypted per RFC 8291 aes128gcm, `encryptPayload()`; checked in the scratchpad against a simulated subscriber). The deploy workflow sets `VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY` on the worker when the repository secret exists; without it the bridge answers `skipped` and nothing is sent.
- Private notifications (since v74, `ROADMAP.md` item 4 at the time): per phone, on unless switched off, under the Reminders switch ("Keep medicine names off the lock screen", `#settings-private`, `syncPrivate()`, shown only while reminders are on). Stored as `pushSubs/{id}.private`; a phone saved before v74 has no field and counts as private. The bridge's `send()` gives a private phone the same notification with a nameless body (`payloadFor()`, `isPrivateSub()`): "A medicine is due", "A medicine is still to take", "Reminder: a medicine to take"; opening it lands on the Meds tab, which shows which one. Unit-tested in the scratchpad (`_test.payloadFor`).
- `sw.js` shows the notification (`push`: title, body, tag, icon) and a tap opens or focuses Daybook on `?tab=meds` (`notificationclick`); `enterApp()` honours `?tab=` and drops it from the address bar.
- Rules: `pushSubs` and `reminders` are family read/write (the bridge's service account bypasses rules). Notifications land a minute or two after the time at most, never to the second; Focus modes silence them like any other; clearing Safari's data means turning the switch on again.

## Bridge (Cloudflare Worker, `worker/`)
- Deployed as `care-log-bridge` on workers.dev by `.github/workflows/deploy-worker.yml` whenever `worker/**` changes on `main` (or by hand from the Actions tab). The job stops early with a notice, not a failure, until the four repository secrets exist: `CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN`, `CLOUDFLARE_ACCOUNT_ID`, `BRIDGE_KEY`, `FIREBASE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT`.
- Writes to Firestore through the REST API with the Firebase service account (JWT signed with WebCrypto, token cached for the hour). A service account bypasses `firestore.rules`, so nothing in the rules changes for it.
- `POST /health`: the inbox for the Health Auto Export app (iOS, Premium/lifetime, background REST automation). Auth is the `X-Care-Log-Key` header carrying the household's inbox key (since v75, see Households; until then the single `BRIDGE_KEY`), and everything below is written inside that household. Metric `step_count` rows are summed per day into `exercise/{day}.steps` (merge, `addedBy: "Apple Health"`). Metric `sleep_analysis` rows become `entries/{day}_sleep` (deterministic id, `day` is the morning the sleep ended, `value` minutes asleep, `core`/`deep`/`rem`/`awake` minutes, `bedAt`/`wokeAt` "HH:MM" from the sleep start and end, `at` the sleep end, `addedBy: "Apple Health"`, `source: "health-auto-export"`), so re-sends update the same document. `data.workouts` (sent when Workouts is ticked in the automation) become `exercise/{day}.workouts`, one map per workout { name, minutes (from start and end, or a bare `duration`), km? (`distanceKm()`: `distance.qty` in km whatever the unit, miles, metres, yards or feet, since v73; before that a pool swim in metres would have read as kilometres), kcal? (`activeEnergyBurned.qty`), start? "HH:MM" }, the day's list replaced on each send. The last raw payload is kept at `households/{hid}/bridge/last` (trimmed to 20 KB) to check the exact field names after the first real send; the parser reads the sleep fields leniently (`asleep`/`totalSleep`, `sleepStart`/`inBedStart`, `sleepEnd`/`inBedEnd`).
- `POST /explain` is Daybook Assistant (since v60, see below). `GET /ping` is a health check; `/last` and `/push-test` take `?key=<inbox key>` and `/remind-now` `?key=<BRIDGE_KEY>`, see Medicine reminders. CORS is allowed only for the GitHub Pages origin, for the app's own future calls (nutrition lookups).
- `POST /food { barcode }` (since v172) looks a barcode up in Open Food Facts for the Food sheet's scanner and caches the public answer for a day at Cloudflare's edge; see Scan a barcode on the Food sheet.

## Report ranges (since v42)
Notes for the team and the Food diary share one range control (`wireReportRange()`, `reportRange()`, `reportRangeLabel()`): the 7/14/30/90 day presets plus Custom, which shows two native date inputs (`#food-custom-from/-to`, `#notes-custom-from/-to`; To defaults to today, From to 14 days earlier, both capped at today). To may not be before From, and the range is capped at `CUSTOM_RANGE_MAX_DAYS` (180) with a short inline message (`.hint-warn`) that leaves the previous range showing. The header reads "From 21 Sep 2026 to 3 Oct 2026" for a custom range and keeps "Last 14 days, from ..." for presets; the PDF, print and the Send/Copy text use the same `from`/`to` (both inclusive; entries are fetched from `from` and filtered to `<= to`, `buildNotesReport(entries, from, to)`, `vitalsFlags(entries, from, to)`), and the Send/Copy text states the range in its first line. The last custom range lives in `state` (`foodRangeMode`, `foodCustomFrom/To`, the same for `notes`) for the session only, never in Firestore. Reason: appointments do not fall on neat 7 or 14 day boundaries.

## Navigation
Six bottom tabs: Today, Meds, Reports (Trends until v118), Treatment, Exercise, More. The topbar names the page (`setBrand()`, `PAGE_TITLES`, since v43): "Care Log: Today", "Care Log: Medicines", "Care Log: Trends", "Care Log: Chemo plan", "Care Log: Food diary" and so on, with the page part lighter and smaller (`.brand-page`), so the report pages and the Chemo tab carry no heading of their own. The Chemo tab is called "Chemo plan" (it was "Chemo Party Plan" until v43; renamed with a wider audience in mind). More is a short menu of rows (`.menu`, reusing the `.checkin-row` look): Letters, results and paperwork; Saved meals; Settings (its own view, `view-settings`, Back returns to More, highlights the More tab) with the Food toggle and the App card; then Who to contact stays inline (since v110; Who to call until then), since phone numbers should be one tap away, and Sign out. "Vitals" is the name of the reading you log (the Today quick-add button and the Log vitals button); "Trends" is the look-back tab: a Reports row (Notes for the team, Food diary, Documents), the Latest tiles and every chart. The tab's internal ids stay `vitals` (`view-vitals`, `data-tab="vitals"`). Documents also lives under More (chemo plan documents under Chemo, and the exemption certificate under Meds); Saved meals, who to call and the app section are under More. Report pages remember the tab they were opened from (`state.reportReturn`, `state.docsReturn`) for Back and for tab highlighting.

## Households (since v75)
One locked folder per household. Everything a household records lives under `households/{hid}/...` (entries, medicines, documents and pages, recordings and parts, profile, days, exercise, meals, nutrition, pushSubs, reminders, plus `private` for its secrets and `bridge` for the bridge's logs). `households/{hid}` is { name, owner (uid), createdAt }. Who belongs is `households/{hid}/members/{uid}` { name, role "family"|"readonly"|"viewer", relation? "patient"|"carer"|"", addedBy, addedAt }, and `users/{uid}` { household } is the pointer a sign-in follows (own-read only). `firestore.rules` gates every path on that members record (`isMember(hid)`, `hasRole(hid, roles)`; roles keep the meanings below), so a member of one household cannot read a byte of another and a stranger nothing at all, enforced by the database, not the app. Nobody writes a household, a members record or a pointer from the app: the bridge (a service account) and the Actions-tab workflows do. A sign-in with no pointer, or one whose household has no members record for it, is refused with "This account is not in a Daybook household yet." One sign-in belongs to one household.
- App: `loadAccount()` reads `users/{uid}` then `households/{hid}/members/{uid}`, returns { name, role, relation, household } and caches it on the phone (a cache from before v75 names no household and is ignored); `enterApp()` sets `state.household`, cleared on sign-out. `hcol()`/`hdoc()` are the only route to data (the module comment above them says so); the shared demo uses the fixed household `demo` (`DEMO_HOUSEHOLD`, in the app and the bridge); the in-memory preview never touches Firestore.
- Bridge: `/health` takes the household's own inbox key (`healthKeys/{sha256(key)}` { household, label, createdAt }; keys are never stored, only hashes); reminders run household by household (`runHouseholdReminders()`), a phone only ever hears about its own; `/explain` finds the household from `users/{uid}.household` and requires a family members record, counting the daily cap in `households/{hid}/bridge/explainLog`; `/last` and `/push-test` take an inbox key, `/remind-now` the operator's `BRIDGE_KEY`. The inbox key is also stored at `households/{hid}/private/health` { key } (family may read it) and shown under More > Settings > Apple Health (`#settings-health`, `syncHealthCard()`), so an automation can be set up without the console.
- Workflows (Actions tab, the bridge's `FIREBASE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT`; nothing secret typed in or printed): **Household admin** (`tools/household_admin.mjs`: create, add-member, remove-member, new-key, list; it replaced "Seed a user record"; since v78 the app does create, invite and remove itself, so the workflow is for a rescue and for `new-key`), **Households, move the data in** (`tools/migrate_households.mjs`: copy, verify, cleanup, see below), **Publish database rules** (`tools/publish_rules.mjs`: the Firebase Rules REST API with the service account, a ruleset from the file then the `cloud.firestore` release, real or demo project, so the file in git is what is live; firebase-tools' own deploy was tried first and failed because the Admin SDK service account may not ask Service Usage whether Firestore is enabled).
- Migration of the pre-v75 shared layout, done once, with no downtime: (1) `copy` creates the household (or reuses the one that exists), makes members from the old `users/{uid}` records and sets their pointers, copies every collection by the same ids (so re-runs only catch stragglers), links `BRIDGE_KEY` as the inbox key; (2) publish the rules, which carry a TRANSITION block that still answers the old layout to the old `users/{uid}.role`, so an unrefreshed phone keeps working; (3) push the app; (4) `copy` again, `verify`, then `cleanup` with the confirmation text deletes the old top-level data (never `households` or `users`); (5) remove the TRANSITION block from the rules and publish again.
- v76 shipped with `hcol()`/`hdoc()` calling themselves (the bulk substitution had rewritten their own bodies), so a real sign-in opened an app that loaded nothing; fixed in v77 and caught by `pw/signin75.js` in the scratchpad harness, which signs in through the pointer and the members record with every database call recorded. Keep that test in the harness for any change to sign-in or the helpers.
- Tests: `tests/rules/households.test.mjs` (eight cases: family in their own household, another household, strangers signed in or not, read-only writing, the viewer slice, the records that decide access, the size guard, the transition layout), run locally on the emulator before anything went near live data, and by `rules-test.yml` on every push touching the rules.
- Legal, for when other households join: registering with the ICO (about £52 a year), the privacy policy (done in v90), and Apple's rule that healthcare apps be submitted by a legal entity (the Paperwork list in `ROADMAP.md`).

## Sign-up and invites (since v78)
Nobody needs the Firebase console or the Actions tab any more. The app makes the sign-in (Firebase Auth, `createUserWithEmailAndPassword`); the bridge alone makes the records that decide access, after checking the ID token: `POST /household` { name, relation } (a sign-in with no household becomes the family owner of a new one; refused with 409 `already` when it has one; `HOUSEHOLDS_A_DAY` 20 across everyone, counted in `bridge/global`), `POST /invite` { name, role, relation } (family only: a one-use code, `randomCode()`, stored as `households/{hid}/invites/{code}` { name, role, relation, createdBy, createdByName, createdAt, expiresAt (7 days, `INVITE_DAYS`), used, usedBy?, usedAt? } plus the lookup `inviteCodes/{code}` { household, expiresAt }; returns { code, url: the app address with `?invite=code`, expiresAt, householdName }), `GET /invite-info?code=` (no sign-in needed, the code is the secret: { valid, householdName, name, role, from } or the reason it is not), `POST /join` { code, name? } (a sign-in with no household takes the invite's role and relation; the invite is marked used and its lookup removed), `POST /member-remove` { uid } (the owner only, never themselves; the members record, the pointer and their push subscriptions go). `whoIs()` is the shared token check; `memberOf()` the pointer-plus-members read. The demo project refuses all five.
- Sign-in screen: **Create an account** (`#signup-button`, hidden without the bridge) opens `#signup`: first name, The patient / A carer / Family or friend chips (`relationChips()`), email, password (8 or more), an invite code field, a one-line privacy note. Submit makes the sign-in, then `/join` with a code or `/household` without, then `enterApp()`. If the sign-in was made but the bridge call failed, the person lands on the no-household screen, never in limbo. **Forgotten your password?** sends Firebase's reset email to the typed address. `friendlyAuthError()` covers email-already-in-use, weak-password, invalid-email, operation-not-allowed.
- `?invite=CODE` in the address (`readInviteLink()`): kept as `state.pendingInvite`, dropped from the address bar, and a banner on the sign-in screen says who invited whom to which household with what access (from `/invite-info`); Create an account pre-fills the code, and signing in with an existing sign-in that has no household joins automatically (`enterApp()`'s missing branch).
- No-household screen (`#nohousehold`, `showNoHousehold()`): a sign-in with no pointer or no members record stays signed in and sees "x is signed in, but not in a Daybook household yet": first name, relation chips, an invite code box (the button reads Join with this invite once a code is typed, else Create my own Daybook), Sign out. Until v78 that case signed the person out with a message.
- Settings: the **Household** card (`#settings-household`, `syncHouseholdCard()`, family only, not the demo): the household's name, every member with role, relation, owner and you (family may read `members`), Remove with an inline confirm for the owner, and **Invite someone** (`openInviteSheet()`: first name, Family / Read only / Medicines and treatment only with a one-line hint each, A carer / Family or friend / The patient, **Make the invite link**, then the link in a `.keybox` and **Share the invite** through the share sheet or the clipboard, "works once and expires in 7 days").
- `bridgeCall(path, payload)` is the one signed-in call to the bridge (`bridgeExplain()` sits on it). Rules: `inviteCodes` and the top-level `bridge` are denied to everyone (tested). Tested in `pw/v78.js` (sign-up with its own household, email already in use, the invite banner and sign-up with a code, an expired link, the no-household screen with a bad and a good code, forgotten password, the Household card, Remove, the invite sheet; axe on the two new screens and the sheet). Firebase Auth must allow sign-up (Authentication > Settings > User actions, on by default).

## Access
Accounts are data, not code (since v44; households since v75, above). Each sign-in is created under Authentication > Users in the Firebase console, then added to a household with the Household admin workflow, which writes `households/{hid}/members/{uid}` and `users/{uid}.household`. No email address appears in the rules, in `config.js` or anywhere on the site; the repository is public, so that matters. A read that fails or times out (weak signal, the phone waking the app) returns `{ error: true }` after three retries and the person stays signed in, with a "Could not reach Daybook to check your account" message and the Sign in button re-running the check (`enterApp()`, which the auth listener also calls); the last good record is cached on the phone (`localStorage`, `daybook.account.{uid}`) so a weak signal at start-up opens the app at once. `stopData()` resets `recentFrom`, `recentEntries` and `dayEntries` (until v52 a sign-out and sign-in without a reload left the recent-entries feed dead). Two family accounts (Mark, the patient, and Shelley, the carer) have full access. Sign-up and invite links since v78 (above).

### Full read-only access (since v35)
Hayley's account sees everything a family member sees: all six tabs, every report (Notes for the team, Food diary, Documents), every chart, every document, exactly like Mark or Shelley, but cannot create, edit or delete anything anywhere. This is deliberately different from the narrower "viewer" role below, added after that narrower slice turned out not to be enough. Set up by adding them to the household with role `readonly` (Household admin workflow); the rules grant read on every collection in the household (`entries` of every type, `medicines`, `documents` and its `pages`, `profile`, `days`, `exercise`, `meals`, `nutrition`, `recordings`) and write stays family-only on all of them, enforced at the database level.
Signing in as read-only (`state.account.role === "readonly"`, `state.readOnly`) sets `setReadOnlyMode(true)`: a "Read only" pill shows in the topbar (`#readonly-pill`); the bottom nav and landing tab are untouched (all six tabs, lands on Today like anyone else); `startData()` still skips `seedMedicinesIfEmpty()` (a write) but otherwise loads every collection exactly as for a family member. Every add/edit/delete control is hidden throughout the app (`body.is-readonly` in `styles.css`, targeting `.qa`, `#pain-now`, `#meds-manage`, `.med-actions`, `#meds-doc-add`, `#vitals-log`, `#chemo-doc-add`, `#ex-steps-edit`, `#ex-goals-edit`, `#doc-add`, `#doc-edit`, `#doc-paste-summary`, `#doc-save-explanation`, `#doc-delete`, `#more-meals`, `#calls-edit`), while the underlying information stays visible: dose-time chips and the timeline's "..." entry menu render as plain non-interactive text/nothing instead of a button (mirroring the existing `state.viewer` pattern), the exercise goal buttons and Today's check-in rows are disabled in place rather than hidden so their status text stays readable, the document explanation textarea is set `readOnly` rather than removed, and the chemo calendar's day cells stay tappable but `openDaySheet()` renders a plain-text summary (chemo status, mood, "one good thing") with a Close button instead of the checkboxes and Save/Remove buttons a family member gets.

### Narrow read-only viewer
A further account can be added for someone who should see only Mark's medicines, the chemo plan and mood, and the Trends charts, with nothing else visible at all (not even Today, Exercise, More or the reports). Set up by adding them to the household with role `viewer` (none exist today). The rules grant read-only access on `medicines`, `days` (chemo/mood) and `entries` of type `med`, `temp`, `vitals`, `weight`, `sleep` or `drink` only; `food` and `note` entries, `documents`, `exercise`, `profile` and `meals` stay family-only, so this is enforced at the database level, not just hidden in the UI.
Signing in as a viewer (`state.account.role === "viewer"`, `state.viewer`) sets `setViewerMode(true)`: the bottom nav drops to three tabs (Meds, Chemo, Trends), landing on Meds; a "View only" pill shows in the topbar; and every add/edit/delete control on those three tabs is hidden (Log now/Other time/Manage medicines, the Meds tab exemption certificate section, Log vitals, the Reports row, the Chemo cycle section (`#chemo-cycle-section`) and plan documents section, and the calendar days stop being tappable). Today, Exercise, More and the reports are never reachable by a viewer.

## Shared demo (since v56)
A demo people can actually use, as opposed to the in-memory preview below: a SEPARATE Firebase project (free tier, its own database, its own rules in `firestore.demo.rules`: any signed-in user reads and writes everything) with one sign-in, the guest, whose email and password are public by design and live in `config.js` as `window.DAYBOOK_DEMO = { firebase: { ...the demo project's web config }, email, password }`. When that is present the Guest button runs `enterLiveDemo()` instead of the preview: it initialises a second Firebase app (`initializeApp(DEMO.firebase, 'demo')`, `memoryLocalCache`, `inMemoryPersistence` so nothing lingers on the phone), signs in as the guest, points the module-level `auth` and `db` (both `let` since v56; everything reads them at call time, `mainAuth`/`mainDb` keep the real ones) at the demo project, seeds the example data if the demo is empty (`seedDemoIfEmpty()`: the same `buildDemoFixture()` the preview uses plus `SEED_MEDICINES`, written in batches with `demoTs` values turned into Timestamps), then runs the app exactly as for a family member through `enterApp()` (`loadAccount()` returns Guest, family, household `demo`, no `users` record needed). The topbar pill reads "Demo", More says "Guest (shared demo, wiped every night)", the sign-in hint says the same, and a toast warns that others can see what is added and to enter no real details. "Leave the demo" under More signs the guest out and switches `auth`/`db` back (`exitLiveDemo()`). Nothing in it can reach the real project: different app object, different database, different rules. The bridge and Apple Health are not connected to it. `.github/workflows/reset-demo.yml` runs `tools/reset_demo.mjs` nightly at 03:20 UTC (and by hand): with the `DEMO_SERVICE_ACCOUNT` repository secret (the demo project's service account, never the real one; the script refuses a service account whose project id equals `REAL_PROJECT_ID`) it deletes every document under `households/demo/` (`entries`, `medicines`, `documents` and their `pages`, `recordings` and their `parts`, `profile`, `days`, `exercise`, `meals`, `nutrition`, `bridge`, `pushSubs`, `reminders`, `private`) and, until they are gone, the same top-level collections from before v75, and the app reseeds on the next visit. Switched on 2026-09-28; the first run removed 111 documents. Set-up, once: create the project, turn on Email/Password sign-in and add the guest user, create the Firestore database (London), publish `firestore.demo.rules`, paste the web config and the guest's details into `config.js`, add `DEMO_SERVICE_ACCOUNT` to the repository secrets. Without `window.DAYBOOK_DEMO`, Guest is the preview below.

## Guest preview mode
A "Guest" button on the sign-in screen gives a fully interactive preview of the app with made-up example data, no Firebase account, no sign-in, no Firestore access at all: nothing to create in the console, nothing to hand out. Tapping it sets `state.demo = true` and calls `startDemoData()`, which fills `state` from `buildDemoFixture()` (realistic made-up entries, a chemo calendar, three cycles of check-ins and carer's views, exercise history). Every quick add, edit and delete throughout the file branches on `state.demo` to mutate that in-memory state and re-render, instead of calling Firestore, so the whole app is explorable but nothing is ever written anywhere; it is gone on refresh or on "Leave preview". A `Preview` pill shows in the topbar throughout. Because this path never touches `auth` or `db`, there is nothing to add to `firestore.rules` for it, and no risk of it ever reaching real data.
